# Phase 1 signal details

One section per signal in the register. Fields come from the generating agent, revised by the vetting agent where it changed them. Claim labels (TESTED HERE, LITERATURE, REASONED, UNKNOWN) appear inside the text where the agents applied them.

## S01 Satellite parking-lot car counts (benchmark only)

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Consumer discretionary: big-box and specialty retail, restaurants, auto dealers with surface parking. Target: revenue, EPS surprise (nowcast).
- Seed item: [physical] Satellite parking-lot car counts (use only as a crowded benchmark; assume mostly priced in)
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: No $0 or $100/month version exists: free Sentinel-2 (10 m) and Landsat (30 m) pixels are larger than a car, and the only usable inputs are paid sub-metre imagery or vendor counts priced far above budget. The signal is crowdedness-1 by the candidate's own admission and the JFQA paper shows the gap is captured by sophisticated traders before the release. Retain the citation as the reference point for crowding in the write-up, but it is not a buildable or tradeable signal for this program.
- Mechanism: Cars parked at a chain's stores during the quarter proxy visit counts; visits times ticket size approximate same-store sales, the main driver of the quarter's revenue and operating leverage for box retailers, casual-dining chains and dealer groups. The chain is direct only where the lot is dedicated, visible from orbit and the format is car-dependent (suburban big-box, dealers), and it breaks for mall, urban and e-commerce-heavy names.
- Industry context: Store-based retailers and restaurants live or die on traffic, and same-store sales is the number the stock moves on at earnings. Parking lots are the only part of that traffic visible from orbit, which is why satellite vendors built the first commercial alternative-data products on them.
- Measurement window: Vendor products deliver near-daily counts with 1-3 day latency, so the full fiscal-quarter count is final 2-5 days after quarter end, roughly 20-40 days before the release. With free imagery there is no usable reading: Sentinel-2 (10 m) and Landsat (30 m) pixels are larger than a car (REASONED), so counting needs 30-50 cm commercial imagery.. Lead time: 20 days.
- Universe coverage: ~6% (about 80 names) in principle; 0% at the program's budget because no free reading exists
- Hypothesis: A positive change of 1 standard deviation in year-over-year quarterly parking-lot car counts (vendor feed) over the fiscal quarter, measured 5 days before the earnings release, predicts a positive T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus the sector ETF and a positive revenue surprise, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum. Expectation: coefficient positive but insignificant after 2018 because the signal is already traded.
- Who already knows: Sell-side retail analysts and most quant funds buy the vendor counts. LITERATURE: Katona, Painter, Patatoukas and Zeng, 'On the Capital Market Consequences of Big Data: Evidence from Outer Space' (JFQA 2025, SSRN 2018) show satellite parking signals predict earnings surprises and that sophisticated traders capture the gap before the release; Zhu, 'Big Data as a Governance Mechanism' (RFS 2019) uses the same data. Assume mostly priced in by T-1.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Orbital Insight, RS Metrics (satellite counts); Placer.ai, Advan, SafeGraph (mobile foot traffic as a substitute). Why ours differs: It does not: there is no cheaper, faster or more granular free version. Keep as the crowdedness-1 reference point against which the other physical ideas are judged, and drop as a trade.
- Vendors found by reviewer: RS Metrics (parking-lot satellite analysis since 2011, hedge-fund clients) - https://qz.com/1652293/how-to-make-money-with-rs-metrics-and-orbital-insight-space-data; Orbital Insight (satellite car counts; ~70 hedge-fund clients by 2016 per press) - https://www.bloombergquint.com/businessweek/orbital-insight-uses-satellite-imagery-to-interrogate-the-world; Planet Labs (sub-metre SkySat imagery, paid) - https://www.planet.com/
- Literature found by reviewer: Katona, Painter, Patatoukas and Zeng, On the Capital Market Consequences of Big Data: Evidence from Outer Space, JFQA 60(2) 2025, pp. 551-579 - https://www.cambridge.org/core/journals/journal-of-financial-and-quantitative-analysis/article/on-the-capital-market-consequences-of-big-data-evidence-from-outer-space/2F5F99D68D1F8940F61578F198D6C005; Berkeley Haas newsroom (2019), How hedge funds use satellite images to beat Wall Street - https://newsroom.haas.berkeley.edu/how-hedge-funds-use-satellite-images-to-beat-wall-street-and-main-street/
- Data sources:
  - Microsoft Planetary Computer STAC (Sentinel-2 L2A, Landsat C2 L2) - free but too coarse for car counts [DATA] https://planetarycomputer.microsoft.com/api/stac/v1/search (update: Sentinel-2 ~5-day revisit, Landsat 8-day combined (REASONED); history: 2015-06 to present (TESTED in Phase 0))
  - Element84 Earth Search STAC (Sentinel-2 COGs on AWS) - same resolution limit [DATA] https://earth-search.aws.element84.com/v1/collections (update: as above; history: 2015+ (Phase 0))
  - Sub-meter commercial imagery (Maxar, Planet SkySat, Airbus Pleiades) - required for counts, paid [UNPROBED] https://www.planet.com/ (update: tasked / daily (REASONED); history: vendor archive, paid)
- Reviewer source checks: https://planetarycomputer.microsoft.com/api/stac/v1/search -> DATA (Phase 0) Sentinel-2/Landsat only; resolution too coarse to count cars (REASONED); https://earth-search.aws.element84.com/v1/collections -> DATA (Phase 0) Same resolution limit; https://www.planet.com/ -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Reachable but paid; no free car-count product; pricing not verified
- Cost: $0 version does not exist; counts require sub-meter imagery or a vendor feed priced far above $100/month (REASONED; typical hedge-fund data contracts are five to six figures per year). Treat as a benchmark, not a buildable signal.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 1 / 3 / 1. Confidence: 3%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): The literature shows the gap closes before the release as institutional buyers of the feed trade it; counts miss basket size, online mix and price, and weather and holidays shift lots without shifting sales.
- Red team (reviewer): Already priced: the JFQA paper documents short sellers capturing the parking-lot gap in the weeks before the release, and the vendors have sold the counts to dozens of funds since 2011-2016. Even with a paid feed, counts miss basket size, price and online mix, and weather and holidays shift lots without shifting sales. At $0 there is literally no reading, so any attempt here would be a proxy for what vendor clients already traded.

## S02 VIIRS night-light and thermal activity at single large plants

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Materials and energy: aluminum and steel mills, refineries, chemical complexes, cement kilns, coke plants. Target: revenue, margin (nowcast).
- Seed item: [physical] Night-time light intensity (NASA VIIRS) and thermal imagery of plants, smelters, refineries
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Drop refiners (PBF, DK) from the target list: Wood Mackenzie Genscape's Refinery Stream Monitor and Kayrros already sell refinery activity to desks, so that slice is crowdedness 1-2. (2) Lead with thermal detections (FIRMS VIIRS 375 m, static/industrial source types; EOG VNF if ever reachable) rather than Black Marble radiance: the plant-level literature (Elvidge 2023) is built on infrared emitters, and radiance is contaminated by moonlight, snow and neighbours at 500 m. (3) Restrict to EAF/blast-furnace, smelter, coke, kiln and cracker sites of sub-$5B names with 1-3 dominant sites (CENX, CLF, SXC, OLN, TROX, LXU, EXP and similar). (4) Reframe the target: the headline on/off state is disclosed by 8-K/press and priced, so the hypothesis must test only quarters where the measured site-days-active drop was NOT preceded by a disclosure before the measurement date, and otherwise treat the series as a duration estimator for disclosed outages. (5) First step is a feasibility count: distinct cloud-free detections per site per quarter over 2019-2025; if the median is below ~20 detections per site per quarter, stop. (6) Lower signal_noise from 3 to 2 because the series is close to binary and the event count will be small.
- Mechanism: Continuous-process plants (smelters, blast furnaces and EAFs, refineries, crackers, kilns) emit light and heat roughly in proportion to whether and how hard they run; a line idling or an unplanned outage darkens and cools the site within a day. For companies whose quarter depends on one to three such sites, days of darkness during the quarter map directly to lost volumes, under-absorbed fixed costs and a revenue/margin miss, and resumption dates feed next-quarter guidance.
- Industry context: Smelters, mills and refineries run around the clock and cannot be throttled cheaply, so volume is nearly binary at the line level and a single outage can erase a quarter's profit. Because fixed costs dominate, days of downtime hit margin more than revenue.
- Measurement window: Daily VIIRS radiance (Black Marble VNP46A2, 500 m) and 375 m thermal anomalies (FIRMS) with 1-3 day latency; the full-quarter site-days-active reading is final about 3 days after quarter end, roughly 20-40 days before the release. Landsat 8/9 thermal (100 m) adds detail every 8 days but with cloud gaps.. Lead time: 20 days.
- Universe coverage: ~3%: about 40 names with EAF/BF, smelter, coke, kiln or cracker sites and 1-3 dominant plants; refiners removed
- Hypothesis: A decline of more than 15% in cloud-screened VIIRS thermal-anomaly site-days (FIRMS 375 m detections of static/industrial sources within the plant polygon) at a company's EAF, blast-furnace, smelter, coke, kiln or cracker sites over the fiscal quarter versus the same quarter a year earlier, measured 20 days before the earnings release and restricted to cases with no prior 8-K or press disclosure of the outage, predicts a negative revenue surprise and negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus the sector ETF, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Major outages are usually disclosed by 8-K or press within days and tracked by IIR Energy and Wood Mackenzie Genscape (refinery/power monitoring), so the headline on/off state is largely known; the under-covered gap is the duration of partial run-rates at small-cap single-site names. Macro use is LITERATURE (Henderson, Storeygard and Weil, AER 2012); plant-level equity use: UNKNOWN.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Wood Mackenzie Genscape (refinery and power plant monitoring), IIR Energy (outage database); SpaceKnow markets satellite activity indices (REASONED on exact coverage). Why ours differs: Free daily 375-500 m thermal and radiance is enough to measure site-days-active at a named plant, and we target sub-$5B single-site names where vendor monitoring is thin, rather than the refinery complexes Genscape already covers.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Wood Mackenzie Genscape Refinery Stream Monitor (weekly US refinery intelligence from in-field monitors plus satellite) - https://www.globenewswire.com/news-release/2019/08/13/1901086/0/en/Genscape-Launches-Refinery-Stream-Monitor.html; Wood Mackenzie commodity trading analytics (live cameras, infrared, satellite, power-line sensors) - https://www.woodmac.com/industry/commodity-trading-analytics/; Kayrros refinery observation (SAR, geolocation, NLP data fusion) - https://www.kayrros.com/blog/data-fusion-enables-predictive-refinery-observation/
- Literature found by reviewer: Elvidge et al., Global Satellite Monitoring of Exothermic Industrial Activity via Infrared Emissions, Remote Sensing 2023 (VIIRS Nightfire; ~20,000 IR emitters; site-level profiles for production, disruption and recovery) - https://mdpi.com/2072-4292/15/19/4760; Yang, Ai and Arkolakis, A Geospatial Approach to Measuring Economic Activity, NBER WP 33619 (2025) (steel plants; nighttime lights, ozone and heat predict plant output, R2 above 0.8 cross-sectionally) - https://www.nber.org/papers/w33619; arXiv 2301.09620 (2023), industrial site development at 419 Chinese sites using panchromatic imagery with VIIRS radiance tested as a proxy - https://arxiv.org/pdf/2301.09620v3
- Data sources:
  - NASA FIRMS area CSV (VIIRS SNPP/NOAA-20 375 m thermal anomalies; static industrial sources and flares) [KEY] https://firms.modaps.eosdis.nasa.gov/api/area/csv/DEMO_KEY/VIIRS_SNPP_NRT/world/1 (update: near-real-time, ~3 h latency (REASONED); history: 2012+ via FIRMS archive download (REASONED))
  - NASA Black Marble VNP46A2 daily radiance (secondary) [KEY] https://ladsweb.modaps.eosdis.nasa.gov/archive/allData/5000/VNP46A2/2025/001/ (update: daily, 1-2 day latency (REASONED); history: 2012+ (REASONED))
  - Microsoft Planetary Computer STAC landsat-c2-l2 thermal band [DATA] https://planetarycomputer.microsoft.com/api/stac/v1/search (update: 8-day combined revisit; history: Landsat archive (deep; REASONED))
  - EOG VIIRS Nightfire (VNF) daily product - alternative if reachable elsewhere [UNPROBED (TESTED HERE: 000 TLS failure from container)] https://eogdata.mines.edu/products/vnf/ (update: daily (REASONED); history: 2012+ (REASONED))
  - EDGAR 8-K latest-filings atom feed (to flag disclosed outages before the measurement date) [DATA] https://www.sec.gov/cgi-bin/browse-edgar?action=getcurrent&type=8-K&count=100&output=atom (update: continuous; history: rolling window; backfill from daily index)
- Reviewer source checks: https://ladsweb.modaps.eosdis.nasa.gov/archive/allData/5000/VNP46A2/2025/001/ -> KEY (Phase 0) Free Earthdata login plus LAADS licence acceptance; demoted to secondary input; https://firms.modaps.eosdis.nasa.gov/api/area/csv/DEMO_KEY/VIIRS_SNPP_NRT/world… -> KEY (Phase 0) Free MAP_KEY; primary thermal input; archive via FIRMS download tool (REASONED); https://gibs.earthdata.nasa.gov/wmts/epsg4326/best/VIIRS_SNPP_DayNightBand_At_S… -> DATA (Phase 0) Visual QA only; https://planetarycomputer.microsoft.com/api/stac/v1/search -> DATA (Phase 0) Landsat thermal band as 8-day supplement; https://eogdata.mines.edu/products/vnf/ -> 000 TLS failure (TESTED HERE) curl: SSL certificate problem through the relay; proxy status reports the CA bu…
- Cost: $0: free Earthdata Login for Black Marble and a free FIRMS MAP_KEY; compute and storage only. Google Earth Engine would simplify but its free tier is non-commercial only (Phase 0 flag), so avoid.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 15%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Outages are disclosed and priced quickly; cloud, snow and moonlight contaminate radiance; 500 m pixels mix the plant with neighbours; and the signal is rare and binary, so few events survive to backtest.
- Red team (reviewer): The equity-relevant event (a line down) is almost always disclosed by 8-K or press within days and sits in consensus by T-1; what remains measurable at 375-500 m (partial run-rates) is contaminated by cloud, snow, moonlight and neighbouring sources. The plant-level literature shows cross-sectional output levels, not within-plant quarterly changes. With ~40 names and rare undisclosed outages, a backtest will have perhaps 10-20 usable quarters, so any coefficient is fragile and the polygon list is easy to overfit.

## S03 Sentinel-5P NO2/CH4 columns and VIIRS flaring over named facilities and basins

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Energy and materials: shale E&Ps (Permian, Bakken), refiners, integrated steel, nitrogen and petrochemical plants. Target: revenue, margin (nowcast).
- Seed item: [physical] Flaring and emissions from Sentinel-5P (methane, NO2) as output proxies for oil, steel, chemicals
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: Three independent kills. (a) Resolution and detection limit: Beirle et al. resolve point sources only after wind-field divergence processing and long averaging, with a detection limit of 0.03-0.11 kg/s NOx (roughly 1,000-3,500 t/yr), and the newer TROPOMI NO2 validation covers 18 large power plants; the steel-plant literature uses CO, not NO2. A single quarter of clear-sky overpasses over one mid-cap plant in an industrial corridor cannot separate the plant's throughput change from wind, boundary-layer and neighbour variance. (b) Legal: Carbon Mapper's current terms (January 2026) grant a non-commercial licence only and prohibit commercial use without a separate agreement, so the E&P incident flag is AMBER and must be dropped. (c) Crowding: flaring and basin methane are Kayrros' core product, and steel volumes are anchored weekly by public AISI production data, so the residual gap is small. The free FIRMS thermal component is folded into S02.
- Mechanism: Combustion at a refinery, steel mill or ammonia plant emits NO2 in proportion to fuel burned, and TROPOMI resolves large point sources daily at 3.5 x 5.5 km (LITERATURE: Beirle et al., Science Advances 2019, 'Pinpointing nitrogen oxide emissions from space'); a quarter of lower NO2 over the site means lower throughput and revenue. Upstream, VIIRS-detected flaring and Carbon Mapper methane plumes in a producer's acreage rise when gas takeaway is constrained, flagging curtailed oil production or wasted-gas costs that hit the quarter's volumes and lease operating margin.
- Industry context: Furnaces, reformers and crackers burn gas continuously, so the site's NO2 plume is a rough fuel meter; shale producers flare when pipelines are full, so flaring marks takeaway bottlenecks that cap oil output. Both are physical traces of volume that the companies report only at quarter end.
- Measurement window: Daily S5P and VIIRS passes with ~1 day latency; site-level NO2 must be aggregated over the quarter (clouds, wind) so the quarter reading is final about 3 days after quarter end, 20-40 days before the release. Carbon Mapper plumes are event-based with irregular revisit, so they flag incidents rather than throughput.. Lead time: 20 days.
- Universe coverage: ~8% nominal (about 100 names); not buildable at single-plant quarterly resolution
- Hypothesis: A negative change of 1 standard deviation in the quarter's cloud-screened, wind-adjusted mean NO2 column over a company's plant polygons versus the trailing four-quarter mean, measured 20 days before the earnings release, predicts a negative revenue surprise and negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return for single-site process companies, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Refinery throughput is public weekly at PADD level from EIA, and Enverus/state RRC data cover producer volumes with a 2-3 month lag, so consensus is well anchored for refiners; Kayrros sells methane and flaring intelligence to funds. The gap is company-specific run-rate at steel, nitrogen and chemical sites, which few equity investors measure (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Kayrros (methane, flaring, energy infrastructure monitoring); Carbon Mapper is public; Enverus for producer volumes. Why ours differs: Apply the free daily TROPOMI NO2 column to a fixed list of mid-cap steel, nitrogen and chemical plants rather than the oil and gas basins vendors cover, and use Carbon Mapper plumes as a free incident flag for E&P acreage.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Kayrros (methane and flaring monitoring from Sentinel-5P; Equity Tracker marketed to equity investors) - https://www.kayrros.com/fr/latest-news/page/2/; Kayrros Methane Watch (ESA business project) - https://business.esa.int/projects/methane-watch
- Literature found by reviewer: Beirle et al., Pinpointing nitrogen oxide emissions from space, Science Advances 5(11) 2019 (divergence method; detection limit ~0.03-0.11 kg/s) - https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC6853767; Schneising et al., TROPOMI CO from steel production sites 2018-2022, ACP 24, 7609 (2024) - https://acp.copernicus.org/articles/24/7609/2024/acp-24-7609-2024.pdf; ACP 25, 555 (2025): CO emissions of the 21 highest-emitting European iron and steel plants from TROPOMI - https://acp.copernicus.org/articles/25/555/2025/; ACP 26, 4405 (2026): TROPOMI NO2 versus bottom-up NOx at 18 European and US power plants - https://acp.copernicus.org/articles/26/4405/2026/acp-26-4405-2026.html; Carbon Mapper Terms of Use (non-commercial licence only; commercial use requires separate agreement) - https://carbonmapper.org/terms
- Data sources:
  - Copernicus Data Space OData catalogue (Sentinel-5P L2 NO2, CH4) [DATA] https://catalogue.dataspace.copernicus.eu/odata/v1/Products?$filter=Collection/Name%20eq%20'SENTINEL-5P' (update: daily; history: 2017-11-14 to present (Phase 0))
  - Copernicus Data Space download (OAuth token) / Sentinel Hub Statistical API CDSE edition [KEY] https://sh.dataspace.copernicus.eu/api/v1/statistics (update: daily; history: full S5P archive (Phase 0))
  - Carbon Mapper plume catalog API (CC BY 4.0) [DATA] https://api.carbonmapper.org/api/v1/catalog/plumes/annotated?limit=1 (update: irregular, campaign and satellite revisit (REASONED); history: 46,503 plumes at Phase 0; start date not queried)
  - NASA FIRMS (VIIRS thermal anomalies; flare detections) [KEY] https://firms.modaps.eosdis.nasa.gov/api/area/csv/DEMO_KEY/VIIRS_SNPP_NRT/world/1 (update: near-real-time; history: 2012+ (REASONED))
  - EOG VIIRS Nightfire (VNF) flaring product, Colorado School of Mines [UNPROBED] https://eogdata.mines.edu/products/vnf/ (update: daily/monthly (REASONED); history: 2012+ (REASONED); TESTED HERE: TLS failure through the container proxy, not reachable from here)
- Reviewer source checks: https://catalogue.dataspace.copernicus.eu/odata/v1/Products?$filter=Collection/… -> DATA (Phase 0) Catalogue search works; downloads need free CDSE token; https://sh.dataspace.copernicus.eu/api/v1/statistics -> KEY (Phase 0) Free CDSE OAuth client; processing-unit quota not verified; https://api.carbonmapper.org/api/v1/catalog/plumes/annotated?limit=1 -> DATA (Phase 0) but licence AMBER Terms found in search: non-commercial only as of January 2026; not CC BY 4.0 as…; https://firms.modaps.eosdis.nasa.gov/api/area/csv/DEMO_KEY/VIIRS_SNPP_NRT/world… -> KEY (Phase 0) Free; used in S02 instead; https://eogdata.mines.edu/products/vnf/ -> 000 TLS failure (TESTED HERE) Not reachable from the container
- Cost: $0: free CDSE account and OAuth client, free FIRMS key; Sentinel Hub Statistical API has a free processing-unit quota on CDSE (REASONED). Compute for per-site daily aggregation is modest.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 1 / 3. Confidence: 8%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): A 3.5 x 5.5 km pixel over an industrial corridor mixes several plants, roads and power stations, and weather drives most day-to-day variance; by the time a quarter of data is averaged, the company's volumes are usually inferable from public EIA, rail and pricing data.
- Red team (reviewer): A 3.5 x 5.5 km column over an industrial corridor mixes several plants, roads and power stations, and day-to-day variance is weather; by the time a quarter is averaged the throughput of refiners, E&Ps and steel mills is already inferable from EIA weekly, AISI weekly and state production data, and Kayrros sells the methane and flaring layer to funds. The one legally free incident flag (Carbon Mapper) is non-commercial.

## S04 AIS vessel calls at single-company bulk, LNG and fertilizer terminals

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Energy, materials, marine transport: coal and met-coal exporters, LNG and FSRU operators, phosphate and salt shippers, Jones Act and tanker owners. Target: revenue, EPS surprise (nowcast).
- Seed item: [physical] AIS ship tracking: vessel queues at single-company ports, LNG terminals, and mining export docks
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Replace the paid live feed: aisstream.io (free API key via GitHub login, websocket stream of terrestrial AIS) is reachable from the container (200) and advertises free access; it is UNPROBED beyond reachability and its terms page was not found (only a privacy policy), so legal_risk is set to 3 until the terms are read and confirmed to allow this use. (2) Drop Global Fishing Watch (API licence CC BY-NC 4.0, non-commercial) and MarineTraffic (paid, bot-protected ToS). (3) NOAA MarineCadastre 2025 directory returns 404 (TESTED HERE), confirming the ~11-month archive lag: use 2010-2024 for backtests only and start archiving the live stream immediately, because no free 2025-2026 history exists. (4) Narrow coverage to berths that are genuinely exclusive-use (CONSOL Marine Terminal for CNR, Mosaic's Tampa/Port Sutton berths, NFE and EE FSRU sites, Compass Minerals Goderich) and verify exclusivity per berth from 10-K/permit text before inclusion; shared terminals (DTA, Lamberts Point) only if a company's share is stable and disclosed. (5) Add the consensus anchor: Census monthly exports by port and HS code (free key) arrive ~5-6 weeks after month end, so the edge exists only for the quarter's final month and must be measured as the gap versus the Census-implied run-rate. (6) Lead time cut to 15 days to reflect the time needed to clean draft-based load inference.
- Mechanism: At a terminal owned or exclusively used by one company, each loaded vessel departure is a shipment, so counting calls and draft changes over the quarter nowcasts tons shipped and therefore revenue. The chain holds for exporters with dedicated docks (CONSOL Marine Terminal in Baltimore, Dominion Terminal Associates, Mosaic's Tampa berths, FSRU regas sites) and for shipowners whose fleet utilisation is visible; it fails where terminals are shared by many shippers.
- Industry context: Bulk exporters and LNG operators sell in shiploads, so quarterly volume is a small integer count of vessels, each worth tens of millions of dollars. AIS broadcasts every large vessel's position publicly, which is why ship tracking became the standard alternative-data tool for commodity trading.
- Measurement window: With a live feed, the quarter's call count is final the day after quarter end, 20-40 days before the release. TESTED HERE: NOAA MarineCadastre daily AIS has the full 2024 year posted but no 2025 files as of 2026-11-11, so the free government archive lags about a year and serves backtests only.. Lead time: 15 days.
- Universe coverage: ~1%: about 8-12 names with verifiably exclusive berths (CNR, MOS, NFE, EE, CMP; HCC/AMR only if berth share is disclosed)
- Hypothesis: A positive change of 10% or more in loaded-vessel departures (count, draft-weighted) from a company's exclusive-use terminal over the fiscal quarter versus the prior quarter, measured 15 days before the earnings release and expressed as the residual versus the run-rate implied by the latest Census port-level monthly export tonnage, predicts a positive revenue surprise and positive T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus the sector ETF, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Kpler and Vortexa sell cargo-level tracking for crude, products, LNG and dry bulk to most energy desks, and EIA weekly coal export estimates plus rail carloads anchor coal consensus; so LNG and crude flows are crowdedness 1-2. Niche single-company met-coal, salt and phosphate berths are less covered (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Kpler, Vortexa (cargo tracking); Spire (AIS data); MarineTraffic, VesselFinder (vessel APIs). Why ours differs: Restrict to a handful of exclusive-use berths where a ship count is a shipment count, which vendors treat as a footnote to their global flow products, and use the free NOAA archive to validate before paying for live data.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Kpler coal and dry-bulk cargo tracking (seaborne coal flows, port congestion from vessel queues) - https://www.kpler.com/solutions/fundamental-intelligence/coal; AXSMarine (coal shipments by load port, API use cases) - https://public.axsmarine.com/data-and-apis/api-use-cases; Vortexa (cargo and freight analytics, per Hellenic Shipping News comparison) - https://www.hellenicshippingnews.com/dry-bulk-and-tanker-analytics-for-smarter-vessel-tracking/
- Literature found by reviewer: IMF Working Paper WP/20/57 (2020), AIS-based nowcasting of seaborne trade (port calls) - https://www.imf.org/-/media/Files/Publications/WP/2020/English/wpiea2020057-print-pdf.ashx; The Data Score, predicting China's exports with AIS data (notes AIS dark periods near some ports) - https://thedatascore.substack.com/p/data-deep-dive-predicting-chinas; Global Fishing Watch API licence and rate limits (CC BY-NC 4.0, non-commercial) - https://api-doc.globalfishingwatch.org/our-apis/documentation/docs/license-rate-limits
- Data sources:
  - aisstream.io free AIS websocket stream (archive yourself from day one) [UNPROBED (TESTED HERE: 200; free API key via GitHub; terms UNKNOWN)] https://aisstream.io/ (update: real-time; history: none; self-archived from start date)
  - NOAA MarineCadastre / CMSP daily AIS zips (backtest only) [DATA] https://coast.noaa.gov/htdata/CMSP/AISDataHandler/2024/AIS_2024_01_01.zip (update: annual batch, ~11+ month lag (TESTED HERE: 2025 folder 404); history: 2010 through 2024)
  - Census international trade API (monthly exports by port and HS; consensus anchor) [KEY] https://api.census.gov/data/timeseries/intltrade/imports/hs?get=CTY_CODE,GEN_VAL_MO&time=2024-01 (update: monthly, ~5-6 week lag (REASONED); history: 2013+ (Phase 0))
- Reviewer source checks: https://coast.noaa.gov/htdata/CMSP/AISDataHandler/2024/AIS_2024_01_01.zip -> DATA (Phase 0) 2010-2024 daily zips for backtests; https://coast.noaa.gov/htdata/CMSP/AISDataHandler/2025/ -> 404 (TESTED HERE) No 2025 folder as of 2026-11-11; archive lags ~11+ months; https://www.aishub.net/api -> KEY (Phase 0) Only for members feeding a receiver; limited range; https://gateway.api.globalfishingwatch.org/v3/vessels/search?query=maersk -> KEY (Phase 0) - EXCLUDED Licence CC BY-NC 4.0; non-commercial; https://services.marinetraffic.com/api/exportvessels/ -> KEY paid (Phase 0) - EXCLUDED Paid plans; ToS forbids scraping; https://aisstream.io/ -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Landing page and /documentation both 200; states free API keys created via GitH…; https://www.portoflosangeles.org/business/statistics/container-statistics -> DATA (Phase 0) Shared port; dropped as irrelevant
- Cost: $0 for backtests (NOAA archive). Live: an AIS receiver and antenna (~$100-200 one-off, REASONED) fed to AISHub unlocks their exchange feed but only covers your own antenna's range plus the exchange; a paid MarineTraffic or VesselFinder API plan exceeds $100/month for meaningful volume (REASONED). No keyless live source exists.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 3 / 3. Confidence: 15%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Companies pre-announce shipped tons and the energy-flow vendors already count the vessels, so the free version is backtest-only and the live version is paid and crowded; draft-based cargo inference is noisy for partial loads.
- Red team (reviewer): Kpler and AXSMarine clients already count these cargoes, and Census monthly port-level exports anchor consensus for two of the three months before most releases, so the residual is one month of tonnage at a handful of berths; revenue is tons times price and the index or contract price dominates the surprise. Draft-based load inference is noisy for partial loads, the free archive stops at 2024 so the live history starts from zero, and the feed's commercial terms are unverified.

## S05 Catalogued mine and quarry blast counts (USGS ComCat)

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Materials and energy: Powder River Basin and Western surface coal, open-pit metals, aggregates and cement quarries. Target: revenue, EPS surprise (both).
- Seed item: [physical] Public seismometer networks picking up quarry and mine blasting frequency
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: The catalogue is far too sparse: TESTED HERE the whole Powder River Basin box returned 48 'mining explosion' events in Q3-2025 across roughly a dozen mines, i.e. about 4 catalogued blasts per mine per quarter, so Poisson noise alone is ~50% of the count and any 25% threshold is indistinguishable from chance. Catalogue completeness depends on network configuration and analyst labelling practice, which change over time. The physical chain is also loose: cast blasting removes overburden weeks to months ahead of coal exposure, while shipped tons are set by rail availability and utility stockpiles, and PRB volumes are already visible in EIA's weekly coal production estimates and MSHA quarterly mine-level data. No vendor or paper on blast counts as a production proxy was found; the literature is on discriminating blasts from earthquakes. A custom sub-M2 waveform detector on IRIS data is technically possible and uncrowded, but it is a substantial signal-processing project for ~10 names whose volumes are already public weekly, so it is not worth the effort now.
- Mechanism: Surface mines remove overburden with cast blasts large enough (M2+) that regional seismic networks catalogue them as 'mining explosion' or 'quarry blast'; blast frequency and magnitude over a quarter track overburden moved, which leads coal or ore exposed and shipped by weeks. For companies whose output is concentrated in a few large surface mines, a drop in catalogued blasts signals lower production volumes and a revenue miss, and a surge signals pre-stripping for higher next-quarter output.
- Industry context: Powder River Basin mines strip tens of metres of overburden with huge cast blasts before draglines and shovels reach the coal seam, and the largest aggregates quarries do the same for rock. The seismic network built to locate earthquakes records these shots as a free, public by-product.
- Measurement window: ComCat events are reviewed and posted within hours to days, so the full-quarter blast count per mine polygon is final about 3 days after quarter end, 25-40 days before the release. TESTED HERE: Q3-2025 query over a Powder River Basin box returned 48 'mining explosion' events (M2.3-3.5, NEIC 'us' network); a Utah/Nevada box returned 29 quarry/mining events (M0.6-3.4), so the catalogue only captures the largest blasts.. Lead time: 25 days.
- Universe coverage: ~2% nominal (20-25 names); rejected
- Hypothesis: A negative change of 25% or more in catalogued blast count (M>=2.3) within a company's mine polygons over the fiscal quarter versus the trailing four-quarter mean, measured 20 days before the earnings release, predicts a negative revenue/production surprise and negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return for surface-mining companies, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: UNKNOWN as a sold product; nobody I can name sells blast counts. But PRB coal consensus already uses EIA's weekly coal production estimates (derived from rail carloads) and MSHA quarterly production, so the gap is mine-level attribution and timing within the quarter, not the industry total.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None that I am sure of (UNKNOWN). Why ours differs: Nobody covers it; the question is only whether the signal is strong enough, not whether it is crowded.
- Literature found by reviewer: Allmann et al., BSSA 2008, spectral discrimination of quarry blasts versus earthquakes in southern California (rms misfit to an omega-squared source model; ripple-fired blast signature) - https://igppweb.ucsd.edu/~shearer/mahi/PDF/2008/allmann_bssa08.pdf; Pure and Applied Geophysics (2024), seismic blast/earthquake discrimination article (Utah catalogue review of 2,199 events; neural-network classification) - https://link.springer.com/article/10.1007/s00024-024-03440-0
- Data sources:
  - USGS ComCat FDSN event query filtered to quarry blast / mining explosion / explosion [DATA] https://earthquake.usgs.gov/fdsnws/event/1/query?format=geojson&eventtype=quarry%20blast,mining%20explosion,explosion&starttime=2025-07-01&endtime=2025-10-01&minlatitude=43.3&maxlatitude=44.8&minlongitude=-106.3&maxlongitude=-104.8 (update: continuous, hours-days review lag (REASONED); history: decades (Phase 0); event-type labelling consistent since ~2000s (REASONED))
  - IRIS/EarthScope FDSN dataselect (raw waveforms for custom sub-M2 blast detection near a mine) [DATA] https://service.iris.edu/fdsnws/dataselect/1/query?net=IU&sta=ANMO&loc=00&cha=BHZ&start=2025-01-01T00:00:00&end=2025-01-01T00:10:00 (update: near-real-time; history: decades)
  - IRIS/EarthScope FDSN station metadata (find stations within 50 km of each mine) [DATA] https://service.iris.edu/fdsnws/station/1/query?net=US&format=text&level=network (update: static; history: 1990+)
- Reviewer source checks: https://earthquake.usgs.gov/fdsnws/event/1/query?format=geojson&eventtype=quarr… -> DATA (Phase 0) 48 PRB events in Q3-2025 per the candidate's own test; ~4 per mine per quarter; https://service.iris.edu/fdsnws/dataselect/1/query?net=IU&sta=ANMO&loc=00&cha=B… -> DATA (Phase 0) Raw waveforms available; custom detector would be a large project; https://service.iris.edu/fdsnws/station/1/query?net=US&format=text&level=network -> DATA (Phase 0) Station metadata
- Cost: $0; ComCat is keyless. A custom waveform detector needs only compute.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 5 / 1 / 1. Confidence: 7%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Only blasts above roughly M2.3 are catalogued, giving perhaps 5-15 events per mine per quarter, so counts are noisy and sensitive to network changes; shipments depend on rail availability and customer stockpiles more than on stripping; and PRB volumes are already visible in weekly carloads.
- Red team (reviewer): With ~4 catalogued blasts per mine per quarter the series is noise, and whatever it contains about stripping is two steps removed from shipped tons, which are already visible weekly from rail carloads (EIA estimates) and quarterly from MSHA. Any apparent predictive power would most likely be a coal-price or sector proxy.

## S06 Weekly rail carloads by commodity and DOT truck counts near distribution centres

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Industrials and materials: coal, chemicals, forest products, metals, grain and ethanol shippers; railcar lessors; intermodal and truckload carriers. Target: revenue, EPS surprise (nowcast).
- Seed item: [physical] Truck counts from state DOT traffic counters near large distribution centers; weekly rail carloads by commodity
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Drop the DOT truck-count component entirely: TMAS lags months, PeMS needs account-based access and has almost no single-tenant distribution-centre stations, GDOT requires browser automation, and no vendor-free company mapping exists; coverage would be a handful of California sites at best. (2) Drop the 10-K exposure-weighting of national AAR commodity totals for diversified shippers: company share drifts and price dominates revenue, so the residual is a sector proxy. (3) Keep only names whose reported quarterly volume is a near-identity with a public weekly series: intermodal loads for JBHT and HUBG from BNSF and Norfolk Southern weekly reports (NS page TESTED HERE 200; UP and CSX return 403 from the container), and coal carloads for BTU, CNR, AMR, HCC from AAR weekly coal plus EIA weekly coal production. (4) Reclassify the output as a nowcast feature and consensus anchor for other signals (notably S07 coal and chemicals names), not a standalone trade; crowdedness set to 1 because AAR weekly data are quoted by every transport and coal analyst. (5) Lead time cut to 15 days: the quarter's final AAR week posts about 5 days after quarter end and the carrier reports a few days later.
- Mechanism: Class I railroads report weekly carloads by commodity group, and shippers' volumes are a near-linear function of those carloads for commodities moved mostly by rail (coal, chemicals, grain, lumber, scrap, crushed stone); summing the quarter's weeks nowcasts the shipper's volume and revenue, while intermodal counts nowcast truckload and intermodal carriers' loads. Truck counts at a DOT continuous-count station adjacent to a company's distribution centre do the same for that one facility, but mapping is laborious and few stations sit next to a single-tenant DC.
- Industry context: Coal, chemicals, grain and lumber move mostly by rail and the railroads publish their weekly carloads, so the industry's physical volume is visible in near real time. Shippers report only quarterly, leaving a window where public rail data already describes their quarter.
- Measurement window: AAR weekly data are released each Wednesday for the prior week, so the quarter's last week is available about 5 days after quarter end, 20-40 days before the release; STB EP724 carrier service data are weekly too. TESTED HERE: the FHWA TMAS directory lists monthly 2025 continuous-count files but nothing for 2026 as of 2026-11-11, so TMAS lags too far for a nowcast; Caltrans PeMS (free account) is near-real-time with truck classification at some stations (REASONED).. Lead time: 15 days.
- Universe coverage: ~3%: about 35 names where a public weekly series maps near 1:1 to reported volume (intermodal: JBHT, HUBG; coal: BTU, CNR, AMR, HCC; chemicals/plastics shippers as a weaker group)
- Hypothesis: For companies whose reported quarterly volume is a near-identity with a public weekly series (intermodal units from BNSF and Norfolk Southern weekly reports for JBHT and HUBG; AAR weekly coal carloads and EIA weekly coal production for BTU, CNR, AMR, HCC), the quarter-to-date volume change measured 10 trading days before the earnings release, expressed as the residual versus the consensus-implied volume change, predicts the revenue surprise and T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus the sector ETF, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum. Expectation: coefficient small, because sell-side already folds AAR data into estimates; the series is primarily a consensus anchor for other signals.
- Who already knows: AAR weekly carloads are free and quoted by every sell-side transport and coal analyst, so industry totals are in consensus (crowdedness 1-2 for the rail version). DOT counter data are uncrowded but the company mapping is the hard part and no vendor I am sure of sells it.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None I am sure of for DOT counters; for rail, the AAR itself and Bloomberg terminal functions redistribute the weekly numbers (REASONED). Why ours differs: Build commodity-to-company exposure weights from 10-K segment disclosures so the national weekly series becomes a company-specific volume nowcast, and add PeMS truck classification at the few California DCs that sit beside a count station.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Association of American Railroads (weekly press releases free; Rail Industry Overview monthly; detailed weekly tables paid) - https://www.aar.org/wp-content/uploads/2026/07/AAR-RIO-JULY-6-2026-FINAL.pdf; ACT Research North America Class I railroad data - https://www.actresearch.net/resources/knowledge-center/north-america-class-i-railroad-data; StreetLight Data (traffic analytics from location data) - https://www.streetlightdata.com/?p=18346; Geotab Altitude freight-corridor AADT from telematics - https://altitude.geotab.com/news-and-releases/5-ways-freight-corridor-aadt-data-fills-federal-gaps/
- Literature found by reviewer: FreightWaves commentary, Hiding in plain sight: railroad KPIs suggest continuing weakness (practitioner; argues the best rail KPIs come from non-railroad sources) - https://www.freightwaves.com/news/commentary-hiding-in-plain-sight-railroad-kpis-suggest-continuing-weakness; Heavy Duty Trucking, What railroad statistics can tell us about truck freight volumes (opinion: carloads lead industrial production by 3-4 months; no methodology) - https://www.truckinginfo.com/articles/what-railroad-statistics-can-tell-us-about-truck-freight-volumes; FHWA Traffic Monitoring Guide 2013, traffic monitoring program (continuous vs short-duration counts) - https://www.fhwa.dot.gov/policyinformation/tmguide/tmg_2013/traffic-monitoring-program.cfm
- Data sources:
  - AAR weekly rail traffic press releases (commodity carloads and intermodal) [DATA] https://www.aar.org/data-center/rail-traffic-data/ (update: weekly (Wednesday); history: ~52 pages of releases (Phase 0))
  - BNSF weekly carload reports (PDF) [DATA] https://www.bnsf.com/about-bnsf/financial-information/index.html#Weekly+Carload+Reports (update: weekly; history: Feb 2025 onward)
  - Norfolk Southern weekly performance reports (xlsx, PDFs) [UNPROBED (TESTED HERE: 200)] https://norfolksouthern.investorroom.com/weekly-performance-reports (update: weekly; history: 2023-2026 PDFs linked)
  - STB rail service data (EP 724) [DATA] https://www.stb.gov/reports-data/rail-service-data/ (update: weekly; history: 2014+ (REASONED))
  - EIA Weekly Coal Production estimates (from rail carloads) [UNPROBED] https://www.eia.gov/coal/production/weekly/ (update: weekly; history: multi-year (REASONED))
- Reviewer source checks: https://www.aar.org/data-center/rail-traffic-data/ -> DATA (Phase 0) curl UA required; press-release totals free; detailed tables paid; https://www.stb.gov/reports-data/rail-service-data/ -> DATA (Phase 0) EP 724 weekly carrier metrics; https://www.bnsf.com/about-bnsf/financial-information/index.html#Weekly+Carload… -> DATA (Phase 0) Weekly PDFs from Feb 2025; parse PDFs; https://norfolksouthern.investorroom.com/weekly-performance-reports -> 200 (TESTED HERE) xlsx and 2023-2026 PDFs linked; UP and CSX equivalents 403 from container per c…; https://pems.dot.ca.gov/ -> KEY (Phase 0) - DROPPED Free account; near-real-time; no usable DC mapping; https://www.fhwa.dot.gov/policyinformation/tables/tmasdata/ -> DATA (Phase 0) - DROPPED Multi-month lag; nothing for 2026 as of 2026-11-11; https://gdottrafficdata.drakewell.com/publicmultinodemap.asp -> DATA (Phase 0) - DROPPED Needs browser automation or reverse-engineered ajax; no ToS flag seen but not w…
- Cost: $0; AAR detailed weekly tables are a paid subscription (price not verified) but the press-release commodity totals are free.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 1 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 10%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): National commodity totals are an industry aggregate; a company's share drifts and pricing dominates revenue, and analysts already fold the weekly data into estimates, so the residual gap is small.
- Red team (reviewer): AAR weekly data are the most-watched freight series in the market and are in every sell-side transport and coal model, so the residual after consensus revisions is close to zero and what remains is likely a sector or momentum proxy; a shipper's share of a national commodity group drifts and pricing dominates revenue; the truck-counter branch has no single-tenant coverage and its data lag months.

## S07 Regulatory exhaust of industrial water and power use (NPDES discharge flows, CEMS heat input, EIA-923 cogeneration)

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Materials, energy and paper: refineries, chemical and chlor-alkali plants, pulp and paper mills, aluminum and steel mills, nitrogen and ethanol plants. Target: revenue, margin (nowcast).
- Seed item: [physical] Municipal water and power usage for large industrial users, and permit renewals that reveal it
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) The candidate's timing claim is wrong in the right direction: TESTED HERE the ECHO effluent chart for TX0002798 (Bayer Baytown) shows 'Flow, in conduit or thru treatment plant' for the monitoring period ending 30-SEP-26 with ValueReceivedDate 19-OCT-26, a 19-day lag (the earlier '31-MAY-26' reading came from sorting DD-MON-YY strings as if they were MM/DD/YYYY). So the full fiscal quarter of monthly DMR flow is available roughly 20-28 days after quarter end, before most mid-cap releases. Lead the signal with DMR flow and raise lead_time from 5 to 7 days (median), with CAMPD quarterly heat input (Q3-2026 served on 2026-11-11, ~41 days after quarter end, TESTED HERE) as secondary confirmation for companies reporting after ~day 42. (2) CAMPD coverage is narrower than assumed: TESTED HERE the facilities-attributes endpoint for Louisiana 2025 returns 78 units, 65 'Electric Utility' and 13 'Cogeneration' (Occidental Chemical Taft, Dow Plaquemine, Westlake's RS Cogen, Nelson Industrial Steam for Citgo/P66/Sasol); pure process boilers are not Part 75 reporters, so CAMPD applies only to sites with grid-connected cogeneration (~15 universe names). (3) Drop EIA-923 except for backfill (2-3 month lag). (4) Map outfalls explicitly: use only process and cooling-water outfalls and exclude stormwater outfalls; weather-adjust with NOAA NCEI daily temperature and precipitation at the nearest station. (5) The DMR lag is TESTED for one Texas (TCEQ NetDMR) permit only; the first Phase 1 task is to measure ValueReceivedDate minus MonitoringPeriodEndDate for every target site and state and to drop sites whose median lag exceeds 35 days. (6) Point-in-time discipline: backtests must use ValueReceivedDate, not the current ECHO snapshot, or the lag reintroduces look-ahead. (7) Get a free api.data.gov key to lift the DEMO_KEY throttle. Confidence raised from 20 to 25.
- Mechanism: Customer-level utility usage is confidential, but the same plants must report cooling-water discharge flow monthly under their NPDES permit (ECHO DMRs) and hourly boiler heat input under EPA's emissions programs (CAMPD CEMS), and industrial cogeneration units report fuel and generation on EIA-923. Each scales with plant throughput, so a quarter of lower heat input or discharge flow at a company's main site implies lower volumes and a revenue and margin miss.
- Industry context: Process plants are large steam and cooling-water users, and federal permits make them report that use to EPA at the unit level. The reports exist for compliance, so nobody designed them to be timely, but they are the most exact public record of how hard a plant ran.
- Measurement window: TESTED HERE: the CAMPD API already served Q3-2026 quarterly unit-level heatInput, opTime and grossLoad on 2026-11-11, i.e. within about 41 days of quarter end, so the full-quarter reading is only available for companies reporting later than ~day 40 (lead 1-10 days) and not at all for early reporters. TESTED HERE: ECHO effluent chart for TX0002798 (Bayer Baytown) returns monthly 'Flow, in conduit or thru treatment plant' MGD values, but the latest monitoring period visible was 31-MAY-26, a 4-5 month lag, so DMR flow covers at best months 1-2 of the quarter. EIA-923 monthly runs 2-3 months behind (page cites data through August 2026).. Lead time: 7 days.
- Universe coverage: ~3%: about 30-40 names with 1-3 dominant NPDES-permitted sites reporting monthly flow (refining, chlor-alkali, chemicals, pulp and paper, aluminum, nitrogen, ethanol); CAMPD subset ~15 names with grid-connected cogeneration
- Hypothesis: A negative change of 10% or more in the fiscal-quarter sum of monthly NPDES DMR discharge flow at the process and cooling-water outfalls of a company's dominant site(s) (ECHO effluent chart, weather-adjusted) versus the same quarter a year earlier, where the final month's DMR ValueReceivedDate falls at least 1 trading day before the earnings release, predicts a negative revenue surprise and negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus the sector ETF, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum; CAMPD quarterly heat input at cogeneration-equipped sites is a secondary confirmation for companies reporting after ~day 42.
- Who already knows: Chemical and refining analysts track outages through ICIS, OPIS and IIR Energy and companies disclose major ones, but CAMPD heat input and DMR flows are compliance datasets almost never used by equity investors (REASONED), so the per-site run-rate is uncrowded. Timing, not crowding, is the limit.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Wood Mackenzie Genscape (power and refinery monitoring), IIR Energy (outage database); none sell DMR- or CEMS-derived equity signals that I am sure of. Why ours differs: Uses legally mandated self-reported plant data rather than imagery or sensors, is free, and resolves to the exact unit and month; the trade-off is a 1-5 month lag that restricts it to late reporters and to the months 1-2 nowcast.
- Literature found by reviewer: EPA CAMD Power Sector Emissions Data Guide (2022): Part 75 CEMS reporting of heat input and operating data; some non-EGU industrial boilers report - https://www.epa.gov/system/files/documents/2022-07/CAMD%27s%20Power%20Sector%20Emissions%20Data%20Guide%20-%2007182022.pdf; EPA ECHO NPDES Monitoring Data Download help (monitoring periods selectable from January 2007 to present) - https://echo.epa.gov/node/366; EPA ECHO Pollutant Loading Report help (facility flow computed from DMR-reported flow at all external outfalls) - https://echo.epa.gov/node/316; EPA Part 75 petition response list (e.g., Morris Cogeneration, ORISPL 55216) showing cogeneration units inside the program - https://www.epa.gov/system/files/documents/2025-07/part_75_petition_response_list.pdf
- Data sources:
  - EPA ECHO effluent chart REST service (monthly DMR values incl. flow, with ValueReceivedDate for point-in-time backtests) [DATA] https://echodata.epa.gov/echo/eff_rest_services.get_effluent_chart?p_id=TX0002798&output=JSON&start_date=01/01/2025&end_date=11/01/2026 (update: monthly DMRs; ~19-day lag for the tested Texas permit (TESTED HERE); other states UNKNOWN; history: 2007+ per ECHO download help)
  - EPA ECHO facility search (NPDES IDs by name and state) [DATA] https://echodata.epa.gov/echo/cwa_rest_services.get_facilities?output=JSON&p_fn=Baytown&p_st=TX&p_act=Y (update: snapshot; history: current)
  - EPA CAMPD API quarterly apportioned emissions and operating data (heat input, op time; cogeneration units only for industrials) [UNPROBED (TESTED HERE: 200 with DEMO_KEY; free api.data.gov key lifts throttle)] https://api.epa.gov/easey/emissions-mgmt/emissions/apportioned/quarterly?api_key=DEMO_KEY&year=2026&quarter=3&page=1&perPage=2 (update: quarterly, ~41 days after quarter end (TESTED HERE); history: 1995+ (REASONED))
  - EPA CAMPD facilities attributes (sourceCategory, ownerOperator, unit type) for site mapping [UNPROBED (TESTED HERE: 200)] https://api.epa.gov/easey/facilities-mgmt/facilities/attributes?api_key=DEMO_KEY&year=2025&stateCode=LA&page=1&perPage=300 (update: annual attributes; history: multi-year)
  - NOAA NCEI Access Data Service (daily temperature and precipitation for weather adjustment) [DATA] https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/access/services/data/v1?dataset=daily-summaries&stations=USW00012960&startDate=2024-01-01&endDate=2024-12-31&format=json (update: daily; history: full period of record)
  - EIA-923 monthly generation and fuel (backfill only) [UNPROBED (TESTED HERE: 200)] https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia923/ (update: monthly, 2-3 month lag; history: 2001+ (REASONED))
- Reviewer source checks: https://api.epa.gov/easey/emissions-mgmt/emissions/apportioned/quarterly?api_ke… -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Returns Q3-2026 unit rows with heatInput, sumOpTime, grossLoad (Barry units 1-2…; https://api.epa.gov/easey/facilities-mgmt/facilities/attributes?api_key=DEMO_KE… -> 200 (TESTED HERE) 78 LA units: 65 Electric Utility, 13 Cogeneration (Oxy Taft, Dow Plaquemine, RS…; https://api.epa.gov/easey/master-data-mgmt/source-categories?api_key=DEMO_KEY -> 400 (TESTED HERE) Not a valid path; use /master-data-mgmt/list to enumerate; https://echodata.epa.gov/echo/eff_rest_services.get_effluent_chart?p_id=TX00027… -> DATA (Phase 0; TESTED HERE) 2,228 DMR rows, 260 flow rows; latest flow period 30-SEP-26 received 19-OCT-26 …; https://echodata.epa.gov/echo/cwa_rest_services.get_facilities?output=JSON&p_fn… -> DATA (Phase 0) NPDES ID lookup; https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia923/ -> 200 (TESTED HERE) 2-3 month lag; backfill only; https://api.eia.gov/v2/electricity/facility-fuel/data/?frequency=monthly&data[0… -> KEY (Phase 0) Free EIA key; backfill only; https://data.epa.gov/efservice/pub_dim_facility/year/2023/state_name/TEXAS/rows… -> DATA (Phase 0) Site identification only
- Cost: $0; free api.data.gov key lifts the CAMPD DEMO_KEY throttle, free EIA key.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 4 / 3 / 1. Confidence: 25%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Most of the quarter's data arrives after the earnings date, outages are disclosed in 8-Ks anyway, and flows and heat input move with weather, maintenance turnarounds and product mix rather than sales.
- Red team (reviewer): Discharge flow and heat input move with weather (cooling demand), scheduled turnarounds (disclosed on calls and priced), permit changes and outfall mix (stormwater), not with sales; few universe names are truly single-site, so one site's utilisation maps weakly to company EPS and the sample is small. The 19-day DMR lag is verified for one Texas permit only; if other states upload to ICIS months later, a backtest on the current ECHO snapshot would silently contain look-ahead unless every observation is stamped with ValueReceivedDate.

## S08 River stage, drought and soil-moisture exposure (Mississippi, Rhine, Corn Belt)

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Industrials, materials, consumer staples: inland barge operators, grain and ethanol handlers, fertilizer shippers, ag equipment, crop inputs, US names with Rhine-side European plants. Target: margin, revenue, guidance (both).
- Seed item: [physical] River levels (Rhine, Mississippi) and soil moisture for chemicals, grain, and bulk shipping
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: The information is public in real time and already in consensus by T-1: river stages are front-page news during low-water episodes, USDA's Grain Transportation Report publishes St. Louis barge spot rates every Thursday, Kirby and the chemical companies pre-flag impacts on calls (Kirby flagged low-water delay days in 2023; Covestro declared force majeure at Dormagen in August 2026; BASF quantified its 2018 Rhine hit at about EUR 250m), and sell-side ag and transport analysts (e.g., Evercore ISI sizing the Mississippi at ~20% of Kirby's inland business) update within days. The sign is ambiguous for barge operators (rates up versus lighter loads and delay days: Kirby said low water cut revenue and raised costs in 2013), exposure is a few percent of costs for most shippers, and the drought-to-equipment channel is slow macro that is already in guidance consensus. The gauges remain useful as free control variables in an earnings model but carry no edge.
- Mechanism: Low Mississippi stages at St. Louis and Memphis force draft and tow-size limits, which spike barge freight rates and widen grain basis; that raises logistics costs for grain, ethanol and fertilizer shippers (margin) while lifting barge operators' pricing. Rhine stages below roughly 80 cm at Kaub trigger low-water surcharges and curtailments for US chemical names' European sites. Drought and soil-moisture deficits in the Corn Belt lower yields and farm income, which cuts next-season ag-equipment and crop-input demand (guidance) and raises irrigation equipment orders.
- Industry context: Roughly 60% of US grain exports move down the Mississippi by barge, and a one-foot drop in stage can cut a tow's payload by several percent, so river level is a direct cost input for the grain and fertilizer chain. In Europe the Rhine plays the same role for the chemical corridor between Rotterdam and Basel.
- Measurement window: Gauges are daily and keyless, so the quarter's low-water days and the drought-monitor area are final the day after quarter end, 25-40 days before the release; the same series read in the pre-earnings window informs next-quarter guidance. SMAP soil moisture is daily with 1-3 day latency; USDA crop progress and the Drought Monitor are weekly.. Lead time: 25 days.
- Universe coverage: ~5% nominal (about 60 names); rejected as a standalone signal, usable as control variables
- Hypothesis: An increase of 20 or more low-water days (St. Louis stage below 0 ft or Kaub below 80 cm) in the fiscal quarter versus the same quarter a year earlier, measured 20 days before the earnings release, predicts a negative margin surprise and negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return for barge-dependent shippers and a positive one for inland barge operators, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Low water is front-page news and USDA's weekly Grain Transportation Report publishes barge rates (REASONED), so sell-side ag and transport analysts price it within days; drought effects on equipment demand are widely modelled. The gap is small and lies in translating gauge days into company-specific cost impact faster than analysts update.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None selling an equity signal that I am sure of; commodity desks use DTN and Gro Intelligence-type ag weather products (REASONED). Why ours differs: Company-level exposure weights (barge share of logistics, Rhine-site share of capacity) applied to free daily gauges, rather than the macro commentary analysts already use.
- Literature found by reviewer: USDA AMS Grain Transportation Report (weekly; St. Louis barge spot rates in table 9) - https://www.ams.usda.gov/sites/default/files/media/GTR09102026.pdf; Seatrade Maritime (2023), Fast moving waters: Evercore ISI puts the Mississippi at ~20% of Kirby's inland business with contractual-minimum cushions - https://www.seatrade-maritime.com/shipping-finance/fast-moving-waters-earnings-season-beginning-for-us-listed-names; Kirby Corp 8-K (2011) ex99.1 quantifying the high-water hit at about $0.07 per share - https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/0000056047/000114036111038250/ex99_1.htm; Reuters via Yahoo Finance, German industry changes tack as river Rhine runs drier (BASF ~EUR 250m 2018 earnings hit) - https://sg.finance.yahoo.com/news/analysis-german-industry-changes-tack-044116454.html; Insurance Journal 2026-08-12, Covestro force majeure at Dormagen on low Rhine - https://www.insurancejournal.com/news/international/2026/08/12/881099.htm
- Data sources:
  - USGS NWIS daily values, Mississippi at St. Louis (gage height) [DATA] https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv/?sites=07010000&parameterCd=00065&startDT=2015-01-01&format=json (update: daily; history: 1982-10-01 to present)
  - USGS NWIS instantaneous values (recent) [DATA] https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/iv/?sites=07010000&parameterCd=00065&format=json&period=P7D (update: 15-minute; history: recent window)
  - NOAA NWPS gauge stage/flow with forecast (Memphis EADM7) [DATA] https://api.water.noaa.gov/nwps/v1/gauges/EADM7/stageflow (update: half-hourly, with forecast; history: ~30 days observed)
  - Pegelonline Rhine at Kaub water level [DATA] https://www.pegelonline.wsv.de/webservices/rest-api/v2/stations/KAUB/W/measurements.json?start=P30D (update: 15-minute; history: 31-day rolling via this API; longer history elsewhere (UNKNOWN))
  - US Drought Monitor comprehensive statistics download [UNPROBED] https://droughtmonitor.unl.edu/DmData/DataDownload/ComprehensiveStatistics.aspx (update: weekly (Thursday); history: 2000+ (REASONED); TESTED HERE: page loads with CSV download forms)
  - NASA SMAP L3 soil moisture via CMR + NSIDC [KEY] https://cmr.earthdata.nasa.gov/search/granules.json?short_name=SPL3SMP&page_size=1&sort_key=-start_date (update: daily; history: 2015-03-31+)
  - USDA Crop Progress weekly reports [DATA] https://usda.library.cornell.edu/concern/publications/8336h188j (update: weekly in season; history: 1995+)
  - USDA NASS QuickStats API [KEY] https://quickstats.nass.usda.gov/api/api_GET/?key=DEMO&commodity_desc=CORN&year=2025&format=JSON (update: survey cadence; history: decades)
- Reviewer source checks: https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv/?sites=07010000&parameterCd=00065&startD… -> DATA (Phase 0) Daily stage 1982+; https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/iv/?sites=07010000&parameterCd=00065&format… -> DATA (Phase 0) Recent only; https://api.water.noaa.gov/nwps/v1/gauges/EADM7/stageflow -> DATA (Phase 0) ~30 days observed plus forecast; https://www.pegelonline.wsv.de/webservices/rest-api/v2/stations/KAUB/W/measurem… -> DATA (Phase 0) 31-day rolling only; https://droughtmonitor.unl.edu/DmData/DataDownload/ComprehensiveStatistics.aspx -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Weekly; CSV download forms; https://cmr.earthdata.nasa.gov/search/granules.json?short_name=SPL3SMP&page_siz… -> KEY (Phase 0) Free Earthdata login; https://usda.library.cornell.edu/concern/publications/8336h188j -> DATA (Phase 0) Weekly in season; https://quickstats.nass.usda.gov/api/api_GET/?key=DEMO&commodity_desc=CORN&year… -> KEY (Phase 0) Free NASS key
- Cost: $0; free Earthdata login for SMAP and free NASS key.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 1 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 8%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Effects are known in real time and already in estimates; for most names exposure is a few percent of costs, swamped by commodity prices, so the quarter's surprise is driven elsewhere.
- Red team (reviewer): Low water is real-time public news with a weekly USDA rate series and immediate company commentary, so by T-1 it is fully in estimates; what the gauges would add is a sector and commodity-price proxy, and for barge operators the sign is ambiguous (pricing up versus lighter loads and delay days), so any backtest hit is more likely a KEX-specific rate cycle than a river effect.

## S09 Wastewater viral load as a lead on respiratory-season volumes

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Healthcare (ex-biotech) and consumer staples: diagnostics makers, reference labs, hospitals and urgent care, OTC cough-cold and vaccine distributors. Target: revenue, EPS surprise, guidance (nowcast).
- Seed item: [physical] Wastewater epidemiology as a demand lead for pharma and consumer-health names
- Verdict: REJECT. Changes: Not kept. If a season-severity idea is revisited, build it on CDC FluView/NREVSS (public domain) as a late-quarter surprise versus mid-quarter company commentary, and treat wastewater at most as a 3-day-faster cross-check; drop WastewaterSCAN unless written permission for commercial use is obtained. Reject reason: The quarter-level cumulative wastewater load carries the same information as CDC FluView ILI and NREVSS percent-positive, which are weekly, free, and the exact clinical series that sell-side healthcare analysts and the companies themselves use for season colour; the claimed one-week lead is not supported for flu A or RSV by the 2026 CDC EID California study I found (no consistent lead; RSV wastewater often lags clinical data), and Peccia et al. report only 0-2 days ahead of specimen collection for SARS-CoV-2. TESTED HERE the CDC flu A dataset starts 2021-09-15 and RSV 2022-02-27, so there are at most four usable respiratory seasons, each a single shock hitting all ~35 names at once: effectively four independent observations, which cannot support a backtest. WastewaterSCAN is CC BY-NC 4.0 per CDC's page (AMBER), so the only clean source is CDC, and CDC-only adds nothing over FluView.
- Mechanism: Influenza A, RSV and SARS-CoV-2 RNA in sewage rises about a week before clinical visits and testing (LITERATURE: Peccia et al., Nature Biotechnology 2020, for SARS-CoV-2), so the quarter's cumulative wastewater load nowcasts respiratory test volumes, OTC cough-cold sell-through and hospital admissions. For diagnostics and OTC names the season's severity is the swing factor in the quarter's revenue; for hospitals it drives volumes and contract-labour costs (margin).
- Industry context: Respiratory test and cough-cold sales are seasonal and swing 30-50% between mild and severe winters, so the quarter is largely a bet on epidemiology. Wastewater surveillance, built out during COVID, now reports flu and RSV weekly and leads doctor visits because people shed virus before they seek care.
- Measurement window: Weekly site data with 5-10 day lag, so the reading covering the quarter's last week is final 7-10 days after quarter end, 15-30 days before the release. TESTED HERE: CDC's current Socrata datasets are 'CDC Wastewater Data for Influenza A' (ymmh-divb), RSV (45cq-cw4i) and SARS-CoV-2 (j9g8-acpt), updated 2026-11-06; the older NWSS metric dataset 2ew6-ywp6 stopped at 2025-09-07, so history must be stitched across the migration.. Lead time: 15 days.
- Universe coverage: ~3% (about 35 names), but only ~4 usable seasons of flu/RSV history and one common shock per season
- Hypothesis: A positive change of 1 standard deviation in population-weighted cumulative influenza A plus RSV wastewater load over the fiscal quarter versus the same quarter a year earlier, measured 10 days before the earnings release, predicts a positive revenue surprise and positive T-1 to T+2 abnormal return for respiratory diagnostics and OTC cough-cold names, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Sell-side healthcare analysts track CDC FluView ILI and NREVSS positivity (weekly, public) and companies give intra-quarter colour, so season severity is largely in consensus; wastewater adds roughly one week of lead and is less used (REASONED). Finance use: UNKNOWN.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None I am sure of selling a wastewater-based equity signal; Biobot Analytics and Verily (WastewaterSCAN) are the data producers, not signal vendors. Why ours differs: Free, weekly, pathogen-specific and one week ahead of the clinical series analysts use; map it to the test makers' and OTC names' fiscal calendars rather than the epidemiological week.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Biobot Analytics weekly COVID/flu/RSV wastewater risk reports (data producer, not an equity-signal vendor) - https://biobot.io/risk-reports/covid-19-influenza-and-rsv-wastewater-monitoring-in-the-u-s-week-of-june-24-2024/; WastewaterSCAN (Stanford/Emory/Verily) dashboard - https://data.wastewaterscan.org/about; Verily public health (WastewaterSCAN partner; permission contact for non-research use) - https://publichealth.verily.com/about
- Literature found by reviewer: Peccia et al. 2020, Measurement of SARS-CoV-2 RNA in wastewater tracks community infection dynamics, Nature Biotechnology (0-2 day lead vs specimen date; DOI 10.1038/s41587-020-0684-z per medicalxpress) - https://medicalxpress.com/news/2020-09-wastewater-covid-outbreaks-days-diagnostic.pdf; CDC Emerging Infectious Diseases 2026, California influenza A and RSV wastewater vs clinical lead-lag analysis (no consistent lead; flu A -16 to +22 days, RSV -8 to +35 days) - https://wwwnc.cdc.gov/eid/article/32/13/26-0306_article; CDC Emerging Infectious Diseases 2026 companion wastewater article - https://wwwnc.cdc.gov/eid/article/32/13/26-0141_article; WastewaterSCAN Influenza A research summary (onset matches ILI baseline; offset more conservative than ILI) - https://www.wastewaterscan.org/WWSCAN_Influenza_A_Research_Summary.pdf; National Academies 2024 report on the National Wastewater Surveillance System - https://www.nationalacademies.org/news/2024/09/national-wastewater-surveillance-strengthens-u-s-public-health-efforts-and-future-pandemic-prevention-says-new-report; CDC About Wastewater Monitoring (states WastewaterSCAN database is under Stanford CC BY-NC 4.0 licence) - https://www.cdc.gov/wastewater/about/index.html
- Data sources:
  - CDC Wastewater Data for Influenza A (Socrata) [UNPROBED] https://data.cdc.gov/resource/ymmh-divb.json?$limit=5 (update: weekly (TESTED HERE via catalog: updated 2026-11-06); history: UNKNOWN (likely 2023+ for flu A, REASONED))
  - CDC Wastewater Data for RSV (Socrata) [UNPROBED] https://data.cdc.gov/resource/45cq-cw4i.json?$limit=5 (update: weekly (catalog: updated 2026-11-06); history: UNKNOWN)
  - CDC Wastewater Data for SARS-CoV-2 (Socrata) [UNPROBED] https://data.cdc.gov/resource/j9g8-acpt.json?$limit=5 (update: weekly (catalog: updated 2026-11-06); history: UNKNOWN)
  - CDC NWSS public SARS-CoV-2 wastewater metric (legacy) [DATA] https://data.cdc.gov/resource/2ew6-ywp6.json?$limit=1 (update: stopped (TESTED HERE: max date_end 2025-09-07); history: 2020-06-21 to 2025-09-07)
  - WastewaterSCAN dashboard (flu A/B, RSV, norovirus and more; download via UI or underlying JSON) [DATA] https://data.wastewaterscan.org/ (update: 2-3x weekly per site (REASONED); history: 2020+ (REASONED))
- Reviewer source checks: https://data.cdc.gov/resource/ymmh-divb.json?$limit=5 -> 200 TESTED HERE: flu A; 342,560 rows, 1,509 sites, sample_collect_date 2021-09-15 t…; https://data.cdc.gov/resource/45cq-cw4i.json?$limit=5 -> 200 TESTED HERE: RSV; 323,044 rows, 1,452 sites, 2022-02-27 to 2026-11-03.; https://data.cdc.gov/resource/j9g8-acpt.json?$limit=5 -> 200 TESTED HERE: SARS-CoV-2; 635,100 rows, 1,964 sites, 2020-01-14 to 2026-11-03.; https://data.cdc.gov/resource/2ew6-ywp6.json?$limit=1 -> DATA (Phase 0) Legacy metric dataset, 2020-06-21 to 2025-09-07; SARS-CoV-2 only, not needed fo…; https://data.wastewaterscan.org/ -> 200 Page loads; no licence text found on the dashboard or site pages probed (/en/da…
- Cost: $0; optional free Socrata app token for rate limits.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 3. Confidence: 8%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Consensus already updates weekly on FluView and the extra week adds little to a quarter-level nowcast; site composition and assays changed across the CDC dataset migration, so history is inconsistent and backtests short.
- Red team (reviewer): It is a repackaging of the 'flu season severity' factor that FluView and NREVSS already publish weekly and that every healthcare analyst tracks; the incremental content of wastewater (a few days of lead, itself contested in the literature) is irrelevant to a quarter-level sum, and with four seasons of data and perfect cross-sectional correlation any backtest hit is indistinguishable from one lucky season. Non-commercial licence on WastewaterSCAN adds legal exposure for no information gain.

## S10 Local planning approvals for data centres, plants and warehouses

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Industrials, real estate, technology: site-work and electrical contractors, HVAC and power equipment, industrial REITs, listed data-centre and compute operators. Target: guidance, capex (forward).
- Seed item: [regulatory] Local planning-committee minutes and permit applications for data centers, plants, warehouses, often filed under shell names
- Verdict: REJECT. Changes: Not kept. The only narrow salvageable piece would be a company's own approvals (APLD, WULF-type compute operators) timing capacity announcements, but those companies announce sites and milestones themselves, so it is not worth Phase 1 effort. Reject reason: The mechanism cannot be attributed: a rezoning or site-plan approval does not identify the listed contractor or equipment maker until the award, and the contractors in the list (STRL, IESC, PRIM, DY, AGX, POWL) press-release material awards, so the market learns the attributable fact from the company. Revenue lands 1-4 quarters later and backlog is reported at the release, so the quarter's approvals mostly do not enter the upcoming print. The aggregate pipeline is already sold by Dodge, ConstructConnect, DC Byte, datacenterHawk and DC Hub, and the stocks trade on hyperscaler capex guidance. The manual cost (thousands of jurisdictions, shell-name LLCs, PDF minutes) is far beyond a $0, one-person effort; Legistar's keyword filter works (TESTED HERE) but Legistar covers only a subset of relevant counties.
- Mechanism: Rezonings, special-use permits and site-plan approvals in county and city agendas precede construction starts by months, and construction starts become bookings and backlog for site-work, electrical and mechanical contractors and orders for switchgear, generators and cooling. Counting approved square footage and megawatts per quarter in the jurisdictions where a contractor or equipment maker is dominant nowcasts its book-to-bill and the backlog figure that drives guidance; for data-centre operators, their own approvals signal capacity timelines.
- Industry context: Nothing large gets built without a public zoning or site-plan hearing, and the minutes are public even when the applicant is an LLC. Contractors and equipment makers are valued on backlog, which these approvals feed months ahead.
- Measurement window: Minutes and agendas post within days of each meeting, so the quarter's approvals are final within a week of quarter end, 20-40 days before the release; the revenue effect lands 1-4 quarters later, so the reading is about backlog and guidance, not the quarter's revenue.. Lead time: 20 days.
- Universe coverage: ~5% nominal (about 60 names) but attributable to a specific listed company in only a handful of cases per quarter
- Hypothesis: A positive change of 1 standard deviation in approved square footage and MW in a contractor's core counties over the fiscal quarter versus the trailing four-quarter mean, measured 15 days before the earnings release, predicts a raise in backlog or full-year guidance and a positive T-1 to T+2 abnormal return, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Hyperscaler capex guidance drives these stocks and datacenterHawk and DC Byte sell data-centre pipeline databases (REASONED-confident), while Dodge Construction Network sells permit and project leads; so the aggregate pipeline is crowdedness 2. Jurisdiction-level counts tied to a specific contractor's footprint are much less covered.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Dodge Construction Network (project and permit data); datacenterHawk, DC Byte (data-centre supply pipeline, REASONED-confident). Why ours differs: Resolve approvals to the contractors and equipment makers that win in that county (from past minutes, bond filings and utility letters) instead of tracking the national megawatt pipeline, and cover warehouses and plants, not only data centres.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Dodge Construction Network (project data, Momentum Index, construction starts) - https://www.construction.com/; ConstructConnect (project leads; data-center starts commentary) - https://www.constructconnect.com/en/dodge-construction-network-alternative; DC Byte (global data-centre database, live/under construction/committed/early stage) - https://www.dcbyte.com/; datacenterHawk (data-centre construction lifecycle data) - https://www.datacenterdynamics.com/en/company/datacenterhawk/; DC Hub (claims crawler monitoring of permit filings and planning applications; 540 projects / 369 GW pipeline) - https://dchub.cloud/news/dc-hub-construction-pipeline-540-projects-369-gw; Construction Monitor permit data in the Bloomberg alternative-data catalog - https://prnewswire.com/news-releases/construction-monitor-data-now-available-as-part-of-an-alternative-data-catalog-through-bloomberg-300940333.html; BuildFax / S&P Global building-permit property intelligence - https://sandpglobal-spglobal-live.cphostaccess.com/marketintelligence/en/news-insights/research/just-the-build-fax-property-intelligence-from-building-permit-data; Data Center Watch report on blocked or delayed data-centre projects - https://www.datacenterwatch.org/report
