- Literature found by reviewer: Cortes and LaPoint, Housing Is the Financial Cycle: Evidence from 100 Years of Local Building Permits (aggregate return volatility, not contractor earnings) - https://papers.ssrn.com/abstract=4855353; Yale SOM Insights summary of the permits paper - https://insights.som.yale.edu/insights/swings-in-building-permits-can-help-predict-financial-downturns; MPRA 76514, Google Trends nowcast of building permits (official series lags ~2 months) - https://mpra.ub.uni-muenchen.de/76514/1/MPRA_paper_76514.pdf
- Data sources:
  - Legistar Web API (hundreds of US municipalities; matters, events, attachments) [DATA] https://webapi.legistar.com/v1/phoenix/events?$top=2 (update: per meeting; history: 2015-2016+ for tested clients)
  - Loudoun County AgendaCenter (CivicPlus; Data Center Alley) [DATA] https://www.loudoun.gov/AgendaCenter (update: per meeting; history: 2016+)
  - Regulations.gov API v4 (federal permits and EIS notices) [DATA] https://api.regulations.gov/v4/documents?api_key=DEMO_KEY&page[size]=5 (update: daily; history: ~2.0M documents)
  - Federal Register API (NEPA notices, FERC and DOE actions) [DATA] https://www.federalregister.gov/api/v1/documents.json?per_page=1&conditions[term]=data%20center (update: daily; history: 1994+ (REASONED))
- Reviewer source checks: https://webapi.legistar.com/v1/phoenix/matters?$filter=substringof('data center… -> 200 TESTED HERE: keyword filter works; returned 2026-06-16 and 2026-03-02 data-cent…; https://www.loudoun.gov/AgendaCenter -> DATA (Phase 0) HTML/RSS plus PDF agendas back to 2016; parsing cost high.; https://api.regulations.gov/v4/documents?api_key=DEMO_KEY&page[size]=5 -> DATA (Phase 0) DEMO_KEY throttled; free api.data.gov key needed for volume.; https://www.federalregister.gov/api/v1/documents.json?per_page=1&conditions[ter… -> DATA (Phase 0) Keyless; federal notices only, rarely jurisdiction-specific.
- Cost: $0; Legistar and CivicPlus are keyless. Labour-intensive: shell-name filings must be resolved by parcel, counsel and utility letters (REASONED).
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 1 / 1. Confidence: 8%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): The contractor award is unobservable from the approval, timing to revenue is long and lumpy, and the stocks already trade on hyperscaler capex and company-announced awards.
- Red team (reviewer): A county-level approval count is an unattributable, lumpy proxy for the data-centre capex theme that is already priced through hyperscaler guidance and company-announced awards; any apparent predictive power in a backtest would be loading on the AI-infrastructure momentum factor, and the award (the attributable event) is disclosed by the company before it reaches backlog.

## S11 ISO interconnection queues and large-load requests

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Utilities, independent power, power equipment, listed compute operators. Target: guidance, capex (forward).
- Seed item: [regulatory] Utility interconnection and power-capacity requests for data centers and factories
- Verdict: REJECT. Changes: Not kept. The one narrow variant worth a later look: commercial-operation-date slips of a listed IPP's named projects in the monthly ISO queue report (ERCOT GIS, NYISO) ahead of the release, but the universe is under ten names (CWEN, ORA, TLN) and events are rare. Reject reason: The half of the mechanism with earnings relevance (large-load requests and signed energy service agreements in a specific utility's territory) is not published in structured public form: large loads interconnect through the transmission owner, not the public generator queue; ERCOT's per-project large-load status reports go to TSPs with a Large Load Portal still in development and the process moved to batch-based after July 2026 (search results), and the ERCOT page I probed lists no such report. Only aggregate GW figures appear in board/PUC slide decks. The public generator queues (NYISO xlsx TESTED HERE downloads, CAISO, ERCOT GIS, PJM with free key) are speculative (LBNL: ~13% of 2000-2019 requests reached operation), years from revenue, and tracked publicly by LBNL/interconnection.fyi, so a queue change rarely moves a single quarter's guidance; utilities pre-announce major customers on calls.
- Mechanism: Large-load interconnection requests, signed energy service agreements and generator queue milestones appear in ISO and utility filings months before a utility raises load-growth and capex guidance, and generator queue withdrawals or in-service slips precede delayed revenue for developers and IPPs. For a regulated utility, incremental contracted large load converts to rate-base capex guidance; for equipment makers, queue megawatts entering construction convert to orders.
- Industry context: Utilities earn a regulated return on capital they deploy, so contracted new load is the main driver of growth guidance, and every such load must enter a public interconnection process. Developers likewise must hold a queue position before they can build.
- Measurement window: Queue reports are monthly or quarterly and posted within about two weeks (TESTED HERE: NYISO links 'NYISO-Interconnection-Queue-09302026.xlsx'), so the quarter-end queue state is available roughly 15-30 days before the release; FERC eLibrary filings are continuous.. Lead time: 15 days.
- Universe coverage: ~7% nominal (about 90 names) but the measurable input exists for fewer than 10 IPP/developer names
- Hypothesis: An addition of 500 MW or more of large-load or late-stage generator requests in a utility's territory during the fiscal quarter, measured 15 days before the earnings release, predicts an upward revision of load-growth or capex guidance and a positive T-1 to T+2 abnormal return, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Utility analysts track queues and LBNL's annual 'Queued Up' report summarises them; Wood Mackenzie and Enverus sell queue analytics. Utilities telegraph load growth on calls, so the aggregate is priced; the gap is the timing of specific large-load contracts for small utilities.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Wood Mackenzie, Enverus (interconnection and project analytics); LBNL 'Queued Up' is public. Why ours differs: Monitor the queue and FERC filings of the dozen sub-$20B utilities and IPPs in the universe rather than the national aggregate, and time the reading to each company's earnings window.
- Vendors found by reviewer: interconnection.fyi (free queue aggregator, LBNL collaborator; page embeds a CSV DataDownload and a /terms licence link) - https://www.interconnection.fyi/; New Project Media (subscription news service tracking ERCOT large-load requests, e.g. 438 GW) - https://newprojectmedia.com/origination-ercot-large-load-requests-climb-to-438-gw-17-new-gas-generation-filings-in-last-60-days/; DC Hub (press release citing ERCOT queue 427 GW) - https://dchub.cloud/news/2026-06-29-ercot-queue-427-gw; Utility Dive coverage of ERCOT large-load queue growth - https://www.utilitydive.com/news/ercots-large-load-queue-jumped-almost-300-last-year/808820/
- Literature found by reviewer: LBNL, Queued Up: 2026 Edition (queues through end-2025; built with interconnection.fyi) - https://emp.lbl.gov/publications/queued-2026-edition-characteristics; LBNL, Queued Up: 2025 Edition PDF (13% of 2000-2019 requests reached commercial operation) - https://emp.lbl.gov/sites/default/files/2025-12/Queued%20Up%202025%20Edition%20-%2012.15.2025.pdf; ERCOT Large Load Update, April 2026 (status reports via TSPs; portal in development; ~410 GW, ~87% data centres) - https://www.ercot.com/files/docs/2026/04/01/ERCOT_LargeLoad_Update_April2026_B-C_-Hearing.pdf; ERCOT Interconnection and Grid Analysis Update, September 2026 - https://www.ercot.com/files/docs/2026/09/07/10-Interconnection-and-Grid-Analysis-Update.pdf; ERCOT Community Impact RFI PUC update, 2026-11-05 (433 of 465 large loads, ~177 GW) - https://www.ercot.com/files/docs/2026/11/05/Community-Impact-RFI-PUC-Update.pdf
- Data sources:
  - PJM Data Miner 2 API (queue and planning feeds) [KEY] https://api.pjm.com/api/v1/ (update: varies per feed; history: multi-year)
  - ERCOT resource adequacy / generator interconnection status (GIS) reports [DATA] https://www.ercot.com/gridinfo/resource (update: monthly; history: 175 documents listed (Phase 0))
  - ERCOT large-load interconnection status report [UNPROBED] https://www.ercot.com/gridinfo/load (update: monthly (REASONED); history: 2024+ (REASONED); TESTED HERE: page loads and mentions Large Load Integration, but the report-type ID I guessed returned an empty document list)
  - CAISO generator interconnection queue reports [DATA] https://www.caiso.com/generation-transmission/generation/generator-interconnection (update: cluster cadence; history: current reports)

## S12 State WARN notices as a plant-closure and restructuring nowcast

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Cross-sector, strongest for manufacturers, auto suppliers, consumer durables, apparel, retail and healthcare services with large US sites. Target: margin, guidance, revenue (both).
- Seed item: [regulatory] WARN Act mass-layoff notices, published by states 60 days ahead
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Add LaborCurrent (the renamed WARN Firehose) as the national source: TESTED HERE its pricing page offers a $0 evaluation REST tier with 30-day-delayed historical WARN data (enough for the backtest) and a $49/month tier for live same-day national coverage including Texas, which fits the later budget; keep the five free state pages for live T-1 reads now. (2) Require an explicit 8-K cross-check: match each notice to EDGAR 8-K Item 2.05/2.06 filings within 10 business days using the keyless EDGAR feeds already in Phase 0, because Item 2.05 must be filed within four business days of a material exit plan, so unmatched notices are by construction the ones management deemed immaterial. (3) Split closures from layoffs: the 2022 production-economics study found plant closures earned +7.9% over the following year while layoff-only events were flat, so test sign separately and do not assume negative. (4) Scale by headcount: use the 10-K employee count; set the threshold at 2% of total employees. (5) Lower signal_noise to 2 and crowdedness to 2 because LaborCurrent already sells an 8-K-cross-referenced version to funds; our residual edge is only the fiscal-quarter alignment and the earnings-window timing. (6) Treat as an overlay with an expected sample of a few dozen qualifying events per year across the universe, not a standalone strategy.
- Mechanism: Employers must give 60 days' notice of mass layoffs or closures (50+ employees), and states publish the notices within days; the affected headcount times average compensation proxies the restructuring charge and the lower run-rate cost base, and closure of a specific plant or DC reveals demand weakness in that segment before the company quantifies it. Where the notice is not already press-released, it moves the quarter's margin (charges) and next quarter's guidance.
- Industry context: The federal WARN Act forces 60 days' public notice of plant closures and mass layoffs, so a company's cost actions surface in state listings before its quarterly report. For manufacturers, a closure also says which product line lost volume.
- Measurement window: Continuous: notices appear days after filing and up to 60 days before the layoff, so the quarter's aggregate affected headcount per company is final at T-1; relative to the company's own disclosure the lead is 0-60 days. Texas (a large manufacturing state) is blocked from the container.. Lead time: 1 days.
- Universe coverage: ~40% searchable (about 500 names with US sites of 50+ employees) but roughly 10-30 qualifying undisclosed events per quarter across the universe (REASONED)
- Hypothesis: WARN-notified headcount of 2% or more of total employees filed in the 75 days before the earnings release, with no matching 8-K Item 2.05/2.06 within 10 business days of the notice, predicts (a) a downward revision of revenue guidance and a negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return when the notice is a layoff without closure, and (b) a non-negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return when it is a plant or DC closure, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size, momentum and short interest; measured at T-1.
- Who already knows: Big closures are press-released or in 8-Ks before the WARN notice, and journalists and LayoffData/WARNTracker subscribers watch the state pages, so the headline is often known; the under-used part is aggregating smaller notices per ticker per quarter. LITERATURE on layoff announcements and returns exists (Farber and Hallock, Labour Economics 2009) but WARN-specific return predictability: UNKNOWN.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): WARNTracker, LayoffData.com (aggregation); Thinknum-type vendors track job postings, not WARN (REASONED). Why ours differs: Per-ticker quarterly headcount affected, resolved through subsidiary names, joined to each company's earnings calendar; the aggregators sell lists, not a ticker-mapped quarterly series.
- Vendors found by reviewer: WARNTracker ($250 upfront + $250/month; 46 states + DC; Airtable view) - https://www.warntracker.com/pricing; WARN Firehose, now LaborCurrent (targets hedge funds; REST API; 8-K cross-reference; $0 evaluation, $49/$199/$499 per month tiers; one-off state file $299, bundle $1,499) - https://laborcurrent.com/pricing; layofftracker.us (free tracker) - https://layofftracker.us/about; Apify WARN layoffs aggregator scraper - https://apify.com/jungle_synthesizer/warn-layoffs-aggregator
- Literature found by reviewer: International Journal of Production Economics, vol. 245 (2022), 574 downsizing events: plant closures +7.9% vs comparables over the following year, layoff-only in line; argues WARN notices must be included to avoid media-sample bias - https://ideas.repec.org/a/eee/proeco/v245y2022ics0925527321003686.html; The impact of the WARN Act on firm value (negative effects for small firms at enactment) - https://bvbvf1.bib-bvb.de/vufind/PrimoRecord/cdi_proquest_journals_213334139; Cleveland Fed WP 16-10, Competitors' stock price reaction to mass layoff announcements - https://www.clevelandfed.org/-/media/project/clevelandfedtenant/clevelandfedsite/publications/working-papers/2016/wp-1610-competitors-stock-price-reaction-to-mass-layoff-announcements-pdf.pdf; Layoff announcements and intra-day market reactions (Review of Managerial Science) - https://link.springer.com/content/pdf/10.1007/s11846-016-0219-7.pdf; WARN Firehose blog, WARN data as a leading economic indicator (vendor marketing, no peer review) - https://warnfirehose.com/blog/warn-data-leading-economic-indicator

- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Most material notices are already announced, the sign is ambiguous (cost cuts can be rewarded), and coverage is patchy because key states such as Texas are inaccessible from the container.

Any backtest hit may also be a proxy for the distress/low-quality factor that is already in short interest.

## S13 UCC-1 liens on inventory and receivables as a liquidity-stress flag

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Cross-sector small caps with asset-based lending: retail, restaurants, trucking, consumer durables, industrial distributors. Target: distress, margin (nowcast).
- Seed item: [regulatory] UCC filings showing inventory or receivables pledged as collateral
- Verdict: REJECT. Changes: Not kept. If revisited, only as a feature inside a distress model for the few names whose operating subsidiaries are organised in Florida or California, which is too narrow for effort. Reject reason: Coverage and timing both fail. Material new or expanded facilities must be disclosed by 8-K Item 2.03 within four business days, so the UCC lead is hours to days and only for immaterial filings; most public parents and operating subsidiaries are Delaware entities whose filings sit behind Delaware's paid UCC search, so the free Florida index (DATA) and the California bizfile SPA (TESTED HERE: redirect loop without cookies, SPA shell with a cookie jar, backend /api reachable but UCC query unprobed) capture only 2-3% of the universe; and most filings are routine equipment leases. No vendor or paper I found uses UCC-1 filings as an equity signal; the credit-data vendors themselves say filings are not a distress signal on their own (Baselayer) and one covers only 10-11 states.
- Mechanism: A new UCC-1 financing statement naming a company's operating subsidiary as debtor with 'all assets', inventory or receivables as collateral signals a new or expanded secured facility, which for a stressed small cap precedes higher interest expense, covenant amendments and going-concern language; equipment-specific filings instead signal capex. Terminations and continuations show deleveraging or roll-overs. The reading is the count and collateral type of new filings in the quarter.
- Industry context: Secured lenders perfect their claims by filing public UCC-1 statements, so every asset-based loan leaves a public trace naming the collateral. Stressed companies borrow against inventory and receivables first, which is why the collateral type carries information.
- Measurement window: Continuous; filings are indexed within 1-3 days so the quarter's filing set is final at T-1. Coverage caveat: UCC-1s are filed in the debtor's state of organisation, which for most public companies is Delaware (paid search), so the free Florida and California indexes capture only subsidiaries organised there.. Lead time: 1 days.
- Universe coverage: ~2-3% of debtor entities reachable through free indexes; nominal 15%
- Hypothesis: A new all-asset or inventory/receivables UCC-1 filed against a company's operating subsidiary by a new secured party during the 45 days before the earnings release, with no corresponding 8-K, predicts a negative EPS surprise (interest expense) and negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return for sub-$3B names, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Lenders, credit desks and D&B-type credit scorers use UCC data routinely; equity-signal vendors: UNKNOWN. Facilities are disclosed in 8-K Items 1.01/2.03 within four business days, so the UCC lead over public disclosure is hours to days.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Dun & Bradstreet and Creditsafe incorporate UCC filings in credit reports (not equity signals); no equity vendor I am sure of. Why ours differs: Nobody tracks it for equities, but the honest answer is that the 8-K usually arrives almost as fast and the free indexes miss Delaware; this only works for subsidiaries organised in open-index states.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Baselayer liens, judgments and UCC public-records docs (UCC coverage ~10-11 states; a UCC-1 is not a distress signal alone) - https://docs.baselayer.com/docs/liens-judgments-and-public-records; Cobalt Intelligence, court records + UCC combined lien and litigation pattern - https://blog.cobaltintelligence.com/post/court-records-ucc-combined-lien-litigation-pattern; gtm.ai marketplace, companies with amended UCC filings (claims 10.6M+ records) - https://gtm.ai/marketplace/gtm-audiences/companies-with-amended-ucc-filings; Octus (Reorg) Restructuring Risk Index - https://octus.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/08/Reorg-Restructuring-Risk-Index.pdf; Neudata, bankruptcy datasets: an alt-data approach - https://neudata.co/intelligence/bankruptcy-datasets-an-alt-data-approach; NCS Credit on rising private-credit defaults - https://www.ncscredit.com/education-center/blog/rising-private-credit-defaults-creditors
- Literature found by reviewer: arXiv 2212.12051, machine-learning bankruptcy prediction for US public firms (financial, market and macro features; no UCC filings) - https://arxiv.org/pdf/2212.12051v1.pdf; AFA/JOF paper on hedge funds serving on unsecured creditors' committees (the acronym UCC there means creditors' committee, not Uniform Commercial Code; trading driven by MNPI access, which is the opposite of this signal) - https://afajof.org/management/viewp.php?n=61152
- Data sources:
  - Florida UCC public search backend API [DATA] https://publicsearchapi.floridaucc.com/ (update: daily; history: filed and lapsed records (Phase 0))
  - Florida UCC site [DATA] https://www.floridaucc.com/ (update: daily; history: unknown)
  - California Secretary of State bizfile UCC search [UNPROBED] https://bizfileonline.sos.ca.gov/search/ucc (update: daily (REASONED); history: UNKNOWN)
  - Delaware Division of Corporations UCC search (paid account) [DATA] https://icis.corp.delaware.gov/ecorp/entitysearch/namesearch.aspx (update: daily; history: entity search free; UCC search paid)
  - Harris County Clerk real property search (deeds of trust, mechanics liens) [DATA] https://www.cclerk.hctx.net/applications/websearch/RP.aspx (update: daily; history: unknown)

- Red team (reviewer): Whatever survives the Delaware gap and the 8-K Item 2.03 disclosure rule is a lagging proxy for the distress factor already visible in short interest and credit spreads; the filing itself (a lender taking collateral) often marks a liquidity solution rather than a problem, so the sign is ambiguous and the sample is a handful of names.

## S14 Federal docket feeds: supplier collection suits, mechanics liens and product-liability clusters

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Cross-sector, most useful for thinly capitalised small caps and companies mid-way through large construction projects. Target: distress, recall, margin (nowcast).
- Seed item: [regulatory] Court dockets: supplier suits over unpaid invoices, mechanics liens, PACER filings
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Restrict to federal courts and make the history problem explicit: the RSS feeds are rolling-only (TESTED HERE: deb 601, txsd 3,817, nysd 3,245, ded 451, ilnd 1,015, txnd 456, njd 1,541 items; cand failed), so start a daily archive now and build the backtest from the PACER Case Locator party-name search (free registration, $0.10 per page, waived under $30 per quarter: roughly 300 result pages per quarter, enough for ~60 names), which returns nature-of-suit codes and filing dates back decades. (2) Use nature-of-suit codes 190 (contract), 195 (contract product liability) and 365/367 (product liability) from PCL or CourtListener rather than keyword matching, because the RSS entries carry only the caption and entry type (e.g. '[Complaint]') with no nature-of-suit field (TESTED HERE). (3) Match defendants to the company's subsidiary list from 10-K Exhibit 21 via the keyless EDGAR submissions API. (4) Drop state-court aggregators (UniCourt, Trellis: paid, over budget) and Harris County mechanics liens (one county, off-thesis). (5) Add short interest (FINRA, DATA in Phase 0) and credit-spread proxies as controls because a suit cluster is likely correlated with the distress factor. (6) Lower signal_noise to 2, crowdedness to 2 (Thomson Reuters Court Wire and UniCourt already sell 'portfolio company sued' alerts to investors) and confidence to 12; treat as an overlay for sub-$3B names only.
- Mechanism: Suppliers sue for unpaid invoices only after months of stretched payables, so a cluster of breach-of-contract and collection suits naming a company or its subsidiaries flags working-capital stress that shows up as margin pressure, vendor terms tightening and going-concern risk; mechanics liens on a company's new plant or stores signal non-payment or disputes that delay capex. Surges in product-liability filings precede recalls and charges. Free court RSS feeds surface filings within minutes.
- Industry context: Suppliers extend trade credit and sue only when payables stretch past tolerance, so suits lag the cash squeeze but lead the company's admission of it. Federal courts publish every filing in real time, which makes the docket a free early-warning system.
- Measurement window: Continuous; the quarter's filing count per company is final at T-1. TESTED HERE: the free PACER RSS feeds at ecf.deb.uscourts.gov (601 recent entries) and ecf.txsd.uscourts.gov (3,817 entries) return case name, number and docket-entry type with no account; REASONED: most districts enable rss_outside.pl. State-court suits (where most collection cases sit) need state portals or paid aggregators.. Lead time: 1 days.
- Universe coverage: ~100% searchable; actionable in ~5% (about 60 sub-$3B names with stretched liquidity or large builds)
- Hypothesis: Three or more new federal civil suits with nature-of-suit 190/195 naming the company or an Exhibit 21 subsidiary as defendant in the 90 days before the earnings release, versus at most one in the prior 90 days, predict a negative margin surprise, a going-concern or covenant disclosure, and a negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return for sub-$3B names, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size, momentum, short interest and the prior-quarter suit count; measured at T-1 from the RSS archive and verified against PCL.
- Who already knows: Bloomberg Law, Lex Machina, Docket Alarm, UniCourt and Trellis sell docket analytics mainly to law firms; a few credit and short-oriented funds count supplier suits (REASONED), and securities litigation is tracked by everyone. The gap is a per-ticker count of commercial collection suits, which no mainstream equity feed provides (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): UniCourt, Trellis, Docket Alarm (Fastcase/vLex), Lex Machina (LexisNexis). Why ours differs: Free federal RSS plus CourtListener, filtered to 'breach of contract' and collection suits where the company is defendant, joined to the earnings calendar; vendors sell the full docket universe to lawyers at legal-industry prices.
- Vendors found by reviewer: UniCourt (court data for investment firms; CEO guest post) - https://www.artificiallawyer.com/2019/08/22/court-data-is-rocket-fuel-for-investment-firms/; Thomson Reuters Court Wire Litigation Intelligence in Eikon (Westlaw filings from 200+ US courts paired with tick data; alerts when a portfolio company is sued) - https://www.waterstechnology.com/data-management/2477698/thomson-reuters-integrates-legal-filings-data-into-eikon-to-show-impact-of-court-cases-on-stock-prices; CourtListener / Free Law Project docket alerts (free) - https://www.courtlistener.com/help/alerts/; Apify PACER RSS docket monitor - https://apify.com/owlabee/pacer-docket-monitor

- Cost: $0 for RSS feeds and CourtListener; PACER is effectively free below $30 per quarter; state-court aggregators (UniCourt, Trellis, Docket Alarm) are paid and exceed $100/month at scale (REASONED).

- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Collection suits are small relative to revenue, most sit in state courts outside the free feeds, and the bulk of docket activity is noise (employment, securities, patents).
- Red team (reviewer): By the time suppliers sue, the distress is priced: high short interest, wide spreads and going-concern chatter precede the suits, so a suit cluster is a proxy for the distress factor rather than new information; most collection suits sit in state courts outside the free feeds, the free RSS carries no nature-of-suit field, and the actionable sample is a few dozen names, so any backtest result is driven by a handful of Hertz/JetBlue-type episodes.

## S15 FDA inspection outcomes and 483s, FCC equipment grants (patents/trademarks as context only)

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Healthcare ex-biotech (single-site medtech, generic and animal-health pharma, CDMOs) and consumer/communications hardware. Target: revenue, guidance, recall (both).
- Seed item: [regulatory] FDA inspection reports and Form 483s, FCC equipment filings, patent and trademark applications
- Verdict: REJECT. Changes: Not kept for Phase 1. The only clean path is prospective: a weekly cron archiving the FDA dashboard, warning-letter and openFDA recall postings with capture dates, which would permit an evaluation in 2 years, not a backtest now. Reject reason: The inspection half has a look-ahead problem that cannot be fixed retroactively: the FDA dashboard and the FY 483 spreadsheets record inspection end dates but not posting dates, classifications are sent to firms 45-90 days after close (FDA official: 86% within 90 days in FY2019) and the dashboard posts only once all classifications are finalised, with some records withheld until a final enforcement action is taken (FDA's own page), which is exactly the OAI cases the hypothesis targets; so a backtest keyed on inspection dates assumes knowledge the market did not have, and the 'before the warning letter' window may be empty. Wayback is blocked from the container, so no point-in-time reconstruction. The market learns OAI from the warning letter or the company's 8-K/press release, both news events. The FCC half concerns launches already guided and many grants sit under short-term confidentiality (REASONED); patents and trademarks are 6-18 months slow by the candidate's own admission. Redica Systems did not appear in my searches, so its product could not be confirmed.
- Mechanism: An OAI inspection classification or a lengthy Form 483 at a company's main plant raises the odds of a warning letter, import alert or shipment hold, which cuts revenue and adds remediation cost over the next 1-3 quarters and often forces a guidance cut; conversely, VAI/NAI clearance of a known problem site removes the overhang. For hardware makers, a new FCC equipment grant under the company's grantee code dates the launch of the next product, which times next-quarter revenue. Patent and trademark filings publish with 6-18 month lags and serve only as product-pipeline context, not a 2-10 day signal.
- Industry context: FDA can stop a plant from shipping, and single-site medtech and generic makers have no backup capacity, so inspection outcomes are the main non-market risk to their quarter. FCC must approve every radio-equipped device before sale, so grants date product launches.
- Measurement window: FDA Data Dashboard classifications post with a 1-3 month lag (REASONED), so the reading covers inspections closed 1-3 months before the release; the annual 483 observation spreadsheets are fiscal-year files. FCC grants appear on fccid.io the day they are granted, but short-term confidentiality hides many for up to 180 days (REASONED), so the reading is the set of grants visible 10 days before the release.. Lead time: 10 days.
- Universe coverage: ~8% nominal (about 100 names); testable now: none, because posting dates are not recoverable
- Hypothesis: An OAI classification or a 483 with 8 or more observations at a plant producing 30% or more of a company's revenue, posted in the 90 days before the earnings release and not yet followed by a warning letter, predicts a guidance cut and a negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Warning letters are posted weekly and reported immediately, and Redica Systems (formerly FDAzilla) sells 483s and inspection intelligence, so OAI outcomes at larger names are priced; the gap is reading the dashboard before the warning letter and at sub-$3B single-site names. FCC filings are followed by tech press for popular brands (crowdedness 2).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Redica Systems (FDA inspection and 483 intelligence); none I am sure of for FCC-grant equity signals. Why ours differs: Free dashboard classifications joined to each company's site list from its 10-K, scored by site concentration, and timed to the earnings window; FCC grants used only for small hardware names with one or two annual launches.
- Vendors found by reviewer: The FDA Group Insider newsletter (consultancy; OMQ letters after inspections) - https://insider.thefdagroup.com/p/more-omq-letters-after-fda-inspections; Maven Regulatory Solutions commentary on warning letters - https://www.mavenrs.com/us-fda-warning-letter-eugia-pharma-aurobindo-gmp-compliance-2026; IntuitionLabs FDA 483 and warning-letter statistics PDF - https://intuitionlabs.ai/pdfs/fda-form-483-warning-letter-statistics.pdf
- Literature found by reviewer: FDA Inspection Classification Database page (dashboard updated weekly; data posted only when all classifications are finalised; some withheld until final enforcement action) - https://www.fda.gov/inspections-compliance-enforcement-and-criminal-investigations/inspection-references/inspection-classification-database; FDAnews, time between FDA inspections and classification letters shrinking (45-90 days; 86% within 90 days FY2019) - https://fdanews.com/articles/187235-time-between-fda-inspections-and-classification-letters-shrinking; Pink Sheet, US FDA getting faster at notifying drug facility owners of inspection status - https://pink.citeline.com/PS141362/US-FDA-Getting-Faster-At-Notifying-Drug-Facility-Owners-Of-Inspection-Status; FDLI, Inside Warning Letters: A Statistical Update (2025) - https://www.fdli.org/2025/10/inside-warning-letters-a-statistical-update/; PrimeInvestor, how investors should navigate USFDA inspections (anecdotal Indian pharma stock reactions to OAI and import alerts) - https://primeinvestor.in/reports/pharma-stocks-investors-navigate-usfda-inspections/
- Data sources:
  - FDA Data Dashboard inspections and citations API (free credentials) [KEY] https://datadashboard.fda.gov/ora/api/inspectionscitations.json?limit=1 (update: monthly-ish updates, 1-3 month lag (REASONED); history: FY2009+ (REASONED))
  - FDA inspection observations (Form 483 citations) FY spreadsheets [DATA] https://www.fda.gov/media/190190/download?attachment (update: annual (TESTED HERE: FY files linked from the inspection-observations page); history: FY2019-FY2025)
  - openFDA device recalls [DATA] https://api.fda.gov/device/recall.json?limit=1 (update: weekly; history: 59,705 records at Phase 0)
  - openFDA drug enforcement reports [DATA] https://api.fda.gov/drug/enforcement.json?limit=1 (update: weekly; history: 2004+ (REASONED))
  - fccid.io (third-party mirror of FCC equipment authorizations; fcc.gov is blocked from the container) [DATA] https://fccid.io/ (update: daily (REASONED); history: mirrors FCC grants; depth unknown)
  - USPTO ODP patent search API (context only) [KEY] https://api.uspto.gov/api/v1/patent/applications/search?q=applicationMetaData.applicantName:Sonos (update: weekly publication; history: full)
  - USPTO TSDR trademark API (intent-to-use filings, context only) [KEY] https://tsdrapi.uspto.gov/ts/cd/casestatus/sn78787878/info.json (update: daily; history: full)
- Reviewer source checks: https://datadashboard.fda.gov/ora/api/inspectionscitations.json?limit=1 -> KEY (Phase 0) Free credentials via the FDA Data Dashboard API page; weekly updates of finalis…; https://www.fda.gov/media/190190/download?attachment -> DATA (Phase 0) Annual FY files; useless for intra-quarter timing.; https://api.fda.gov/device/recall.json?limit=1 -> DATA (Phase 0) Weekly; recalls are news on posting.; https://fccid.io/ -> DATA (Phase 0) Mirror; no documented API; short-term confidentiality hides many grants (REASON…; https://api.uspto.gov/api/v1/patent/applications/search?q=applicationMetaData.a… -> KEY (Phase 0) Context only; too slow for the trade.
- Cost: $0; free FDA dashboard credentials, free USPTO keys.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 1 / 1. Confidence: 10%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Warning letters and recalls hit the tape as news and the dashboard lag means the market often knows first; FCC grants mostly concern launches already guided; patents and trademarks are far too slow.
- Red team (reviewer): Look-ahead bias: historical FDA files carry inspection end dates, not the dates the classification became public, and OAI records can be withheld until enforcement; any backtest would credit the signal with 1-3 months of knowledge the market did not have, and the real public event (warning letter, import alert, company 8-K) is already a news-driven move.

## S16 Summed federal contract obligations (FPDS/USAspending) and quarterly lobbying spend

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Industrials and IT services: government services, defence electronics, engineering and health-program contractors. Target: revenue, guidance, EPS surprise (both).
- Seed item: [regulatory] Lobbying disclosure spikes and small government contract awards on USAspending.gov
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Split by agency: TESTED HERE on 2026-11-11, civilian actions for CACI were visible dated 2026-11-05 (6-day lag) while the latest DoD actions visible for Kratos and KBR were dated 2026-07-23 and 2026-08-06 (97-111 days), confirming the DFARS PGI 204.6 90-day withholding; so the quarterly sum uses civilian-agency obligations for all names, and DoD obligations only for actions signed at least 90 days before T-1 (roughly the first 2-5 weeks of the quarter), modelled explicitly rather than assumed complete. (2) Replace the trailing-four-quarter mean with the same fiscal quarter a year earlier, because the federal Sep-30 year-end surge dominates variance. (3) Resolve recipients by UEI hierarchy (parent UEI field) rather than name, including subsidiaries. (4) Drop lobbying as a signal (keep LD-2 only as context; Quiver already publishes lobbying analyses; TESTED HERE Q3-2026 LDA count 26,567 on 2026-11-11). (5) Use USAspending spending_by_transaction as the primary feed; the FPDS ATOM feed works for simple queries (TESTED HERE q=KBR returned 10 entries) but my fielded SIGNED_DATE query returned 0 entries, so its syntax is unverified and it is a cross-check only. (6) Build point-in-time by filtering on action_date and, where available, last-modified dates (REASONED) and restrict the first test to civilian-heavy names (ICF, MMS, TTEK, PSN, CACI, BAH, SAIC partial). (7) Crowdedness 2: Quiver Quantitative sells ticker-mapped contract data for $30/month and Apify actors do the same, so the only edge is fiscal-quarter alignment, UEI roll-up and the lag model.
- Mechanism: Every federal contract action, including task orders under IDIQs that sell-side rarely aggregates, is reported to FPDS within days and to USAspending within about a month; summing a contractor's obligated dollars over its fiscal quarter nowcasts bookings and book-to-bill, which drive revenue recognition over the next 1-2 quarters and the backlog guidance given at earnings. Lobbying filings (LD-2) are quarterly and spikes flag regulatory or M&A exposure, a weaker second-order signal.
- Industry context: Government contractors recognise revenue as they burn funded backlog, and every dollar of that backlog starts as a public contract action. Federal fiscal-year-end (30 September) obligation surges make Q4-government quarters especially informative.
- Measurement window: FPDS actions appear within about 3 business days for civilian agencies, but DoD actions can be delayed up to 90 days (REASONED), so the quarter's civilian obligations are near-final 7-10 days after quarter end (20-30 days before the release) while defence totals are incomplete. TESTED HERE: LDA.gov already lists 27,013 Q3-2025 filings; LD-2s are due 20 days after quarter end, so lobbying is available ~10-20 days before most releases.. Lead time: 15 days.
- Universe coverage: ~4% (about 50 names with over 30% federal revenue); civilian-heavy first-test subset ~15-20 names
- Hypothesis: A positive change of 1 standard deviation in a contractor's summed civilian-agency obligations (all UEIs under the parent) over its fiscal quarter versus the same fiscal quarter a year earlier, plus the visible portion of DoD obligations signed at least 90 days before T-1 treated as a separate regressor, measured 10 days before the earnings release, predicts a positive book-to-bill surprise, a backlog or revenue guidance raise, and a positive T-1 to T+2 abnormal return, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size, momentum and the company's own pre-announced awards.
- Who already knows: GovWin IQ (Deltek) and Bloomberg Government sell contract and task-order intelligence, Quiver Quant publishes government-contract and lobbying datasets, and defence analysts track large awards and book-to-bill; so headline awards are priced (crowdedness 2). The less-used part is the full sum of small task orders mapped to the company's fiscal quarter.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): GovWin IQ (Deltek), Bloomberg Government, Quiver Quant. Why ours differs: Free full-population summation by recipient DUNS/UEI including subsidiaries, aligned to the company's fiscal quarter and option-exercise timing, rather than curated big-award alerts.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Quiver Quantitative (government contracts dashboard built on the USAspending API; lobbying since 1999; API $30/month Hobbyist, $75/month Trader; quiverquant Python package) - https://api.quiverquant.com/; Apify, US Government Contract Awards Monitor (maps recipients to tickers and CIKs) - https://apify.com/datasignalslab/gov-contract-awards-monitor; Apify, US Federal Spending Monitor (USAspending to JSON) - https://apify.com/aaddss/usaspending-monitor.md; Apify, federal contract spending intelligence actor - https://apify.com/nexgendata/federal-contract-spending-intelligence.md; cleat.ai FPDS developer documentation - https://www.cleat.ai/developers/fpds; Setup4Alpha substack, free alternative-data edges (anecdotal Palantir awards chart) - https://setup4alpha.substack.com/p/free-alternative-data-edges
- Literature found by reviewer: Bank of Lithuania working paper (2026), firm-level US government procurement event study: competitive awards show unanticipated return variation, noncompetitive awards none - https://www.lb.lt/uploads/publications/docs/73022_04682f1bf8398995fa1dcdde4e2f716e.pdf; CESifo Working Paper 7534, competitive federal contracts: no anticipation effects, winners earn abnormal returns - https://www.cesifo.org/DocDL/cesifo1_wp7534.pdf; NBER w27034 data appendix (DoD contracts; USAspending supplemented with FPDS to extend the series) - https://papers.nber.org/data-appendix/w27034/w27034.appendix.pdf; FPDS DoD data availability notice (90-day delay) - https://www.fpds.gov/common/html/dodDataAvailability.html; DFARS PGI 204.6 Contract Reporting (CARs withheld from public view until 90 days after Date Signed) - https://www.acquisition.gov/dfarspgi/pgi-204.6-contract-reporting; DoD announcement April 2025 (FPDS reports delayed; SAM notices not delayed) - https://www.globalsecurity.org/military/library/news/2025/04/mil-250403-dod01.htm
- Data sources:
  - USAspending API spending_by_transaction (POST; keywords or recipient UEI filters, agency filter, action_date sort) [DATA (TESTED HERE 200; keyless)] https://api.usaspending.gov/api/v2/search/spending_by_transaction/ (update: daily loads; civilian actions visible within ~1 week (TESTED HERE), DoD actions after 90 days (TESTED HERE); history: FY2008+ via API (REASONED); earlier via bulk downloads)
  - USAspending API spending_by_award (award-level roll-up) [DATA] https://api.usaspending.gov/api/v2/search/spending_by_award/ (update: daily; history: FY2008+ (REASONED))
  - FPDS ATOM feed (cross-check only; fielded SIGNED_DATE syntax unverified) [DATA (TESTED HERE 200, 10 entries for simple query)] https://www.fpds.gov/ezsearch/FEEDS/ATOM?FEEDNAME=PUBLIC&q=KBR (update: continuous for civilian; DoD withheld 90 days; history: FY2004+)
  - LDA.gov public API (lobbying; context only) [DATA (TESTED HERE 200; count 26,567)] https://lda.gov/api/v1/filings/?filing_year=2026&filing_period=third_quarter&page_size=1 (update: quarterly, due 20 days after quarter end; history: 1999+)
  - SAM.gov contract award notices (DoD notices not delayed per 2025 DoD announcement; possible patch for the DoD gap) [UNPROBED (TESTED HERE: 404 with DEMO_KEY; sam.gov site 200; needs a free api.data.gov/SAM key to verify)] https://api.sam.gov/opportunities/v2/search?api_key=DEMO_KEY&limit=1&postedFrom=11/01/2026&postedTo=11/11/2026&ptype=a (update: continuous (REASONED); history: UNKNOWN)
- Reviewer source checks: https://api.usaspending.gov/api/v2/search/spending_by_transaction/ -> 200 TESTED HERE: CACI civilian transactions dated up to 2026-11-05 visible on 2026-…; https://www.fpds.gov/ezsearch/FEEDS/ATOM?FEEDNAME=PUBLIC&q=VENDOR_FULL_NAME:%22… -> 200 (0 entries, 503 bytes) Fielded query returned an empty feed; syntax or index issue unresolved.; https://www.fpds.gov/ezsearch/FEEDS/ATOM?FEEDNAME=PUBLIC&q=KBR -> 200 TESTED HERE: 10 entries (2020-dated), feed works but ordering/paging needs work.; https://lda.gov/api/v1/filings/?filing_year=2026&filing_period=third_quarter&pa… -> 200 TESTED HERE: 26,567 Q3-2026 filings available on 2026-11-11.; https://api.sam.gov/opportunities/v2/search?api_key=DEMO_KEY&limit=1&postedFrom… -> 404 Demo key rejected or path differs; needs a free SAM/api.data.gov key to verify.; https://www.federalregister.gov/api/v1/documents.json?per_page=1&conditions[ter… -> DATA (Phase 0) Context only.
- Cost: $0; keyless.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 3 / 1. Confidence: 25%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Obligations are lumpy and lag recognition, DoD reporting delays leave defence totals incomplete at the release, and companies pre-announce major awards, so the residual gap is modest.
- Red team (reviewer): Obligations mostly fund previously awarded IDIQ task orders, so the quarterly sum tracks revenue already in consensus rather than new bookings; the Sep-30 fiscal-year surge dominates variance; the 90-day DoD withholding blanks most of the quarter for defence-heavy names; companies pre-announce large awards; and a $30/month retail vendor already maps the same data to tickers, so any residual is small and may load on analyst-revision and size factors.

## S17 Foreign subsidiary statutory accounts as a hidden-segment check

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Cross-sector mid-caps with material UK, French or Norwegian subsidiaries: consumer brands, industrial equipment, food, specialty chemicals. Target: margin, guidance, distress (forward).
- Seed item: [regulatory] Foreign subsidiary accounts (UK Companies House, EU registries) revealing segment data the parent hides
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: Fails program requirement (a): the accounts cover a period that ended 9-21 months earlier and say nothing about the quarter being reported or next-quarter guidance; the 30-day lead is admitted to be nominal. The mechanism is also structurally broken: a US parent's UK/French/Norwegian subsidiary is usually a limited-risk distributor or cost-plus service entity whose operating margin is fixed by transfer-pricing policy (REASONED; the search results confirm OECD transfer-pricing, management fees and royalties shape these accounts), so a 300 bp divergence from the parent's segment margin measures tax structure, not operations. No vendor or paper supports the segment-gap signal (UNKNOWN), and any genuine deterioration would already be visible in the parent's own segment note and goodwill-impairment-test language several quarters before the subsidiary accounts land. Legal position is clean (Crown copyright, Licence Ouverte, NLOD), which is the only thing in its favour; could be kept as a non-tradable impairment watchlist, but not as a Phase 1 signal.
- Mechanism: Foreign subsidiaries file statutory accounts with revenue, margin, headcount and sometimes customer and segment detail that the US parent consolidates away; when one subsidiary is effectively the parent's European segment, its trend reveals margin erosion, intercompany debt build-up or goodwill impairment risk that the parent's segment note hides. The reading is a structural revaluation input (margin, impairment, guidance), not a nowcast of the quarter being reported, because the accounts arrive 9-21 months after the period they cover.
- Industry context: Many US mid-caps run Europe through a single UK or French legal entity that must publish audited accounts, while the parent reports only a consolidated 'international' line. The subsidiary's filing is the only public look inside that line.
- Measurement window: Filings arrive continuously; UK private-company accounts are due 9 months after the subsidiary's year end, so a reading taken any time before the release covers the subsidiary's prior fiscal year. There is no event timing; the 30-day lead is nominal.. Lead time: 0 days.
- Universe coverage: ~5% nominal (about 60 names where one subsidiary approximates a segment); effectively 0% for the 2-10 day earnings trade because the reading has no event timing.
- Hypothesis: A decline of 300 basis points or more in a segment-equivalent foreign subsidiary's operating margin in its latest filed accounts relative to the parent's reported segment margin for the overlapping period, observed before the next earnings release, predicts a goodwill impairment or guidance cut within the following two releases and a negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return on those releases, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Fundamental long/short funds and activist short sellers routinely cite Companies House filings in reports (REASONED), and OpenCorporates sells structured registry data, but no vendor I am sure of sells a 'subsidiary-versus-segment gap' signal (UNKNOWN). The information is public but rarely joined to the parent's segment note.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): OpenCorporates (registry data, not signals); none for the segment-gap signal that I am sure of. Why ours differs: Automated join of subsidiary accounts to the parent's XBRL segment data (SEC companyfacts) to flag divergences, rather than manual forensic reading; the limitation is staleness, not crowding.
- Vendors found by reviewer: OpenCorporates (registry data only, paid or public-benefit access; in Phase 0 list) - https://api.opencorporates.com/v0.4/companies/search?q=apple&per_page=1; Companexia (third-party Companies House mirror seen in results, not a signal vendor) - https://companexia.com/en/companies/taskrabbit-limited.P07kQAcbUJq2SAIWKu4NHA.html; City AM routinely reports UK-subsidiary results of US groups from Companies House filings (press, not a vendor) - https://www.cityam.com/people-and-organizations/companies-house/page/13
- Literature found by reviewer: No paper found testing foreign-subsidiary accounts against US parent earnings or returns (two searches). Related only: Companies House report 'FTSE 350 companies and the disclosure of their subsidiaries' - https://gov.uk/government/publications/ftse-350-companies-and-the-disclosure-of-their-subsidiaries-companies-house-report; UK filing-deadline reference (private companies: 9 months after period end) - https://www.gov.uk/government/publications/life-of-a-company-annual-requirements/life-of-a-company-part-1-accounts
- Data sources:
  - UK Companies House public data API (free key) [KEY] https://api.company-information.service.gov.uk/company/00000006 (update: continuous; history: full register)
  - UK Companies House bulk accounts data (iXBRL zips) [DATA] https://download.companieshouse.gov.uk/en_accountsdata.html (update: daily and monthly bulk files; history: multi-year bulk files)
  - French registry (recherche-entreprises, finances by fiscal year) [DATA] https://recherche-entreprises.api.gouv.fr/search?q=Apple%20France&per_page=2 (update: continuous; history: multi-year finances)
  - Norway Enhetsregisteret (data.brreg.no) [DATA] https://data.brreg.no/enhetsregisteret/api/enheter?navn=Apple%20Norway&size=2 (update: continuous; history: registry records)
  - GLEIF LEI API (parent-subsidiary relationship mapping) [DATA] https://api.gleif.org/api/v1/lei-records?filter[entity.legalName]=Apple%20Inc&page[size]=1 (update: daily; history: current relationships)
  - OpenCorporates API (subsidiary discovery; paid or public-benefit access) [KEY] https://api.opencorporates.com/v0.4/companies/search?q=apple&per_page=1 (update: continuous; history: unknown)
- Reviewer source checks: https://api.company-information.service.gov.uk/company/00000006 -> KEY (Phase 0: free Companies House account + REST key) Crown copyright; reuse permitted.; https://download.companieshouse.gov.uk/en_accountsdata.html -> DATA (Phase 0) Daily/monthly iXBRL bulk zips.; https://recherche-entreprises.api.gouv.fr/search?q=Apple%20France&per_page=2 -> DATA (Phase 0) Licence Ouverte; finances by fiscal year.; https://data.brreg.no/enhetsregisteret/api/enheter?navn=Apple%20Norway&size=2 -> DATA (Phase 0) NLOD licence; registry records, accounts not in this endpoint.; https://api.gleif.org/api/v1/lei-records?filter[entity.legalName]=Apple%20Inc&p… -> DATA (Phase 0) Current relationships only; many mid-cap subsidiaries lack LEIs (REASONED).; https://api.opencorporates.com/v0.4/companies/search?q=apple&per_page=1 -> KEY (Phase 0: paid or public-benefit programme) robots.txt disallows /search; not needed if GLEIF plus 10-K Exhibit 21 are used.
- Cost: $0; Crown copyright and open-government licences permit reuse (Phase 0).
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 1 / 1. Confidence: 3%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): The accounts are 9-21 months stale and distorted by transfer pricing and intercompany charges, so they rarely say anything about the quarter being reported, and the market has months to absorb them.
- Red team (reviewer): Subsidiary operating margins are set by transfer-pricing policy (limited-risk distributor target margins, cost-plus service fees, royalty charges), so divergence from the parent's reported segment margin is a tax-structure artifact rather than information; and because the accounts arrive 9-21 months after the period, anything real in them has already been in the parent's 10-K segment disclosures and impairment-test commentary for several quarters, leaving nothing for a T-1 to T+2 window.

## S18 Clustered open-market insider buying (Form 4) and corporate-jet movements

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Cross-sector; Form 4 applies to all names, jet tracking to the subset owning or leasing aircraft. Target: EPS surprise, guidance; M&A (jets) (forward).
- Seed item: [regulatory] Clustered insider buying (Form 4) and corporate jet flight tracking before M&A
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Drop the corporate-jet leg entirely: it targets M&A announcements, not the earnings-announcement abnormal return the program trades; the only historical sources are non-commercial (OpenSky free tier, AMBER) or paid (ADS-B Exchange); live feeds need self-archiving with no history; FAA LADD/PIA hides many aircraft; and JetTrack, Quandl and Gordon Haskett already sell it to funds. (2) Build the Form 4 leg from the SEC structured Form 3/4/5 data sets (2006q1-2026q3) with point-in-time filing dates from the EDGAR daily index, not from OpenInsider (unreachable from the container). (3) Exclude 10b5-1 plan trades, option exercises and routine traders per the Cohen-Malloy-Pomorski classification; keep only open-market purchases (transaction code P) by officers. (4) Correct lead time: blackouts start 2-4 weeks before quarter end and releases come 3-6 weeks after, so the last qualifying buy is typically 40-50 days before the release, not 30. (5) Reframe as a conditioning overlay and control variable (interaction with short interest from FINRA and with the sign of the prior-quarter surprise), not a standalone trade; the generic insider-buy factor must be a control. (6) Cost-only differentiation from vendors is acknowledged; if the earnings-window conditioning adds nothing over the generic cluster screen in backtest, drop it.
- Mechanism: Multiple insiders buying in the open window after the prior release signals that the current quarter is tracking above internal plans, which predicts a positive surprise or guidance raise at the next release (LITERATURE: Lakonishok and Lee, RFS 2001; Cohen, Malloy and Pomorski, 'Decoding Inside Information', JF 2012, who separate opportunistic from routine trades). Because trading blackouts typically start 2-4 weeks before quarter end (REASONED), the final reading is fixed 30-45 days before the release. Corporate-jet flights to a counterparty's headquarters or an unusual plant site precede M&A announcements or operational problems (REASONED; the Occidental-Omaha 2019 case was widely reported), but that is an M&A event, not an earnings event.
- Industry context: Section 16 forces insiders to disclose trades within two business days, and the long academic record shows purchases carry information; company policies restrict those trades to windows shortly after each release. Corporate aircraft broadcast their positions publicly, so executives' travel is visible to anyone who maps the tail number.
- Measurement window: Form 4: cluster score over the open window (roughly days 2-45 after the prior release), final about 30 days before the next release; EDGAR posts filings within two business days of the trade. Jets: continuous live positions that must be self-archived (adsb.lol is live-only); OpenSky gives historical flights by aircraft under non-commercial terms.. Lead time: 40 days.
- Universe coverage: ~100% of the ~1,300 names for Form 4 eligibility, but qualifying clusters (3+ officers, >$250k, open-market, non-10b5-1) are rare in mid-caps, REASONED at a few dozen events per quarter; jet leg dropped (0%).
- Hypothesis: Among open-market purchases (code P, non-10b5-1, excluding routine traders by the Cohen-Malloy-Pomorski rule) by three or more distinct officers totalling over $250k inside the open trading window that closes 40 or more days before the earnings release, the cluster predicts a positive EPS surprise and a positive T-1 to T+2 sector-ETF-adjusted abnormal return, incremental to the generic insider-buy factor, 30-day consensus revisions, short interest, sector, size and 12-1 momentum.
- Who already knows: Insider-cluster signals are sold by Verity (InsiderScore), 2iQ and Quiver Quant, published free by OpenInsider and studied for decades, so the mid-cap edge is mostly arbitraged (crowdedness 1-2). Jet tracking is sold to hedge funds by JetTrack (REASONED-confident, reported by the WSJ) and listed by Quiver Quant (REASONED), crowdedness 2-3.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Verity/InsiderScore, 2iQ, Quiver Quant (insider transactions); JetTrack (corporate aircraft, REASONED). Why ours differs: Condition the Form 4 cluster on the earnings-window structure (buys late in the open window, by officers rather than directors, at names with short interest) rather than the generic cluster screen; for jets, limit to a watch-list of universe names with a known tail number and alert on first-ever visits to another universe company's HQ airport.
- Vendors found by reviewer: 2iQ Research global insider transaction data feed (FactSet listing) - https://insight.factset.com/resources/2iq-research-global-insider-transaction-datafeed; 2iQ on WRDS - https://wrds-www.wharton.upenn.edu/pages/about/data-vendors/2iq/; InsiderScore / VerityData (analyst-reviewed Form 4 data, 10b5-1 tags; third-party comparison page) - https://www.findmymoat.com/vs/insiderscore-veritydata-insiderscore-vs-openinsider; JetTrack by Paragon Intel (corporate flight analytics, 1,000+ companies, distributed via Eagle Alpha and Bloomberg) - https://data.bloomberglp.com/professional/sites/10/JetTrack-Overview_Use-Cases.pdf; JetTrack product page - https://www.jetnet.com/products/jettrack; Quandl corporate jet dataset for predicting M&A (Waters Technology) - https://www.waterstechnology.com/node/3608906; Gordon Haskett corporate jet tracking database - https://gordonhaskett.com/news/13172736

- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Decades of publication and several vendors mean the Form 4 effect is small in liquid mid-caps and the blackout window separates the signal from the event; jet trips are ambiguous (board meetings, roadshows, personal use) and privacy programs hide many aircraft.
- Red team (reviewer): Insider cluster buying is a decades-old, vendor-sold and free-screened factor; in liquid mid-caps the drift is captured in the days after the Form 4 posts, weeks before the earnings event, so by T-1 it is in the price and what remains is a proxy for contrarian/value (insiders buy after price drops), making any event-window abnormal return mean reversion rather than a surprise forecast.

## S19 ATS open-requisition count and wording shifts

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Labor-driven growth companies with public ATS boards: software/internet, consumer services, healthcare services, staffing-heavy industrials. Target: guidance + margin (opex); secondary revenue (both).
- Seed item: Job postings: counts by region, hiring freezes, and wording shifts (build versus maintain, restructuring roles)
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Admit the real limitation: there is zero history and the Wayback Machine is blocked from the container, so no Phase 1 backtest is possible; this must be run as a start-archiving-now pipeline with a go/no-go after 3 quarters of snapshots, using Gutierrez et al. (2020) as the prior. (2) Redefine the signal at requisition-ID level: a req that disappears before the board's own median time-to-fill is a pull (negative); one that disappears after it is a fill (neutral or positive). A raw count decline is unsigned without this. (3) Cross-sectionally demean within sector and within ATS platform each week to strip the sector hiring cycle, year-end freezes and the AI-driven headcount restraint affecting all of software. (4) Add Workday tenants via the POST endpoint only after reading each tenant's careers-site terms; keep iCIMS as AMBER. (5) Lower confidence from 18 to 15 for the no-history problem; the margin/opex-guidance channel stays primary. (6) Vendor differentiation is narrow but real on the program's own test: Lightcast refreshes monthly and Revelio's postings start in 2021 (per their pages), so daily req-level pull/fill is faster and more granular, at $0.
- Mechanism: Open requisitions on a company's own applicant-tracking board are a real-time read of management's internal demand plan: a firm that expects to hit or beat its quarter keeps hiring into it, while one seeing bookings soften lets reqs lapse and stops posting weeks before it tells the market. For software, internet, consumer-services and healthcare-services companies, a quarter-to-date collapse in open reqs (or a shift from 'build/new product' roles toward 'maintain/restructuring/transformation' roles) maps to next-quarter guidance and current-quarter opex (margin). The requisition count is a direct input to opex, so the margin channel is tighter than the revenue channel. LITERATURE: Gutierrez, Lourie, Nekrasov and Shevlin (Management Science, 2020) report that changes in online job postings predict future sales and earnings news; REASONED that the public-ATS version is a noisier subset of what they used.
- Industry context: Hiring plans are set against internal bookings and pipeline forecasts that management sees weeks before investors do, and requisitions are the cheapest lever to pull when a quarter wobbles. Because ATS boards are public and updated in real time, the posting count leaks that internal view without any disclosure event.
- Measurement window: Quarter-to-date daily snapshots of open postings from fiscal-quarter start to T-2; signal = log change in open reqs over the final 8 weeks of the quarter versus the first 5 weeks, plus the share of postings in 'build' versus 'maintain/restructure' keyword classes and region concentration. Final reading at T-2. History: zero at start; must be archived daily from now because ATS APIs expose only current postings (TESTED HERE).. Lead time: 2 days.
- Universe coverage: ~20-25% (250-320 names) on documented keyless ATS APIs; the Workday POST endpoint (TESTED HERE) could roughly double this across industrials, retail and healthcare services (REASONED), subject to per-tenant terms review.
- Hypothesis: A rise of one standard deviation (sector- and platform-demeaned) in the share of requisitions pulled before median time-to-fill over the final 8 weeks of the fiscal quarter, measured 2 trading days before the release, predicts lowered forward guidance or a lower opex outlook and a negative T-1 to T+1 sector-ETF-adjusted abnormal return, controlling for 30-day consensus revisions, sector, size and 12-1 momentum.
- Who already knows: Sell-side rarely tracks postings systematically for mid-caps, but LinkUp, Revelio Labs, Lightcast and Thinknum sell daily company-level posting counts to many funds, so the aggregate posting-count factor is largely priced for large caps (REASONED). The gap is in mid-cap names and in the wording/role-mix classification, which vendors expose less granularly. Expect modest residual edge, showing up more in opex guidance than revenue.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): LinkUp, Revelio Labs, Lightcast, Thinknum (all sell company-level job-posting counts and derived series).. Why ours differs: Cheaper ($0 versus vendor contracts), daily rather than weekly/monthly refresh, and more granular: requisition-level IDs let us measure reqs pulled before being filled (freeze proxy) and classify wording (build versus maintain, 'restructuring', 'transformation', 'interim'), which aggregate vendor counts do not expose. Honest caveat: vendors have multi-year history and we have none until archived.
- Vendors found by reviewer: LinkUp (job-listing data; market-reaction use case) - https://www.linkup.com/use-cases/the-market-reaction-to-job-listing-data; Revelio Labs COSMOS job postings (postings history from 2021; investor pitch) - https://reveliolabs.com/job-postings-cosmos and https://www.reveliolabs.com/solutions/investors; Lightcast API 'Investment Support' (postings refreshed monthly, delivered via Snowflake) - https://docs.lightcast.io/lightcast-api/docs/investment-support; Thinknum Job Listings dataset - https://blog.thinknum.com/job-listings; Textkernel hiring signals for investors - https://www.textkernel.com/products-solutions/hiring-signals-for-investors/
- Literature found by reviewer: Gutierrez, Lourie, Nekrasov and Shevlin, 'Are Online Job Postings Informative to Investors?', Management Science 66(7) 2020, pp. 3133-3141 (LinkUp data; postings lead one-year-ahead sales and earnings growth, stronger for growth hiring; positive market reaction) - https://papers.ssrn.com/abstract=3342669 and https://pubsonline.informs.org/doi/suppl/10.1287/mnsc.2019.3450; 'Is hiring fast a good sign? The informativeness of job vacancy duration for future firm profitability' (listing) - https://ouci.dntb.gov.ua/works/4EVwWZKl; Ivey working paper 'Identifying overvalued stocks with corporate job postings' - https://test2.ivey.uwo.ca/media/h01llll0/identifying-overvalued-stocks-with-corporate-job-postings.pdf
- Data sources:
  - Greenhouse Job Board API [DATA] https://boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/duolingo/jobs?content=false (update: live; poll daily (TESTED HERE); history: current postings only, updated_at present; no history (TESTED HERE))
  - Lever Postings API [DATA] https://api.lever.co/v0/postings/plaid?mode=json (update: live; history: current postings only; createdAt epoch (TESTED HERE))
  - Ashby Job Board API [DATA] https://api.ashbyhq.com/posting-api/job-board/ramp (update: live; history: current postings only; publishedAt present (TESTED HERE))
  - SmartRecruiters Posting API [DATA] https://api.smartrecruiters.com/v1/companies/Visa/postings?limit=1 (update: live; history: current postings; releasedDate present (TESTED HERE))
  - iCIMS careers portal (per-tenant HTML) [DATA] https://careers-ulta.icims.com/jobs/search?ss=1 (update: live; per-tenant terms flag; history: snapshot only)
  - Workday job board JSON (per tenant) [UNPROBED] https://<tenant>.wd5.myworkdayjobs.com/wday/cxs/<tenant>/<site>/jobs (update: live (REASONED); history: snapshot only (REASONED))
  - California EDD WARN xlsx (freeze/layoff complement) [DATA] https://edd.ca.gov/en/jobs_and_training/Layoff_Services_WARN/ (update: roughly weekly (REASONED); history: current fiscal year xlsx; prior years as separate files (TESTED HERE))
