- Cost: $0: all ATS APIs are keyless public endpoints; only storage for daily snapshots. No paid tier needed.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 2. Confidence: 15%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Posting counts are dominated by seasonal and structural hiring patterns (year-end freezes, AI-driven headcount restraint across all of software), so a QTD decline is a sector factor rather than a company signal; vendors already sell the clean version to larger funds, and the remaining mid-cap edge may be smaller than round-trip costs.
- Red team (reviewer): Open-requisition counts move with sector-wide hiring cycles, year-end freezes and the current economy-wide headcount restraint, so a QTD decline is mostly a sector-time factor that the sector-ETF adjustment already removes; the firm-specific residual is exactly what LinkUp and Revelio clients model with multi-year history, and with zero history this version cannot even sign a disappearing req (pulled vs filled) for its first several quarters.

## S20 Employee-voice negativity (Reddit/HN) ahead of earnings

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Consumer-facing and tech employers with active employee communities: software, consumer internet, retail, restaurants, healthcare services. Target: EPS surprise + guidance (both).
- Seed item: Employee review sentiment trends, especially leadership complaints
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: The only dense, validated source of employee voice (Glassdoor; Green et al. 2019 used over one million structured reviews across 1,200 firms) is blocked and terms-prohibited, and the proposed substitute fails on three fronts at once: (i) legal, because Reddit's Data API terms restrict commercial use and the free script tier does not cover trading, which makes the primary source RED rather than AMBER, and Blind's terms restrict automated access; (ii) data, because Reddit search caps at ~1,000 results with no backfill, so there is no history to backtest and HN's corpus is near-empty for mid-cap employers outside developer tools; (iii) measurement, because posts cannot be attributed to employees versus customers, investors or journalists, and negativity keyword counts spike on already-public events (announced layoffs, CEO departures), which is news sentiment, not inside-the-building information. Sparse, non-attributable, non-backtestable and terms-restricted is a REJECT by the program's own rules.
- Mechanism: Current and former employees post about layoffs, leadership turmoil, missed internal targets and quiet hiring freezes on Reddit (company and industry subreddits) and Hacker News weeks before the facts reach a filing. For firms where line employees see demand directly (retail, restaurants, logistics, SaaS sales teams), a rising share of negative, leadership-focused posts in the 90 days before earnings proxies internal deterioration that feeds revenue and guidance misses. LITERATURE: Green, Huang, Wen and Zhou (Journal of Financial Economics, 2019) find that changes in crowdsourced Glassdoor employer ratings predict earnings surprises and returns; REASONED that Reddit/HN mentions are a noisier, sparser substitute because Glassdoor and Indeed are blocked and ToS-prohibited. This is one of the two permitted sentiment-type ideas.
- Industry context: Employees learn about missed internal targets, layoffs and leadership churn before investors because those events are operational rather than financial, and anonymous forums are where that knowledge surfaces. The effect is strongest where frontline staff observe demand directly, such as retail floors, restaurants and SaaS sales teams.
- Measurement window: Rolling 90-day window ending T-2: count and negativity share of posts/comments mentioning the company as an employer (keywords: layoff, RIF, freeze, PIP, leadership, CEO, reorg) versus the company's trailing 4-quarter baseline. Reddit search returns at most ~1,000 results per query (TESTED HERE), so backfill is limited; HN Algolia has full corpus history with created_at filters (TESTED HERE).. Lead time: 2 days.
- Universe coverage: Claimed ~15%; realistically well under 5% of names would have enough employee-authored posts per quarter to score (REASONED), and the author cannot be verified as an employee.
- Hypothesis: A one-standard-deviation rise versus the company's trailing 4-quarter baseline in the negativity share of employee-authored Reddit/HN posts over the 90 days before earnings, measured 2 trading days before the release, predicts a negative EPS surprise and a lower T-1 to T+1 sector-adjusted abnormal return, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Glassdoor-derived ratings are sold by Thinknum and Revelio Labs (REASONED) and have been used by quant funds since the 2019 JFE paper, so the employer-rating effect is largely arbitraged for liquid names. Reddit/HN employee chatter is less systematically harvested but is also sparse and noisy. Sell-side ignores it.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Thinknum and Revelio Labs (Glassdoor/Indeed-derived employer review series; REASONED that both sell such series).. Why ours differs: Not a cheaper copy of Glassdoor; it is a different, lawful source (Reddit API with a registered app, HN) focused on leadership-complaint and freeze keywords rather than star ratings. Granularity is per post with timestamps, letting us measure spikes within the quarter rather than monthly rating averages.
- Vendors found by reviewer: No vendor found selling Reddit/HN employee-chatter series (two searches). Glassdoor-derived series from Thinknum or Revelio were not confirmed in results (UNKNOWN).; Neudata note 'Crowdsourced employee reviews: a predictor of stock returns?' (data-broker commentary on Glassdoor-type data) - https://www.neudata.co/intelligence/crowdsourced-employee-reviews-predictor-of-stock-returns
- Literature found by reviewer: Green, Huang, Wen and Zhou, 'Crowdsourced Employer Reviews and Stock Returns', Journal of Financial Economics 134 (2019), pp. 236-251 (1M+ Glassdoor reviews, 1,200+ firms; rating changes forecast one-quarter-ahead earnings surprises; 0.84%/month alpha) - https://faculty.georgetown.edu/qw50/EmpReviews.pdf; Moniz, 'Inferring Employees' Social Media Perceptions' (NY Fed 2015 conference paper) - https://www.newyorkfed.org/medialibrary/media/research/conference/2015/econ_culture/Moniz_Andy_InferringEmployeesSocialMediaPerceptions.pdf; 'What Do Employees Know? Evidence from a Social Media Platform' (arXiv listing; platform-based employee information) - https://bohrium.dp.tech/paper/arxiv/6bfb058fabcca6775c65d59084adf419ffeeec9c05481044d605fff5b40d29a0; CFA Digest summary of Green et al. - https://rpc.cfainstitute.org/research/cfa-digest/2020/05/dig-v50-n5-3
- Data sources:
  - Reddit Data API (OAuth client credentials) [KEY] https://www.reddit.com/api/v1/access_token (update: live; history: search capped at ~1,000 results per query (TESTED HERE))
  - Hacker News Algolia search API [DATA] https://hn.algolia.com/api/v1/search?query=datadog&tags=story&numericFilters=created_at_i>1735689600 (update: live; history: full HN corpus (TESTED HERE))
  - Glassdoor (blocked from container; scraping prohibited by Glassdoor terms) [UNPROBED] https://www.glassdoor.com/ (update: ; history: )
  - Indeed company reviews (blocked; terms prohibit scraping) [UNPROBED] https://www.indeed.com/cmp/ (update: ; history: )
  - Blind (terms restrict automated access) [UNPROBED] https://www.teamblind.com/ (update: ; history: )
- Reviewer source checks: https://www.reddit.com/api/v1/access_token -> KEY (Phase 0: registered script app; terms restrict commercial use) AMBER/RED: Reddit Data API terms restrict commercial use without approval; trad…; https://hn.algolia.com/api/v1/search?query=datadog&tags=story&numericFilters=cr… -> DATA (Phase 0) Full corpus, but HN volume about mid-cap employers outside developer-tool compa…; https://www.glassdoor.com/ -> TESTED HERE: 403 Blocked and terms-prohibited.; https://www.indeed.com/cmp/ -> TESTED HERE: 403 Blocked and terms-prohibited.; https://www.teamblind.com/ -> TESTED HERE: 200 Reachable, but terms restrict automated access; reachability does not make scra…
- Cost: $0 for Reddit (registered script app on the free tier; Reddit Data API terms require registration and restrict commercial use, check before trading on it) and HN. Glassdoor/Indeed data would require a licensed vendor feed (Thinknum/Revelio-class contracts, well above $100/month).
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 1 / 4. Confidence: 3%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Reddit/HN volume is driven by consumer brand salience and news coverage, not operations, so negativity spikes are reactions to already-public events (layoff headlines) and carry no information beyond price; the keyless substitute for Glassdoor may simply be too sparse to score more than a handful of names per quarter.
- Red team (reviewer): Reddit/HN mention volume about a mid-cap employer is driven by newsworthy, already-priced events rather than operations, and authorship cannot be verified, so the negativity share is a noisy public-news sentiment proxy; the one source with demonstrated predictive content (Glassdoor ratings) is unavailable, and the substitute has perhaps 1% of its density.

## S21 App-rating velocity and chart rank for app-centric consumer tech

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Consumer internet, mobile games, subscription apps, connected fitness and app-first consumer services (ex-GICS Financials). Target: revenue (nowcast).
- Seed item: App downloads, store rankings, and web-traffic trends for consumer tech
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: This is the program's explicit exclusion (app-download panels sold at scale by Sensor Tower, Apptopia and data.ai) re-entered through a weaker proxy. The published evidence (AFA/AEA 2025 papers) is for vendor-measured downloads, and sell-side previews for these ~90 app-centric names quote those panels, so modelled installs and in-app revenue are already in consensus; rating velocity is a noisier estimate of the same quantity, which means the gap between the signal and consensus is mostly measurement error, not information. The series is also structurally contaminated: in-app rating prompts (which firms toggle with releases) and Apple's periodic rating resets drive rating counts independently of installs (REASONED), top-chart rank is ordinal and snapshot-only, and no history exists until daily archiving runs for several quarters, so nothing can be backtested in Phase 1. No paper tests rating velocity. Cheaper is not enough when the information is already priced and the proxy is worse; the only surviving angle (review-text complaint spikes for outages and price changes) is a different, event-driven idea that Sensor Tower also sells as review analytics and should be proposed separately if at all.
- Mechanism: For companies whose revenue is a function of active users and in-app subscriptions, the daily rate of new App Store ratings (delta of the cumulative userRatingCount from the iTunes lookup API) and the app's rank in Apple's top-free and top-grossing charts track new-user acquisition and monetization within the quarter. A QTD rating-velocity change versus the prior-year quarter nowcasts bookings growth for subscription apps (Duolingo-type), mobile games (Playtika-type) and dating apps (Grindr/Bumble-type), and a sustained drop in grossing rank nowcasts in-app purchase revenue. REASONED: ratings are a roughly fixed fraction of installs, so their velocity is an install proxy; the grossing chart is a direct if ordinal read of IAP revenue.
- Industry context: App-first consumer companies monetize users through subscriptions and in-app purchases, so user acquisition and chart position within the quarter map almost mechanically to bookings. Because Apple exposes chart rank and cumulative rating counts publicly, a daily archive reconstructs the install curve without a paid panel.
- Measurement window: Fiscal quarter start to T-1: daily archived snapshots of (a) cumulative rating count per app (iTunes lookup), (b) daily position in US top-free and top-grossing charts (Apple RSS), (c) new-review flow from the customer-reviews RSS (50 per page, about 10 pages deep, TESTED HERE). Signal = YoY change in QTD rating velocity and average grossing rank. History: none until archived (snapshot endpoints, TESTED HERE); Tranco daily lists are retrievable historically for web-rank context.. Lead time: 1 days.
- Universe coverage: ~7% claimed (about 90 names); several (Bumble, Grindr, Life360) sit near the market-cap floor, and the testable set with daily archives is zero until snapshots accumulate.
- Hypothesis: A YoY change of at least 15% in QTD App Store rating velocity for the company's primary app, measured 1 trading day before earnings, predicts the sign of the revenue surprise versus consensus and the T-1 to T+1 sector-ETF-adjusted abnormal return, controlling for 30-day consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Sensor Tower, data.ai, Apptopia and Similarweb sell modelled downloads, DAU and in-app revenue to essentially every fund covering these names, and sell-side analysts quote those panels in previews, so download trends are heavily priced. Crowdedness 1.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Sensor Tower, data.ai, Apptopia, Similarweb (downloads, usage and web traffic at scale).. Why ours differs: Only on cost ($0) and one angle vendors publish less: rating velocity is a free, daily, per-app install proxy, and the review-text flow lets us measure complaint spikes (pricing changes, outages) at daily granularity. It is a weaker proxy, not a cheaper copy of the same thing. If backtests show it adds nothing over vendor-implied consensus, drop it.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Sensor Tower finance solutions (app downloads, usage, revenue estimates for investors; acquired data.ai in 2024 per results) - https://sensortower.com/solutions/finance; Apptopia for quantitative investors (claims app data leads public-company earnings 70%+ of the time; marketing claim) - https://apptopia.com:443/en/quantitative-investors; Apptopia data overview - https://apptopia.com/our-data
- Literature found by reviewer: AFA 2025 session paper: quarterly app downloads (Sensor Tower) predict next-quarter earnings; a 10% rise in downloads predicts about $10M more quarterly earnings for an average firm - https://afajof.org/management/viewp.php?n=114548; AEA 2025 version of the same study (apps of US public companies 2012-2023; market does not fully price app information) - https://www.aeaweb.org/conference/2025/program/paper/tsKtGb3z; HKU 2019 ICIS short paper: abnormal Google Play downloads and daily users associate with next-month abnormal returns - https://repository.hku.hk/bitstream/10722/275443/1/Content.pdf; No paper found that uses App Store rating count or rating velocity as the predictor (two searches).
- Data sources:
  - Apple iTunes lookup API (userRatingCount) [DATA] https://itunes.apple.com/lookup?id=284882215 (update: live snapshot; archive daily; history: none (TESTED HERE))
  - Apple top charts RSS (marketingtools) [DATA] https://rss.marketingtools.apple.com/api/v2/us/apps/top-free/10/apps.json (update: daily; history: snapshot only (TESTED HERE))
  - App Store customer reviews RSS [DATA] https://itunes.apple.com/us/rss/customerreviews/id=284882215/sortBy=mostRecent/json (update: live; history: ~500 most recent via paging (TESTED HERE))
  - Google Play store page [DATA] https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.duolingo&hl=en&gl=us (update: live; Google ToS flag on scraping; history: snapshot only)
  - Tranco top-1M list [DATA] https://tranco-list.eu/top-1m.csv.zip (update: daily; history: older lists retrievable by list id (TESTED HERE))
  - Cloudflare Radar domain ranking [KEY] https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/radar/ranking/top?limit=5 (update: daily/weekly; history: CC BY-NC 4.0 non-commercial licence flag)

- Reviewer source checks: https://itunes.apple.com/lookup?id=284882215 -> DATA (Phase 0) Snapshot only; must archive daily; no history.; https://rss.marketingtools.apple.com/api/v2/us/apps/top-free/10/apps.json -> DATA (Phase 0) Snapshot only.; https://itunes.apple.com/us/rss/customerreviews/id=284882215/sortBy=mostRecent/… -> DATA (Phase 0) ~500 most recent reviews: for a high-volume app that is days of history.; https://play.google.com/store/apps/details?id=com.duolingo&hl=en&gl=us -> DATA (Phase 0, Google ToS flag) AMBER: Google terms on automated access.; https://tranco-list.eu/top-1m.csv.zip -> DATA (Phase 0) Web-rank context only.; https://api.cloudflare.com/client/v4/radar/ranking/top?limit=5 -> KEY (Phase 0: CC BY-NC 4.0) AMBER: non-commercial licence; unusable for trading.; https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/metrics/pageviews/per-article/en.wikipedia/al… -> DATA (Phase 0) CC0; daily since 2015.
- Cost: $0: Apple endpoints and Tranco are keyless; Cloudflare Radar needs a free token but its CC BY-NC licence makes it unusable for trading (REASONED); Google Play scraping is a ToS question. Vendor panels (Sensor Tower, data.ai) cost far more than $100/month.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 1 / 1 / 2. Confidence: 5%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Vendor panels already measure downloads and revenue directly and are in consensus, so rating velocity adds no incremental information; ratings are also distorted by in-app rating prompts that companies toggle, producing spurious spikes unrelated to installs.
- Red team (reviewer): Sensor Tower and Apptopia-modelled downloads and in-app revenue are in every sell-side preview for these names, so rating velocity is a noisier version of information already in consensus; worse, the rating-count series is driven by in-app rating prompts that firms switch on and off with releases, so YoY velocity changes mostly measure prompt policy and release cadence rather than new users.

## S22 HS-code import nowcast (Census) for import-dependent hardgoods

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Importers of finished goods: toys, footwear and apparel brands, small appliances, powersports, furniture, consumer-electronics accessories, HVAC equipment. Target: revenue + margin (inventory) (nowcast).
- Seed item: Customs import records (bills of lading) showing supplier shifts and inventory build
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Restrict to names with verified HTS-10 dominance, checked against the free Datamyne/Panjiva top-importer pages and the company's own 10-K sourcing disclosure; drop pooled categories (general footwear, apparel, furniture, HVAC) where the universe company is a minority of the line. (2) Use quantity as well as value, by origin country, and deflate value with the BLS HS import price index (FRED IP-series) so tariff-driven price moves are not read as volume. (3) Reframe as an inventory/gross-margin signal (import build versus consensus-implied revenue growth), not a revenue nowcast; test a 1-3 month import-to-shipment lag explicitly. (4) Exclude or dummy quarters with announced tariff changes, since 2025 pull-forwards broke the import-to-sales link (REASONED). (5) Timing confirmed: the FT-900 for month M lands about 5 weeks after month end, so months 1-2 are available 10-30 days before most releases and month 3 only for late reporters; keep lead time 14 and note that federal shutdowns delay releases. (6) The reason to keep it over S19/S21: monthly history to 2013 with a free key makes it the only digital-lens candidate here that can be backtested in Phase 1.
- Mechanism: Companies that import finished goods from Asia book revenue when they sell through, but their inventory and sales plan show up first as customs entries. Monthly US import value and quantity by HS-10 code and origin country (Census FT-900 detail) nowcasts the category's sell-in for months 1-2 of the quarter; for a company that dominates its HS category (toys, powersports, grills, e-bikes), the category series is close to its own inbound shipments. A sharp import build with flat consumer demand signals an inventory overhang (gross-margin risk from markdowns), while an import drop signals revenue shortfall or supplier shifts. Company-level bills of lading (Panjiva/ImportGenius) are the direct version but are paid and vendor-saturated; the HS-level Census series is the cheaper, lawful aggregate version. REASONED throughout.
- Industry context: Consumer hardgoods brands outsource manufacturing to Asia and must land inventory 1-3 months before sale, so customs data is a physical leading indicator of what they plan to sell. Because the brands own the goods on arrival, import volume also predicts inventory and markdown risk, not just sales.
- Measurement window: Months 1 and 2 of the fiscal quarter only: Census releases a month's detail roughly 5-6 weeks after month end (REASONED from the FT-900 schedule), so for a calendar quarter the February release lands in early April and most companies report late April to May; month 3 arrives after the announcement. Signal = YoY change in category import value and units for months 1-2 versus consensus-implied revenue growth. History: monthly from 2013 via the API (per probe), longer via DataWeb.. Lead time: 14 days.
- Universe coverage: ~1-2% (15-25 names, REASONED) once restricted to companies that verifiably dominate a narrow HTS-10 line (toys, clogs, powersports, premium coolers, selected small appliances); the claimed 8% pools lines shared by dozens of importers.
- Hypothesis: For companies that account for a verifiable majority of a narrow HTS-10 import line, a YoY change of at least 15% in price-deflated months-1-to-2 import quantity for that line, measured 14 days before the earnings release and net of the consensus-implied revenue growth, predicts the sign of the inventory-related gross-margin surprise (build with flat demand: negative; drop: revenue shortfall) and the T-1 to T+2 sector-ETF-adjusted abnormal return, controlling for 30-day consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum, excluding quarters with announced tariff changes.
- Who already knows: Bills of lading are sold at scale by Panjiva, ImportGenius and Descartes Datamyne and appear in sell-side previews for the largest importers (REASONED). HS-level Census series are public and used by some sell-side consumer analysts, but are rarely mapped to mid-cap tickers at HS-10 granularity; the edge, if any, is in the mapping and in names where one company dominates a narrow HS line.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Panjiva (S&P Global), ImportGenius, Descartes Datamyne (bill-of-lading data).. Why ours differs: Cheaper and lawful: free government aggregates instead of a paid BoL feed; different granularity: HS-10 by origin country, which also captures supplier-country shifts (China to Vietnam/Mexico) that affect tariff cost and gross margin. Not faster: it is slower and covers only 2 of 3 months, so it should be tested as a margin/inventory signal rather than a precise revenue nowcast.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Panjiva (S&P Global) bill-of-lading data; published research on HS 9503 toy imports vs Hasbro and Mattel - https://panjiva.com/research/trade-policy-is-not-a-game-for-toymakers-hasbro-and-mattel/8784 and https://panjiva.com/trends/prime+ndiez; ImportGenius (300M+ US import bills of lading from CBP vessel manifests since 2006; searchable by HS code and company) - https://www.importgenius.com/features/bill-of-lading-database; Descartes Datamyne HS pages listing top importers per HTS-10 line (e.g., Walmart, Mattel, Melissa & Doug under 9503.00.0090) - https://www.datamyne.com/hts/95/9503000090; usimportdata.com (HS 9503 importer lists, reseller) - https://www.usimportdata.com/data/us-95-toys-import-data/country-canada
- Literature found by reviewer: Federal Reserve FEDS 2021-066 working paper assessing shipment-level bill-of-lading data as a source for trade research - https://federalreserve.gov/econres/feds/files/2021066pap.pdf; AEA 2023 version of the same (notes US law limits public access to BoL records to vessel shipments) - https://aeaweb.org/conference/2023/program/paper/znBYD347; BLS import price index for HS 9503 on FRED (price-volume decomposition) - https://fred.stlouisfed.org/series/IP9503; No paper found that maps Census HS-level imports to firm-level revenue surprises (two searches).
- Data sources:
  - Census international trade API (imports by HS) [KEY] https://api.census.gov/data/timeseries/intltrade/imports/hs?get=CTY_CODE,GEN_VAL_MO&time=2025-06&I_COMMODITY=9503 (update: monthly, ~5-6 week lag (REASONED); history: monthly series back to 2013 (per probe))
  - USITC DataWeb [KEY] https://dataweb.usitc.gov/ (update: monthly; history: multi-decade (REASONED))
  - Panjiva (company-level bills of lading) [KEY] https://panjiva.com (update: daily; history: paid enterprise subscription only)
  - ImportYeti (blocked from container) [UNPROBED] https://www.importyeti.com/ (update: ; history: )
  - Port of Los Angeles container statistics (volume context) [DATA] https://www.portoflosangeles.org/business/statistics/container-statistics (update: monthly; history: TEUs back to 1995 (TESTED HERE))
- Reviewer source checks: https://api.census.gov/data/timeseries/intltrade/imports/hs?get=CTY_CODE,GEN_VA… -> KEY (Phase 0: free Census API key) Monthly HS-10 by origin country, value and quantity, back to 2013: backtest-rea…; https://dataweb.usitc.gov/ -> KEY (Phase 0: free DataWeb account) Longer history; not verified.; https://panjiva.com -> KEY (Phase 0: paid enterprise) Excluded by programme rules; the free Panjiva research and Datamyne HTS top-imp…; https://www.importyeti.com/ -> TESTED HERE: 403 Blocked; dropped.; https://www.portoflosangeles.org/business/statistics/container-statistics -> DATA (Phase 0) Volume context only.; https://www.datamyne.com/hts/95/9503000090 -> UNPROBED (seen in search results; public HTS page listing top importers) Use only to verify which universe company dominates a line; not a data feed.
- Cost: $0: free Census API key (api.census.gov/data/key_signup.html) and free DataWeb account. Company-level bills of lading are paid-only (Panjiva/ImportGenius; well above $100/month) and excluded by programme rules unless a cheaper angle exists.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 12%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): HS categories pool many companies, and the Census series lags so much that the market already has retailer commentary, port volumes and vendor BoL counts by the time months 1-2 print; company-specific timing (port congestion, tariff pull-forwards) can break the import-to-revenue link entirely in a given quarter.
- Red team (reviewer): HS lines pool many importers, so the category series is an industry demand and tariff-timing factor that the sector-ETF adjustment already strips; for the handful of names that do dominate a line, Panjiva publishes the mapping and sell-side consumer analysts watch the same Census tables, so the residual that reaches the earnings window is small and arrives after retailer commentary and port volumes have already moved the price.

## S23 Google Trends YoY for single-product consumer small caps

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Single-brand consumer discretionary and staples: beverages, footwear, outdoor gear, beauty, consumer audio. Target: revenue (nowcast).
- Seed item: Google Trends for single-product small caps
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Make Wikimedia pageviews (CC0, keyless, daily since 2015) the primary series and backtest it now; Google Trends becomes a low-volume cross-check only, pulled through pytrends at most once per name per week with a fixed anchor term and fixed window, and its terms status stays AMBER (legal 3). (2) Use Trends topic entities (mids) rather than raw strings to handle ambiguous brand names, and drop names that have no clean entity. (3) Add an event-exclusion filter: remove weeks with product-launch, recall, lawsuit or viral-news spikes (detected as pageview z-scores above 3 with matching news) so the QTD mean reflects baseline interest, not attention shocks. (4) Report the signal net of the pre-announcement price drift (T-20 to T-2 return) as an explicit test of whether it is already priced; Da-Engelberg-Gao's finding that search information is incorporated only at the announcement was measured on 2004-2008 data, before the chart became a standard sell-side tool. (5) Lower confidence from 20 to 15 for crowding and the 429/normalization fragility.
- Mechanism: For a company whose revenue is dominated by one consumer brand, search interest for that brand is a direct proxy for purchase intent and sell-through within the quarter. A YoY change in the quarter-to-date average of the Google Trends index (pulled in one request with a fixed anchor term to keep normalization stable) nowcasts revenue growth versus consensus-implied growth; Wikipedia pageviews for the brand article give a licensed (CC0), keyless cross-check. LITERATURE: Da, Engelberg and Gao (Journal of Finance, 2011) show Google search volume captures attention and predicts short-run returns; the revenue-nowcast use is REASONED and widely attempted by sell-side.
- Industry context: Single-brand consumer companies live or die on brand heat, and search is where consumers reveal it before they buy. When one product line is most of revenue, the brand search curve and the revenue curve are tightly coupled, which is not true for diversified companies.
- Measurement window: Fiscal quarter start to T-2 (Trends daily data lags about 1-2 days): QTD mean of the brand's daily US search index relative to the same days a year earlier, computed within a single 5-year weekly pull plus a 90-day daily pull to avoid normalization drift. Final reading at T-2. History: Trends back to 2004 weekly (REASONED), Wikimedia pageviews daily from 2015.. Lead time: 2 days.
- Universe coverage: ~3% (about 35 names, REASONED) after restricting the ~60 single-brand names to those whose brand term or Trends topic entity is unambiguous (Crocs, Sonos, HOKA yes; Celsius, YETI, e.l.f. only via topic entity or not at all).
- Hypothesis: A YoY change of at least 15% in the QTD mean of daily Wikipedia pageviews for the company's dominant-brand article (with attention-shock weeks excluded, and cross-checked where available against a weekly Google Trends topic-entity pull), measured 2 trading days before earnings, predicts the sign of the revenue surprise versus consensus and the T-1 to T+1 sector-ETF-adjusted abnormal return, controlling for 30-day consensus revisions, the T-20 to T-2 price drift, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Every sell-side consumer analyst and most fundamental PMs chart Google Trends for these brands in earnings previews; it is in consensus for the obvious names (Celsius, Crocs). Edge, if any, is in less-followed names, disciplined normalization and YoY construction, and combining with pageviews to reduce Trends noise.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None sell Google Trends itself; Similarweb sells web-traffic panels that overlap in information. Sell-side publishes Trends charts for free.. Why ours differs: It is free and already the cheapest possible version; the only differentiation is construction quality and breadth across about 60 small names that analysts chart casually rather than model. If the backtest shows the signal is fully reflected in pre-earnings price moves, drop it.
- Vendors found by reviewer: No vendor sells Google Trends itself. Packagers seen: TickerTrends (search and web-traffic signals ahead of earnings; vendor blog) - https://blog.tickertrends.io/p/using-alternative-financial-data-a5b; AltIndex (alt-data earnings pages; vendor product, no track record) - https://altindex.com/earnings-calendar/googl; Man Group 'Crystal ball' note describing use of Google search and website visits before earnings and warning signals vanish as other managers find them - https://www.man.com/insights/crystal-ball
- Literature found by reviewer: Da, Engelberg and Gao, 'In Search of Fundamentals' (working paper; product search volume predicts revenue surprises, earnings surprises and earnings-announcement returns, strongest for few-product and growth firms; information incorporated only at the announcement) - https://www3.nd.edu/~zda/Google_Earnings.pdf; Da, Engelberg and Gao, 'In Search of Attention', Journal of Finance 2011 - https://rady.ucsd.edu/faculty/directory/engelberg/pub/portfolios/GOOGLE.pdf; Rice Business summary: search trends predict retail revenue up to three quarters ahead and are not fully priced before announcements - https://business.rice.edu/wisdom/forecasting-retail-sales-just-got-smarter-thanks-google-searches; Journal of Investment Strategies: keyword choice is the largest source of bias in Google Trends backtests - https://www.risk.net/journal-of-investment-strategies/technical-paper/2400584/does-google-trends-data-contain-more-predictability-than-price-returns; Challet and Ayed, 'Predicting financial markets with Google Trends and not so random keywords' (arXiv 1307.4643) - https://ar5iv.labs.arxiv.org/html/1307.4643
- Data sources:
  - Google Trends via pytrends [DATA] https://trends.google.com/ (pytrends build_payload(['allbirds'], timeframe='today 3-m')) (update: daily/weekly; unofficial endpoint, 429s under volume (TESTED HERE); history: 2004 onward weekly (REASONED); 3-month daily window tested)
  - Wikimedia Pageviews API [DATA] https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/metrics/pageviews/per-article/en.wikipedia/all-access/user/Crocs/daily/20250101/20251101 (update: daily; history: 2015-07 onward (REASONED))
  - Google Trends explore page CSV export (manual, no API) [UNPROBED] https://trends.google.com/trends/explore (update: ; history: )
- Reviewer source checks: https://trends.google.com/ (pytrends build_payload(['allbirds'], timeframe='tod… -> DATA (Phase 0, unofficial endpoint, 429s under volume) AMBER: unofficial endpoint, robots flag; per-request 0-100 normalization and sa…; https://trends.google.com/trends/explore -> TESTED HERE: 429 on a single GET Rate-limited from the container even at one request; manual CSV export is brows…; https://wikimedia.org/api/rest_v1/metrics/pageviews/per-article/en.wikipedia/al… -> DATA (Phase 0) CC0, keyless, daily since 2015-07: the backtestable primary series.
- Cost: $0. pytrends is unofficial and gets 429s under volume (TESTED HERE), so pulls must be slow; about 60 names at one pull per name per day is feasible. No paid tier exists for Trends.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 3. Confidence: 15%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Search interest spikes on news, controversies and marketing campaigns rather than purchases, and because the chart is on every analyst's screen the pre-earnings drift already reflects it; the unofficial pytrends endpoint can also silently change normalization between pulls, creating spurious YoY changes.
- Red team (reviewer): Search and pageview interest respond to marketing campaigns, news and controversy rather than purchases, and for single-brand consumer names the Trends chart is in every sell-side preview, so the pre-announcement drift already carries it; the Da-Engelberg-Gao result that the information is priced only at the announcement predates its adoption as a standard analyst tool, and the per-request-normalized, sampled Trends series adds spurious YoY changes on top.

## S24 Complaint-spike precursor to recalls and warranty charges (NHTSA, CPSC, MAUDE)

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Vehicle, powersports and RV OEMs; auto and powersports suppliers; medical device makers (ex-biotech); consumer durables subject to CPSC. Target: recall + margin (warranty); secondary guidance (both).
- Seed item: Product review velocity (rate of new reviews) and defect-complaint spikes preceding recalls
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Drop the supplier leg (component-to-supplier mapping is too uncertain to trade) and the CPSC incident-report leg (public Incident endpoint returns 404; recalls endpoint kept as a flag only). (2) Promote openFDA MAUDE to a primary channel for device makers: it is live from the container with a 1-2 week lag (TESTED HERE), not monthly. (3) Aggregate complaints to issuer level per quarter (sum across models/brands) rather than per model, so rare 2x spikes become a continuous rate; normalize by the issuer's trailing-4-quarter daily baseline as proposed. (4) Exclude the 60 days after any recall, NHTSA investigation opening (ODI), FDA safety communication or major media story, to remove reverse-causal complaint surges, and require the spike to be concentrated in one component or event type. (5) Backtest now with point-in-time dates: NHTSA dateComplaintFiled and MAUDE date_received plus a conservative publication lag; both have multi-decade history. (6) Lower signal_noise from 3 to 2: most spikes never become recalls and accruals often land in a later quarter; the literature's negative abnormal return arrives after the recall announcement, so the earnings-window payoff is the smaller within-quarter warranty-cost and guidance channel. (7) Confidence 22 to 20; crowdedness 4 to 3 because the NHTSA-complaint-return link is published and plaintiff firms mine the same data.
- Mechanism: Consumer complaints to NHTSA (by make, model year and component), CPSC incident reports and FDA MAUDE device adverse-event reports accumulate for weeks to months before a recall is announced, and the manufacturer books warranty and recall accruals in the quarter the loss becomes probable and estimable (ASC 450), hitting gross margin and EPS. For OEMs in the universe (Harley-Davidson, Polaris, BRP, Winnebago, Thor, Rivian, Lucid) a 90-day complaint rate per model that is 2-3x the trailing baseline and concentrated in one component predicts elevated recall probability and a margin miss; for device makers (Inspire Medical, Penumbra, iRhythm, Tandem, Glaukos) MAUDE event spikes precede FDA recalls that drive shipment holds and guidance. REASONED: the complaint-to-recall link is mechanical because NHTSA and FDA open investigations off these reports; the link to the earnings move depends on whether the recall or accrual lands inside the quarter.
- Industry context: Regulators require manufacturers to track and report defects, and the public complaint databases fill up well before a recall is decided because that is the evidence the recall rests on. Warranty and recall accruals are booked when probable, so a recall decided inside the quarter hits that quarter's margin.
- Measurement window: Rolling 90-day complaint count per make/model-year (NHTSA), per product (CPSC incident reports) or per device brand (MAUDE) ending T-3, versus the trailing 4-quarter daily baseline; plus flags for an open NHTSA investigation or a new recall posted during the quarter. NHTSA complaints post with a lag of days (REASONED); MAUDE posts monthly with a lag (REASONED). History: NHTSA complaints multi-decade; MAUDE from 1991 (REASONED); CPSC recalls queryable by date range (TESTED HERE).. Lead time: 3 days.
- Universe coverage: ~3% (35-40 names, REASONED): powersports, RV and EV OEMs mapped by make/model in NHTSA, plus medical-device makers mapped by brand name in MAUDE; suppliers (Gentherm, Visteon, Dorman) and CPSC-only durables dropped.
- Hypothesis: An issuer-level 90-day complaint rate (NHTSA complaints summed across the OEM's models, or MAUDE events summed across the device maker's brands) at least 2x the trailing-4-quarter daily baseline, concentrated in one component or event type, with no recall, investigation or major media event in the prior 60 days, measured 3 trading days before earnings, predicts a higher probability of a warranty charge, recall or shipment-hold disclosure at that release, a negative gross-margin surprise and a negative T-1 to T+2 sector-ETF-adjusted abnormal return, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Sell-side auto and med-tech analysts watch announced recalls, not the complaint flow that precedes them; some specialist funds and plaintiff law firms mine NHTSA/MAUDE, but no vendor is known to package complaint velocity as an earnings signal (UNKNOWN). Once a recall is announced it is priced within minutes, so the edge is the pre-announcement window and the accrual size.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None known at scale for complaint-velocity-as-earnings-signal (UNKNOWN). Recall announcements themselves are free and instantly priced.. Why ours differs: Nothing to differentiate against; this is unsold regulatory exhaust mapped to tickers, where the work is the mapping (model to OEM, device brand to company, component to supplier).
- Vendors found by reviewer: No vendor found packaging complaint velocity as an earnings signal (two searches; UNKNOWN). Adjacent only: ConsumerAffairs recall news pages (not a data vendor) - https://www.consumeraffairs.com/news/automotive-safety-and-recalls/?page=2
- Literature found by reviewer: Kumar et al. (Richmond), 'Customer complaints, auto recalls and stock returns' (NHTSA complaints and 526 recalls 1996-2012: complaints raise recall likelihood, predict negative abnormal returns after the recall announcement, and insiders are net sellers before recalls), Review of Quantitative Finance and Accounting - https://doi.org/10.1007/s11156-019-00866-9 and manuscript https://facultystaff.richmond.edu/~akumas/JFR%20submitted%20manuscript%20Full%20Manuscript.pdf; Barbosa Slivinskis, Agi Maluli and Broder, 'A Machine Learning Algorithm to Predict Medical Device Recall by the FDA', West J Emerg Med 2025 (recalls predicted with lead times up to 12 months from public data) - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC11908527/; JAMA Network Open study of 903 AI-enabled devices: MAUDE use-related problems and missing clinical-study information associated with recall - https://jamanetwork.com/journals/jamanetworkopen/fullarticle/2850190; Recall campaign timing and stock-market performance (auto recalls elicit about -0.22% average reaction; event study) - https://www.ebsco.com/articles/psychology/f6e8adba-3253-53a6-807c-ae88e2f12df8/recall-campaign-timing-the-impact-on-sales-and-stock-market-performance; NHTSA ESV paper on complaint data - https://static.nhtsa.gov/esv/pdf/ESV/Proceedings/28/ESV26-289.pdf
- Data sources:
  - NHTSA complaints API [DATA] https://api.nhtsa.gov/complaints/complaintsByVehicle?make=tesla&model=model%203&modelYear=2023 (update: continuous; history: multi-decade by model year (REASONED))
  - NHTSA recalls API [DATA] https://api.nhtsa.gov/recalls/recallsByVehicle?make=tesla&model=model%203&modelYear=2023 (update: continuous; history: )
  - CPSC SaferProducts recalls API [DATA] https://www.saferproducts.gov/RestWebServices/Recall?format=json&RecallDateStart=2025-10-01&RecallDateEnd=2025-11-10 (update: continuous; history: date-range queries (TESTED HERE))
  - CPSC SaferProducts incident reports (public consumer reports) [UNPROBED] https://www.saferproducts.gov/RestWebServices/Incident?format=json (update: ; history: )
  - openFDA device recalls [DATA] https://api.fda.gov/device/recall.json?limit=1 (update: weekly-ish (REASONED); history: ~59,700 device recall records (TESTED HERE))
  - openFDA device adverse events (MAUDE) [UNPROBED] https://api.fda.gov/device/event.json?search=device.brand_name:%22inspire%22&limit=1 (update: monthly (REASONED); history: )
  - openFDA drug enforcement reports [DATA] https://api.fda.gov/drug/enforcement.json?limit=1 (update: weekly (REASONED); history: )

  - App Store customer reviews RSS (defect complaints for connected devices) [DATA] https://itunes.apple.com/us/rss/customerreviews/id=284882215/sortBy=mostRecent/json (update: live; history: )
- Reviewer source checks: https://api.nhtsa.gov/complaints/complaintsByVehicle?make=tesla&model=model%203… -> DATA (Phase 0) Keyless; dateComplaintFiled allows point-in-time reconstruction (posting lag of…; https://api.nhtsa.gov/recalls/recallsByVehicle?make=tesla&model=model%203&model… -> DATA (Phase 0) Recall flag and post-recall exclusion windows.; https://www.saferproducts.gov/RestWebServices/Recall?format=json&RecallDateStar… -> DATA (Phase 0; re-confirmed TESTED HERE 200) Recalls only.; https://www.saferproducts.gov/RestWebServices/Incident?format=json -> TESTED HERE: 404 (also 404 with ReportDateStart/End parameters) Public Incident REST endpoint not found; drop the CPSC incident-report channel …; https://api.fda.gov/device/recall.json?limit=1 -> DATA (Phase 0) Device recall flag.; https://api.fda.gov/device/event.json?search=device.brand_name:%22inspire%22&li… -> TESTED HERE: 200; meta.last_updated 2026-11-04, events with date_received through 2026-10-30, 26.8M records MAUDE via openFDA refreshes roughly weekly with a 1-2 week receipt-to-publicati…; https://api.fda.gov/drug/enforcement.json?limit=1 -> DATA (Phase 0) Not needed for ex-biotech device names; drop.; https://www.fda.gov/media/190190/download?attachment -> DATA (Phase 0) Annual Form 483 file; too slow for the quarter, keep as a context flag.; https://itunes.apple.com/us/rss/customerreviews/id=284882215/sortBy=mostRecent/… -> DATA (Phase 0) Connected-device defect complaints; ~500 most recent only.
- Cost: $0: all government APIs are keyless; an optional free openFDA key raises rate limits. Amazon and Trustpilot review scraping (blocked, ToS-prohibited) is not needed.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 20%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Complaint spikes often follow media coverage or class-action solicitation rather than new defects, most spikes never become recalls, and when they do the accrual may fall in a later quarter; for suppliers the component-to-supplier mapping is too uncertain to trade.
- Red team (reviewer): Most complaint spikes never become recalls, and those that do are accrued in the quarter of the recall decision, which is frequently after the earnings date in question; a visible spike usually follows media coverage or an announced investigation that is already priced, and the published evidence (RQFA) locates the negative abnormal return after the recall announcement rather than at the earnings release, so at the event the signal is largely a weak, already-public quality-risk factor.

## S25 Own-site stock-outs and markdown depth (Shopify storefronts, Best Buy API)

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: DTC-heavy consumer brands on Shopify-class storefronts; consumer-electronics brands sold through Best Buy. Target: margin (gross) + revenue (nowcast).
- Seed item: Out-of-stock frequency and discount depth scraped from the retailer own site
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: 1) Drop the Best Buy API leg: commercial use needs Best Buy approval and caching is limited to a temporary basis, which conflicts with a daily archive (AMBER, legal 3 if kept). 2) Drop the Common Crawl backfill and Target/Walmart/Amazon legs (blocked or useless for variant-level data). 3) Replace the asserted ~50-name universe with a verified list: run a one-time sweep of every universe domain for /products.json and keep only names that return JSON with 'available' and 'compare_at_price'; Olaplex confirmed, Allbirds below cap floor, Figs/YETI/Hims/e.l.f./Oddity/Sonos/Warby Parker/Peloton/Crocs/Arlo/Corsair all fail. 4) Make markdown depth (gross margin) the primary feature and treat stock-outs as a secondary, sign-ambiguous feature. 5) Compare against the same weeks of the prior year only; the 'versus prior quarter' fallback is confounded by holiday promotions, so no trading until 12 months of archive exist. 6) Check each brand's site terms for automated-access clauses before enrolling it; legal 2 reflects the mild Shopify-endpoint flag plus per-site ToS uncertainty.
- Mechanism: A brand's own storefront exposes, per variant, whether it is purchasable and its current and compare-at price. For DTC-weighted apparel, footwear, beauty and gear brands, a rising share of in-season variants out of stock in weeks 6-13 of the quarter signals sell-through ahead of plan (revenue beat) or supply failure (revenue miss, distinguishable by whether new arrivals are also sold out), while deepening and broadening markdowns (share of variants with compare_at_price above price, and average discount) in the last four weeks signal inventory clearance and a gross-margin miss. For consumer-electronics brands, Best Buy's product API gives availability and sale price at the largest US electronics retailer, nowcasting channel sell-through and promotional intensity. REASONED throughout.
- Industry context: DTC brands manage inventory in public: every stock-out and markdown is visible on their site the day it happens, and gross margin is largely determined by how much of the quarter's volume moved at full price. Clearance before quarter-end is the standard way to hit a sales number at the expense of margin.
- Measurement window: Daily archived snapshots from fiscal-quarter start to T-1: (a) share of variants with available=false, (b) share of variants discounted and mean discount depth, (c) count of new products added. Signal = QTD change versus the same weeks last year once a year of archive exists; until then, versus the prior quarter. History: none (snapshot endpoints, TESTED HERE); must be archived from day one.. Lead time: 1 days.
- Universe coverage: Unknown but small: 1 in-universe name confirmed (Olaplex) out of 17 probed; REASONED fewer than 10 names after a full sweep. Kill the signal if a one-time sweep of all ~1,300 universe domains for /products.json yields fewer than 10 eligible names.
- Hypothesis: For US-listed consumer brands (cap $500M to $30B) whose own storefront serves Shopify /products.json (verified per name), an increase of at least 10 percentage points in the share of variants with compare_at_price above price over the final 4 weeks of the fiscal quarter versus the same 4 weeks one year earlier (own daily archive), measured 1 trading day before earnings, predicts a negative gross-margin surprise and a negative T-1 to T+1 sector-adjusted abnormal return, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size, momentum and the brand's prior-year markdown share.
- Who already knows: YipitData and similar web-scraped pricing vendors (REASONED: Yipit sells scraped e-commerce pricing and promotion series) cover large retailers and Amazon; sell-side consumer analysts do manual site checks before earnings. Small DTC brands' own storefronts are thinly covered. Markdown intensity is partly anticipated from management commentary and retailer inventory data, so the margin channel is only partially priced.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): YipitData (scraped e-commerce pricing and promotions; REASONED), DataWeave and Profitero (price intelligence sold to brands rather than funds).. Why ours differs: More granular (variant-level availability and compare-at price on the brand's own site, daily) and cheaper ($0) than vendor panels that focus on Amazon and big-box sites; covers brands below vendor radar. Weakness: no history until archived and only about 50 names.
- Vendors found by reviewer: YipitData e-commerce and retail (pricing, promotions, web data; sold to 450+ funds) - https://www.yipitdata.com/companies/ecommerce-retail; YipitData datafeeds (promotions, market share) - https://www.yipitdata.com/platform/datafeeds; Potent Pages (web-crawler firm marketing stock-out/ship-time signals to hedge funds; vendor claim, not a product at scale) - https://potentpages.com/web-crawler-development/web-crawlers-and-hedge-funds/inventory-availability-signals-stock-outs-ship-times-backorders
- Literature found by reviewer: Park et al. 2019, scraped retailer price/sales data and scarcity ('only N left') messages; a sales-behaviour study, not an earnings test - https://anderson-review.ucla.edu/wp-content/uploads/2021/03/Park-et-al_2019_SSRN-id3400431.pdf; Mitrofanov and Knight, Instacart stock-out disclosure field experiment (Management Science, per BC news); retailer-side, not an investor signal - https://www.bc.edu/content/bc-web/bcnews/nation-world-society/business-and-management/low-stock-announcements.html; No paper found that tests scraped own-site stock-outs or markdown depth against earnings surprises (UNKNOWN)
- Data sources:
  - Shopify storefront /products.json, Olaplex (confirmed in-universe) [DATA (TESTED HERE, 200 JSON)] https://www.olaplex.com/products.json?limit=250 (update: live snapshot; archive daily; history: none; own archive from day one)
  - Shopify storefront /products.json pattern (template; per-brand sweep required) [DATA (Phase 0)] https://www.allbirds.com/products.json?limit=2 (update: live snapshot; history: none)
  - SEC XBRL companyfacts (reported gross margin for labelling) [DATA (Phase 0)] https://data.sec.gov/api/xbrl/companyfacts/CIK0000320193.json (update: per filing; history: XBRL era, ~2009 onward)
- Reviewer source checks: https://www.allbirds.com/products.json?limit=2 -> DATA (Phase 0) Pattern works; Allbirds is below the $500M cap floor so it is a template only.; https://www.olaplex.com/products.json?limit=250 -> 200 application/json (TESTED HERE) 140 products / 140 variants; every variant carries 'available' and 'compare_at_…; https://www.wearfigs.com/products.json?limit=1 -> 404 Figs does not serve the Shopify endpoint.; https://www.yeti.com/products.json?limit=1 -> 403 Blocked / not Shopify.; https://www.hims.com/products.json?limit=1 -> 403 Cloudflare 'Just a moment' challenge.; https://www.elfcosmetics.com/products.json?limit=1 -> 404 Not Shopify.; https://www.ilmakiage.com/products.json?limit=1 -> 404 Oddity brand; not Shopify. spoiledchild.com also 404.; https://www.sonos.com/products.json?limit=1 -> 404 Not Shopify.; https://www.warbyparker.com/products.json?limit=1 -> 403 Blocked / not Shopify.; https://www.onepeloton.com/products.json?limit=1 -> 404 Not Shopify. crocs.com 500, arlo.com 403, corsair.com 403, revolve.com and figs…; https://www.canadagoose.com/products.json?limit=1 -> 200 application/json after redirect to /en-ca/ Shopify endpoint exists but returned products=0; needs locale-specific path ver…; https://api.bestbuy.com/v1/products?apiKey=DEMO&format=json -> KEY (Phase 0) Third-party summaries of the API terms: commercial use requires Best Buy approv…; https://index.commoncrawl.org/collinfo.json -> DATA (Phase 0) Common Crawl does not systematically capture /products.json or per-variant avai…; https://www.target.com/c/electronics -> 404 with CAPTCHA marker (TESTED HERE) Homepage returned 200 but category pages are bot-challenged; consistent with th…
- Cost: $0: Shopify storefront JSON is keyless; the Best Buy developer key is free but its API terms are for non-commercial use (flag; REASONED: confirm before relying on it). Vendor-grade scraped pricing (YipitData-class) is far above $100/month.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 2 / 2. Confidence: 12%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Promotional calendars are seasonal and management pre-announces promotional intensity, so markdown depth is already in consensus margins; stock-outs are ambiguous in sign; and the DTC site may be a minority of total sales for most candidates, so site behaviour does not map to blended margin.
- Red team (reviewer): Own-site markdown cadence is seasonal and management pre-announces promotional intensity, so compare-at depth is largely in consensus margins; for all but a handful of names the DTC site is a minority of blended revenue, and the obtainable universe collapsed to one confirmed in-universe name in this pass, so any early result is idiosyncratic noise rather than a cross-sectional signal. The Best Buy leg is AMBER (approval-required commercial use, temporary-caching clause) and has been removed.

## S26 Distributor catalog repricing frequency versus PPI pass-through

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Industrial and MRO distributors; building-products, HVAC, pool and electrical distributors; industrial consumables makers. Target: margin (gross) + revenue (price contribution) (nowcast).
- Seed item: Price-change frequency in B2B and industrial-distributor catalogs
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: Every target catalog tested (MSC, Global Industrial, Zoro, Grainger, Applied, WESCO, Transcat) sits behind Incapsula, DataDome, Cloudflare or a captcha, so the SKU-level leg cannot be collected lawfully from this environment, and circumventing bot protection is a terms-of-use conflict (legal 4). The only obtainable leg, BLS PPI, is a widely published series already modelled by sell-side (crowdedness 2 on that leg). Even if catalog data were available, web list prices are not realized prices for contract-heavy distributors (MSC's own 2024 list-price reset illustrates the gap), so the mechanism to blended gross margin is weak and the universe is tiny. No clean alternative found.
- Mechanism: Distributors earn a spread between supplier cost and list/sell price, and their gross margin in a quarter depends on how quickly and how often they reprice relative to supplier cost inflation or deflation. Counting price changes (frequency, direction, average size) across a fixed basket of SKUs on a distributor's own public catalog (MSC Industrial, Global Industrial, Zoro, DXP-type) during the quarter nowcasts the price contribution to revenue and the direction of gross margin; the BLS PPI for the matching commodity and wholesaling industry provides the cost side. Rising catalog prices with flat PPI implies spread expansion (margin beat); flat catalog prices with rising PPI implies compression. Grainger and Fastenal, the usual targets, exceed the cap ceiling and their sites are bot-blocked from the container anyway. REASONED throughout.
- Industry context: Industrial distributors pass supplier price increases through with a lag, and their gross margin expands when they reprice faster than costs rise and compresses when costs fall faster than they cut prices. Because catalogs are public, the repricing behaviour that determines that spread is observable in real time.
- Measurement window: Catalog version: daily or weekly snapshots of a fixed basket of 500-2,000 SKUs per distributor from quarter start to T-2; signal = share of SKUs repriced, mean price change, and the gap to the BLS PPI for the matching commodity. PPI for the quarter's final month is released about 2 weeks after month end (REASONED), so it is available roughly 10-20 days before most distributor reports and covers the full quarter. History: PPI decades; catalog none until archived.. Lead time: 2 days.
- Universe coverage: Effectively 0 of the ~35 named distributors have a catalog that is reachable from the container without defeating a WAF or captcha; only the PPI leg (industry-level, fully priced) remains.
- Hypothesis: A positive gap of at least 2 percentage points between a distributor's QTD basket price change and the matching PPI change, measured 2 trading days before earnings, predicts a positive gross-margin surprise and a positive T-1 to T+1 sector-adjusted abnormal return, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Sell-side industrial analysts model price/cost for distributors from management commentary and PPI, so the PPI leg is fully priced. Catalog-level repricing frequency is not sold by any vendor we know of (UNKNOWN) and is rarely measured systematically; the edge is in the timing and breadth of repricing, which management discloses only qualitatively.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None known for distributor catalog repricing (UNKNOWN); price-intelligence vendors such as DataWeave and Profitero serve consumer retail rather than industrial MRO.. Why ours differs: Direct SKU-level measurement of the distributor's own repricing behaviour against an industry PPI, which nobody sells; cheaper (free) and more granular than analyst models. Honest caveat: the universe is tiny and several target sites may block scraping (legal/ToS risk 3).
- Vendors found by reviewer: Apify 'Grainger Scraper' actor by crawloop (unofficial list-price/stock scraper; contract prices out of scope; sold to businesses, not funds) - https://apify.com/crawloop/grainger-scraper; Actowiz Solutions Grainger / industrial MRO data scraping (vendor marketing) - https://www.actowizsolutions.com/industrial-mro-data.php; Modern Distribution Management, 'Inside MSC Industrial's Big Pricing Reset' (March 2024; MSC CFO on fixing a 20-year-old list-price structure) - https://www.mdm.com/company_article/inside-msc-industrials-big-pricing-reset/; No vendor seen packaging distributor repricing frequency as an earnings signal (UNKNOWN)
- Literature found by reviewer: Cavallo, 'Scraped Data and Sticky Prices', NBER w21490 / Review of Economics and Statistics 2018 (frequency and size of online price changes; macro pricing, not earnings) - https://www.nber.org/papers/w21490; Gorodnichenko and Talavera, 'Price Setting in Online Markets' (online prices change more often, stronger pass-through; macro, not earnings) - https://eml.berkeley.edu/~ygorodni/internet_loop_Gorodnichenko_Talavera.pdf; Cavallo, Jackson Hole 2018 (online competition raises short-run pass-through; uses PriceStats indices) - https://www.kansascityfed.org/documents/6985/Cavallo_JH2018.pdf

- Cost: $0 for BLS (free registration key) and own-site snapshots. If distributor sites block automated access, the catalog leg is unavailable and only the PPI leg remains, which is a weak industry-level proxy.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 4. Confidence: 3%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): List prices on the website are not realized prices (large customers buy on contract), so catalog changes may not move blended margin; PPI pass-through is already modelled by every analyst; and the handful of eligible names makes any backtest statistically weak.
- Red team (reviewer): Web list prices are not realized prices (large customers buy on contract, and MSC's 2024 list-price restructuring shows list and transaction prices diverge), PPI pass-through is modelled by every sell-side industrial analyst, and every target catalog is behind a WAF or captcha from the container, so the only obtainable leg is already in consensus.

## S27 Package-download and repo-activity velocity for open-core developer tools

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Open-core and developer-platform software: observability, databases, CI/CD, API and communications platforms, cloud infrastructure. Target: revenue (consumption) + guidance (both).
- Seed item: GitHub activity (stars, forks, issues, fix speed) for developer-tool companies
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: 1) Restrict the backtestable core to npm downloads (history from 2015) and GH Archive issue open/close velocity (history from 2011); pypistats (180 days), Docker Hub pull_count and Homebrew (snapshots) become live-only secondary features. 2) Drop star counts as a feature (gamed, hype-driven; the VC sources themselves warn against stars). 3) Fix the package-to-company mapping point-in-time: enrol only packages that existed and were company-owned at each historical quarter, to avoid hindsight selection of the 'primary SDK' and package-rename survivorship. 4) Add the prior quarter's download growth as a control because download series are heavily autocorrelated and CI-driven. 5) Lower signal_noise to 2: downloads mostly reflect re-installs by existing users and CI pipelines, and management guidance already embeds internal usage telemetry. 6) Record that the one public event study on commit activity (CommitTrader) found no abnormal returns; the download-velocity version is untested.
- Mechanism: Consumption-priced developer platforms recognize revenue as customers' usage grows, and usage follows adoption of the client SDKs and agents that developers install. Weekly downloads of the company's official packages (PyPI, npm, Docker Hub pulls, Homebrew installs) and the velocity of GitHub stars, forks, issues opened and median time-to-close on its core repositories track developer adoption and product health within the quarter. For companies like GitLab, Elastic, JFrog, DigitalOcean, Fastly, PagerDuty, Twilio, Braze, Amplitude, UiPath and Dynatrace (verify caps), a QTD YoY acceleration in SDK downloads nowcasts net-new usage, and a rising unresolved-issue backlog signals product friction that precedes churn. REASONED: downloads are a leading, noisy proxy for consumption; most revenue comes from existing customers' expansion, which downloads capture only partially.
- Industry context: Open-core companies give away the developer tooling and charge for hosted usage, so the free tier's adoption curve leads paid consumption by a few quarters. Package registries publish download counts, making that adoption curve observable without a paid panel.
- Measurement window: Fiscal quarter start to T-1: weekly package downloads (npm has daily counts since 2015-01, TESTED HERE; pypistats about 180 days), daily Docker pull-count deltas (snapshot only, archive), Homebrew 30/90/365-day install counts, GitHub issue open/close velocity (search API, full history) and star counts (snapshot; GH Archive hourly events from 2011 for backfill). Signal = YoY change in QTD download growth and change in issue backlog.. Lead time: 1 days.
- Universe coverage: ~2-3% (REASONED): 25-35 open-core and developer-platform names in the cap range with at least one official, long-lived npm package; the list must be fixed ex ante and verified per name against the cap and ADV screens.
- Hypothesis: For open-core and developer-platform companies in the universe with a fixed, ex-ante-mapped set of official npm packages, a year-over-year acceleration of at least 20 percentage points in quarter-to-date weekly npm downloads, measured 1 trading day before earnings, predicts a positive revenue surprise versus consensus and a positive T-1 to T+1 sector-adjusted abnormal return, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size, momentum and the prior quarter's download growth; the hypothesis is tested on 2016-2025 npm history before any live use.
- Who already knows: Software analysts and some quant funds watch GitHub stars and npm downloads informally; no established vendor is known to package download velocity as an earnings signal (UNKNOWN). Consumption guidance from management dominates consensus, so the signal is useful only where downloads diverge from guided consumption.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None confirmed (UNKNOWN); Thinknum has tracked GitHub metrics in the past (REASONED, unverified).. Why ours differs: Free, daily, repo- and package-level, with the issue-backlog dimension that star-counting misses; the universe is small and specialized, so even a modest effect is tradable in only about 10 names per quarter.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Two Sigma Ventures Open Source Index (VC-side index built from the GitHub API using watcher counts; not a fund data product) - https://twosigmaventures.com/blog/article/launching-the-two-sigma-ventures-open-source-100-index; market.dev open-source signals for venture funds (VC sourcing, not an earnings feed) - https://data.market.dev/vc; Common Room, 'open source signals' (community/GTM tooling, not a fund product) - https://commonroom.io/blog/coss-leaders-open-source-signals; No institutional data vendor seen packaging package-download velocity as an earnings signal (UNKNOWN)
- Literature found by reviewer: Emery, Lim and Ye, 'The Private Value of Open-Source Innovation' (2025; event study of returns around repository releases; OSS value predicts annual sales, profit and employment growth) - https://abfer.org/media/abfer-events-2025/annual-conference/papers-tech-ai/AC25P10048_The-Private-Value-of-Open-Source-Innovation.pdf; CommitTrader (Tom Almog, not peer reviewed): 1,413 commit-activity events across 31 companies, no statistically significant abnormal returns - https://tomalmog.github.io/CommitTrader/report.html; Aksoy-Yurdagul and Shah, 'Does It Pay to Be Open?' (stock response to open-source contribution announcements; R&D Management 2025 version) - https://escp.eu/es/news/how-open-source-and-user-communities-drive-corporate-innovation-and-profit
- Data sources:
  - npm downloads API (range endpoint for history) [DATA (Phase 0)] https://api.npmjs.org/downloads/point/last-week/@datadog/browser-rum (update: daily; history: 2015-01-10 to present)
  - GH Archive bulk (issue and release events, point-in-time) [DATA (Phase 0)] https://data.gharchive.org/2025-01-01-0.json.gz (update: hourly; history: 2011-02-12 to present)
  - GitHub REST search issues (live backlog) [DATA (Phase 0)] https://api.github.com/search/issues?q=repo:hashicorp/terraform+is:issue+created:>2025-10-01 (update: live; history: full issue history)
  - PyPI download stats (live only) [DATA (Phase 0)] https://pypistats.org/api/packages/snowflake-connector-python/recent (update: daily; history: ~180 days)
- Reviewer source checks: https://api.npmjs.org/downloads/point/last-week/@datadog/browser-rum -> DATA (Phase 0) Daily counts from 2015-01-10; this is the only download series with enough hist…; https://data.gharchive.org/2025-01-01-0.json.gz -> DATA (Phase 0) Hourly events from 2011; issue open/close and star events can be reconstructed …; https://api.github.com/search/issues?q=repo:hashicorp/terraform+is:issue+create… -> DATA (Phase 0) Live; anonymous search limited to 10 req/min, free PAT recommended.; https://api.github.com/search/repositories?q=repo:hashicorp/terraform&per_page=1 -> DATA (Phase 0) Snapshot star counts only; dropped from the core spec (stars are gamed and hype…; https://pypistats.org/api/packages/snowflake-connector-python/recent -> DATA (Phase 0) Only ~180 days of history; usable live, not for backtest.; https://hub.docker.com/v2/repositories/elastic/elasticsearch/ -> DATA (Phase 0) Cumulative pull_count snapshot only, no history; demote to live-only secondary …; https://formulae.brew.sh/api/analytics/install/30d.json -> DATA (Phase 0) Rolling windows only, no history; secondary.; https://hn.algolia.com/api/v1/search?query=datadog&tags=story -> DATA (Phase 0) Full corpus; useful as a sentiment control, not a core feature.
- Cost: $0: all keyless; a free GitHub personal access token lifts search rate limits. GH Archive backfill costs disk (about 70 MB per hour-file in 2025, TESTED HERE).
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 4 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 10%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Downloads are inflated by CI pipelines, mirrors and bots and are dominated by existing users re-installing, so they reflect neither new customers nor consumption; revenue is driven by enterprise contract expansion that is invisible in registries, and guidance already embeds management's own usage telemetry, which is far better than ours.
- Red team (reviewer): Package downloads are a CI-, mirror- and bot-dominated series with heavy autocorrelation; choosing each company's 'primary SDK' with hindsight and ignoring package renames induces look-ahead and survivorship; revenue for these firms is enterprise contract expansion that is invisible in registries and already embedded in management's telemetry-based guidance; and the only public event study on repository activity (CommitTrader) found no abnormal returns.

## S28 Trademark intent-to-use filings and CT-log subdomains as launch precursors

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Consumer products, beverages, restaurants, video games and entertainment, medical devices, consumer electronics. Target: guidance (launch timing); secondary revenue in the launch quarter (forward).
- Seed item: Domain registrations and trademark filings months before launches
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: The mechanism does not meet program requirement (a): it neither nowcasts the quarter being reported nor maps to a specific next-quarter guidance change, since intent-to-use filings precede launches by 3-12 months and launches routinely slip. The trademark leg is already a published annual mispricing factor (Hsu et al., Management Science) that trades at a 12-month horizon, not an earnings event; the CT-log leg is supported only by security-literature anecdotes and mostly reflects IT infrastructure (staging, CDN, regional hosts). The data are lawful and mostly keyless, but the TSDR API cannot enumerate filings by owner, so the owner-level count requires parsing daily bulk XML and resolving subsidiary and shell-company filers, a large effort for a signal with no defined event window. Lower signal_noise to 1.
- Mechanism: Companies file intent-to-use trademark applications 3-12 months before a product launch and issue TLS certificates for launch subdomains days to weeks before. A cluster of new ITU filings and new certificate names tied to one company signals a launch pipeline that drives next-quarter guidance (new SKUs, menu platforms, game titles, device generations); the absence of filings where a launch was promised signals slippage. This is a forward signal about guidance and the launch quarter's revenue, not a nowcast of the quarter being reported. REASONED throughout; no literature cited.
- Industry context: Brand owners must secure names before they market them, so trademark offices receive launch plans months in advance, and web infrastructure for a launch is provisioned before it is announced. Both are public by design.
- Measurement window: Trailing 180-day count of new USPTO applications by owner (TSDR daily updates; bulk daily XML) and trailing 90-day count of newly logged certificate names under the company's domains (crt.sh), both read at T-1; the informative horizon is 1-3 quarters ahead, so the final reading before an earnings date is a stock of pipeline rather than a flow for the quarter. History: USPTO bulk back over a century; certificate transparency logs since about 2013 (REASONED).. Lead time: 1 days.
- Universe coverage: ~10% of the universe (about 130 names) files trademarks at a rate where launches matter, but the link from a trailing-180-day filing count to a specific quarter's guidance is too loose to define an event population.
- Hypothesis: A count of new intent-to-use trademark applications in the trailing 180 days that is at least 2x the company's trailing 3-year average, measured 1 trading day before earnings, predicts raised next-quarter revenue guidance and a positive T-1 to T+2 sector-adjusted abnormal return, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Trademark filings are public and occasionally cited by sell-side when a filing leaks a product name, and consumer-tech press mines them; whether retail-facing sites such as Quiver Quant publish trademark data specifically is UNKNOWN. The market rarely prices launch pipelines systematically for mid-caps, but the link to a specific quarter's results is weak.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None confirmed at scale for earnings use (UNKNOWN).. Why ours differs: Free regulatory exhaust combined with certificate-transparency timing, which is rarely used; the CT-log leg gives day-level launch timing that trademark data cannot.
- Vendors found by reviewer: No vendor seen selling trademark-filing or certificate-transparency data as an earnings signal (UNKNOWN)
- Literature found by reviewer: Hsu, Li, Teoh and Tseng, 'Misvaluation of New Trademarks' (Management Science; 305,422 USPTO registrations 1976-2014; annual new-trademark intensity scaled by assets is underpriced; analysts err more for high-intensity firms) - https://ira.lib.polyu.edu.hk/bitstream/10397/110402/1/hsu-et-al-2021-valuation-of-new-trademarks.pdf; Foley summary of Hsu et al. ('High Ratio of U.S. Trademark Registrations to Assets Increases Annual Value') - https://www.foley.com/insights/publications/2023/05/high-ratio-us-trademark-registrations-assets-value/; Zhu 2022, 'Expectation and Stock Returns: Evidence from Trademark Applications' (SciTePress conference paper; intent-to-use filings; weak provenance) - https://scitepress.org/PublishedPapers/2022/113604; Roberts and Levin 2019, certificate transparency logs leaking product information before public announcement and customer-vendor relationships (security literature) - https://par.nsf.gov/servlets/purl/10170344; Detectify, 'SSL certificates could be leaking company secrets' (practitioner) - https://blog.detectify.com/news/new-research-ssl-certificates-could-be-leaking-company-secrets/
- Data sources:

  - USPTO Open Data Portal bulk trademark files [KEY] https://data.uspto.gov/ (update: daily/annual bulk XML; history: full register (REASONED))
  - crt.sh certificate search [DATA] https://crt.sh/?q=%25.allbirds.com&output=json (update: live; volunteer-run, rate-limit gently (TESTED HERE); history: full CT log history for the name)
  - Google Certificate Transparency log (get-sth) [DATA] https://ct.googleapis.com/logs/us1/argon2025h1/ct/v1/get-sth (update: live; history: )
  - RDAP domain records [DATA] https://rdap.org/domain/allbirds.com (update: live; history: current record only (TESTED HERE))

  - EUIPO trade mark search API [KEY] https://euipo.europa.eu/eSearch/ (update: daily (REASONED); history: )

Dropped.; https://euipo.europa.eu/eSearch/ -> KEY (Phase 0) Free developer key; EU filings add little for US launch timing.
