Changes: (1) Effluent chart endpoint confirmed (200, Flow rows present in 2026); the DMR custom-data endpoint is unusable as called (record-cap error regardless of filters), so bulk history must come from the annual loading files (UNPROBED). (2) Inserted a hard validation gate before any trade: correlate monthly flow and TOC/ammonia load with disclosed quarterly volumes for 5 single-site names; drop the idea if the within-company correlation is under 0.5. (3) Restrict to permits with monthly monitoring on process outfalls, excluding once-through cooling and stormwater outfalls by outfall description; drop refiners and multi-product complexes. (4) Exclude quarters where the company already disclosed an outage via 8-K or press release, since that is the main volume-relevant information and it precedes the 6-week DMR lag. (5) Lead time set to 14 days (months 1-2 only); signal_noise cut from 3 to 2 given GAO-21-290's data-quality findings and DMR revisions; confidence 15. Crowdedness stays 5 (no vendor or research use found).
- Mechanism: Every NPDES permittee must submit monthly discharge monitoring reports to EPA/state systems with flow (parameter 50050) and pollutant loads (BOD, TOC, ammonia) per outfall, and EPA ECHO exposes them per permit through a keyless REST endpoint. For a process plant, discharge volume and organic/ammonia load scale with production throughput, so YoY change in monthly-average flow and load over the quarter nowcasts volumes at that plant. For companies whose revenue comes from one or two US plants (AdvanSix Hopewell, LSB Industries' three ammonia plants, CVR Partners' two, specialty pulp mills), the plant nowcast is a company nowcast; the gap versus consensus volume assumptions is the edge.
- Industry context: Continuous-process plants (ammonia, caprolactam, pulp, refining) use and discharge large volumes of process water in proportion to output, and an unplanned outage shows up as weeks of near-zero process discharge. The Clean Water Act makes the plant report those discharges monthly, so a turnaround or rate cut that management will only disclose at earnings is already visible in federal data.
- Measurement window: Months 1-2 of the quarter are available 3-4 weeks before early-November earnings; month 3 arrives about 6 weeks after month end. TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: the AdvanSix Hopewell permit (VA0005291) effluent chart showed Flow monthly-average values for the monitoring period ending 30-SEP-26, so the lag was about 6 weeks. Some parameters on this permit appear bimonthly or quarterly rather than monthly (TESTED HERE pattern), so the window varies by permit.. Lead time: 14 days.
- Universe coverage: ~1-1.5%: 12-20 single- or few-site names after requiring monthly-monitored process outfalls (AdvanSix, LSB, CVR Partners, Clearwater Paper, Mercer US mills, Sylvamo US mills, Rayonier Advanced Materials, Ingevity, Koppers, Kronos, Tronox, Hawkins, Intrepid Potash; refiners and multi-product chemical complexes drop out).
- Hypothesis: Gate first: for 5 single-site producers (AdvanSix Hopewell, LSB's three plants, CVR Partners' two, Clearwater Paper, Rayonier AM) the monthly-average process-outfall flow (50050) and organic/ammonia load must show a within-company correlation of at least 0.5 with disclosed quarterly production over 2023-2026; only then test whether a YoY change of at least 10% in months 1-2 of the quarter, measured 14 days before the release and not explained by a company-disclosed outage, predicts a same-direction volume surprise and T-1 to T+1 abnormal return versus XLB, controlling for the relevant PPI, consensus revisions, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Environmental consultants and NGOs use DMR data for compliance, not earnings (REASONED). No equity-research or alt-data use is known (UNKNOWN). Priced in: essentially none, but these companies' results are dominated by commodity spreads, which are observable.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None known (UNKNOWN). Adjacent physical proxies sold at scale: Kayrros and Orbital Insight (satellite thermal/activity), NO2 from Sentinel-5P (seed 3).. Why ours differs: Plant-level, self-reported under permit with monthly resolution, including organic/ammonia loads that track specific product lines, free and keyless; satellite thermal and NO2 proxies are coarser and need cloud-free scenes.
- Vendors found by reviewer: KETOS (NPDES-related data product centred on violations and PFAS, research subscription $999/yr; not a production indicator) - https://ketos.co/?p=35271; EPA ECHO Water Effluent Charts (official, free) - https://catalog.data.gov/dataset/epa-enforcement-and-compliance-history-online-water-effluent-charts-details
- Literature found by reviewer: GAO-21-290 on NPDES eReporting: data not reliable for identifying changes in activities since 2015 - https://www.gao.gov/assets/gao-21-290.pdf; Exponent summary: GAO finds eReporting NPDES data incomplete and inaccurate - https://exponent.com/article/gao-finds-ereporting-npdes-data-incomplete-and-inaccurate; EPA ECHO About Loading Tool Data (DMR data from ICIS-NPDES; metered flow; varied units) - https://echo.epa.gov/trends/loading-tool/resources/about-the-data; EPA ECHO NPDES Monitoring Data Download help (monthly/quarterly/biannual monitoring periods; weekly refresh) - https://echo.epa.gov/node/366; No equity-research or alternative-data use of DMR flow found (UNKNOWN).
- Data sources:
  - EPA ECHO effluent chart REST (per permit, DMR values by outfall/parameter) [DATA (200 TESTED HERE)] https://echodata.epa.gov/echo/eff_rest_services.get_effluent_chart?p_id=VA0005291&p_start_date=01/2024&p_end_date=12/2024&output=JSON (update: monthly DMR periods; ~6 weeks lag; ECHO refreshed weekly; history: 07/2023 onward by default; earlier via loading tool files)
  - EPA ECHO DMR annual loading files (bulk history; replaces the capped custom-data endpoint) [UNPROBED (help page seen in search; bulk file URLs not yet probed)] https://echo.epa.gov/trends/loading-tool/resources/about-the-data (update: annual; history: 2007 onward (REASONED))
  - EPA ECHO facility REST (permit lookup) [DATA] https://echodata.epa.gov/echo/echo_rest_services.get_facilities?output=JSON&p_st=TX&p_act=Y (update: weekly; history: current)
  - BLS PPI API (product price control) [DATA (free BLS v2 key optional)] https://api.bls.gov/publicAPI/v1/timeseries/data/WPU10 (update: monthly; history: decades)
  - EDGAR 8-K feed (exclude company-disclosed outages) [DATA] https://www.sec.gov/cgi-bin/browse-edgar?action=getcurrent&type=8-K&count=100&output=atom (update: real time; history: backfill via daily index)
- Reviewer source checks: https://echodata.epa.gov/echo/eff_rest_services.get_effluent_chart?p_id=VA00052… -> 200 TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: JSON returned; a 01/2026-10/2026 window contains 13 ref…; https://echodata.epa.gov/echo/dmr_rest_services.get_custom_data_facility?p_id=V… -> 200 (error body) Returns 'search results returned 574,248 records; maximum 115,000' with or with…; https://echodata.epa.gov/echo/echo_rest_services.get_facilities?output=JSON&p_s… -> DATA (working-sources file) ; https://api.bls.gov/publicAPI/v1/timeseries/data/WPU10 -> DATA (working-sources file) Free BLS v2 key raises the daily query cap.
- Cost: $0
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 5 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 15%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Flow at many outfalls is dominated by once-through cooling water or stormwater rather than process throughput; some permits report quarterly or semiannually; DMRs get revised; multi-product plants blur the mapping; and for commodity chemicals the quarter's margin is set by spreads the market already tracks, so a volume nowcast adds little.
- Red team (reviewer): For single-site commodity producers the volume-relevant events are unplanned outages that the company discloses weeks before the DMR appears, and the rest of the quarter is set by observable product prices, so the DMR flow is a lagged, noisy echo of public information; where flow does move independently it is usually cooling water, rainfall or a permit-reporting change rather than throughput.

## N04 Great Lakes iron-ore loadings by port (Lake Carriers' Association) as a Cleveland-Cliffs mine and blast-furnace nowcast

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Materials and shipping: integrated steel and taconite mining on the Great Lakes. Target: revenue (steel shipments), margin (fixed-cost absorption), guidance (nowcast).
- Merged duplicates: Lake Carriers' Association monthly iron-ore loadings by port as a blast-furnace feed nowcast for Cleveland-Cliffs
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) All three LCA URLs and the monthly PDF naming pattern confirmed (200; glore0723 through glore1026 seen), so at least ~3 years of port-level monthly data exist, which is 12-13 CLF earnings events: too few to test the stated hypothesis, so the row is demoted from a trade signal to a single-name sanity check on consensus shipment assumptions. (2) Crowdedness lowered to 2: the LCA headline is republished by Skillings, MarineLink and Professional Mariner within days, leaving only the port split as less-watched. (3) Signal_noise cut from 4 to 3: pellets move to mill and port stockpiles, Cliffs sells pellets to third parties and now supplies Stelco outside the US-flag data, and the Minorca idling and partial Hibtac idling change the port mix independent of furnace demand; the port-to-mine map must be verified against the 10-K (Two Harbors is U.S. Steel/Minntac, so it serves as placebo). (4) USACE monthly tonnage report requires APEX session navigation (302 to home); treat as functionally unprobed. (5) Confidence 15; lead time 15 days unchanged.
- Mechanism: LCA publishes monthly iron-ore shipments by loading port within 2-3 days of month end. Port-to-mine mapping is nearly one-to-one: Silver Bay = Northshore (CLF), Marquette = Tilden (CLF), Two Harbors = Minntac (Nippon/US Steel), Duluth and Superior = United Taconite, Minorca, Hibbing (CLF-majority) and Keetac (REASONED). Cliffs' pellets mostly feed its own blast furnaces (Burns Harbor, Indiana Harbor, Cleveland, Middletown, Dearborn), so loadings at CLF ports track pellet production (mine costs, idlings) and the furnaces' planned run-rate, hence steel shipments and fixed-cost absorption in the quarter being reported. A year-over-year drop concentrated at CLF ports while non-CLF ports are flat is the edge versus consensus volume assumptions.
- Industry context: Integrated steel makes iron in blast furnaces fed by taconite pellets shipped by laker from Minnesota and Michigan ports during the late-March to mid-January shipping season. Pellet flow therefore reflects the furnaces' planned run-rate weeks ahead, and an idled furnace cuts it sharply.
- Measurement window: Monthly by port; the quarter's final month is posted ~3 days after month end (TESTED HERE: the October 2026 report was posted 2026-11-03); CLF reports ~20-25 days after quarter end. January-March is the lock-closure season so Q1 reads are mostly inventory draws.. Lead time: 15 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.1%: Cleveland-Cliffs only (4 earnings events per year); no second in-universe name maps to the port data.
- Hypothesis: A year-over-year change of more than 10% in quarterly iron-ore loadings at CLF-mapped ports (Silver Bay and Marquette, plus CLF's REASONED share of Duluth/Superior, with the port-to-mine map verified against Cliffs' 10-K and adjusted for the Minorca/Hibtac idlings), measured 3 days after quarter end, is directionally consistent with CLF's reported steel shipments and pellet production in the same quarter; because n is four events per year and the headline is published in trade press within days, the series is used only as a single-name sanity check on consensus volume assumptions, with non-CLF ports (Two Harbors) as placebo, and is not traded on its own.
- Who already knows: Steel trade press (Fastmarkets AMM) reports the LCA monthly totals; CLF's roughly ten sell-side analysts model shipments from auto and service-center demand and company guidance; few translate port-level loadings into CLF-specific furnace utilization (REASONED). Priced in: the headline total partly; the port-to-mine split is the less-watched layer.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None from the named list for lake loadings. RS Metrics and Kayrros sell satellite steel and ore stockpile products for large names (REASONED), not port-level lake shipments. Fastmarkets/Argus cover LCA numbers as news (REASONED).. Why ours differs: Free, 3-day lag, mine-level mapping via loading port; a single-name, high-signal measure of an integrated steelmaker's physical input flow rather than a satellite stockpile estimate.
- Vendors found by reviewer: No data vendor found. Trade press republishes the LCA monthly totals within days: Skillings Mining Review - https://www.skillings.net/great-lakes-iron-ore-shipments-rise-8-4-in-may-2026/; MarineLink (July 2026 Great Lakes iron ore trade) - https://www.marinelink.com/news/july-great-lakes-iron-ore-trade-increases-541810; Professional Mariner (October iron ore cargoes) - https://professionalmariner.com/great-lakes-iron-ore-cargoes-up-8-5-percent-in-october
- Literature found by reviewer: No academic or practitioner finance study found. Primary source history confirmed by URL pattern: LCA glore0723.pdf (July 2023) - https://lcaships.com/wp-content/uploads/2023/08/glore0723.pdf ; glore0724.pdf - https://lcaships.com/wp-content/uploads/2024/08/glore0724.pdf ; glore0325.pdf - https://lcaships.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/04/glore0325.pdf ; glore0925.pdf - https://lcaships.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/10/glore0925.pdf ; glore1225.pdf - https://lcaships.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/01/glore1225.pdf ; glore0326.pdf - https://lcaships.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/04/glore0326.pdf; Cleveland-Cliffs to temporarily idle Minorca and partially idle Hibbing Taconite (630 workers; working-capital/pellet inventory rebalancing; date not confirmed in excerpt) - https://www.yahoo.com/news/cleveland-cliffs-temporarily-idle-2-180554678.html; Cleveland-Cliffs Q2 2026 results (adjusted EBITDA $286M; Q3 expected to more than double) - https://secure.businesswire.com/news/home/20260723244749/en/Cleveland-Cliffs-Reports-Second-Quarter-2026-Results
- Data sources:
  - LCA monthly iron-ore-from-ports PDF (glore<MMYY>.pdf) [DATA (200 TESTED HERE)] https://lcaships.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/glore1026.pdf (update: monthly, ~3 days after month end; history: URL pattern confirmed from search results back to July 2023 (glore0723.pdf); earlier REASONED)
  - LCA cargo reports category index [DATA (200 TESTED HERE)] https://lcaships.com/category/cargo-reports/ (update: monthly; history: multi-year posts)
  - USACE Corps Locks monthly tonnage (Soo Locks cross-check) [UNPROBED functionally (report path 302s to APEX home, 200)] https://ndc.ops.usace.army.mil/ords/r/lpms/corps-locks/home (update: daily; history: 2021-2026)
  - Cleveland-Cliffs 10-K/10-Q (mine list, pellet sales to third parties, Stelco) [DATA (EDGAR submissions API in working-sources file; CIK for Cleveland-Cliffs REASONED, verify)] https://data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK0000764065.json (update: quarterly; history: full)
- Reviewer source checks: https://lcaships.com/cargo-reports-october-2026-iron-ore-from-ports-text-tabula… -> 200 ; https://lcaships.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/11/glore1026.pdf -> 200 ; https://lcaships.com/category/cargo-reports/ -> 200 ; https://ndc.ops.usace.army.mil/ords/r/lpms/corps-locks/monthly-tonnage-report -> 302 -> 200 Redirects to the Oracle APEX home page (corps-locks/home); the report page need…; https://coast.noaa.gov/htdata/CMSP/AISDataHandler/2024/AIS_2024_01_01.zip -> DATA (working-sources file) Backtest only; annual archive.
- Cost: $0
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 3 / 1. Confidence: 15%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Pellets can ship to mill stockpiles rather than to consumption; Cliffs sells some pellets to third parties and now owns Stelco in Canada outside LCA's US-flag data; steel earnings are dominated by price and contract mix, not tonnage; the monthly number is public and in trade press.
- Red team (reviewer): Cleveland-Cliffs' earnings are set by HRC and auto-contract pricing, not pellet tonnage, and loadings are decoupled from furnace output by stockpiles, third-party pellet sales and idlings, so even a correct volume read rarely changes the earnings surprise; with one name, four events a year and a headline that is public in trade press within days, any apparent predictive power is unfalsifiable noise.

## N05 FAA aircraft-registry diffs as a Textron Aviation (and Bell) delivery nowcast

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Industrials: business-jet, turboprop and helicopter OEMs. Target: revenue, EPS surprise (nowcast).
- Merged duplicates: FAA registry airworthiness-date counts as a Textron Aviation / Bell delivery nowcast
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Single name: universe cut to TXT; lessors, BRS and ERJ dropped. (2) Count only Citation jets and 208B/408 turboprops (the two figures Textron reports); drop pistons and Bell (Bell is defence-heavy and not reported as unit deliveries). (3) Backfill: rebuild a ~6-8 year series from CERT ISSUE DATE in the current MASTER.txt with the YEAR MFR = cert-year-or-prior filter and DEREG.txt, and state the survivorship caveat; archive nightly from now for a clean series. (4) Gap definition: there is no free consensus for delivery counts, so the gap is built against guidance-implied seasonal deliveries, which weakens the 'not in consensus' claim; flagged. (5) Crowdedness lowered from 3 to 2: a free public report (Flycraft/Radar) and Aviation Week already count deliveries from registry diffs, and JETNET sells fleet data. (6) Signal-noise lowered from 4 to 3: certificate-issue lag pushes quarter-end deliveries (Textron's largest and least predictable) into the next quarter's count. (7) Lead time 18 -> 16 (Q3 2026: count final ~Oct 7, print Oct 29 = 16 trading days). (8) Confidence 25 -> 20 because four events a year on one name cannot produce statistical evidence for years.
- Mechanism: Every aircraft delivered to a US customer receives an N-number registration with manufacturer, model, serial, year of manufacture, registrant type and certificate-issue date, and the FAA publishes the entire registry nightly. Diffing nightly snapshots and counting new Textron Aviation serials (Citation 525/525B/525C, 560XL, 680A, 700 jets; 208B and 408 turboprops; 172/182/206 pistons) and Bell helicopters, excluding entries registered to the manufacturer itself (inventory and demonstrators) and tracking 'registration pending' rows, gives a running count of US deliveries by model during the quarter. Textron discloses jet and turboprop deliveries each quarter and the stock trades on them; the US share of Citation deliveries is roughly 65-75% (REASONED from this session's 2026 counts versus recent annual delivery rates), so the scaled count predicts the delivery number and Aviation-segment revenue.
- Industry context: Business-jet OEMs recognize revenue on delivery, and deliveries bunch at quarter end as aircraft finish flight test and customer acceptance. A handful of $10-30M jets slipping into the next quarter moves segment revenue by a few percent, the typical size of a Textron surprise.
- Measurement window: Nightly snapshots; the quarter's count is final ~3-7 days after quarter end allowing for certificate-issue lag and pending entries (REASONED); Textron reports ~22-25 days after quarter end.. Lead time: 16 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.1%: TXT only (ERJ only if ADRs are admitted; lessors and BRS dropped as too weak).
- Hypothesis: For TXT only: the count of Citation jets (525/525B/525C/560XL/680A/700) and 208B/408 turboprops newly certificated to non-Textron registrants with YEAR MFR equal to the current or prior calendar year, by certificate-issue date, measured 5 business days after quarter end (~16-22 days before the release), scaled by the trailing-8-quarter ratio of US registrations to reported deliveries, and compared with the quarterly delivery count implied by management's annual delivery guidance pro-rated by prior-year seasonality (no free delivery consensus exists), predicts the sign of the jet-delivery and Aviation-segment revenue surprise and the close T-1 to close T+2 abnormal return versus XLI, after controlling for 30-day consensus revisions, size and momentum. Treated as a single-name overlay, not a cross-sectional signal.
- Who already knows: JETNET and AMSTAT sell business-aviation fleet and transaction data built partly from the FAA registry (vendor existence confident; not on the named list). Aerospace sell-side generally waits for Textron's delivery disclosure and GAMA's quarterly report (REASONED). Priced in: backlog and annual guidance are known; the quarter's delivery timing (slips between quarters) is not.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): JETNET, AMSTAT (business-aviation fleet data; confident). None from the named list.. Why ours differs: Free nightly registry diffs give a model-level delivery count within days of quarter end; JETNET/AMSTAT are priced for brokers and OEMs and do not publish a pre-earnings count, and a $10k account cannot buy them.
- Vendors found by reviewer: JETNET iQ Pulse (business-aviation market intelligence built partly on registry and transaction data) - https://jetnet.com/documents/iq-pulse/JETNET iQ Pulse - Issue 47 - March 4 2024.pdf; JETNET blog: Flight activity data and alternative data provide critical insights in quest for alpha - https://www.jetnet.com/resources/blog/flight-activity-data-and-alternative-data-provide-critical-insights-in-quest-for-alpha; Flycraft/Radar research: Business jet registrations by month (monthly FAA-registry snapshot deltas, maker deliveries, removals; free web report updated 2026-10-09) - https://www.flycraft.com/research/data/business-jet-registrations-by-month; Apify: FAA Aircraft Registry new registrations and ownership changes feed (scraper product) - https://apify.com/jobito/faa-aircraft-registry-feed
- Literature found by reviewer: Aviation Week: Redacted: A New Phase In Business Aviation Privacy (press already counts monthly deliveries from the registry; notes 3 of 66 April deliveries had owner data redacted) - https://aviationweek.com/business-aviation/safety-ops-regulation/redacted-new-phase-business-aviation-privacy-0; AIN: Textron Aviation Q3 jet deliveries climb 21% (2026-10-29 release date reference) - https://www.ainonline.com/aviation-news/business-aviation/2026-10-29/textron-aviation-q3-jet-deliveries-climb-21; GAMA quarterly shipments and billings page (2026 schedule: Q1 May 28, Q3 week of Nov 16, year-end Feb 17-18; i.e., after Textron prints) - https://gama.aero/facts-and-statistics/quarterly-shipments-and-billings/
- Data sources:
  - FAA Releasable Aircraft Registry zip (MASTER.txt, ACFTREF.txt, DEREG.txt) [DATA] https://registry.faa.gov/database/ReleasableAircraft.zip (update: nightly; history: current snapshot; partial backfill from CERT ISSUE DATE + YEAR MFR (survivorship: resold airframes carry the latest certificate date); archive nightly from now)
  - SEC EDGAR submissions API (Textron 8-K/10-Q delivery disclosures) [DATA] https://data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK0000217346.json (update: continuous; history: 2001+)
  - GAMA quarterly shipments (validation only; published after Textron reports) [DATA (200)] https://gama.aero/facts-and-statistics/quarterly-shipments-and-billings/ (update: quarterly, ~6-8 weeks after quarter end; history: multi-year databooks linked on page)
  - Flycraft/Radar monthly registry-delta report (crowding monitor and cross-check) [DATA (200)] https://www.flycraft.com/research/data/business-jet-registrations-by-month (update: monthly; history: 2026 reports visible; depth UNKNOWN)
- Reviewer source checks: https://registry.faa.gov/database/ReleasableAircraft.zip -> DATA (phase0 list) MASTER.txt carries CERT ISSUE DATE and YEAR MFR, so a partial history can be re…; https://gama.aero/facts-and-statistics/quarterly-shipments-and-billings/ -> 200 Page text shows the 2026 schedule: Q3 report week of November 16, year-end Febr…; https://data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK0000217346.json -> DATA (phase0 list) Delivery disclosures in 8-K/10-Q for labels.; https://www.flycraft.com/research/data/business-jet-registrations-by-month -> 200 Free public monthly registry-delta report; use as crowding monitor and cross-ch…
- Cost: $0
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 3 / 1. Confidence: 20%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Registration lags delivery unevenly (pending entries, trust registrations); export deliveries are invisible; Textron Aviation is only ~40-45% of company revenue and Bell or Industrial can swamp it; the delivery number is often stated at the top of the call so the trade needs the surprise, not the level.
- Red team (reviewer): This is a one-name signal with four observations a year, so any backtest is anecdote: a hit rate cannot be distinguished from noise for the life of the programme. The delivery count is only ~40-45% of Textron revenue; the Q3 2025 print was an EPS beat on a revenue miss driven by Bell and Industrial, which the registry cannot see. Sell-side already models quarterly deliveries from backlog and production rates and the press counts registrations monthly, so the level is largely in consensus; what remains is the late-quarter slip, which is exactly where the registry's certificate-issue lag is worst. Owner redaction under the FAA privacy programme is growing and could in future hide manufacturer-registered inventory (the exclusion the method depends on). AMSTAT was not seen in search results and is cited by the proposer only; treat as unverified.

## N06 ADS-B airborne-callsign census for regional, ULCC and cargo-charter operators as a block-hour and completion nowcast

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Regional airlines under capacity purchase agreements, ultra-low-cost carriers, cargo and charter operators. Target: revenue (block hours, departures); EPS surprise via completion-factor incentives (nowcast).
- Merged duplicates: ADS-B block hours and completion factor for small airlines, regional capacity-purchase flyers and cargo/helicopter operators
- Verdict: REJECT. Changes: None retained. If a lawful feed appears (a written OpenSky licence under $100/month, or airplanes.live confirming commercial use in writing), the salvageable fragment is a completion-shortfall flag for SKYW only, which is still a weak signal given 99.9% adjusted completion. Reject reason: The only tested, usable feed (OpenSky) is prohibited for for-profit use without a written licence, which the brief's legal line forbids; the keyless fallback (adsb.lol) has no published terms and returned 429 on the second call, so a quarter-long census cannot be run on it; paid alternatives (FlightAware, Spire, ADS-B Exchange) are enterprise-priced. Independently of data, the information content is small: SkyWest reports 99.9% adjusted completion and guides block hours (+5% for 2026, +1-2% for 2027), ULCCs publish monthly traffic, and Sun Country's cargo flying is contractual, so a count of completed flights adds little beyond published schedules while the drivers of the surprise (fares, prorate fuel, CPA rate resets, deferred revenue) are invisible to a transponder census.
- Mechanism: SkyWest is paid per completed block hour and departure under capacity purchase agreements with incentives tied to completion factor; Sun Country's cargo segment is paid per block hour flying freighters and its charter segment per flight; ULCC revenue is departures x seats x load x fare. Counting distinct airborne callsigns by ICAO prefix (SKW, SCX, AAY, FFT, JBU, ASA/QXE, and cargo ATN/ABX/GTI) at fixed times each day is a direct physical count of completed flying, including unscheduled cargo and charter flying that schedule databases omit; summed over the quarter it nowcasts block hours and completion factor. TESTED HERE: one anonymous OpenSky call over CONUS at 08:28 UTC returned 664 airborne aircraft with JBU 24, ASA 13, FFT 11, SCX 7, ATN 6, ABX 6, GTI 5 (overnight sample, regionals absent).
- Industry context: Regional carriers and cargo operators are paid for flying completed, not for seats sold, so block hours and completion factor are the revenue line. Every airliner broadcasts its position and callsign over ADS-B, which volunteer receiver networks aggregate and publish for free.
- Measurement window: Full quarter, sampled several times per day; final reading on the last day of the quarter, 3-5 weeks before the release.. Lead time: 20 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.5%: 6-8 names at most (SKYW, SNCY, ALGT, ULCC, JBLU, ALK).
- Hypothesis: A shortfall of at least 3% in quarter-to-date distinct daily SKW callsigns (or SCX cargo tails) versus the run-rate implied by the company's last block-hour guidance, measured 5 days before the SKYW or SNCY release, predicts a negative revenue surprise and a negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus JETS/IYT, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Cirium, OAG and FlightAware sell schedules and completed-flight data to institutions and sell-side uses scheduled capacity from them; SkyWest guides block hours and Allegiant publishes monthly traffic. Scheduled capacity is priced in; within-quarter completion shortfalls and unscheduled cargo/charter flying are only partly priced.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): FlightAware (AeroAPI/Firehose), Cirium, OAG, Flightradar24 sell flight tracking and schedule data (these vendors exist; whether any packages block-hour nowcasts for investors is UNKNOWN).. Why ours differs: Free continuous census instead of five-figure data licences, granular to callsign prefix so it captures cargo and charter flying absent from schedules, and tied to each operator's block-hour revenue model.
- Vendors found by reviewer: FlightAware Foresight (commercial flight data and predictions) - https://flightaware.com/commercial/foresight; Spire aviation satellite ADS-B data (Skailark airline cost platform) - https://spire.com/blog/aviation/airline-cost-savings-with-space-based-flight-data/; Neudata: Alternative data for airline investors, beyond capacity and traffic - https://neudata.co/intelligence/alternative-data-for-airline-investors-beyond-capacity-traffic; ADS-B Exchange support: any project for a commercial entity requires a commercial API licence - https://support.adsbexchange.com/hc/en-us/articles/37363886073613-I-m-building-a-project-for-my-company-but-we-aren-t-making-money-off-of-it-can-I-get-a-free-API-key; JETNET blog on flight-activity data for alpha - https://www.jetnet.com/resources/blog/flight-activity-data-and-alternative-data-provide-critical-insights-in-quest-for-alpha
- Literature found by reviewer: Scientific Reports (Nature) 2020: ADS-B data estimates airline-specific flight volumes (Warwick/Turing) - https://www.nature.com/articles/s41598-020-63734-w; WBS news: Aircraft location messages reveal how economic crises affect aviation - https://www.wbs.ac.uk/news/aircraft-location-messages-reveal-how-economic-crises-affect-aviation/; Data Science Lab: Using aircraft location data to estimate current economic activity - https://datasciencelab.co.uk/research_paper/using-aircraft-location-data-to-estimate-current-economic-activity/; OpenSky Network General Terms of Use and Data License Agreement (fetched here) - https://opensky-network.org/about/terms-of-use
- Data sources:
  - adsb.lol point API (only keyless live feed; terms unspecified, rate-limited 429 on second call) [DATA (200, rate-limited)] https://api.adsb.lol/v2/point/39.86/-104.67/250 (update: live; history: none)
  - airplanes.live API (whitelist by email; commercial terms UNKNOWN) [KEY] https://api.airplanes.live/v2/point/40.7/-74/50 (update: live; history: none)
  - FAA Releasable Aircraft Registry (hex to operator mapping) [DATA] https://registry.faa.gov/database/ReleasableAircraft.zip (update: nightly; history: current snapshot)
- Reviewer source checks: https://opensky-network.org/about/terms-of-use -> 200 Terms text fetched here: 'Any use by a for-profit or commercial entity ... requ…; https://opensky-network.org/api/states/all?lamin=24&lomin=-125&lamax=50&lomax=-… -> DATA (phase0 list) but TERMS-PROHIBITED for commercial use Technically works; legally unusable without a written licence.; https://api.adsb.lol/ -> 200 Swagger UI only; no terms-of-use page found on site or in search (third-party c…; https://api.airplanes.live/v2/point/40.7/-74/50 -> KEY (phase0 list) Whitelist by email; terms for commercial use UNKNOWN.; https://www.adsbexchange.com/data/ -> KEY (phase0 list, paid) Commercial licence required and priced for enterprises.
- Cost: $0 (OpenSky free account recommended; airplanes.live whitelist by email optional)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 4. Confidence: 8%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Anonymous ADS-B coverage has gaps and the callsign count is not block hours (stage length varies), airlines pre-disclose capacity and traffic monthly or at conferences, and block-hour guidance variance for SKYW is small; OpenSky's free tier is non-commercial by its terms, which is a legal flag for a fund.
- Red team (reviewer): Flight counts are capacity, and capacity is already in consensus via Cirium/OAG schedules and the carriers' own guidance; the residual (cancellations, ad-hoc charters) is tiny for a 99.9%-completion operator, so the series is a sector-activity factor, not firm-specific revenue. Published ADS-B nowcasting evidence (Nature Sci Rep 2020) is about flight volumes, not earnings surprises, and warns that in-sample fit overstates accuracy. Legal: OpenSky's licence text is explicit that for-profit use needs written permission, so this is a terms breach, not a grey area.

## N07 Taiwan dedicated-OEM monthly revenue as a sell-in nowcast for US branded golf, bicycle, apparel and footwear mid-caps

- Category: Second-order. Sector focus: Consumer discretionary: golf equipment, bicycle/suspension components, performance apparel and footwear brands that outsource manufacturing to Taiwan-listed contract manufacturers. Target: revenue, guidance (nowcast).
- Merged duplicates: Taiwan-listed OEM monthly revenue (TWSE mandatory disclosure) mapped through a customer-exposure matrix to US apparel, footwear, bicycle-component and golf brands
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Dropped the footwear and apparel pairs (Pou Chen/Feng Tay -> NKE, Eclat -> LULU/NKE/UAA, and DECK, CROX, COLM, WWW, SHOO): the suppliers are multi-brand, the Nike/Lululemon read-through is published monthly in trade press and Bloomberg Opinion, and no concentrated Taiwan supplier was identified for the mid-cap footwear names. Kept golf (Fusheng 6670, AIM 8938 -> GOLF, MODG) and bike (Giant 9921, Merida 9914 -> FOXF). (2) Window: supplier revenue leads brand wholesale recognition by 1-2 months, so the matched window is the three months ending one month before the brand's quarter end, with the lag calibrated per pair, instead of the same calendar quarter. (3) Timing corrected: the TWSE OpenAPI refreshes ~1 week after the 10th (TESTED HERE), so lead time is 15 days via OpenAPI for TWSE-listed suppliers; TPEx is same-day. (4) Backfill is a blocker: the OpenAPI endpoints are current-month snapshots and the MOPS history interface was not reachable from the container; no trading until at least 5 years of monthly prints are assembled (MOPS or company IR pages) and the pair lags are calibrated. (5) Legal raised to 2 pending a read of the TWSE/TPEx open-data licence text (REASONED: Taiwan OGDL permits commercial use, not verified here). (6) Signal-noise lowered from 3 to 2 for multi-brand attribution, FOB timing and FX; crowdedness kept at 3 for the retained pairs.
- Mechanism: Taiwan-listed companies must publish prior-month revenue by the 10th of each month, and several are near-single-category suppliers to US brands: Fusheng Precision (6670, golf club heads) and Advanced International Multitech (8938, TPEx, carbon shafts/heads) supply Callaway, Titleist and TaylorMade; Giant (9921) and Merida (9914) are the largest bicycle OEMs and buy suspension from Fox Factory; Eclat (1476) supplies performance garments to Lululemon, Nike and Under Armour; Pou Chen (9904) is the largest athletic-footwear OEM. The supplier's shipment revenue is the brand's wholesale cost-of-goods sell-in roughly 1-2 months before the brand recognizes wholesale revenue, so the full quarter's supplier revenue is known by the 10th of the month after quarter-end, 2-5 weeks before the US brand reports. A gap between the mapped suppliers' 3-month YoY growth and the consensus-implied revenue growth for the brand is the signal. Applies to brands with a dominant wholesale/sell-in model and a Taiwan supplier whose revenue is concentrated in that category (REASONED; supplier-customer pairs from the brands' 10-K supplier disclosures and the Taiwan firms' annual reports).
- Industry context: Branded sporting-goods and apparel companies own design and marketing but outsource nearly all manufacturing to Asian contract manufacturers, many of them listed in Taiwan, where exchange rules force monthly revenue disclosure. The supplier's shipments are the brand's inventory purchases, so they lead the brand's wholesale revenue by the shipping-and-recognition lag.
- Measurement window: All three months of the brand's fiscal quarter when the brand reports 25-45 days after quarter-end (Acushnet, Topgolf Callaway, Fox Factory, Columbia, Crocs); for FY-offset brands reporting 20-25 days after quarter-end (Deckers, Under Armour) months 1-2 are certain and month 3 arrives 10-15 days before the release.. Lead time: 15 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.3%: 3-4 names after pruning (GOLF, MODG, FOXF; ACU-type pairs to be added only when a supplier is at least 50% concentrated in the brand's category).
- Hypothesis: For brand-supplier pairs where the Taiwan supplier derives at least 50% of revenue from the brand's product category and the brand recognises at least 50% of revenue on wholesale sell-in (Fusheng 6670 and AIM 8938 -> GOLF and MODG golf equipment; Giant 9921 and Merida 9914 as customer-demand reads -> FOXF bike components), a gap of at least 10 percentage points between the supplier basket's 3-month YoY revenue growth, measured over the three months ending one month before the brand's fiscal quarter end (lag calibrated per pair in backtest) and converted at monthly average TWD/USD, and the consensus-implied YoY revenue growth, observed at least 10 trading days before the release, predicts the sign of the revenue surprise and the close T-1 to close T+2 abnormal return versus XLY, controlling for 30-day consensus revisions, size and 12-1 momentum.
- Who already knows: Taiwan sell-side covers every one of these suppliers and some US consumer analysts cite Eclat for Lululemon/Nike and Fusheng/AIM for golf (REASONED). Bloomberg and FactSet carry the monthly series. For the largest pairs (Nike, Lululemon) it is likely priced within days; for mid-caps with 5-12 analysts (Acushnet, Fox Factory, Wolverine) the month-3 print is rarely folded into consensus before the release (REASONED). The edge is the gap between the supplier-implied growth and consensus, not the level.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): No vendor known to sell Taiwan monthly revenue mapped to US mid-cap brand pairs at scale (UNKNOWN). Bloomberg/FactSet distribute the raw monthly series.. Why ours differs: Free, same-day, and pair-mapped to options-listed mid-caps; the gap is computed against consensus-implied growth rather than used as a level.
- Vendors found by reviewer: SGB Online monthly 'Asia footwear factories' shipment series (Feng Tay, Pou Chen, Yue Yuen tracked monthly in trade press) - https://sgbonline.com/exec-top-asia-footwear-factories-june-2026-hold/; Daiwa research note on Eclat Textile 1476 (2026-05-19), mirrored copy - https://stock-llm-wiki.pages.dev/data_base/Raw_data/報告/報告_Daiwa_儒鴻1476_20260519; winvest.tw Eclat monthly revenue page (retail aggregator of MOPS prints) - https://winvest.tw/en/Stock/Symbol/Comment/1476
- Literature found by reviewer: Bloomberg Opinion (via Yahoo Finance, 2023): Nike's slump was foretold by its key Taiwan shoe supplier (Pou Chen) - https://sg.finance.yahoo.com/news/nikes-slump-was-foretold-by-its-key-taiwan-shoe-supplier-220020114.html; Cohen and Frazzini 2008, Economic Links and Predictable Returns, Journal of Finance 63(4) - https://ideas.repec.org/a/bla/jfinan/v63y2008i4p1977-2011.html; Fusheng Precision 6670 February 2026 revenue NT$2.415bn +29.3% YoY (aggregator; confirms monthly print exists) - https://aeo.washinmura.jp/ai/%E5%BE%A9%E7%9B%9B%E6%87%89%E7%94%A8/en/news/2026-04-08-fusheng
- Data sources:
  - TPEx OpenAPI OTC monthly revenue (AIM 8938 and other TPEx suppliers) [DATA (200 after retry)] https://www.tpex.org.tw/openapi/v1/mopsfin_t187ap05_O (update: monthly, same day as the 10th deadline (TESTED HERE 2026-11-11); history: current month only; archive daily)
  - TWSE OpenAPI listed-company monthly revenue (6670, 9921, 9914) [DATA (200)] https://openapi.twse.com.tw/v1/opendata/t187ap05_L (update: monthly, ~1 week after the 10th (TESTED HERE: Sep data dated 10-17 still served 11-11); history: current month only; archive daily)
  - MOPS monthly revenue (per-company, on the 10th; history interface still to be derived) [UNPROBED (302->200 shell)] https://mops.twse.com.tw/mops/web/t21sc04_ifrs (update: monthly by the 10th; history: UNKNOWN from here; required for any backtest)
  - Nasdaq earnings calendar [DATA] https://api.nasdaq.com/api/calendar/earnings?date=2026-11-12 (update: daily; history: one day per request)
  - Nasdaq analyst earnings forecast (consensus; archive daily) [DATA] https://api.nasdaq.com/api/analyst/GOLF/earnings-forecast (update: daily snapshot; history: none; archive)
  - Yahoo chart API for TWD=X (FX conversion; keyless) [DATA (phase0 list, ticker UNPROBED)] https://query1.finance.yahoo.com/v8/finance/chart/TWD=X?range=5y&interval=1d (update: daily; history: multi-year)
- Reviewer source checks: https://openapi.twse.com.tw/v1/opendata/t187ap05_L -> 200 1,088 rows; every row 出表日期=1151017, 資料年月=11509 (September 2026 data, file produ…; https://www.tpex.org.tw/openapi/v1/mopsfin_t187ap05_O -> 520 then 200, 200 First call 520 (transient), two retries 200; 897 rows dated 1151111 for month 1…; https://mops.twse.com.tw/mops/web/t21sc04_ifrs -> 302 -> 200 Redirects to the same path and serves the SPA shell; the history query interfac…; https://api.nasdaq.com/api/calendar/earnings?date=2026-11-12 -> DATA (phase0 list) Release dates.; https://api.nasdaq.com/api/analyst/AAPL/earnings-forecast -> DATA (phase0 list) Consensus snapshot; must be archived; robots.txt disallows all (phase0 flag).
- Cost: $0
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 2 / 2. Confidence: 25%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Most suppliers serve several brands (Pou Chen is dominated by Nike and Adidas; Giant sells its own brand), so pair-level attribution is noisy; TWD/USD moves and FOB versus landed timing blur the mapping; and the brand's quarter can be driven by DTC sell-through and discounting rather than sell-in, so supplier revenue can rise while the brand's margin falls. For the best-known pairs the data is already in sell-side notes.
- Red team (reviewer): The strongest objection is attribution: Fusheng and AIM also supply TaylorMade, Ping and Mizuno, Giant and Merida sell their own brands and buy from Shimano, SRAM and RockShox as well as Fox, so the supplier's month can rise while the mapped US brand's quarter falls (share loss, channel destocking, DTC mix). The brand's print is then decided by sell-through and promotions that the supplier never sees. For GOLF, MODG and FOXF, the covering analysts can and do read the same TWSE prints on the 10th, so the 'gap' may already be in the last pre-print revisions; with only 3-4 names the test has little power, and the backfill needed to show anything does not yet exist in the container.

## N08 Taiwan monthly-revenue chain map: TWSE/TPEx open-data prints of Taiwanese peers, customers and distributors as a three-month nowcast for US mid-cap semis and hardware

- Category: Second-order. Sector focus: Semiconductors, semiconductor equipment and test, electronic components, optical/AI-server hardware. Target: revenue, EPS surprise (nowcast).
- Merged duplicates: Taiwan OSAT, test-house and ODM monthly revenue as a utilization and build nowcast for US back-end semiconductor equipment, fabless and AI-server supply-chain mid-caps
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Removed competitor pairs (SIMO vs Phison, HIMX vs Novatek/Raydium, AMKR vs ASE/PTI, FORM vs MPI, COHU vs WinWay) from the primary spec: a competitor's strong month can mean share loss for the US name, so the sign is ambiguous; keep as a separate sign-free test. (2) Kept only directional links (customer output, distributor sell-through, ODM build). (3) Timing corrected: the TWSE OpenAPI lags the 10th by ~1 week (TESTED HERE), so for TWSE-listed basket members use MOPS directly for the timely read and the OpenAPI as the archived record; lead time 15 -> 12. (4) Crowdedness lowered from 3 to 2: Taiwan monthly prints are tracked monthly by DigiTimes, Culpium, Borecraft, KGI, SemiAnalysis and are on Bloomberg/FactSet; the AI-server ODM sub-basket (Wiwynn, Quanta) is the most crowded and should be weighted down. (5) Universe cut from ~80-100 to ~25-40 names with a defensible link. (6) Backfill blocker: history must be assembled from MOPS or company IR before any claim; the two OpenAPI endpoints are current-month snapshots. (7) Legal 1 -> 2 pending a read of the TWSE/TPEx open-data licence text.
- Mechanism: Taiwan-listed companies must publish prior-month revenue on MOPS by the 10th of each month, so the full quarter of a Taiwanese firm is public roughly 10 days after quarter end, before most US mid-caps report. For a US company whose revenue is (a) a function of a Taiwanese customer's output (test and assembly equipment sold to OSATs; probe cards consumed per wafer out at TSMC/UMC/Winbond/Nanya), (b) directly competitive with a Taiwanese peer in the same product (SIMO vs Phison 8299 in NAND controllers; HIMX vs Novatek 3034 and Raydium 3592 in display drivers; FormFactor vs MPI 6223 in probe cards; Cohu vs WinWay 6515 in test sockets; Amkor/ChipMOS vs ASE 3711, PTI 6239, KYEC 2449, Chipbond 6147), or (c) sold through Taiwanese distributors (WPG 3702, WT 3036, Supreme 8112 for Diodes, Vishay, Semtech, Power Integrations), a weighted basket of the three monthly prints nowcasts the US company's quarter. AI-connectivity and optical names (Credo, Astera Labs if within the cap band, Fabrinet, Applied Optoelectronics) map to the AI-server ODMs Wiwynn 6669 and Quanta 2382. Data labels: TESTED HERE for the two open-data endpoints; mapping is REASONED from 10-K customer and competitor disclosures.
- Industry context: Taiwan hosts the foundry, OSAT and ODM core of the electronics supply chain, and its exchange rules force every listed company to disclose monthly revenue by the 10th, the fastest public revenue series in the world. US component and equipment makers sell into or compete with these firms, so much of their quarter has effectively been printed in Taipei before it is reported in the US.
- Measurement window: Full fiscal quarter: three monthly revenue prints of the mapped Taiwan basket (month 3 published by the 10th of the following month). Interim one- and two-month readings are available 45-75 days before the US print. Most mapped US names report 25-50 days after quarter end, so the final reading leads by 15-35 days.. Lead time: 12 days.
- Universe coverage: ~2-3%: 25-40 names after restricting to directional customer and distributor links (equipment and consumables sold to Taiwanese foundries and OSATs; US component makers sold through WPG, WT, Supreme; optical and connectivity names sold to Wiwynn and Quanta).
- Hypothesis: For US mid-caps with a directional link to Taiwan-listed firms (customers: KLIC, COHU, FORM, ACLS-type equipment and consumables to TSMC 2330, UMC 2303, Winbond 2344, Nanya 2408, ASE 3711, PTI 6239, KYEC 2449, Chipbond 6147; distributors: DIOD, VSH, SMTC, POWI through WPG 3702, WT 3036, Supreme 8112; AI-server suppliers: FN, AAOI, CRDO to Wiwynn 6669, Quanta 2382), a gap of more than 5 percentage points between the USD-converted YoY growth of the three-month revenue of the mapped basket (weights from 10-K customer disclosures) and the consensus-implied YoY revenue growth, measured when the month-3 print is published (MOPS on the 10th; TWSE OpenAPI about a week later), at least 8 trading days before the release, predicts a same-sign revenue surprise and T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus SMH, controlling for 30-day consensus revisions, size and momentum; the effect is expected to concentrate in names with fewer than 8 covering analysts.
- Who already knows: Taiwanese brokers publish monthly revenue trackers and US large-cap semi analysts use TSMC/Hon Hai monthly prints; Bloomberg/FactSet carry the series so institutions can see it. For mid-caps the peer/customer basket is rarely constructed, and the information requires a mapping step, the kind of complexity the market has been shown to under-react to (LITERATURE: Cohen and Frazzini 2008, Economic Links and Predictable Returns, Journal of Finance; Cohen and Lou 2012, Complicated Firms, JFE). REASONED: partially priced via sell-side revisions in the last two weeks before the print; the edge is in names with few analysts.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): No alternative-data vendor is known to sell a ticker-mapped Taiwan-basket nowcast (UNKNOWN); the raw monthly series is on Bloomberg/FactSet terminals.. Why ours differs: Free source, built as per-company weighted baskets for ~80 US mid-caps that terminal users rarely assemble; three-month full-quarter reading available before the print.
- Vendors found by reviewer: DigiTimes Taiwan monthly tracker (monthly revenue of Taiwan-listed AI hardware supply chain by segment) - https://www.digitimes.com/tag/taiwan_monthly_tracker/00114587.html; Culpium: Tracking the AI compute rollout with Taiwan monthly data - https://www.culpium.com/p/how-to-use-taiwan-monthly-data-to; Borecraft: Taiwan semiconductor packaging revenue tracker, April gains - https://borecraft.com/2026/05/13/taiwan-semiconductor-packaging-revenue-tracker-april-gains/; SemiAnalysis ChipBook May 2026 (server ODM revenue tied to NVDA, AMD, AVGO) - https://semianalysis.com/wp-content/uploads/2026/05/ChipBook-May-26-Edition-1.pdf; KGI semiconductor sector report with monthly sales review (2026-07-14) - https://www.kgi.com.hk/en/-/media/files/kgishk/research-reports/us-daily/semiconductor-sector_20260714_en.pdf; TrendForce news on Taiwan top IT firms' monthly revenue - https://www.trendforce.com/news/2025/05/15/news-tariff-concerns-and-early-shipments-drive-april-revenue-surge-for-taiwans-top-it-firms/
- Literature found by reviewer: Cohen and Frazzini 2008, Economic Links and Predictable Returns, Journal of Finance 63(4) 1977-2011 (customer momentum, >150 bp monthly alpha) - https://ideas.repec.org/a/bla/jfinan/v63y2008i4p1977-2011.html; Cohen and Lou 2012, Complicated Firms, Journal of Financial Economics 104(2) 383-400 - https://econpapers.repec.org/RePEc:eee:jfinec:v:104:y:2012:i:2:p:383-400
- Data sources:
  - TPEx OpenAPI OTC monthly revenue [DATA (200 after retry)] https://www.tpex.org.tw/openapi/v1/mopsfin_t187ap05_O (update: monthly, same day as the 10th deadline; history: current month only; archive daily)
  - TWSE OpenAPI listed-company monthly revenue [DATA (200)] https://openapi.twse.com.tw/v1/opendata/t187ap05_L (update: monthly, ~1 week after the 10th (TESTED HERE); history: current month only; archive daily)
  - MOPS monthly revenue (timely per-company print on the 10th; history interface to be derived) [UNPROBED (302->200 shell)] https://mops.twse.com.tw/mops/web/t21sc04_ifrs (update: monthly by the 10th; history: UNKNOWN; blocker for backtest)
  - SEC XBRL companyfacts (segment and geographic revenue weights) [DATA] https://data.sec.gov/api/xbrl/companyfacts/CIK0000320193.json (update: per filing; history: 2009+)
  - Yahoo chart API TWD=X (FX conversion) [DATA (phase0 list, ticker UNPROBED)] https://query1.finance.yahoo.com/v8/finance/chart/TWD=X?range=5y&interval=1d (update: daily; history: multi-year)
- Reviewer source checks: https://openapi.twse.com.tw/v1/opendata/t187ap05_L -> 200 September 2026 rows (file dated 2026-10-17) still served on 2026-11-11: refresh…; https://www.tpex.org.tw/openapi/v1/mopsfin_t187ap05_O -> 520 then 200, 200 October 2026 data dated 2026-11-11; same-day refresh; transient 520 needs retry.; https://mops.twse.com.tw/mops/web/t21sc04_ifrs -> 302 -> 200 (SPA shell) History query not derived; legacy static path 404 for 2026 months; API guess 30…; https://data.sec.gov/api/xbrl/companyfacts/CIK0000320193.json -> DATA (phase0 list) Segment and geographic revenue for weights.
- Cost: $0 (two JSON endpoints, a daily cron to archive snapshots, one-off MOPS backfill)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 3 / 2. Confidence: 30%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Company-level Taiwan revenue mixes many products, so the basket is a noisy proxy; shipment timing and FX differ from US recognition; large-cap read-throughs (TSMC, Hon Hai) move the sector ETF so the relative abnormal return may be small; and sell-side revisions in the final two weeks may already absorb the information.
- Red team (reviewer): The basket is a sector-activity proxy: Taiwanese foundry, OSAT and distributor revenue moves with the semiconductor cycle that SMH already prices, so the relative abnormal return may be near zero even when the basket is right. The documented customer-momentum effect (Cohen-Frazzini) is a monthly return lag, not an earnings-day surprise, and the easiest-to-read baskets (AI ODMs, packaging) are now tracked monthly by free newsletters and brokers, which is where the sell-side revisions in the last two weeks come from. Weights are REASONED from 10-K customer lists that name only >10% customers, so most of the mapping is guesswork; FX and shipment-versus-recognition timing add a quarter of slippage for equipment names.

## N09 Package-registry consumption telemetry: PyPI, npm, Docker Hub and Homebrew downloads of vendor agents and SDKs as a usage nowcast for consumption-priced infrastructure software

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Infrastructure software, developer tools, observability, data platforms, communications APIs. Target: revenue, guidance (nowcast).
- Merged duplicates: Package-registry and container-image download velocity for official SDKs, CLIs and images of consumption-priced software
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Backtest first, npm only: the npm API has daily history since 2015 for every package, so a full 10-year cross-sectional test costs nothing; PyPI (180 days), Docker Hub (cumulative count, no history) and Homebrew (CLI tools, rolling windows) are dropped from the primary spec. (2) Measure share of registry-wide downloads, not raw QoQ growth, to strip the secular ecosystem trend, holiday weeks and CI-platform outages; registry totals endpoint confirmed (TESTED HERE). (3) Package selection restricted to vendor-owned production SDKs that gate billable usage (twilio, @braze/web-sdk, @pagerduty/pdjs, @elastic/elasticsearch, @confluentinc/kafka-javascript, @okta/okta-auth-js, @amplitude/analytics-browser, @fastly/js-compute, jfrog-cli-v2-jf); third-party clients (gitbeaker, kafkajs) excluded. (4) Crowdedness revised from 4 to 2: TickerTrends sells npm downloads to investors and Apify actors exist. (5) Signal-noise 3 -> 2 and confidence 25 -> 15: no literature links registry downloads to revenue, and downloads are build-time events that count CI pipelines, not usage. (6) Kill rule stated in the hypothesis.
- Mechanism: Consumption- and seat-priced infrastructure vendors bill for what runs, and what runs is first installed from public registries that publish download counts daily. Agents and SDKs are re-pulled on every deployment and CI run, so weekly downloads of a vendor's primary packages (ddtrace, elastic-apm, confluent-kafka, pymongo, twilio, @braze/web-sdk, amplitude-analytics, python-gitlab, pagerduty, jfrog CLI, okta SDKs, DigitalOcean doctl via Homebrew) track deployment activity, a leading indicator of consumption revenue and net revenue retention. The measured quantity is mirror-filtered weekly downloads aggregated across registries, compared quarter over quarter. Applies to Elastic, Confluent, MongoDB (if within the cap band), GitLab, JFrog, Dynatrace, PagerDuty, Twilio, Braze, Amplitude, DigitalOcean, Fastly, Okta, Progress. Labels: endpoints TESTED HERE in this run and listed in the working file; link to revenue is REASONED.
- Industry context: Consumption-priced software charges for hosts, events or API calls, and the client code that generates those charges is distributed through public package registries that expose download counts. Because agents are re-installed on every deployment, download velocity reflects real deployment activity rather than marketing attention.
- Measurement window: Trailing fiscal quarter of weekly downloads (final reading at T-1); the full-quarter reading is complete the day after quarter end, 25-40 days before most prints.. Lead time: 1 days.
- Universe coverage: ~1-1.5%: 12-20 names whose primary runtime SDK is npm-distributed and within the cap band (TWLO, BRZE, PD, ESTC, CFLT, GTLB, FROG, OKTA, DOCN, FSLY, PRGS, AMPL, DT; MDB only if under $30B).
- Hypothesis: For infrastructure-software vendors whose production SDK is distributed on npm, the YoY change in the vendor's share of total npm registry downloads (package downloads divided by registry-wide downloads, same weeks) over the fiscal quarter, measured 1 trading day before the release, predicts the sign of the revenue surprise and the T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus IGV, controlling for consensus revisions, size, momentum and the previous quarter's net revenue retention; the test is run first on npm only (full history since 2015), and the signal is dropped if the cross-sectional sign agreement is not better than chance over 2016-2025.
- Who already knows: Some fundamental analysts cite npm/PyPI trends for Datadog or MongoDB anecdotally; no systematic sell-side use known. LITERATURE on registry downloads predicting earnings: UNKNOWN. REASONED: lightly priced for mid-caps; crowding risk is moderate because the data is trivially accessible.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None known for certain to sell registry download counts as a financial dataset (UNKNOWN); Thinknum-type vendors sell web-scraped KPIs but registry coverage is UNKNOWN.. Why ours differs: Direct, free, daily, and aggregated across registries per vendor; adjacent to seed 27 (GitHub stars/forks) but measures deployment rather than developer attention.
- Vendors found by reviewer: TickerTrends: NPM Downloads data type (sold to investors) - https://docs.tickertrends.io/tickertrends/documentation-v2/2.-data-coverage-and-data-types/2.2-data-types/npm-downloads; TickerTrends blog: Tracking Developer Momentum with NPM Data - https://blog.tickertrends.io/p/tracking-developer-momentum-with; Apify: npm and PyPI Package Monitor (versions and downloads) - https://apify.com/vertaizen/package-registry-monitor
- Literature found by reviewer: PyPI Stats guide (counts include CI/CD and mirror traffic; Linehaul under-reported before 2018-07-26) - https://pypistats.com/guide; DEV Community: npm's hidden API, downloads as a leading indicator of developer-tool adoption (practitioner post, no validation) - https://dev.to/0012303/npms-hidden-api-package-downloads-dependencies-and-ecosystem-data-for-free-2p2d
- Data sources:
  - npm downloads API, daily range per package (history since 2015-01-10) [DATA (200)] https://api.npmjs.org/downloads/range/2016-01-01:2016-12-31/twilio (update: daily, ~48 h lag; history: 2015-01-10 to present)
  - npm registry-wide totals (denominator) [DATA (200)] https://api.npmjs.org/downloads/point/last-week (update: daily; history: range endpoint available for totals as well (REASONED, UNPROBED))
  - pypistats overall daily series with mirrors excluded (secondary; 180 days only) [DATA (200)] https://pypistats.org/api/packages/ddtrace/overall?mirrors=false (update: daily, ~24 h lag; history: ~180 days; archive)
  - Google BigQuery bigquery-public-data.pypi.file_downloads (PyPI backfill; needs Google Cloud project) [UNPROBED (console 200)] https://console.cloud.google.com/marketplace/product/gcp-public-data-pypi/pypi (update: daily; history: 2016+ (REASONED))
- Reviewer source checks: https://pypistats.org/api/packages/ddtrace/recent -> DATA (phase0 list) Recent counts; ~180 days daily history via /overall.; https://pypistats.org/api/packages/ddtrace/overall?mirrors=false -> 200 15 KB daily series with mirrors excluded.; https://api.npmjs.org/downloads/point/last-week/@braze/web-sdk -> DATA (phase0 list) Weekly point.; https://api.npmjs.org/downloads/range/2024-01-01:2024-03-31/twilio -> 200 Daily series for a full quarter; history to 2015-01-10.; https://api.npmjs.org/downloads/point/last-week -> 200 Registry-wide total (303.0bn downloads for 2026-11-03 to 11-09): the denominato…; https://hub.docker.com/v2/repositories/elastic/elasticsearch/ -> DATA (phase0 list) Cumulative pull_count only; no history; dropped from the primary spec.; https://formulae.brew.sh/api/analytics/install/30d.json -> DATA (phase0 list) Rolling windows only; CLI tools; dropped from the primary spec.; https://console.cloud.google.com/marketplace/product/gcp-public-data-pypi/pypi -> 200 (landing page only) BigQuery access needs a Google Cloud project and billing account on file even f…
- Cost: $0 (optional Google Cloud free tier for backfill)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 15%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Downloads are dominated by CI pipelines, mirrors and version-bump re-installs that are uncorrelated with paid usage; open-source community users never pay (Elastic, MongoDB); and the series may simply track the generic developer-activity cycle, a sector factor rather than company-specific revenue.
- Red team (reviewer): Downloads are build-time events: an SDK is pulled once per CI run regardless of how much the customer sends through the product, so the series counts pipelines and mirrors, not billable consumption, and open-source users who never pay dominate for Elastic and Confluent. Any QoQ move is mostly the ecosystem cycle (a sector factor already in IGV) plus version-bump artefacts; what is left is a slow secular curve with no quarter-specific information. Expect the npm-only backtest to show no sign agreement beyond chance; keep only because the test is nearly free and fully historical.

## N10 FPDS and USAspending funded-obligation flow rolled up to the parent UEI by fiscal quarter as a revenue nowcast for government-services and defense mid-caps

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Industrials and IT services: government-services contractors, defense electronics, engineering and environmental services. Target: revenue, guidance (book-to-bill), EPS surprise (both).
- Seed item: 16. [regulatory] Lobbying disclosure spikes and small government contract awards on USAspending.gov
- Merged duplicates: FPDS and USAspending daily award feed aggregated by vendor for lumpy project-revenue transport names (dredging, marine construction, military airlift, helicopter services)
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) The decisive correction: FPDS and USAspending withhold Department of Defense contract actions for 90 days (FPDS notice, DoD April 2025 policy, Fed-Spend), so for the defense-heavy names on the list the quarter's obligations are published after the earnings release; those names are removed and the hypothesis is restricted to civilian-agency obligations and contractors with at least 60% civilian-federal revenue. (2) Parent-child roll-up uses the recipient/duns POST endpoint (TESTED HERE with CACI). (3) Obligations are de-seasonalised by federal fiscal quarter because the September flush dominates. (4) Crowdedness kept at 2 but noted that HigherGov at ~$500/yr puts the same data in any analyst's hands. (5) Signal-noise 3 -> 2 and confidence 30 -> 15: obligations fund multi-year task orders and do not map to the quarter's revenue; the surviving universe is 6-10 names with mixed state, local and commercial revenue (MMS, TTEK, ICFI).
- Mechanism: Federal contract actions must be reported to FPDS within three business days of award (FAR 4.604; REASONED) and USAspending ingests FPDS nightly, so obligation dollars by recipient (new awards plus funding modifications on existing IDIQ task orders) are nearly complete for a quarter within 1-2 weeks of quarter-end. For services contractors that recognize revenue as costs are incurred against funded task orders, quarterly funded obligations lead revenue by zero to two quarters and directly drive book-to-bill, which is the guidance metric these companies are judged on. A quarter-end shortfall caused by a continuing resolution or shutdown (the October-November 2025 lapse) or de-obligations and terminations for convenience shows up in FPDS before the contractor reports. This deepens seed 16 by replacing award-level anecdotes with a parent-UEI roll-up of federal_action_obligation by federal fiscal quarter, calibrated against each company's historical revenue and backlog. Applies to contractors with at least 60% federal revenue and mostly unclassified work.
- Industry context: Government-services firms earn revenue by billing hours and costs against task orders that only exist once an agency obligates money to them, and every obligation is a public record within days. Continuing resolutions and shutdowns stop new obligations, which is why these firms' quarters swing with the appropriations calendar.
- Measurement window: The full fiscal quarter of obligations, complete within 10-14 days after quarter-end; contractors report 25-45 days after quarter-end, so the whole quarter is in hand 2-4 weeks before the release. Classified contracts and subcontract flow are invisible, so coverage is partial for some names.. Lead time: 14 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.5-0.8%: 6-10 names with at least 60% civilian-federal revenue or a separable civilian segment (MMS, ICFI, TTEK, PSN civilian, KBR government solutions civilian, SAIC and CACI civilian agencies); DoD-heavy names (KTOS, MRCY, AVAV, BWXT, DRS, VSEC, CW, VVX) removed from the nowcast.
- Hypothesis: For contractors with at least 60% civilian-federal revenue, a YoY decline of at least 15% in civilian-agency funded obligations (sum of federal_action_obligation across the parent-child recipient hierarchy, excluding DoD awarding agencies, de-seasonalised by federal fiscal quarter), measured 14 days before the earnings release, predicts a negative revenue surprise, a book-to-bill below 1.0 and a negative close T-1 to close T+2 abnormal return versus XLI, controlling for last-reported backlog, 30-day consensus revisions, size and momentum; DoD obligations are used only as a lagged (90-day) check, never as a pre-print input.
- Who already knows: Government-services analysts track book-to-bill from company disclosures and some subscribe to Deltek GovWin IQ, Bloomberg Government or HigherGov, which sell federal award and pipeline data (REASONED), so large awards are priced quickly. The quarter-level funded-obligation roll-up against each company's own revenue history, especially in CR and shutdown quarters, is less commonly done by the generalist holders of mid-cap names (REASONED). Moderately priced.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Deltek GovWin IQ, Bloomberg Government, HigherGov (federal award and opportunity data, REASONED).. Why ours differs: Free and same-day from the primary feeds, focused on funded obligations and de-obligations by parent UEI (not ceiling values or headline awards), calibrated per company, and timed to the 2-4 week gap before earnings.
- Vendors found by reviewer: HigherGov market intelligence (federal award data; markets to investors; third-party reports $500/yr single user) - https://www.highergov.com/market-intelligence/; NationGraph vs HigherGov 2026 (HigherGov pricing: $500/yr one user, $2,500/yr up to ten) - https://www.nationgraph.com/post/nationgraph-vs-highergov-sled-sales-intelligence-2026; GovTribe vs HigherGov comparison - https://govtribe.com/compare/govtribe-vs-highergov; Deltek GovWin IQ (third-party listing: $12K-$42K per year) - https://prospeo.io/s/deltek-govwin-iq-alternatives; Bloomberg Government news: Defense contracts' 9% rise in FY25 - https://news.bgov.com/bloomberg-government-news/defense-contracts-9-rise-in-fy25-fueled-new-government-record; Fed-Spend: DoD FY2026 Q4 contract spending still missing (illustrates the 90-day DoD lag) - https://fed-spend.com/blog/dod-fy2026-q4-contract-spending-still-missing; GovChime spending trends and methodology - https://www.govchime.com/analytics/methodology
- Literature found by reviewer: FPDS: DoD data availability notice (availability of DoD data entered into FPDS-NG is subject to a 90-day delay) - https://www.fpds.gov/common/html/dodDataAvailability.html; USAspending: About the data download (contract data pulled from FPDS-NG; nightly refresh) - https://www.usaspending.gov/data/about-the-data-download.pdf; Naval Postgraduate School Acquisition Research Symposium SYM-AM-19-061 (FPDS data quality; seen, not read) - https://dair.nps.edu/bitstream/123456789/1751/1/SYM-AM-19-061.pdf
- Data sources:
  - USAspending spending_by_award (civilian awarding agencies, recipient hierarchy filters) [DATA] https://api.usaspending.gov/api/v2/search/spending_by_award/ (update: nightly from FPDS; DoD actions appear 90 days late; history: FY2008+ (REASONED))
  - USAspending recipient hierarchy (parent P to child C UEIs) [DATA (POST 200)] https://api.usaspending.gov/api/v2/recipient/duns/ (update: nightly; history: current hierarchy)
  - USAspending bulk award download (backtest pulls) [DATA (POST 200)] https://api.usaspending.gov/api/v2/bulk_download/awards/ (update: on request; history: FY2008+ (REASONED))
  - FPDS ATOM feed (same-week civilian actions, modifications, de-obligations) [DATA] https://www.fpds.gov/ezsearch/FEEDS/ATOM?FEEDNAME=PUBLIC&q=apple (update: near-real-time for civilian; DoD 90-day delay; history: FY2004+)
  - SEC XBRL companyfacts (revenue, backlog) [DATA] https://data.sec.gov/api/xbrl/companyfacts/CIK0000320193.json (update: per filing; history: 2009+)
- Reviewer source checks: https://api.usaspending.gov/api/v2/search/spending_by_award/ -> DATA (phase0 list) Filter by recipient and agency; sum federal_action_obligation.; https://www.fpds.gov/ezsearch/FEEDS/ATOM?FEEDNAME=PUBLIC&q=apple -> DATA (phase0 list) Near-real-time for civilian agencies; DoD actions withheld 90 days per FPDS not…; https://api.usaspending.gov/api/v2/bulk_download/awards/ -> 405 on GET, 200 on POST POST with filters returned a status_url and file_url for a generated zip (TESTE…; https://api.usaspending.gov/api/v2/recipient/duns/ -> 405 on GET, 200 on POST POST {keyword:'CACI'} returned parent 'CACI INTERNATIONAL INC' (level P, UEI QS…; https://api.usaspending.gov/api/v2/recipient/children/ -> 400 Needs a recipient id in the path; use /recipient/children/<id>/ (UNPROBED).; https://data.sec.gov/api/xbrl/companyfacts/CIK0000320193.json -> DATA (phase0 list) Revenue and backlog calibration.
- Cost: $0
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 15%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Obligations are not revenue: multi-year funding, classified work, subcontract flow and recipient-name mismatches make the roll-up noisy; fiscal-year-end flushes dominate seasonality; the companies pre-announce large awards; and paid federal-data vendors already give the best-covered funds a cleaner version.
- Red team (reviewer): Funded obligations are not revenue: they fund IDIQ task orders that burn over 12-36 months, so a quarter's obligation total is dominated by option exercises and fiscal-year-end timing rather than the quarter's billings, and the companies' own book-to-bill uses contract awards (often ceiling values) rather than funded obligations, so the signal and the target are different quantities. For the names where the data is timely (civilian), state, local and commercial work dilutes federal exposure; for the names where federal exposure is purest (defense), the data arrives 90 days late. Large awards are press-released and priced within a day, so the residual is small and noisy.

## N11 STB EP 724 weekly rail-service metrics (cars on line by car type, train speed, dwell) as a demand nowcast for railcar lessors and makers

- Category: Second-order. Sector focus: Railcar lessors and makers: GATX, Trinity, Greenbrier; rail equipment and services.. Target: revenue (renewal lease-rate change, utilisation, orders) and guidance (nowcast).
- Merged duplicates: Class I rail service metrics (STB EP 724 weekly filings) as a second-order nowcast for intermodal marketers and railcar lessors
- Verdict: REJECT. Changes: None retained. The EP 724 workbook remains a useful, free covariate for seed 6 (carloads) and for railroad names outside the universe, not a standalone signal. Reject reason: The transmission mechanism is real but far too slow for a 2-10 day earnings trade: GATX's average renewal term is 54 months and its renewal success rate and Lease Price Index move over years, so a quarter's change in cars-on-line or train speed barely touches that quarter's lease revenue, while GATX's quarterly EPS variance is dominated by the timing of remarketing gains and TRN's and GBX's by backlog-scheduled deliveries, none of which the STB data sees. The weekly STB and railroad-published velocity and dwell metrics are on every rail analyst's dashboard and are discussed by lessor management each quarter, so the direction is already in consensus; the conditional hypothesis (cars-on-line up 3% and train speed down 5%) describes rare congestion episodes, leaving a 3-name universe with a handful of qualifying events and no statistical power. No vendor or paper was found that ties EP 724 metrics to lessor surprises, which here is evidence of a weak link rather than an unexploited one.
- Mechanism: Class I railroads file weekly service metrics with the Surface Transportation Board under EP 724, and the STB posts a consolidated workbook (TESTED HERE: 502 weekly columns from 2017-03-29 to 2026-11-04 with 'Cars On Line (Count)' by car type - box, covered hopper, gondola, intermodal, multilevel, open hopper, tank, total - plus average train speed, terminal dwell, loaded and empty cars in revenue service by commodity, and carloads by 22 categories). Slower velocity and higher dwell mean shippers need more cars to move the same freight, so lease utilisation and renewal lease rates rise (GATX's Lease Price Index, Trinity's lease fleet) and cars leave storage, lifting new-car orders at Trinity and Greenbrier; the reverse when service improves (REASONED; TRN and GBX management routinely cite velocity as a demand driver). Car-type-level cars-on-line (tank, covered hopper) maps directly onto the lessors' fleet mix.
- Industry context: Railcar lessors earn from utilisation and lease-rate renewals on fleets of around 100,000 cars; when railroads run slower, each car completes fewer cycles, so shippers lease more cars, and the railroads' own service metrics are filed weekly with the regulator. Railcar makers' orders follow with a lag as cars leave storage.
- Measurement window: The 13 weeks of the quarter; railroads file weekly and the STB workbook is updated with roughly a one-to-two-week lag, so the quarter-end week is available ~10 days after quarter end, three to four weeks before TRN, GATX and GBX report.. Lead time: 10 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.2%: 3 names (GATX, TRN, GBX).
- Hypothesis: A 3% or larger YoY increase in Class I tank plus covered-hopper cars on line, combined with a 5% or larger decline in system average train speed over the quarter, with the final week available at least 3 trading days before the release, predicts a positive renewal-lease-rate/utilisation and revenue surprise and positive T-1 to T+2 abnormal return vs XLI for GATX, TRN and GBX, controlling for consensus revisions, carload volumes, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Rail analysts watch these metrics for the railroads themselves (UNP, CSX, NSC - all outside the universe); the mainstream railcar-demand metrics are AAR's monthly cars-in-storage and the Railway Supply Institute's quarterly orders and backlog, both published after quarter end (REASONED). GATX and Trinity management comment on velocity each quarter, so the direction is partly priced; weekly car-type-level cars-on-line is rarely used in lessor print models (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): FTR and the Railway Supply Institute/ARCI publish railcar order and backlog data (paid or industry; REASONED). None of the brief's listed alt-data vendors.. Why ours differs: Weekly, car-type-level and free, available before the lessors print, versus monthly storage counts and quarterly order statistics that arrive later; also measures service quality rather than carloads (seed 6).
- Literature found by reviewer: Federal Register 2016-29131: STB final rule, United States Rail Service Issues, Performance Data Reporting (EP 724 Sub-No. 4) - https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2016-12-05/html/2016-29131.htm; FreightWaves: Tight railcar market benefits lessors like GATX, executives say (management already frames velocity as a demand driver) - https://www.freightwaves.com/news/tight-railcar-market-benefits-lessors-like-gatx-executives-say; MarketBeat: GATX Q2 2026 earnings call highlights (utilisation 98%, renewal success 82.6%, Lease Price Index +16.8%, average renewal term 54 months) - https://www.marketbeat.com/instant-alerts/gatx-q2-earnings-call-highlights-2026-07-31/; STB rail service data page (EP 724 weekly filings) - https://www.stb.gov/reports-data/rail-service-data/
- Data sources:
  - STB rail service data page (EP 724 weekly filings) [DATA] https://www.stb.gov/reports-data/rail-service-data/ (update: Weekly (per file; TESTED HERE page lists consolidated and per-railroad files).; history: Weekly since EP 724 (2017-03-29 in the consolidated workbook, TESTED HERE).)
  - EP724 consolidated weekly workbook, all Class I railroads [DATA] https://www.stb.gov/wp-content/uploads/files/rsir/All%20Class%201%20Railroads/EP724%20Consolidated%20Data%20through%202026-11-04.xlsx (update: Weekly; file name carries the latest week (TESTED HERE: 7.8 MB download parsed; 3,839 rows of measures by railroad).; history: 2017-03-29 to 2026-11-04 (TESTED HERE).)
  - Per-railroad weekly CSVs (STB-1145 and STB-1251 series) [DATA] https://www.stb.gov/wp-content/uploads/STB-1145-ALL-CARRIERS.csv (update: Weekly (TESTED HERE links present).; history: UNKNOWN (not parsed).)
