- Reviewer source checks: https://www.stb.gov/reports-data/rail-service-data/ -> DATA (phase0 list) Weekly carrier service data since EP 724.; https://www.stb.gov/wp-content/uploads/files/rsir/All%20Class%201%20Railroads/E… -> DATA (proposer TESTED HERE) Dated file name; must be re-discovered weekly from the page.; https://www.stb.gov/wp-content/uploads/STB-1145-ALL-CARRIERS.csv -> DATA (proposer TESTED HERE) Depth unknown.
- Cost: $0.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 10%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Velocity is mostly a railroad-operations story that is already discussed on calls; lease rates reset on multi-year contracts so quarterly revenue moves little; cars on line include railroad-owned fleets; carload volume may dominate the lessor outcome; and the relationship inverts when slow service reflects weak demand rather than congestion.
- Red team (reviewer): Velocity is a railroad-operations variable whose quarter-to-quarter changes mostly reflect weather and crew availability; when it reflects weak demand (fewer trains, faster network) the sign of the lessor relationship inverts, and cars-on-line includes railroad-owned and shipper-owned fleets that lessors do not price. Even a correct read of fleet tightness is already stated by management on the prior call and in the GATX Lease Price Index, so the print surprise is unrelated to the signal.

## N12 EIA-860M monthly generator status transitions (planned to under-construction) as a shipment nowcast for solar trackers, eBOS, storage and switchgear

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Electrical equipment: utility-scale solar trackers and eBOS (Nextracker, Array, Shoals), battery storage integrators (Fluence), power-plant switchgear/electrical (Powell), wind blades (TPI).. Target: revenue and guidance (both).
- Merged duplicates: EIA-860M planned-operation-date slippage and ERCOT GIS milestone changes as a revenue-timing nowcast for solar/storage equipment suppliers and renewable IPPs
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Scope narrowed to tracker and eBOS names where shipments coincide with mechanical construction (ARRY, SHLS, NXT); FLNC (milestone-based storage integration), POWL (12-24 month switchgear lead times), BE, BW, AZZ, PLPC removed because the status transition does not time their quarter. (2) Transition definition fixed to MW entering U or moving U to V, normalised by the company's disclosed US backlog, instead of a raw YoY MW count; NXT weighted down for its substantial non-US revenue (REASONED; Wood Mackenzie data show most of its ~40 GW 2025 shipments were outside the US). (3) Backfill via the free PUDL changelog (200 here) rather than parsing every monthly xlsx; point-in-time vintages preserved for a look-ahead-free backtest. (4) Timing: month-3 file lands ~24th of the month after quarter end, so lead time is 5 trading days for ARRY/SHLS (second-week reporters) and often negative for NXT; the proposer's 7 days revised to 5 and per-event checking made mandatory. (5) Crowdedness kept at 2 but with the note that PUDL makes the transitions free to everyone and Kayrros sells a faster satellite version; signal-noise 3 -> 2 for survey lag and status jumps.
- Mechanism: Form EIA-860M is a mandatory monthly survey; its Planned sheet lists every planned generator with nameplate MW, technology, state, planned operation month/year and a status code P/L/T/U/V/TS (TESTED HERE: September 2026 file has 1,100 planned solar PV, 483 battery, 236 gas combined-cycle and 192 gas CT units; status codes read 'Under construction <=50%', '>50%', 'construction complete, not yet in operation', etc.). Trackers and eBOS ship when mechanical construction starts, so MW entering status U during the quarter nowcasts tracker/eBOS shipments and revenue (recognised on shipment, REASONED); MW moving to V/TS nowcasts storage-integration milestone revenue (FLNC) and switchgear deliveries for gas plants (POWL, REASONED). Month-over-month slippage of planned operation dates flags project delays that cut guidance.
- Industry context: Utility-scale solar and storage projects buy trackers, eBOS and inverters at construction start, so equipment makers' quarterly revenue follows the construction-start cadence of a few hundred large projects, each of which must report monthly status to EIA. Status codes and planned operation dates slip when interconnection, tariff or financing problems arise, which is exactly what cuts equipment makers' guidance.
- Measurement window: Months 1-3 of the quarter; month M's file is published in the last week of M+1 (REASONED from EIA's cadence), so months 1-2 arrive 30-60 days before a print and month 3 only ~0-15 days before (sometimes after it; check per event). Each monthly file is a vintage, so point-in-time backtests are natural.. Lead time: 5 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.3-0.5%: ARRY, SHLS, NXT (NXT only when its release follows the month-3 EIA drop); FLNC, POWL, BE, BW, AZZ, PLPC dropped.
- Hypothesis: For US utility-scale solar tracker and eBOS suppliers (ARRY, SHLS, and NXT when the month-3 file precedes its release), the MW of US utility-scale solar PV generators that transition into status U (under construction, at most 50%) or from U to V (more than 50%) during the three months of the fiscal quarter, taken from the EIA-860M monthly vintages (PUDL changelog for history) and expressed as a ratio to the company's last-disclosed US backlog, predicts, when it exceeds the trailing-four-quarter ratio by at least 15%, a positive revenue surprise and a positive close T-1 to close T+2 abnormal return versus XLI, controlling for 30-day consensus revisions, last-reported backlog, size and momentum; a month-over-month slip of planned operation dates on at least 20% of U/V MW is a separate negative guidance flag.
- Who already knows: Sell-side renewables analysts use EIA-860M for installation tracking and lean on Wood Mackenzie/SEIA US Solar Market Insight (paid, published ~2-3 months after quarter) (REASONED). Companies guide and disclose backlog, so the aggregate level is priced; monthly status-transition counts by technology and slippage of planned COD dates are less used in print models (REASONED). Kayrros sells satellite construction monitoring of solar farms (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Wood Mackenzie/SEIA (US Solar Market Insight), Kayrros (satellite solar-construction monitoring) - REASONED.. Why ours differs: Free, monthly, project-level status transitions framed as construction starts rather than cumulative installs, with COD slippage as a guidance-risk flag; the satellite product answers the same question at enterprise prices.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Kayrros Solar Monitor for traders (satellite construction tracking; claims forecasts six months ahead of ISO/EIA reports) - https://www.kayrros.com/products/solar-monitor-for-traders/; Wood Mackenzie global solar tracker shipment reports (via PV Tech) - https://www.pv-tech.org/woodmac-solar-pv-trackers-hit-92gw-shipments-2023/; Global Energy Monitor: Global Solar Power Tracker (free, project phases) - https://globalenergymonitor.org/projects/global-solar-power-tracker; Catalyst Cooperative PUDL: core_eia860m__changelog_generators (free, pre-built monthly EIA-860M status changelog) - https://data.catalyst.coop/pudl/core_eia860m__changelog_generators
- Literature found by reviewer: PUDL documentation: pudl.transform.eia860m (changelog records only month-to-month changes; valid_until_date per record) - https://docs.catalyst.coop/pudl/en/stable/autoapi/pudl/transform/eia860m/index.html; EIA Today in Energy on the Preliminary Monthly Electric Generator Inventory - https://www.eia.gov/todayinEnergy/detail.php?id=24152; TaiyangNews: 2025 global solar tracker shipments top 134 GW DC (Wood Mackenzie data; Nextracker/Nextpower ~40 GW, largely non-US) - https://taiyangnews.info/business/2025-global-solar-tracker-shipments-top-134-gw-dc
- Data sources:
  - EIA-860M monthly xlsx vintages (Planned sheet with status codes P/L/T/U/V/TS and planned operation month) [DATA (200)] https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/ (update: monthly, ~24th of the following month (REASONED from the Sep-2026 file being latest on 2026-11-11); history: monthly files linked from 2016 (proposer) or 2021 (this probe's link scan))
  - PUDL core_eia860m__changelog_generators (point-in-time status transitions for backfill) [DATA (200)] https://data.catalyst.coop/pudl/core_eia860m__changelog_generators (update: per PUDL release (nightly builds exist; REASONED); history: 2015+ monthly (REASONED from PUDL docs))
  - EIA API v2 (optional operating-capacity series) [KEY] https://api.eia.gov/v2/ (update: monthly; history: multi-year)
- Reviewer source checks: https://www.eia.gov/electricity/data/eia860m/ -> 200 Latest file on 2026-11-11 is september_generator2026.xlsx; files back to 2021 v…; https://data.catalyst.coop/pudl/core_eia860m__changelog_generators -> 200 Free changelog of EIA-860M generator records (status transitions, dates) with p…; https://api.eia.gov/v2/ -> KEY (phase0 list) Free EIA API key; optional.
- Cost: $0 (optional free EIA API key).
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 20%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Survey responses lag and update sporadically (status can jump P to V in one month); NXT and ARRY have substantial non-US revenue (REASONED); shipments can precede the status change by a quarter; tariff and FEOC policy headlines dominate these stocks; and the month-3 file often lands after the print, leaving only two months of signal.
- Red team (reviewer): EIA-860M is a respondent survey with sporadic updates: projects jump from P to V in a single month or stay in U for years, so the quarter's 'construction starts' is an artefact of when developers answered, not when piles were driven, and the sell-side already uses the same file for installation tracking. Tracker shipments are scheduled by the supplier's contracted backlog and guided a quarter ahead, so the quarter's revenue surprise is mostly recognition timing and margin (steel, tariffs, 45X domestic-content), while the stocks trade on policy headlines (FEOC, ITC safe-harbour) that swamp a 15% change in a noisy MW ratio. With three names and a 3.5-week publication lag, the usable sample is small and the month-3 file sometimes arrives after the print, leaving two months of signal.

## N13 Used-truck storefront census: listing counts, mix and asking prices on lessor and carrier used-vehicle sites

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Truck lessors and asset-heavy truckload carriers with disclosed equipment-gain lines; commercial truck dealers. Target: EPS surprise (gains on sale, residual charges); margin (nowcast).
- Merged duplicates: Dealer own-site inventory listings (used and new unit counts, mix, asking prices, listing age) for truck and equipment dealers
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: Both issuer-specific storefronts (Ryder, Penske) carry Terms of Use that explicitly forbid robots, scrapers and automated extraction (TESTED HERE), Werner returns 403, and Schneider lists about 8 units; the only lawful residue is the market-level used Class 8 price trend that ACT Research and J.D. Power already sell and trade press reprints monthly, i.e. a crowded, non-issuer signal. No clean issuer-specific alternative exists.
- Mechanism: Ryder's used-vehicle sales results and its used-vehicle inventory KPI, which management guides and discusses each quarter, are visible daily on its own storefront: 5,916 listings with asking prices on 2026-11-11 (TESTED HERE). Changes in listing count, mix (sleeper tractors versus straight trucks), listing age and price cuts over the quarter nowcast used-vehicle gains or losses, residual-value adjustments and accelerated depreciation, which swing Ryder's EPS by several cents (REASONED). Truckload carriers' gain on sale of equipment (Werner, Knight-Swift, Heartland, Schneider) follows the same used-tractor price level, and Penske's and Schneider's storefronts give market-wide asking prices.
- Industry context: Lessors and large carriers sell thousands of tractors a year and book gains or losses against book value, so used-truck prices and inventory levels flow straight into earnings. These companies market their used fleets on public websites that show every unit and its asking price.
- Measurement window: Daily snapshots over the quarter; final reading at quarter end, roughly four weeks before Ryder's release.. Lead time: 20 days.
- Universe coverage: ~1%: R (~$7B) primary; WERN, KNX, HTLD, SNDR, MRTN, CVLG via gain on sale; RUSHA (truck dealer, used-truck margins). About 8-10 names (market caps approximate, REASONED).
- Hypothesis: A rise of at least 10% in Ryder's listed used-vehicle count over the quarter combined with a decline of at least 3% in the median asking price of 3-5-year-old sleeper tractors, measured 7 days before the release, predicts a negative EPS surprise and a negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus IYT for R, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: J.D. Power Valuation Services and ACT Research publish used Class 8 price indices (paid, with partly free monthly commentary) and sell-side cites them; Ryder discloses its inventory count and UVS results only quarterly. The market-level price trend is priced; Ryder-specific inventory build and price-cutting pace are not.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): J.D. Power Valuation Services (Commercial Truck Guidelines) and ACT Research sell used-truck price data; neither sells a company-specific storefront census (REASONED).. Why ours differs: Daily, issuer-specific inventory and asking-price changes from the company's own public listings rather than a monthly market index.
- Vendors found by reviewer: ACT Research U.S. Used Truck Price Outlook (Class 8 sleeper tractors, dealer-transaction database) - https://www.actresearch.net/reports-data/forecasts-market-data/u-s-used-truck-price-outlook; J.D. Power Valuation Services Commercial Truck Guidelines monthly used-truck retail/wholesale/auction prices, as reprinted in trade press - https://www.ttnews.com/articles/used-truck-price-june
- Literature found by reviewer: FreightWaves: Falling used truck values weigh on Ryder earnings - https://www.freightwaves.com/news/falling-used-truck-values-weigh-on-ryder-earnings; FreightWaves: Ryder sees weak market for used vehicles lingering, and it has a lot of them - https://www.freightwaves.com/?p=284192; FreightWaves: Earlier Ryder writedowns on used fleet value positive for bottom line - https://www.freightwaves.com/?p=300798
- Data sources:
  - Ryder used-truck inventory - TESTED HERE: HTTP 200, '5916 results', asking prices in HTML; www.ryder.com robots.txt only disallows /episerver/ and /utils/ [UNPROBED] https://www.ryder.com/en-us/used-trucks/inventory (update: Continuous; history: Snapshot only; archive daily)
  - Penske Used Trucks storefront - TESTED HERE: HTTP 200 (robots.txt not checked) [UNPROBED] https://www.penskeusedtrucks.com/ (update: Continuous; history: Snapshot only)
  - Schneider used equipment - TESTED HERE: HTTP 200, robots.txt allows; listing count not confirmed [UNPROBED] https://schneider.com/used-equipment (update: Continuous; history: Snapshot only)
  - Werner Fleet Sales - TESTED HERE: HTTP 403 from this container [UNPROBED] https://www.wernerfleetsales.com/ (update: Continuous; history: Snapshot only)
  - BLS PPI series via API (used/new truck price context; free v2 key for volume) [DATA] https://api.bls.gov/publicAPI/v1/timeseries/data/WPU10 (update: Monthly; history: ~3 years keyless, longer with key)
- Reviewer source checks: https://www.ryder.com/en-us/used-trucks/inventory -> 302 -> 200 (TESTED HERE 2026-11-11); '5916 results' in HTML TERMS-PROHIBITED: ryder.com Terms of Use state the user 'will not use any robot…; https://www.penskeusedtrucks.com/ -> 200 (TESTED HERE) TERMS-PROHIBITED: penskeusedtrucks.com/terms-and-conditions.html: 'You shall no…; https://schneider.com/used-equipment -> 200 (TESTED HERE) robots.txt allows all; no ToS page found at guessed URLs (404); page shows only…; https://www.wernerfleetsales.com/ -> 403 (TESTED HERE) Blocked from the container.; https://api.bls.gov/publicAPI/v1/timeseries/data/WPU10 -> DATA (in working-sources file) Market-level PPI context only; free BLS v2 key for volume.
- Cost: $0
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 5. Confidence: 5%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Asking prices are not realized prices, wholesale channels are invisible, Ryder guides inventory and UVS ranges and analysts already model the J.D. Power and ACT indices, and website redesigns or deduplication changes can break the count series.
- Red team (reviewer): Even if the data were obtainable, asking prices and listing counts on Ryder's own lot are a noisy, lagging reflection of the ACT Research and J.D. Power transaction indices that sell-side already models; Ryder guides its used-vehicle inventory and UVS results each quarter and realized gains depend on wholesale channel mix and book values, so the series proxies the market-level used-truck cycle (already in consensus) rather than a Ryder-specific surprise.

## N14 EIA weekly state coal production plus EIA-923 mine-to-plant receipts and EPA CEMS burn at the named mine's customer plants

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Energy and materials: thermal and metallurgical coal producers. Target: revenue (volume), guidance (nowcast).
- Seed item: Truck counts from state DOT traffic counters near large distribution centers; weekly rail carloads by commodity
- Merged duplicates: Customer-filed EIA-923 coal receipts by mine as a volume-and-price nowcast for thermal coal producers
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: Mechanism is spurious by construction: EIA's weekly state figures are a national estimate allocated by fixed MSHA shares (confirmed from EIA's published estimation method), so mapping them to producers adds nothing to the weekly rail-carload seed; the company-specific sources lag past most earnings dates and the CEMS burn check helps only one or two mid-November reporters.
- Mechanism: EIA publishes weekly coal production by state with a 5-day lag, MSHA publishes quarterly production per mine, and EIA-923 fuel receipts name the supplying mine (MSHA ID) for each power plant each month. Combining them gives, per producer, (a) a weekly production nowcast weighted by its mines' share of each state and (b) a demand check from EPA CEMS heat input at the plants that buy from those mines. For thermal producers whose tons are contracted but whose shipments slip with utility burn and stockpiles (Alliance, Hallador, Peabody's PRB and Illinois Basin, Core Natural Resources' PRB and PAMC), the gap between guided shipments and the production/customer-burn read predicts the volume component of revenue; for met producers (Warrior, Alpha, Ramaco) state production in Alabama and Central Appalachia proxies output before the company reports.
- Industry context: Thermal coal volumes depend on utility burn and stockpile levels, met coal on steel demand and export logistics; producers guide annual tons but quarterly shipments slip with rail service and weather. Production and shipments diverge when stockpiles build at the mine.
- Measurement window: EIA weekly through the quarter's last week, published ~5 days after week end (TESTED HERE: the 2026-11-05 release covers the week ended 2026-10-31); EIA-923 monthly receipts with ~10-week lag (TESTED HERE: August 2026 posted by 2026-11-11) cover months 1-2 for the mapping; EPA CEMS quarterly apportioned data for Q3 2026 were already served on 2026-11-11 (TESTED HERE), i.e. 4-6 weeks after quarter end, so the burn check helps only late reporters; MSHA 2026Q3 mine-level data were ~97% populated on 2026-11-11 (TESTED HERE), useful for calibration, not for lead.. Lead time: 21 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.7%: Peabody (BTU), Core Natural Resources (CNR), Alliance Resource Partners (ARLP), Hallador (HNRG), NACCO (NC), Alpha Metallurgical (AMR), Warrior Met (HCC), Ramaco (METC); coal-burning utilities as a secondary fuel-cost read.
- Hypothesis: A change of more than 5% between a producer's mine-weighted state production for the quarter and its guidance-implied run-rate, measured 7 days after quarter end (~21 days before the release), predicts the sign of the coal-volume surprise and the close T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus XLE/XME, controlling for consensus revisions, benchmark coal price moves (API2, Australian HCC index), size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Coal analysts track EIA weekly production and rail carloads routinely (crowded). The mine-to-plant mapping through EIA-923 receipts and CEMS burn is used by coal-market consultancies (REASONED) more than by equity sell-side. Priced in: state-level production largely; customer-plant burn versus guided shipments less.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None from the named list. Coal consultancies sell mine-level supply/demand models (REASONED).. Why ours differs: Free, mine-mapped and demand-checked. EIA's weekly state numbers are themselves modeled from AAR railcar loadings (REASONED from EIA's published methodology), so this deepens the rail-carload seed by converting carload-derived state estimates into company-level volume and customer burn.
- Vendors found by reviewer: OPIS coal: quarterly MSHA production by region and producer plus coal-producer earnings coverage - https://www.opis.com/?p=19723; Catalyst Cooperative PUDL core_eia923__entity_coalmine (free EIA-923 mine-to-plant table with MSHA IDs; warns mine IDs are inconsistent) - https://data.catalyst.coop/pudl/core_eia923__entity_coalmine; The Coal Trader (Substack) quarterly MSHA production highlights - https://thecoaltrader.substack.com/p/q4-2022-highlights-and-recent-msha
- Literature found by reviewer: EIA Weekly Coal Production Estimation Method (national autoregressive model on AAR railcar loadings; state allocation by latest MSHA share, usually two-quarter lag) - https://www.eia.gov/coal/production/weekly/estimation_method.pdf; S&P Global Market Intelligence: US coal production falls 3.4% in Q4 2023 (MSHA-based producer rankings) - https://www.spglobal.com/market-intelligence/en/news-insights/articles/2024/2/us-coal-production-falls-3-4-in-q4-2023-more-pressure-coming-in-2024-80517017; EIA Quarterly Coal Report - https://eia.gov/coal/production/quarterly/
- Data sources:
  - EIA Weekly Coal Production xlsx by state and region (TESTED HERE: HTTP 200, report DOE/EIA 0218/44 released 2026-11-05 for week ended 2026-10-31, next release 2026-11-12) [UNPROBED] https://www.eia.gov/coal/production/weekly/xls/weekly_production.xlsx (update: weekly, ~5-day lag (TESTED HERE); history: current week, year-to-date and 52-week columns in the file; longer history via EIA API coal series (REASONED))
  - EIA API v2 (EIA-923 fuel receipts by plant with mine MSHA ID; coal production series) [KEY] https://api.eia.gov/v2/ (update: monthly (923) with ~10-week lag; history: 2008+ (REASONED))
  - MSHA Mines quarterly employment/production dataset (TESTED HERE: HTTP 200, 54 MB zip, pipe-delimited mine_id / mine name / state / year / quarter / employees / hours / coal production; 2026Q3 present with 22,433 rows vs ~23,180 for full quarters) [UNPROBED] https://arlweb.msha.gov/OpenGovernmentData/DataSets/MinesProdQuarterly.zip (update: quarterly, ~6 weeks after quarter end (TESTED HERE for one quarter); history: 2000+ quarterly (REASONED))
  - EPA CAMPD apportioned quarterly emissions by unit (gross load, heat input) (TESTED HERE: HTTP 200 with DEMO_KEY for year=2026&quarter=3&stateCode=IN; free api.data.gov key for production use) [UNPROBED] https://api.epa.gov/easey/emissions-mgmt/emissions/apportioned/quarterly?api_key=DEMO_KEY&year=2026&quarter=3&stateCode=IN&page=1&perPage=2 (update: quarterly, available ~4-6 weeks after quarter end (TESTED HERE); history: 1995+ (REASONED))
  - BNSF weekly carload reports (PRB coal cross-check) [DATA] https://www.bnsf.com/about-bnsf/financial-information/index.html#Weekly+Carload+Reports (update: weekly; history: Feb 2025 onward visible (per file))
  - AAR rail traffic data (coal carloads) [DATA] https://www.aar.org/data-center/rail-traffic-data/ (update: weekly; history: press-release archive (per file))
- Reviewer source checks: https://www.eia.gov/coal/production/weekly/xls/weekly_production.xlsx -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Reachable, but per EIA's methodology PDF (200, TESTED HERE) the state columns a…; https://api.eia.gov/v2/ -> KEY (in working-sources file; free EIA key) EIA-923 receipts with mine IDs lag ~10 weeks; months 1-2 of a quarter arrive af…; https://arlweb.msha.gov/OpenGovernmentData/DataSets/MinesProdQuarterly.zip -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Quarterly, ~6 weeks after quarter end; calibration only.; https://api.epa.gov/easey/emissions-mgmt/emissions/apportioned/quarterly?api_ke… -> 200 (TESTED HERE with DEMO_KEY) Free api.data.gov key for production use; quarterly burn arrives ~30+ days afte…; https://www.bnsf.com/about-bnsf/financial-information/index.html#Weekly+Carload… -> DATA (in working-sources file) This and AAR are the registered rail-carload seed; EIA's weekly number is deriv…
- Cost: $0 (free EIA key; free api.data.gov key)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 1 / 1. Confidence: 5%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): State totals are dominated by private or too-large producers; weekly figures are modeled estimates later revised to MSHA; prices rather than volumes drive earnings surprises; rail carloads give the same read without the extra mapping.
- Red team (reviewer): The 'mine-weighted state production' is algebraically the national AAR rail-loadings estimate multiplied by a fixed, two-quarter-lagged MSHA share, so it carries zero company-specific weekly information beyond the AAR/BNSF carload seed already registered; the genuinely producer-specific inputs (MSHA quarterly, EIA-923 receipts) arrive 6-10 weeks after quarter end, after most producers have reported, and coal-equity surprises are driven by realized price (API2, HCC) rather than volume.

## N15 NRC daily power-reactor status: unplanned outage days at owner-mapped nuclear units

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Utilities / merchant power: owners of US nuclear units. Target: revenue and EPS surprise (merchant owner); margin/O&M (regulated owners) (nowcast).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Ownership corrected: Evergy owns 94% of Wolf Creek (two 47% subsidiaries; LITERATURE, NRC letter), not 47%; WEC owns no nuclear unit (Point Beach sold to FPL Energy in 2007, REASONED) and is removed; DTE (Fermi 2, 100%, near the cap, REASONED) added as a regulated owner; Talen's ~$13-17B 2026 market cap keeps it in universe. (2) Primary test narrowed to the merchant owner (TLN); PNW, EVRG, AEE, TXNM, DTE kept only for O&M/deferred-fuel disclosure checks because fuel riders mute the EPS effect. (3) Crowdedness lowered to 2: EIA republishes the NRC file daily as a dashboard and CSV and power traders use it, so the signal must be framed as 'not yet in consensus EPS' and conditioned on the stock's move during the outage. (4) Signal_noise lowered from 5 to 3: lost MWh is clean but the EPS mapping runs through hedge buy-backs at spot (needs PJM West LMPs, free key) and the sample is one merchant name. (5) Lead time 30-40 days (TLN reports ~5-6 weeks after quarter end, REASONED). (6) NRC Event Notification Reports added to tag scram causes. (7) Framed as a veto/filter rather than a standalone long/short.
- Mechanism: The NRC posts every US reactor's percent power at 00:00 each day. Summing (100% minus reported power) x unit MW across the quarter gives lost MWh per unit; applying ownership shares gives each company's nuclear generation shortfall versus the refueling plan it guided. For a merchant owner (Talen's Susquehanna) lost MWh x hedged/realized price is lost revenue plus replacement-power cost that hits EPS directly; for regulated owners (Pinnacle West 29% of Palo Verde, Evergy 47% of Wolf Creek, Ameren 100% of Callaway, TXNM ~10% of Palo Verde) fuel riders mute it to deferred fuel and O&M. The edge is the unplanned extension or trip: guidance embeds the planned outage duration, consensus EPS usually does not move until management comments.
- Industry context: A nuclear unit earns only when it runs; refueling outages every 18-24 months are scheduled and guided, but trips and outage extensions are not. One 1,200 MW unit down for 10 unplanned days loses about 290 GWh, roughly $12-17M of revenue or replacement-power cost at $40-60/MWh.
- Measurement window: Full quarter at daily resolution; final reading the day after quarter end; owners report 30-45 days after quarter end. TESTED HERE: 365-day file current to 11/10/2026.. Lead time: 30 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.4%: TLN primary; PNW, EVRG, AEE, TXNM, DTE secondary (5-6 names). WEC removed (no nuclear ownership).
- Hypothesis: For Talen (merchant owner of Susquehanna), an unplanned loss of more than 10 equivalent-full-power unit-days in the quarter (NRC daily status net of the planned refueling schedule disclosed in guidance), measured 1 day after quarter end (~30-40 days before the release) and valued at PJM West day-ahead LMP over the outage hours, predicts a negative adjusted-EBITDA/EPS surprise and a negative close T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus XLU only when consensus EPS has not already fallen by at least the valued loss in the prior 30 days; for regulated owners (PNW 29% Palo Verde, EVRG 94% Wolf Creek, AEE Callaway, TXNM ~10% Palo Verde, DTE Fermi 2) the effect is confined to O&M and deferred-fuel disclosures and is expected to be near zero.
- Who already knows: Power traders and the Platts/Argus daily nuclear-outage tables use the NRC file (REASONED). Equity sell-side on TLN/PNW/EVRG models planned refueling outages from company guidance; unplanned extensions are visible to anyone who looks, but consensus EPS tends to update only after an 8-K or call commentary (REASONED). Priced in: planned outages fully; unplanned days partly in the stock, less in consensus.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None from the named list. Platts/Argus publish nuclear outage summaries for traders (REASONED); Bloomberg nuclear outage pages (UNKNOWN).. Why ours differs: Free, unit-level, daily, zero lag; the signal is the delta between actual lost MWh and the planned-outage assumption in guidance, computed per owner share and valued at the owner's realized price, which no vendor packages as an equity earnings signal.
- Vendors found by reviewer: EIA Status of U.S. Nuclear Outages dashboard (free daily repackaging of the NRC status file, with CSV download) - https://www.eia.gov/nuclear/outages; NRC Power Reactor Status Reports yearly raw-data text files plus Excel data dictionary - https://www.nrc.gov/reading-rm/doc-collections/event-status/reactor-status/2025/index.html
- Literature found by reviewer: Rothwell (1989), Stock Market Reaction to Nuclear Reactor Failures, Contemporary Economic Policy - https://ideas.repec.org/a/bla/coecpo/v7y1989i3p96-106.html; Intra-industry effects of the accident at Three Mile Island, JFQA (TMI/Chernobyl utility event studies) - https://cambridge.org/core/journals/journal-of-financial-and-quantitative-analysis/article/intraindustry-effects-of-the-accident-at-three-mile-island/077D874A2D9B7D976C40E43D521BBB81; FPL Group 2009: extended Seabrook outage cut adjusted EPS by $0.17-0.21 (earnings-channel example) - https://electricityforum.com/news/outagewillaffectfpladjustedearnings; GridStatus: What happens when a nuclear plant suddenly shuts off (ISO-NE price response) - https://blog.gridstatus.io/isone-nuclear-outage/; NRC letter ML19352E138: Evergy holds 94% of Wolf Creek, KEPCo 6% - https://www.nrc.gov/docs/ML1935/ML19352E138.pdf; Talen Energy market cap ~$13.2B (Sept 2026, Trefis) - https://www.trefis.com/data/companies/tln
- Data sources:
  - NRC Power Reactor Status, last 365 days (pipe-delimited) [PROBED 200 (2026-11-11)] https://www.nrc.gov/reading-rm/doc-collections/event-status/reactor-status/powerreactorstatusforlast365days.txt (update: daily; history: 365 days; yearly raw text files in per-year directories)

- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): The data are public daily and watched by power traders, so by earnings day the stock already reflects the outage and management often pre-announces it; regulated owners' riders neutralize most of the EPS effect; the sample is tiny (5-6 names x 4 quarters per year).
- Red team (reviewer): The outage is visible the same day to every power trader and on the free EIA dashboard, so the stock reprices during the quarter and any residual on earnings day is indistinguishable from guidance commentary; with one merchant name (Talen) and at most one or two qualifying unplanned outages a year, the hypothesis cannot be validated statistically and works at best as a veto rule on long-into-earnings positions.

## N16 USACE Corps Locks tonnage by commodity and lock-queue delay hours for inland barge and river-dependent shippers

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Shipping and industrials: inland tank-barge operators, grain and ethanol handlers, fertilizer and aggregates producers that ship by barge. Target: revenue (KEX), margin (freight-cost absorbers), guidance (nowcast).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Dropped the tonnage-share-to-revenue leg: lock tonnage is the whole market and Kirby's inland revenue is set by term-contract pricing and utilization that sits in the low-90s regardless, so a 5% market tonnage move is not a Kirby revenue surprise. (2) Dropped the fertilizer/aggregates margin leg (barge freight is a small, largely passed-through cost line for CF/MOS/LXU/UAN/MLM; untestable). (3) Kept only the delay channel for KEX: quarter-to-date lock delay hours, closures and queue length at the GIWW and Illinois/upper-Mississippi locks Kirby names in its filings, versus the same quarter last year, mapped to Kirby's reported delay days and inland operating margin. (4) Backtest on the Monthly Key Lock Report (2016-2026, 11 key locks) instead of the 15-minute queue report, which has no history and must be archived from now. (5) Signal_noise to 2 and confidence to 15: LPMS month-end entry lags unknown, APEX extraction needed, Kirby gives mid-quarter delay commentary. (6) Lead time of 25 days holds only if the monthly report is complete 5 days after month end, which was not verified (UNKNOWN).
- Mechanism: The Corps' Lock Performance Monitoring System records every lockage with vessel, barge count, commodity tonnage and queue time; the public Corps Locks app shows monthly tonnage by lock (updated daily), a monthly key-lock commodity report and 15-minute queue and status reports. Petroleum and chemical tonnage through Gulf Intracoastal and lower Mississippi locks (Bayou Sorrel, Port Allen, Algiers, Harvey, Calcasieu, Leland Bowman) tracks inland tank-barge demand where Kirby is the largest operator and prices on utilization; grain tonnage through Locks 27, LaGrange and Melvin Price tracks export grain and ethanol flows for Andersons, Bunge and Green Plains; lock closures and queue hours raise barge rates and freight costs for fertilizer (CF, MOS, LXU, UAN) and aggregates (ACA, MLM). The quarter-to-date tonnage and delay-hours versus last year are read before the company reports volumes.
- Industry context: Inland tank barges are a fixed, slow-growing fleet, so demand changes show up in utilization and spot rates within weeks. Locks are chokepoints: a closure on the Illinois River or at Bayou Sorrel strands or reroutes barges and spikes rates.
- Measurement window: Monthly tonnage by lock, 'updated daily at 10 am ET' (TESTED HERE, page text); queue reports every 15 minutes; the quarter's tonnage is nominally complete a few days after month end (REASONED, LPMS entry lags unknown); companies report 25-40 days after quarter end.. Lead time: 25 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.1%: KEX only (grain names via USDA GTR optional but that series is crowded in ag circles).
- Hypothesis: For Kirby, quarter-to-date lock delay hours plus closure days at the GIWW (Bayou Sorrel, Port Allen, Algiers, Harvey, Calcasieu, Leland Bowman) and Illinois/upper-Mississippi key locks, measured 5 days after quarter end (~25 days before the release) and expressed as a percentile of the 2016-2025 distribution for that calendar quarter, predicts the sign of Kirby's inland-marine operating-margin surprise and its close T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus XLI, controlling for USGS river stage at St. Louis, diesel price, consensus revisions, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Ag economists and barge brokers use the weekly USDA Grain Transportation Report, which itself draws on Corps lock data, so grain barge counts are well known in ag circles; Kirby's sell-side relies on Kirby's own utilization commentary (REASONED). Petroleum and chemical tonnage through GIWW locks is not routinely published for equity purposes (UNKNOWN).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None from the named list. USDA GTR (free) and barge brokers publish weekly grain barge counts; no scale vendor known for chemical/petroleum lock tonnage.. Why ours differs: Commodity-specific (chemicals and petroleum) lock tonnage mapped to Kirby, plus lock delay-hours as a cost shock for river-dependent manufacturers; free and daily rather than a weekly ag-only summary.
- Vendors found by reviewer: CISA MTS Resilience Resources: Lock Performance Monitoring System (raw lockage data only on USACE ACE-IT machines; public site gives summarized reports) - https://www.cisa.gov/mts-resilience-resources/lock-performance-monitoring-system-lpms; data.defense.gov Featured API: LPMS Data Web Services (XML lock-queue 24h, tonnage and 30-day traffic reports) - https://data.defense.gov/Featured-API/
- Literature found by reviewer: Kirby Corp MD&A definition of delay days (weather, lock congestion and other navigational factors) - https://ca.marketscreener.com/quote/stock/KIRBY-CORPORATION-13249/news/KIRBY-Management-s-Discussion-and-Analysis-of-Financial-Condition-and-Results-of-Operations-form-33197619/; Kirby 2024 Q4 results: lock maintenance on high-traffic routes drove a 30% sequential increase in delay days - https://quantisnow.com/insight/kirby-corporation-announces-2024-fourth-quarter-and-full-year-results-5862565; National Academies: Illustrations of Delay and Possible Metrics by Major River System (LPMS delay metrics) - https://nap.nationalacademies.org/read/21763/chapter/13; Kirby Q1 2026 call: utilization low-90% range; seasonal weather, lock delays and navigational disruptions - https://financialfilings.com/filings/kirby-corp/call-transcript/2026/48492250/
- Data sources:
  - USACE Corps Locks (LPMS) home: Monthly Key Lock Report 2016-2026, Monthly Tonnage Report 2021-2026, Lock Queue Flotilla Report (15 min), Lock Status Report [PROBED 200 (2026-11-11)] https://ndc.ops.usace.army.mil/ords/r/lpms/corps-locks/home (update: daily (monthly tonnage), 15 min (queue), monthly (key lock); history: 2016-2026 (key lock); 2021-2026 (monthly tonnage); queue report snapshot only, archive from now)
  - USACE Corps Locks monthly tonnage report (APEX page; parameters begin/end year) [PROBED 302->200 (2026-11-11)] https://ndc.ops.usace.army.mil/ords/r/lpms/corps-locks/monthly-tonnage-report (update: daily; history: 2021-2026)
  - USGS NWIS daily gage height, Mississippi at St. Louis (control) [DATA] https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv/?sites=07010000&parameterCd=00065&startDT=2015-01-01&format=json (update: daily; history: 1982-present)
  - NOAA NWPS gauge stage/flow (control) [DATA] https://api.water.noaa.gov/nwps/v1/gauges/EADM7/stageflow (update: half-hourly; history: ~30 days observed)
- Reviewer source checks: https://ndc.ops.usace.army.mil/ords/r/lpms/corps-locks/home -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Oracle APEX public app, no login; US government data, no ToS conflict seen.; https://ndc.ops.usace.army.mil/ords/r/lpms/corps-locks/monthly-tonnage-report -> 302 -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Page carries P1_MONTHLY_TONNAGE_BEGIN_YEAR=2021 / END_YEAR=2026 parameters; ext…; https://ndc.ops.usace.army.mil/ords/r/lpms/corps-locks/data-web-services -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Page only links the APEX reports; no XML endpoint URL exposed. Legacy corpslock…; https://www.ams.usda.gov/services/transportation-analysis/gtr -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Weekly Grain Transportation Report; crowded in ag circles.; https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv/?sites=07010000&parameterCd=00065&startD… -> DATA (in working-sources file) River-stage control.; https://api.water.noaa.gov/nwps/v1/gauges/EADM7/stageflow -> DATA (in working-sources file) Half-hourly stage; ~30 days observed.
- Cost: $0
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 15%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Lock tonnage is the whole market, not Kirby's share; most of Kirby's revenue is term contracts repriced with a lag; LPMS data gaps and entry lags distort month-ends; the registered river-level seed already captures the big drought-driven moves.
- Red team (reviewer): Lock delays are the industry's shared weather and infrastructure shock, visible to Kirby's analysts through Corps notices to navigation and Kirby's own mid-quarter conference commentary, and the already-registered river-stage seed captures the largest drought and flood episodes; what remains is a small, partly priced operating-margin effect at a single name, with unverified month-end entry lags in LPMS.

## N17 USDA Cold Storage frozen-fry stocks and NASS Potato Stocks processing use as a Lamb Weston volume and price nowcast

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Consumer staples: frozen potato processing. Target: margin, revenue, guidance (nowcast).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Timing corrected per fiscal quarter: Cold Storage is released about the 22nd-25th of the following month (August 2026 data on Sept 25). LW reports fiscal Q1 (late-Aug quarter) around Oct 1 (~4-6 days lead); fiscal Q2 (late-Nov quarter) around Dec 19 (FY26 precedent), so the November reading arrives AFTER the release and the October (month-2) reading must be used; fiscal Q3 (Feb) around Apr 2 (~8 days); fiscal Q4 (May) around Jul 23 (~29 days). Minimum lead is 4 trading days, not 10. (2) Added the NASS Potato Stocks publication page (esmis.nal.usda.gov/publication/potato-stocks; Cornell ID 47429915b) for the processing-use leg. (3) Signal_noise lowered to 2 (one name, three fully usable quarters a year, competitor inventory and export staging in the industry total, LW's own finished-goods inventory in its 10-Q as a confounder). (4) Hypothesis restated with the month-2 fallback and gross-margin target. (5) LW's ~40% North American capacity share remains REASONED and unverified.
- Mechanism: USDA's monthly Cold Storage report gives end-of-month stocks of frozen french fries and other frozen potato products; NASS Potato Stocks reports (December to June) give raw potatoes used for processing in the fall-storage states. Industry frozen-fry inventory rising year-over-year while processing use flattens signals oversupply: processors cut run-rates (lower fixed-cost absorption) and discount, the dynamic behind Lamb Weston's 2024-25 margin collapse. With Lamb Weston at roughly 40% of North American frozen-potato capacity (REASONED), the direction of industry stocks versus the prior year, read at its fiscal quarter end, predicts volume and price/mix in the quarter being reported and the tone of guidance.
- Industry context: Frozen fries are made in continuous plants from stored potatoes and sold mostly to QSR chains on annual contracts; when capacity outruns restaurant traffic, inventory builds in cold storage and processors idle lines and cut prices. Lamb Weston's margins swing with plant utilization.
- Measurement window: End-of-month stocks released about the 22nd-25th of the following month; Lamb Weston's fiscal quarters end in late August, November, February and May and it reports about five weeks later, so the quarter's final-month reading lands 8-30 days before the release depending on the quarter (REASONED from recent report dates); the minimum is used as lead time.. Lead time: 4 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.1%: LW only (McCain, Simplot, Cavendish private); weak read-through to QSR fry costs.
- Hypothesis: A year-over-year change of more than 8% in USDA Cold Storage frozen french-fry stocks for the last month of Lamb Weston's fiscal quarter when that report precedes the release (fiscal Q1, Q3, Q4), or for month 2 of fiscal Q2, with the same-sign change in NASS processing use when a Potato Stocks issue is available, measured at least 4 trading days before the release, predicts the sign of Lamb Weston's gross-margin surprise and its close T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus XLP (rising stocks predict negative surprises), controlling for consensus revisions, QSR traffic proxies, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Lamb Weston's roughly ten analysts and the company discuss industry capacity additions and QSR traffic; whether they systematically use USDA cold-storage stocks is UNKNOWN. Expana (Urner Barry) sells potato price assessments (REASONED). Priced in: the oversupply narrative is well known; the monthly inventory inflection is less so.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None from the named list; Expana/Urner Barry price assessments and trade-press reporting (REASONED).. Why ours differs: Free government inventory data aligned to Lamb Weston's unusual fiscal calendar and read as a year-over-year inventory-versus-processing gap, not a narrative; nobody sells a frozen-fry inventory signal.
- Literature found by reviewer: USDA NASS Cold Storage, August 31, 2026 (released September 25, 2026; French fries 976,006 thousand lb vs 1,017,533 a year earlier) - https://esmis.nal.usda.gov/sites/default/release-files/796075/cost0926.txt; Potato Grower: February report from USDA on frozen potato supply - https://potatogrower.com/2024/03/february-report-from-usda-frozen; American Farm Bureau Market Intel: Reviewing trends and capacity of cold storage - https://www.fb.org/market-intel/reviewing-trends-and-capacity-of-cold-storage; Lamb Weston to announce FY2026 Q2 results on December 19, 2025 (timing precedent) - https://secure.businesswire.com/news/home/20251119447876/en/Lamb-Weston-to-Announce-Fiscal-Year-2026-Second-Quarter-Financial-Results-on-December-19-2025; NASS Potato Stocks publication page (ESMIS; April 2025 issue: processors used 147M cwt, down 5%) - https://esmis.nal.usda.gov/publication/potato-stocks
- Data sources:
  - USDA NASS Cold Storage monthly TXT/PDF (ESMIS publication pg15bd892; French fries and total frozen potatoes lines) [PROBED 200 (2026-11-11)] https://usda.library.cornell.edu/concern/publications/pg15bd892 (update: monthly, released ~22nd-25th of the following month (Aug 2026 issue on Sept 25, TESTED HERE); history: 1990s to present (REASONED); TXT files per issue)
  - USDA NASS Potato Stocks (ESMIS publication page; Cornell mirror /concern/publications/47429915b) [PROBED 200 (2026-11-11)] https://esmis.nal.usda.gov/publication/potato-stocks (update: December, February, April, June (four issues per season); history: 2010+ visible in search results; longer REASONED)
  - USDA NASS QuickStats API (optional structured pull) - KEY: free key requested by email at https://quickstats.nass.usda.gov/api [KEY] https://quickstats.nass.usda.gov/api/api_GET/?key=DEMO&commodity_desc=POTATOES&year=2026&format=JSON (update: per report; history: decades (per file))

- Cost: $0 (free NASS QuickStats key)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 20%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Industry stocks include competitors' inventory and export staging; contracts reprice annually so single-quarter effects are muted; the oversupply is widely known and in the stock; one name gives only four events a year, too few to test cleanly.
- Red team (reviewer): The frozen-potato oversupply narrative has been the whole Lamb Weston story since 2024 and sits in every sell-side model; LW's customer contracts reprice annually so a one-month industry stock change barely moves the quarter; industry stocks include McCain, Simplot and Cavendish inventory and export staging; and with one name and roughly 27 usable events since the 2016 spin-off, any backtest result is indistinguishable from noise.

## N18 USDA broiler-hatchery placements, weekly federally inspected slaughter and shell-egg price/inventory as protein-processor nowcasts

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Consumer staples: chicken, egg, pork and beef processors. Target: revenue, margin, EPS surprise (nowcast).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Primary test restricted to CALM and PPC, where revenue approximates volume times a USDA price and consensus is thin; VITL dropped from the price leg (premium pasture-raised pricing not tied to the AMS commodity index); HRL, SFD, TSN, SEB moved to a secondary list (hedged, diversified, contract pricing). (2) Blocked APHIS HPAI feed replaced with NASS Chickens and Eggs monthly layer inventory (reachable) and the AMS weekly Egg Markets Overview; free AMS MARS API key to be registered for machine-readable history. (3) Price averages are final at quarter end, so lead time is ~25-30 days for CALM (fiscal quarters end late Aug/Nov/Feb/May, reports 30-40 days later) and ~28 for PPC; the 12-day slaughter finalization only matters for the secondary names. (4) Signal_noise to 3, confidence to 25, crowdedness kept at 2 for the two thinly covered names only.
- Mechanism: NASS's weekly Broiler Hatchery report (eggs set, chicks placed) leads broiler slaughter by about seven weeks, so placements through week five of a quarter fix industry chicken supply for that quarter; AMS's weekly Actual Slaughter report gives cattle and hog head counts by day; AMS daily and weekly shell-egg reports give price and inventory. Pilgrim's Pride revenue is roughly industry volume times the AMS chicken cutout; Cal-Maine revenue is layer flock times the AMS egg price, with HPAI flock losses driving price; Smithfield, Hormel and Tyson volumes and spreads follow hog and cattle slaughter and cutouts. The edge is the quarterly average of these series versus consensus revenue, which for CALM, VITL and PPC is set by a few analysts who update infrequently.
- Industry context: Chicken and egg producers are price takers on commodity products with a fixed biological lead: chicks placed today are slaughtered in about seven weeks, and a laying flock lost to HPAI takes months to replace. Quarterly revenue is largely set by USDA-reported prices and volumes before the company reports.
- Measurement window: Weekly (hatchery on Wednesdays for the prior week; slaughter final about 12 days after the week; egg prices daily); the quarter's reading is final about 12 days after quarter end; PPC reports ~28-30 days and CALM ~30-40 days after its fiscal quarter end.. Lead time: 25 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.2%: CALM and PPC primary; VITL, HRL, SFD, TSN, SEB secondary (about 7 names).
- Hypothesis: For CALM and PPC, a quarterly-average AMS National Shell Egg Index (CALM) or AMS chicken cutout (PPC) more than 10% above or below the price implied by final consensus revenue divided by trailing-four-quarter reported volume, measured at the company's fiscal quarter end (~25 days before the release), predicts the direction of the revenue and EPS surprise and the close T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus XLP, controlling for consensus revisions in the prior 30 days, corn and soymeal price moves, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Protein analysts track USDA cutouts, hatchery and egg prices; Expana (Urner Barry) sells egg and chicken quotations (vendor existence confident). Crowded for the large names; for CALM and VITL consensus is thin and stale (REASONED). Priced in: price levels largely; quarterly averages versus stale consensus partially.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Expana / Urner Barry for prices (confident); none from the named list.. Why ours differs: Free USDA series aggregated to each company's fiscal quarter and compared with consensus revenue, with the hatchery lead used to fix supply before quarter end; the value is concentrated in thinly covered names rather than in new data.
- Literature found by reviewer: USDA NASS Broiler Hatchery weekly (week ending Oct 31, 2026: 246M eggs set, cumulative placements 8.40B, +2%) - https://esmis.nal.usda.gov/sites/default/release-files/796138/brls4426.pdf; The Poultry Site: Cal-Maine fiscal Q3 2026 revenue $667M vs $642M consensus, EPS $1.06 vs $0.80 (consensus-staleness evidence) - https://www.thepoultrysite.com/news/2026/04/cal-maine-q3-profit-falls-but-beats-expectations; 247wallst CALM earnings-surprise history - https://247wallst.com/companies/calm/earnings/trends
