- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): The series are public and widely used; large names hedge and have contract pricing; feed cost and mix dominate margins; CALM's stock often prices the egg cycle well before earnings and the announcement-day move is driven by dividend and guidance commentary.
- Red team (reviewer): The USDA price series are the most-watched numbers in protein and Cal-Maine's stock tracks the egg price through the quarter, so the earnings-day move is driven by the dividend formula and guidance tone rather than a revenue surprise anyone can compute; recent CALM beats may reflect specialty-egg mix and formula-priced contracts rather than consensus staleness, and Pilgrim's has Mexico, Europe and prepared foods diluting the cutout mapping.

## N19 Snowpack, streamflow and water-supply forecasts as a hydro-generation and net-power-cost nowcast for Northwest and Mountain utilities

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Utilities: hydro-heavy regulated electrics in the Pacific Northwest and Northern Rockies. Target: EPS surprise, guidance (both).
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: The content is already in guidance and sell-side models (hydro ranges are re-guided quarterly), the retained EPS exposure after sharing mechanisms is a few cents at most for IDA/AVA/NWE, and only POR (ROE-deadband PCAM) carries meaningful exposure where Mid-C prices rather than inflow drive the surprise; four names and a heavy gauge-to-plant mapping effort for a near-zero expected surprise. Data are reachable and lawful, so this is a mechanism rejection, not a data rejection.
- Mechanism: NRCS SNOTEL snow-water-equivalent, USGS streamflow at the gauges above each utility's own dams and NWRFC water-supply forecasts determine the quarter's hydro output at Idaho Power's Hells Canyon complex, Avista's Spokane and Clark Fork plants, Portland General's Clackamas and Deschutes plants and NorthWestern's Missouri and Madison plants. Low hydro means buying replacement power at Mid-C prices; power-cost adjustment mechanisms pass most of this to customers but each company retains a slice (Idaho Power about 5%; Avista, PGE and NorthWestern deadbands then about 10%, REASONED), so a dry quarter lowers EPS by a computable amount that sell-side updates only after management flags it. The signal is forecast-versus-normal generation for the quarter, valued at Mid-C prices and passed through each company's sharing formula.
- Industry context: Northwest utilities own run-of-river and storage hydro that supplies a third to a half of their energy; output depends on snowpack and runoff timing. Sharing mechanisms pass most power-cost variance to customers, but the retained slice is material relative to quarterly EPS.
- Measurement window: Daily SNOTEL and streamflow; NWRFC water-supply forecasts monthly January-June; the quarter's generation is effectively observed by quarter end and reported ~30 days later. Q2 (runoff) and Q3 (summer) matter most; the forward element is the April-September forecast available by early April.. Lead time: 28 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.4%: IDACORP (IDA), Avista (AVA), Portland General (POR), NorthWestern (NWE); TXNM only via purchased power; about 4-5 names.
- Hypothesis: A deviation of more than 15% between observed quarterly inflow-implied generation at a utility's own hydro plants and the normal assumed in its latest guidance, measured at quarter end (~28 days before the release), predicts the sign of the EPS surprise and the close T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus XLU, controlling for consensus revisions, Mid-C price moves, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Utility analysts ask about hydro each spring and managements give hydro ranges in guidance decks (REASONED); Mid-C power traders model hydro from the same public forecasts. Priced in: largely in guidance by the Q1 call; within-year deviations after guidance are less so.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None from the named list; power-market consultancies model Northwest hydro for traders (REASONED).. Why ours differs: Free, gauge-level mapping to each utility's own plants and translation through each company's specific sharing mechanism to EPS, rather than a regional narrative.
- Literature found by reviewer: IDACORP Q1 2026 call: 2026 hydro forecast cut to 5.5-7.0M MWh on poor snowpack, EPS guidance reaffirmed - https://www.fool.com/earnings/call-transcripts/2026/04/30/idacorp-ida-q1-2026-earnings-transcript/; Avista 10-K: Washington ERM deadband is the first $4.0M of annual power-supply cost variance - https://sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/104918/000095017022001672/ava-20211231.htm; Avista Idaho PCA report noting the 90%/10% customer sharing band in Washington - https://puc.idaho.gov/Fileroom/PublicFiles/ELEC/AVU/AVUE2109/Company/20210730ID PCA Final Report 10.2020.pdf; Missouri PSC exhibit comparing fuel-cost sharing mechanisms incl. PGE PCAM ROE deadband - https://efis.psc.mo.gov/Document/Display/153114
- Data sources:
  - USGS NWIS daily values (streamflow at gauges above each utility's dams; verify site IDs such as Snake River at Hells Canyon Dam, Spokane River at Post Falls, Clackamas River near Estacada, Missouri River at Toston) [DATA] https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv/?sites=13290450&parameterCd=00060&startDT=2015-01-01&format=json (update: daily; history: decades per gauge)
  - NRCS AWDB REST API (SNOTEL snow-water-equivalent by station) [UNPROBED] https://wcc.sc.egov.usda.gov/awdbRestApi/services/v1/data (update: daily; history: 1980s+ for many stations (REASONED))
  - NOAA NWRFC water-supply forecasts [UNPROBED] https://www.nwrfc.noaa.gov/water_supply/ws_forecasts.php (update: monthly January-June, more often in spring (REASONED); history: UNKNOWN)
  - EIA API v2 (EIA-923 monthly plant-level hydro generation for backtests; ~10-week lag) [KEY] https://api.eia.gov/v2/electricity/facility-fuel/data/ (update: monthly; history: 2001+ (REASONED))
  - NOAA NCEI Access Data Service (precipitation and temperature controls) [DATA] https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/access/services/data/v1?dataset=daily-summaries&stations=USW00024131&startDate=2024-01-01&endDate=2024-12-31&format=json (update: daily; history: full period of record)
- Reviewer source checks: https://waterservices.usgs.gov/nwis/dv/?sites=13290450&parameterCd=00060&startD… -> DATA (in working-sources file) Site IDs above each utility's dams still to be verified.; https://wcc.sc.egov.usda.gov/awdbRestApi/services/v1/data -> 400 bare; 200 with parameters (stationTriplets=1082:ID:SNTL&elements=WTEQ&duration=DAILY) returning an empty array (TESTED HERE) Host works; station triplet or parameter format needs correction before use.; https://www.nwrfc.noaa.gov/water_supply/ws_forecasts.php -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Water-supply forecast page loads.; https://api.eia.gov/v2/electricity/facility-fuel/data/ -> KEY (in working-sources file) Plant-level hydro generation, ~10-week lag.; https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/access/services/data/v1?dataset=daily-summaries&stati… -> DATA (in working-sources file) Weather controls.
- Cost: $0 (free EIA key)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 1 / 1. Confidence: 5%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Guidance already embeds spring hydro forecasts and management updates on the Q1 call; the retained share is small and deadbands can zero it; consensus rarely moves for weather so the surprise may be tiny; four names give few events.
- Red team (reviewer): Managements re-guide hydro on every call (IDACORP cut its hydro range on the Q1 2026 call and reaffirmed EPS), the PCA/ERM/PCAM mechanisms leave shareholders roughly 5-10% of the variance outside deadbands, and within a quarter Mid-C prices and load, not inflow, dominate net variable power cost; the signal is a weather proxy that is in guidance before the quarter starts and in consensus by the time inflows are observed.

## N20 FERC Electric Quarterly Report (EQR) wholesale sales filed 30 days after quarter end as a revenue nowcast for merchant generators and small IPPs

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Utilities / independent power: merchant generators, renewable yieldcos, coal-plant owners, retail energy marketers. Target: revenue, EPS surprise (nowcast).
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: Lead time is 0-8 trading days in three quarters of four and negative for late-October reporters; the data omit financial hedges that dominate merchant-generator earnings; extraction requires driving an ASP.NET postback with no stable download URL (ferc.gov itself is 403 from the container); and FERC's March 2026 final rule (Order No. 917) extends the EQR filing window to four months after quarter end once implemented, so the signal has an announced sunset before it could be validated. If ever revisited, only the Q4 filing (Jan 31 deadline vs late-February earnings) for contracted names such as Ormat and Hallador offers a usable window while the old deadline holds.
- Mechanism: Every FERC-jurisdictional public utility and power marketer must file an Electric Quarterly Report within 30 days after quarter end listing each wholesale transaction: MWh, dollars, counterparty, product and delivery point. Summing transaction revenue and volume across a company's filing subsidiaries gives the quarter's wholesale electricity revenue and realized price before the company reports. For merchant and contracted generators whose revenue is mostly wholesale power sales, this is close to a direct read of reported revenue; the gap versus consensus revenue (which is built from forward curves and hedge disclosures) is the edge. Applies to companies with few generating assets and simple structures, not to vertically integrated utilities.
- Industry context: Merchant generators earn revenue by selling MWh into wholesale markets at prices that swing with gas prices and weather, and hedge part of it with financial swaps. The physical sales must be reported transaction by transaction to FERC, whereas the financial hedges are not, so EQR reveals realized physical revenue earlier than the company does.
- Measurement window: Full fiscal quarter (EQR covers all transactions in the calendar quarter). EQR deadlines are Apr 30, Jul 31, Oct 31 and Jan 31 (REASONED from FERC Order 2001 rules), so lead time is 3-10 days for companies reporting in the second week after the deadline and zero or negative for companies that report before the EQR deadline (e.g., late-October reporters).. Lead time: 5 days.
- Universe coverage: ~1-2%: about 10-20 names (REASONED, verify caps): Talen Energy, Ormat Technologies, Clearway Energy, Hallador Energy (single Merom plant), XPLR Infrastructure, Genie Energy; plus wholesale marketing affiliates of mid-cap utilities (Otter Tail, Black Hills, IDACORP, Avista, Portland General, TXNM) where off-system sales margins swing quarters. Vistra, NRG and Constellation are above the cap.
- Hypothesis: An EQR-implied quarterly wholesale revenue (sum of transaction dollars across the seller's filing entities) at least 5% above the final consensus revenue estimate, measured 3-10 days before the earnings release, predicts a positive revenue and EBITDA surprise and a positive close T-1 to close T+1 abnormal return versus XLU, controlling for the quarter's forward power-price change, disclosed hedge ratios, consensus revisions, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: FERC market monitors, state regulators and power-market consultants use EQR routinely (REASONED). Equity sell-side covering IPPs models hedged EBITDA from guidance and forward curves; no evidence they parse EQR transaction files quarterly (UNKNOWN). Priced-in share: low for realized price and volume, moderate for the hedged outcome.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): UNKNOWN. Power-market data platforms probably ingest EQR (REASONED), but none is known to sell an equity-earnings product derived from it.. Why ours differs: Direct, company-level transaction totals filed under FERC rules days before earnings, free; vendors in the alt-data list do not cover wholesale power transactions at all.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Catalyst Cooperative PUDL: FERC Form 920 Electric Quarterly Report (open, analysis-ready version in progress) - https://docs.catalyst.coop/pudl/en/stable/data_sources/ferceqr.html; Systrends EQR filing software (filer tool, not analytics) - https://www.systrends.com/eqr-software
- Literature found by reviewer: FERC Electric Quarterly Reports page: due dates April 30, July 31, October 31, January 31 at 5 p.m. ET (18 CFR 35.10b) - https://www.ferc.gov/industries-data/electric/power-sales-and-markets/electric-quarterly-reports-eqr; McGuireWoods: FERC final rule reforms EQR process and reporting, key takeaways (Order No. 917, March 2026; filing window extended to four months after quarter end once XBRL-CSV is implemented) - https://www.mcguirewoods.com/client-resources/alerts/2026/3/ferc-final-rule-reforms-eqr-process-and-reporting-key-takeaways/; Federal Register 2026-05709: EQR XBRL-CSV final rule - https://www.govinfo.gov/content/pkg/FR-2026-03-24/pdf/2026-05709.pdf; FERC EQR Reassessment staff presentation (bulk CSV/XML by quarter; only the latest filed version is published) - https://ferc.gov/sites/default/files/2020-09/20180605104754-Final-Presentation.pdf; Ormat Q3 2025 results released Nov 3 after close, call Nov 4 (three days after the Oct 31 EQR deadline) - https://www.barchart.com/story/news/35250848/ormat-technologies-inc-to-host-conference-call-announcing-third-quarter-2025-financial-results; Hallador Q3 2025 results released Nov 10 - https://halladorenergy.com/news-events/news/news-details/2025/Hallador-Energy-Company-Reports-Third-Quarter-2025-Financial-and-Operating-Results/default.aspx
- Data sources:
  - FERC EQR Report Viewer (TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: landing page 200, ASP.NET viewer with a Downloads tab for CSV; programmatic extraction path UNPROBED) [UNPROBED] https://eqrreportviewer.ferc.gov/ (update: Quarterly filings, due 30 days after quarter end; refiled/amended filings appear later; history: EQR data exist from 2002; current viewer format from 2013 (REASONED))
  - FERC eLibrary search (in working-sources file) for EQR-related and rate filings [DATA] https://elibrary.ferc.gov/eLibrary/search (update: Continuous; history: Decades (UNKNOWN exact))
  - EIA Hourly Electric Grid Monitor six-month bulk CSV (in working-sources file) for RTO-level price/load context [DATA] https://www.eia.gov/electricity/gridmonitor/sixMonthFiles/EIA930_BALANCE_2025_Jan_Jun.csv (update: Hourly data, files refreshed periodically; history: 2018 onward (REASONED))
- Reviewer source checks: https://eqrreportviewer.ferc.gov/ -> 200 (TESTED HERE) ASP.NET viewer; Downloads tab is a postback (no stable download URL; /Download …; https://www.ferc.gov/industries-data/electric/power-sales-and-markets/electric-… -> 403 (TESTED HERE) ferc.gov blocked from the container; eqrdds.ferc.gov also 403.; https://elibrary.ferc.gov/eLibrary/search -> DATA (in working-sources file) Not the EQR data path.; https://www.eia.gov/electricity/gridmonitor/sixMonthFiles/EIA930_BALANCE_2025_J… -> DATA (in working-sources file) Load context only; no prices.
- Cost: $0 (FERC data is public; some scripting or a headless browser to drive the viewer's CSV export)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 5%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Financial hedges (swaps, not in EQR) can offset most of the spot-revenue variance, so EQR revenue is not reported revenue; consensus for IPPs keys off guided hedged EBITDA; amended or late filings and multi-entity structures make the mapping noisy; and for late-October reporters the EQR arrives after earnings.
- Red team (reviewer): EQR reports physical wholesale transactions only; the financial hedge settlements that determine an IPP's reported revenue and EBITDA are absent and sell-side consensus keys off guided hedged EBITDA, so the EQR-versus-consensus gap is mostly hedge noise; filings cluster on the deadline day, which is one to three trading days before Ormat and Talen and after Clearway, XPLR and Portland General, and FERC Order No. 917 moves the deadline to four months after quarter end once the XBRL-CSV system is live, making the lead time permanently negative.

## N21 MSHA closure orders and quarterly Form 7000-2 hours/tonnage by controller as a production nowcast for miners and aggregates producers

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Materials and energy: coal (met and thermal), silver/gold, rare earths, potash, lime, frac sand, aggregates and cement. Target: revenue (volumes), EPS surprise, guidance (both).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Timing fix: MSHA's own page says the 7000-2 quarterly file posts on the first Friday of the second month after quarter end (Nov 6 for Q3 2026), which is AFTER many of the listed companies report (Peabody, Minerals Technologies, Eagle Materials, US Lime typically late October; REASONED from usual dates). The candidate's claim of 0-7 days lead is therefore look-ahead for roughly half the names; the 7000-2 leg is demoted to a secondary check used only when the posting date precedes the release by at least 1 trading day, verified per firm-quarter from the file's last-modified history. (2) The weekly Violations file (closure orders with issue and termination dates) becomes the primary, intra-quarter signal; revised lead time 10 days reflects the orders leg. (3) Dropped EIA-923 (2-month lag, needs a key). (4) signal_noise lowered 4 to 3: tons mined differ from tons sold, met-coal earnings are price/cost driven, aggregates hours are weather driven. (5) crowdedness lowered 4 to 3: MSHA's Mine Data Retrieval System is public and mining sell-side reads it. (6) Phase 1 must first count qualifying orders at >=20%-of-production mines across the ~25 names since 2015; if fewer than ~40 events, fold the orders flag into the N27-style event-flag composite instead of trading it alone. API keys needed: none (all MSHA files are keyless).
- Mechanism: Every US mine operator must file MSHA Form 7000-2 within 30 days after each quarter with employee-hours and (for coal) tons produced per mine, and MSHA publishes the quarterly dataset weekly as filings arrive, along with every citation and order. Section 103(k), 104(d) and 107(a) orders withdraw miners from a section or mine until abated, which directly cuts tons in the quarter. Summing hours worked (metal/nonmetal) or tons (coal) across a listed controller's mines and comparing with the prior-year quarter and guided volumes gives a physical production nowcast; closure orders issued mid-quarter flag shortfalls earlier. Applies to companies whose revenue is volume-driven from a handful of US mines.
- Industry context: Mining revenue is tons times price; price is observable daily but tons shipped from a given mine are not disclosed until earnings, and a longwall move, roof fall or regulator closure can remove weeks of output. MSHA makes operators report hours and tons for every mine and publishes every enforcement order, so the physical side of the quarter is on the public record before the company speaks.
- Measurement window: Quarterly 7000-2 data: TESTED HERE 2026-11-11, MinesProdQuarterly.zip (last-modified 2026-11-10) already held 22,433 rows for 2026Q3 versus ~23,100 in a complete quarter (~97%), i.e., the quarter is near-complete about 40 days after quarter end, which is 0-7 days before early/mid-November coal and metals earnings (REASONED from typical dates) and later for companies that report in late November. Orders and violations: issued intra-quarter with issue dates, usable 10-60 days before earnings.. Lead time: 10 days.
- Universe coverage: ~2%: about 20-25 names after excluding Vulcan/Martin Marietta (cap) and verifying caps. The 7000-2 leg applies only to the subset reporting after the first Friday of month 2 (REASONED: for Q3 that is Nov 6; Peabody, Minerals Technologies, Eagle Materials, US Lime typically report before it; Alpha, Warrior, Ramaco, Core, Hallador, Knife River, Coeur, Hecla, MP Materials typically report around or after it). The orders leg applies to all.
- Hypothesis: Primary (intra-quarter, weekly data): a 103(k), 104(d) or 107(a) order issued during the quarter and open for 7+ calendar days at a mine that contributed at least 20% of the controller's prior-year hours (metal/nonmetal) or tons (coal), with the issue date at least 5 trading days before the earnings release, predicts a negative volume/revenue surprise versus consensus and a negative close T-1 to close T+2 abnormal return versus XLE (coal) or XLB (materials), controlling for the quarter's benchmark commodity-price change, consensus revisions, the order-date return, size and momentum. Secondary (only for controllers whose release date falls after the first Friday of the second month after quarter end): a YoY decline of at least 10% in quarterly coal tons or employee-hours summed across the controller's active mines in the posted 7000-2 file predicts the same outcomes.
- Who already knows: Coal and mining sell-side know MSHA data exist and use it mainly for safety and cost commentary (REASONED); companies' own quarterly volume pre-announcements (some met-coal names pre-release sales) partially pre-empt it. No evidence of systematic hours/tons nowcasts at the controller level (UNKNOWN). Priced-in share: low for volumes, but these stocks trade mostly on commodity prices.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None known selling MSHA-derived equity signals (UNKNOWN). Adjacent: Kpler and Vortexa (seaborne coal cargoes, seed 4), AIS vessel queues.. Why ours differs: Mine-level, regulator-filed hours and tonnage with controller names, free, covering domestic aggregates, lime, potash and metals that vessel trackers never see; closure orders give an intra-quarter physical disruption flag with exact dates.
- Vendors found by reviewer: None selling MSHA-derived equity signals in results; MSHA data only republished on data.gov - https://catalog.data.gov/dataset/msha-operator-employment-and-production-data-set-quarterly
- Literature found by reviewer: MSHA Employment/Production (Quarterly) data set page, release timing: 'posted on the first Friday of the second month following the quarter' and 'released between 30 and 45 days after the end of each quarter' - https://www.msha.gov/employment-production-quarterly-data-set; 30 CFR 50.30: Form 7000-2 due within 15 days after quarter close - https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-30/chapter-I/subchapter-I/part-50/subpart-D; MSHA By The Numbers FY1978-2025 (citation/order counts) - https://www.msha.gov/sites/default/files/MSHA-Glance/By%20The%20Numbers%20FY%201978%20-%202025.pdf; Example 10-Q (American Resources) explaining that citations vary with mine size and are often reduced or dismissed on contest - https://capedge.com/filing/1590715/0001477932-24-003175/AREC-10Q-2024Q1/file/7; No academic paper linking MSHA hours/tons or orders to earnings surprises or returns was found (two searches)
- Data sources:
  - MSHA Violations.zip (citations and orders with SECTION_OF_ACT, VIOLATION_ISSUE_DT, TERMINATION_DT, CONTROLLER_NAME) - primary, weekly [DATA (HEAD 200 here 2026-11-11; no key)] https://arlweb.msha.gov/OpenGovernmentData/DataSets/Violations.zip (update: Weekly; history: 2000 onward (REASONED from candidate's definition-file read))
  - MSHA Mines.zip (CURRENT_CONTROLLER_NAME, mine status) for controller-to-ticker mapping [DATA (HEAD 200 here)] https://arlweb.msha.gov/OpenGovernmentData/DataSets/Mines.zip (update: Weekly; history: Current snapshot; ControllerOperatorHistory.zip for history (UNPROBED))
  - MSHA MinesProdQuarterly.zip (Form 7000-2 hours and coal tons) - secondary, late reporters only [DATA (HEAD 200 here; 54 MB; last-modified 2026-11-10)] https://arlweb.msha.gov/OpenGovernmentData/DataSets/MinesProdQuarterly.zip (update: Posted first Friday of the second month after quarter end per MSHA; then weekly fill-in; history: 2000Q1 to 2026Q3)
  - MSHA Open Government Data index [DATA (200 here)] https://arlweb.msha.gov/OpenGovernmentData/OGIMSHA.asp (update: Weekly; history: n/a)
  - MSHA quarterly data set page (official release schedule statement) [DATA (200 here)] https://www.msha.gov/employment-production-quarterly-data-set (update: n/a; history: n/a)
- Reviewer source checks: https://arlweb.msha.gov/OpenGovernmentData/OGIMSHA.asp -> 200 GET, 2026-11-11; https://arlweb.msha.gov/OpenGovernmentData/DataSets/MinesProdQuarterly.zip -> 200 HEAD; content-length 54,381,816; last-modified Tue 10 Nov 2026 13:16 GMT; https://arlweb.msha.gov/OpenGovernmentData/DataSets/Violations.zip -> 200 HEAD (121 MB file, not downloaded); https://arlweb.msha.gov/OpenGovernmentData/DataSets/Mines.zip -> 200 HEAD; https://www.msha.gov/employment-production-quarterly-data-set -> 200 Redirects to msha.gov/data-and-reports/...; page text confirms first-Friday-of-…; https://api.eia.gov/v2/petroleum/sum/sndw/data/?frequency=weekly&data[0]=value&… -> KEY (working-sources file) Not re-probed; dropped from the spec (2-month lag, needs free EIA key)
- Cost: $0
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 3 / 1. Confidence: 30%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Raw tons differ from tons sold (inventory, wash-plant yield) and met-coal earnings are dominated by realized price and freight; the quarterly file may still be incomplete at earnings time for some mines; hours worked are a loose proxy for aggregates output where weather drives both; and hard-rock miners often pre-release quarterly production, leaving little gap versus consensus.
- Red team (reviewer): Closure orders at a mine that matters (>=20% of a controller's output) are rare (a handful per year across ~25 names) and the operator usually discloses them in an 8-K or press release within days, so the earnings-day residual after controlling for the order-day return is likely tiny and the sample too small for a 2-10 day event strategy; and the hours/tons leg is confounded with commodity price and weather, which consensus already embeds.

## N22 NHTSA Part 573 recall filings and quarterly remedy-completion reports as a warranty-accrual nowcast for RV, powersports, truck, EV and component makers

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Consumer discretionary and industrials: RV and powersports OEMs, trailer and specialty-vehicle makers, EV startups, motorcycle makers, and the component suppliers that self-file equipment recalls. Target: margin, EPS surprise, recall (nowcast).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Restricted to self-filing OEMs and equipment makers; dropped Tier-1 attribution (the candidate's own text-match test shows it is too sparse). (2) Replaced the 2%-of-units trigger with a remedy-cost trigger (>=1% of consensus quarterly revenue) built from a CORRECTIVE_ACTION text classifier, because unit counts without a cost class are uninformative. (3) signal_noise lowered 3 to 2 and confidence 20 to 15: per-unit cost is unobservable, late-quarter decisions may be accrued next quarter, and large campaigns are usually quantified by the company on the filing day. (4) Keep because the data is free, daily and timestamped, and the JFR manuscript documents post-recall drift, which is the only literature hint that recall information is absorbed with a lag; Phase 1 should first count qualifying firm-quarters since 2015 and abandon if under ~60. (5) Add the complaints file as a pre-recall feature. API keys needed: none.
- Mechanism: Manufacturers must file a Part 573 defect report with NHTSA within 5 business days of deciding a defect exists, stating the potentially affected unit count (POTAFF) and remedy, and must file quarterly completion reports within 30 days of quarter end with units remedied. A recall decided in the quarter forces a warranty/campaign accrual in that quarter's cost of sales, so the quarter's new campaigns times a per-unit remedy-cost estimate (hardware versus software) nowcasts the accrual, and the completion counts give realized cash cost. For filers in the universe (RV and powersports OEMs, bus and truck makers, EV startups, Lippert-type equipment suppliers), the gap versus consensus gross margin is the edge, because sell-side rarely quantifies campaign accruals before the call.
- Industry context: Vehicle and equipment makers must recall and fix defects at their own cost, and accounting rules require the estimated campaign cost to be booked when the defect decision is made, which usually lands inside the quarter before results are reported. The Part 573 filing is the public, time-stamped record of that decision and of how many units it covers.
- Measurement window: Full quarter: each campaign is public within ~5 business days of the defect decision, so the quarter's tally is final about a week after quarter end, 20-30 days before earnings. Quarterly completion reports for quarter Q arrive by ~30 days after quarter end (TESTED HERE: 2026-Q3 reports with submission dates through 2026-11-07), i.e., just before or around earnings.. Lead time: 20 days.
- Universe coverage: ~1.5-2%: about 15-20 self-filing OEMs and self-filing equipment makers (Thor, Winnebago, LCI, Patrick, Polaris, Harley-Davidson, REV Group, Blue Bird, Oshkosh, Wabash, Rivian, Lucid, Fox Factory, Goodyear, Dorman, Standard Motor Products; caps to be verified). Tier-1 suppliers whose parts trigger OEM recalls (Dana, American Axle, Visteon, Modine, Allison, Garrett, Phinia) are dropped because the candidate's own test found supplier attribution is sparse.
- Hypothesis: For OEMs that file their own Part 573 reports, a quarter in which newly filed campaigns (defect-decision date inside the quarter) sum to an estimated remedy cost of at least 1% of consensus quarterly revenue, using a per-campaign cost table that classifies CORRECTIVE_ACTION text as software-only, inspect/adjust, or hardware replacement and scales by POTAFF, measured at least 10 trading days before the release, predicts gross margin at least 50 bp below consensus and a negative close T-1 to close T+1 abnormal return versus XLY (XLI for truck/bus/specialty vehicle makers), controlling for the cumulative filing-day returns, consensus revisions, size and momentum. Tier-1 supplier attribution from OEM recalls is excluded.
- Who already knows: Auto and RV sell-side note large recalls when announced; the stock reacts on the recall day (REASONED). Systematic quarterly accrual nowcasts from POTAFF and completion counts are not evident in published research (UNKNOWN). Priced in: the headline, not the quarter's accrual size or its timing.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None known to sell a recall-accrual nowcast (UNKNOWN); Quiver Quant and others publish some government datasets but recall-accrual products are not known.. Why ours differs: Uses the filer's own regulator-mandated unit counts and quarterly completion data with exact timestamps, free, and focuses on sub-$30B OEMs and self-filing suppliers where no vendor coverage exists.
- Vendors found by reviewer: api.ai-analytics.org NHTSA recall completion dataset (73,643 quarterly completion snapshots, CC0 repackaging of the public file) - https://api.ai-analytics.org/datasets/nhtsa-recall-completion; data.gov 'Recalls Data' catalog entry (public source, not a vendor) - https://catalog.data.gov/dataset/recalls-data; No institutional alt-data vendor selling a recall-accrual nowcast appeared in results
- Literature found by reviewer: Journal of Financial Research submitted manuscript: NHTSA consumer complaints predict negative abnormal returns over the three months after a recall decision (post-recall drift) - https://facultystaff.richmond.edu/~akumas/JFR%20submitted%20manuscript%20Full%20Manuscript.pdf; Recall market-reaction studies seen in results (negative average reaction; CEO characteristics; exact attribution of the 2,576-recall 2010-2021 finding not verified) - https://doi.org/10.1007/s11156-019-00866-9 and https://www.informahealthcare.com/doi/abs/10.1080/13571516.2023.2213127; Transportation Journal 2025, recall campaign timing relative to competitors and firm performance (NHTSA 2010-2015) - https://ideas.repec.org/a/wly/transj/v64y2025i2ne70003.html; Kelley School blog on recall herding and the larger stock penalty for leading recalls - https://blog.kelley.iu.edu/?p=2550; No paper linking recall filings to warranty/campaign accrual surprises was found
- Data sources:

- Cost: $0
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 15%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Per-unit remedy cost is unknown and ranges from a free software update to a full component replacement; accruals for late-quarter decisions may fall into the next quarter; OEMs often recover from suppliers; adjusted EPS may exclude large campaigns; and the market has already reacted to the recall headline, so the earnings-day residual may be nil.
- Red team (reviewer): The recall headline and its order of magnitude are priced on the filing day and large campaigns are typically quantified by the company at the time, while the per-unit remedy cost is unobservable, so the estimation error in the accrual proxy is larger than the 50 bp gross-margin threshold being tested; what remains is mostly a proxy for negative news-flow momentum, which the controls should absorb, leaving little earnings-day residual.

## N23 FDA import-alert additions and warning letters naming a company's own foreign plant or its disclosed sole-source supplier

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Healthcare ex-biotech and consumer health: generic and specialty pharma, OTC/consumer health, animal health, medical devices with foreign manufacturing. Target: revenue, guidance (both).
- Seed item: FDA inspection reports and Form 483s, FCC equipment filings, patent and trademark applications
- Verdict: REJECT. Changes: Not kept; see reject_reason for the salvage path (fold warning-letter postings into N27). Reject reason: Three independent kills. (1) Event count: own-plant warning letters or import-alert additions for the ~40 listed names are a handful per year (REASONED from the weekly letter flow, which is dominated by small foreign firms), far too few for a 2-10 day event strategy, and the sell-side and the company's own 8-K or press statement quantify the impact within days, so the earnings-day gap versus consensus is closed before the release. (2) The novel piece, joining import-alert facilities to sole-source suppliers named in 10-Ks, is infeasible: 10-Ks almost never name a supplier's plant address, and the Financial Statement and Notes data sets do not carry facility names, so the join is manual and mostly empty. (3) Data reality: the import-alert pages on accessdata.fda.gov now bounce the container to an abuse-detection page and the FDA Data Dashboard returns 403 without credentials; only the warning-letters list is reachable. Salvage: add 'FDA warning letter posted naming the company' as one more negative-event flag in the N27 composite, sourced from the reachable warning-letters page (no key needed); openFDA key optional.
- Mechanism: FDA adds foreign facilities to import alerts (e.g., IA 66-40 for drug GMP violations, IA 89-xx for devices) which triggers detention without physical examination of every shipment from that plant, and posts warning letters weekly naming the inspected site. If the facility is a universe company's own plant or a sole-source API or component supplier named in its 10-K risk factors, US shipments of the affected products stop within days until a successful re-inspection, cutting the quarter's revenue and often the year's guidance. Sell-side usually only updates estimates when the company discloses the impact at earnings. Applies to firms with concentrated foreign manufacturing (India, China, Europe).
- Industry context: Most generic drugs and many device components are made in a small number of foreign plants, and FDA can stop a plant's products at the border administratively, without a court. Companies tend to disclose the sales impact only when they report, while the regulator's list is public immediately.
- Measurement window: Event-based: the import-alert entry carries a published date (TESTED HERE: IA 66-40 page lists 5,864 'Date Published' entries, most recent sample 06/22/2026) and warning letters are posted weekly with issue dates; typical lead is 1-90 days before the next earnings release.. Lead time: 30 days.
- Universe coverage: ~1%: fewer than 15 universe names have had an own-plant warning letter or import alert in a typical two-year span (REASONED); the supplier-side leg is near-empty because 10-K risk factors rarely name the supplier's facility.
- Hypothesis: Addition of a company's own plant, or of a plant the company's latest 10-K names as a sole or principal supplier, to an FDA import alert (or a warning letter citing that plant) at least 20 days before the earnings release predicts a revenue miss of at least 2% versus final consensus and a negative close T-1 to close T+2 abnormal return versus XLV, controlling for the posting-day return, consensus revisions, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Pharma and device sell-side track warning letters for their covered names and the stock reacts the day a letter is posted (REASONED); import-alert additions, especially for third-party suppliers, get far less attention (REASONED). Regulatory-intelligence vendors such as Redica Systems sell FDA inspection and 483 intelligence (REASONED, not verified) to compliance teams rather than as an earnings signal.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Redica Systems (FDA inspection intelligence, REASONED); none in the brief's alt-data list cover this.. Why ours differs: Joins the import-alert/warning-letter facility list to each universe company's 10-K supplier and plant disclosures (via SEC Financial Statement and Notes datasets) so supplier-side disruptions are captured, free and timestamped.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Redica Systems quality and regulatory intelligence platform (inspection, 483 and enforcement data; import alerts tracked in its roundups; sold to compliance teams) - https://redica.com/what-is-quality-and-regulatory-intelligence/ and https://www.redica.com/fda-483-database; Global Key Solutions warning-letter record pages (republisher) - https://www.globalkeysolutions.net/records/warning_letter/yangzhou-sion-commodity-co-ltd/b79ce0a6-6e37-4385-af54-c34a68063226; The FDA Group newsletter analysing warning letters that triggered import alerts - https://insider.thefdagroup.com/p/inside-3-fda-warning-letters-the
- Literature found by reviewer: Conti, Huskamp, Berndt, NBER w17528, 'The Effect of FDA Advisories on Branded Pharmaceutical Firms' Valuations and Promotion Efforts' (targeted firms fall ~3% in 3 days, ~11% in 5 days) - https://www.nber.org/papers/w17528; Event study of the April 2009 FDA internet-marketing warning letters (SSRN 1780346, J. Medical Marketing 2010) - https://digitalcommons.sacredheart.edu/wcob_fac/301; Atlas compliance blog (practitioner commentary that share prices often drop after warning letters) - https://atlascomplianceblogs.azurewebsites.net/?p=662; Princeton 2026 thesis: limited abnormal returns but higher volatility after FDA actions in biotech (minute data) - https://theses-dissertations.princeton.edu/entities/publication/bd23f089-38e8-4418-98ad-0d470d265e28
- Data sources:
  - FDA Import Alert 66-40 (drug GMP) firm list - TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: 200, 2 MB HTML with per-firm 'Date Published' entries [UNPROBED] https://www.accessdata.fda.gov/cms_ia/importalert_189.html (update: Updated as firms are added/removed (weekly-ish, REASONED); history: Current list with publication dates; removals must be archived by polling)
  - FDA Warning Letters list - TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: 200, 77 KB HTML (paginated table) [UNPROBED] https://www.fda.gov/inspections-compliance-enforcement-and-criminal-investigations/compliance-actions-and-activities/warning-letters (update: Weekly postings; history: Letters from ~2017 on the current site; older on archived pages (REASONED))
  - FDA Data Dashboard inspections/citations API (in working-sources file) for inspection classification (NAI/VAI/OAI) [KEY] https://datadashboard.fda.gov/ora/api/inspectionscitations.json?limit=1 (update: Monthly-ish (REASONED); history: FY2009 onward (REASONED))
