- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 2 / 2. Confidence: 10%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Plants are multi-product and companies keep redundant sites or inventory, so the quarter's revenue hit may be small or deferred; most import-alert entries concern small foreign firms outside the universe; the stock already reacts on posting day; and analysts covering the biggest names do watch warning letters.
- Red team (reviewer): The stock already moves on the posting day (NBER w17528 finds the bulk of the reaction within five days), companies disclose the expected supply impact promptly, and plants are multi-product with inventory buffers, so by earnings day the signal is a stale, already-priced headline rather than a nowcast.

## N24 Proxy-statement (DEF 14A) annual bonus-plan targets versus consensus as a forward guidance-direction signal

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Universe-wide; strongest for mid-caps with a single dominant bonus metric (adjusted EBITDA, revenue, EPS) and wide guidance ranges. Target: guidance, EPS surprise (forward).
- Verdict: REJECT. Changes: Not kept. Reject reason: The mechanism assumes the board target carries information not in consensus, but the literature found says the opposite: boards set bonus targets using analyst forecasts and management guidance (JMAR paper; Chen et al.), and Indjejikian et al. show targets are ratcheted from past performance; so target-minus-consensus is near zero by construction and what remains is metric-definition noise (plan 'adjusted EBITDA' versus consensus EBITDA, discretionary adjustments). Structured target data is already sold to investors by Equilar and ISS Incentive Lab, so the idea fails the brief's vendor test and the free EDGAR version needs an expensive CD&A table parser for ~1,300 proxies a year. The horizon (a forward tilt across three announcements) does not fit a 2-10 day event trade, and the one supportive result (ASU dissertation) concerns realized performance versus target and annual returns, not pre-announcement gaps. Crowdedness revised from 4 to 2.
- Mechanism: The DEF 14A, filed about 90-120 days after fiscal year end, discloses the annual cash-incentive plan's threshold, target and maximum values for the metrics the board chose (adjusted EBITDA, revenue, EPS, free cash flow) plus the prior year's actual achievement and payout percentage. The target is the board-approved internal budget, set with more information than the initial public guidance and usually as a point value rather than a range. A target materially above or below the consensus full-year estimate signals which way guidance and surprises will drift over the next three quarterly announcements; the prior-year payout percentage reveals whether the firm hit plan before the 10-K reconciliations are digested. Applies to any company disclosing numeric targets.
- Industry context: Boards set annual bonus targets from the internal budget after the year has started, and SEC rules force companies to publish those targets in the proxy. Public guidance is a range management chooses to communicate; the bonus target is the number management is actually paid on.
- Measurement window: FY target disclosed in Q1-Q2 proxy season; the signal applies to the following two or three quarterly announcements (30-250 days ahead). Final reading is the proxy filing date (REASONED).. Lead time: 45 days.
- Universe coverage: ~60% of the universe discloses numeric annual-plan targets (REASONED, unverified), but the exploitable gap exists only where the plan metric is defined the same way as the consensus metric, which is a minority.
- Hypothesis: A proxy-disclosed annual target for the primary bonus metric at least 3% above (below) the consensus full-year estimate for the same metric on the proxy filing date, measured at least 30 days before the next earnings release, predicts upward (downward) guidance revisions and positive (negative) close T-1 to close T+1 abnormal returns versus the sector ETF across the following three announcements, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size, momentum and the prior-year payout percentage.
- Who already knows: Compensation consultants, ISS and Glass Lewis read these for pay votes; some fundamental analysts cite bonus targets anecdotally (REASONED). Systematic use as an earnings-direction signal: UNKNOWN; no literature cited here because none can be verified. Priced in: low, since consensus typically anchors on the guidance midpoint rather than the proxy target.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Equilar and ISS sell executive-compensation databases (REASONED) to governance and HR clients, not as an earnings signal (UNKNOWN).. Why ours differs: Reframes a governance disclosure as a point estimate of management's internal plan, compares it to consensus at the filing timestamp, and is free from EDGAR.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Equilar Incentive Plan Analysis (standardized threshold/target/maximum and payout data from proxies) - https://www.equilar.com/incentive-plan-analysis.html; ISS executive compensation raw data / Incentive Lab (sold to investors to analyze targets and goals) - https://issgovernance.com/esg/raw-data/executive-compensation-data
- Literature found by reviewer: Indjejikian, Matejka, Merchant, Van der Stede (2014), 'Earnings targets and annual bonus incentives', The Accounting Review 89(4) 1227-1258 (target setting from past performance; ratcheting) - https://cris.maastrichtuniversity.nl/en/publications/earnings-targets-and-annual-bonus-incentives; 'Analyst Forecasts and Target Setting in Executive Annual Bonus Contracts', Journal of Management Accounting Research (boards use analyst forecasts to set targets) - https://www.sciencegate.app/document/10.2308/jmar-18-075; Wharton working paper (Clara Chen et al.): guidance issued before compensation-committee meetings is more pessimistic; consensus is incrementally associated with the EPS target - https://accounting.wharton.upenn.edu/wp-content/uploads/2018/11/Clara-Chen-paper-UPDATED.pdf; ASU dissertation (Lee): internal earnings relative to bonus targets predict annual stock returns incremental to GAAP/street surprises - https://keep.lib.asu.edu/system/files/c7/Lee_asu_0010E_22725.pdf; Harvard Law corpgov: Annual incentive plan design and trends (Equilar 500 target-setting statistics) - https://corpgov.law.harvard.edu/2025/10/14/annual-incentive-plan-design-and-trends/
- Data sources:
  - EDGAR daily index (in working-sources file) for point-in-time DEF 14A filing timestamps [DATA] https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/daily-index/2025/QTR4/ (update: Daily; history: 1994 onward)
  - EDGAR latest DEF 14A feed (same browse-edgar getcurrent pattern as the working-sources 8-K feed) - TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: 200, 10 entries [DATA] https://www.sec.gov/cgi-bin/browse-edgar?action=getcurrent&type=DEF+14A&count=100&output=atom (update: Rolling latest filings; history: Latest only; backfill from daily index)
  - EDGAR submissions API (in working-sources file) for per-company filing lists [DATA] https://data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK0000320193.json (update: Continuous; history: 2001 onward per company)
  - SEC Financial Statement and Notes data sets (in working-sources file) for pay-versus-performance iXBRL (ecd) tags since 2023 [DATA] https://www.sec.gov/files/dera/data/financial-statement-and-notes-data-sets/2025q3_notes.zip (update: Monthly since 2025; history: 2009q1 onward)
- Reviewer source checks: https://www.sec.gov/cgi-bin/browse-edgar?action=getcurrent&type=DEF+14A&count=1… -> DATA (same getcurrent pattern as working-sources 8-K feed) Not re-probed; https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/daily-index/2025/QTR4/ -> DATA (working-sources file) ; https://data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK0000320193.json -> DATA (working-sources file) ; https://www.sec.gov/files/dera/data/financial-statement-and-notes-data-sets/202… -> DATA (working-sources file) ecd pay-versus-performance tags do not carry forward bonus targets, only realiz…
- Cost: $0 (parsing CD&A tables from HTML needs an extraction script; an LLM pass costs cents per proxy)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 10%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Boards set targets conservatively or aggressively by culture, metrics are non-GAAP with discretionary adjustments, the target predates mid-year shocks, and for many firms the target simply equals the guidance midpoint already in consensus, leaving no gap to trade.
- Red team (reviewer): Targets are set from consensus and guidance, so the 'gap' is a proxy for whichever non-GAAP adjustments the plan uses, not for management's private information.

## N25 JPML monthly pending-action counts per MDL as a litigation-charge nowcast for product-liability defendants

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Healthcare devices, chemicals, consumer products: companies that are primary defendants in a federal multidistrict litigation. Target: EPS surprise, distress, margin (both).
- Verdict: REJECT. Changes: Not kept. Reject reason: The accounting link is the weak point: under ASC 450 companies accrue only when a loss is probable and estimable, which in mass torts happens at a settlement framework or adverse bellwether, not when monthly filings rise; defense costs are small relative to SG&A and adjusted EPS excludes litigation charges, so the hypothesised 'legal-expense increase of at least 10%' is not a consensus-traded number. The monthly count conflates new filings with dismissals, remands and settlements (the Roundup MDL fell from 3,925 to 1,104 between two 2026 reports with no stated reason), so a 25% move is not a filing surge. Filing surges are driven by plaintiff advertising and are followed in real time by the mass-tort bar and event-driven desks (crowdedness revised 4 to 3). The on-site archive begins late 2024 and the Stanford mirror fails TLS through the proxy, leaving too little history to test. Universe coverage is ~1%, so even a true effect yields a few trades a year.
- Mechanism: The Judicial Panel on Multidistrict Litigation publishes a monthly PDF listing every MDL with the number of pending actions. For a company defending a product-liability MDL, the month-over-month change in pending actions drives the litigation accrual, defense-cost run-rate and settlement probability that will show up in the quarter's SG&A, 'other' charges or reserve updates, and sometimes in guidance. Consensus rarely models these numbers, so a surge in new filings during the quarter predicts a charge or elevated legal expense at earnings, or a settlement announcement timed with results. Applies to names such as Solventum (Bair Hugger MDL), Cooper Companies (Paragard MDL), Chemours (AFFF MDL co-defendant), Zimmer Biomet (M/L Taper MDL) and Indivior (Suboxone film MDL) (REASONED list, verify current MDLs).
- Industry context: Federal product-liability suits against one defendant are consolidated into an MDL, and plaintiff firms file in waves driven by advertising and bellwether outcomes. Companies must accrue when a loss becomes probable and estimable and must disclose reasonably possible losses, so filing surges translate into quarterly charges and footnote changes that the JPML count anticipates.
- Measurement window: Monthly report dated the 1st-15th of each month and published within days (TESTED HERE: November 2, 2026 report posted; archive holds 22 monthly reports from late 2024 to 2026). The last report before earnings covers filings through roughly the end of the quarter, 20-40 days before the release.. Lead time: 25 days.
- Universe coverage: ~1%: roughly 8-15 universe names are primary defendants in an active product-liability MDL at any time (REASONED); several named examples (Solventum, Zimmer Biomet) need cap verification.
- Hypothesis: A month-over-month increase of at least 25% (and at least 200 actions) in pending actions in an MDL where the company is a primary defendant, measured at least 20 days before the earnings release, predicts a litigation-related charge or a legal-expense increase of at least 10% versus the prior quarter and a negative close T-1 to close T+1 abnormal return versus the sector ETF, controlling for returns on prior litigation-news days, consensus revisions, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: The mass-tort bar, litigation funders and event-driven desks follow large MDLs closely; the sell-side mentions them qualitatively (REASONED). Monthly count-based accrual nowcasts for mid-cap defendants: UNKNOWN. Priced in: the existence of the litigation, not the quarter's filing surge or its accounting timing.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Lex Machina (LexisNexis) sells litigation analytics to law firms (REASONED); none of the brief's alt-data vendors sell MDL counts as an equity signal.. Why ours differs: Uses the court system's own monthly tally per MDL, free, mapped to defendants in the universe, with a focus on the accounting consequence inside the quarter rather than the long-run settlement value.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Lex Machina (LexisNexis) product liability and torts analytics, integrates JPML MDL counts; sold to law firms, insurers and in-house counsel, no investor product seen - https://lexmachina.com/?p=16834; OpenClassActions monthly MDL report summaries (free practitioner site) - https://openclassactions.com/news/mdl-report-october-2026.php
- Literature found by reviewer: JPML pending MDL reports page (uploaded first business day of each month since Jan 2024) - https://www.jpml.uscourts.gov/pending-mdls-0; Stanford Law securities clearinghouse JPML reports mirror (older monthly PDFs) - https://clp.law.stanford.edu/jpml-reports/; No paper or practitioner report linking monthly MDL pending-action counts to defendant earnings, accruals or returns was found
- Data sources:
  - JPML pending MDL dockets page with links to the current monthly PDFs - TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: 200 [UNPROBED] https://www.jpml.uscourts.gov/pending-mdls-0 (update: Monthly; history: Current month on this page)
  - JPML monthly 'Pending MDL Dockets by Actions Pending' PDF (example current file) - TESTED HERE: 200, 480 KB PDF [UNPROBED] https://www.jpml.uscourts.gov/sites/jpml/files/Pending_MDL_Dockets_By_Actions_Pending-November-2-2026.pdf (update: Monthly; history: n/a (single report))
  - JPML pending MDL reports archive - TESTED HERE: 22 monthly PDFs (1 from 2024, 11 from 2025, 10 from 2026); older reports would need other archives (Wayback is blocked from the container) [UNPROBED] https://www.jpml.uscourts.gov/pending-mdl-reports-archive (update: Monthly; history: Late 2024 to present on the site)
  - CourtListener search API v4 / RECAP (in working-sources file) for docket-level filing dates and bellwether schedules [KEY] https://www.courtlistener.com/api/rest/v4/search/?q=%22unpaid%20invoices%22&type=r&order_by=dateFiled%20desc (update: Continuous; history: RECAP archive (varies by court))
- Reviewer source checks: https://www.jpml.uscourts.gov/pending-mdls-0 -> 200 ; https://www.jpml.uscourts.gov/sites/jpml/files/Pending_MDL_Dockets_By_Actions_P… -> 200 ; https://www.jpml.uscourts.gov/pending-mdl-reports-archive -> 200 Site archive starts late 2024; https://clp.law.stanford.edu/jpml-reports/ -> 000 curl 60 TLS: unable to get local issuer certificate through the proxy; older re…; https://www.courtlistener.com/api/rest/v4/search/?q=%22unpaid%20invoices%22&typ… -> KEY (working-sources file) Free token; not needed if rejected
- Cost: $0 (free CourtListener token for docket detail; PDF table extraction with pdfplumber)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 1 / 1. Confidence: 8%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Companies often accrue nothing until a settlement framework exists, adjusted EPS excludes litigation charges, filing surges reflect plaintiff marketing rather than merit, and the stock reaction is concentrated on bellwether verdicts and settlement announcements rather than on earnings day.
- Red team (reviewer): Litigation risk for these names is priced on bellwether verdicts and settlement announcements, not on earnings day, and the count series is a noisy mixture of plaintiff marketing and docket housekeeping with no reliable path into the quarter's reported numbers.

## N26 openFDA MAUDE device-event report velocity per manufacturer and product code as a recall and quality-cost lead

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Healthcare devices ex-biotech: insulin pumps and CGMs, cardiovascular and neuro devices, orthopedic implants, surgical consumables. Target: recall, margin, revenue (both).
- Seed item: Product review velocity (rate of new reviews) and defect-complaint spikes preceding recalls
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Count only injury/death event types: malfunction reports for many product codes arrive in quarterly summary batches under FDA's Voluntary Malfunction Summary Reporting program (REASONED), which manufactures fake 'spikes' in date_received; the Insulet series cited by the candidate (4,834 in Jan to 11,236 in Jul) is consistent with batch reporting plus installed-base growth, not a quality break. (2) Normalise by installed base where disclosed, else segment revenue. (3) Demote to a conditioning flag; signal_noise lowered 3 to 2 and confidence 20 to 15 because the literature supports recall prediction (West J Emerg Med 2025; GWU thesis) but nothing supports an earnings-day channel, and manufacturers file roughly a third of MDRs late (claim seen only in a search summary without a captured URL, so treat as UNVERIFIED), which erodes the months-1-2 completeness assumption. (4) Drop low-volume names. API keys needed: none required; optional free openFDA key (api.fda.gov) for higher rate limits.
- Mechanism: Device makers must file medical device reports (MDRs) with FDA within 30 days of learning of a malfunction, injury or death, and openFDA republishes them weekly with manufacturer, product code, event type and date received. A sustained rise in monthly MDR counts for a company's key product code, normalized by installed base or revenue, precedes field actions, Class I/II recalls, elevated warranty and remediation costs and, for consumables, lower utilization or share loss. The edge is that the sell-side reacts to the recall announcement, not to the report velocity that precedes it. Applies to companies with one or two dominant device families.
- Industry context: Device companies carry warranty, field-correction and remediation costs and can lose share quickly when a product acquires a safety reputation. Federal law makes every malfunction report public with a short lag, so quality deterioration is visible months before the company's financials or a recall notice show it.
- Measurement window: Months 1-2 of the quarter are substantially complete (30-day MDR reporting lag plus ~1-week openFDA publication lag: TESTED HERE 2026-11-11 last_updated 2026-11-04 with date_received through 2026-10-30); month 3 is only partial at earnings time.. Lead time: 14 days.
- Universe coverage: ~2-3%: about 25-35 medtech names with enough monthly MDR volume (at least ~50 reports a month in the top product code) for a 50% change to be meaningful; low-volume names (LeMaitre, Treace, OrthoPediatrics, Pulmonx, Tactile) drop out.
- Hypothesis: For device makers with one or two dominant product codes, a rise of at least 50% in monthly injury-or-death MDRs (event_type excluding malfunction, to avoid summary-reported malfunction batches) for the top product code over months 1-2 of the quarter, per unit of trailing installed base or trailing-12-month segment revenue, versus the trailing-four-quarter mean and measured at least 14 days before the release, predicts (a) a Class I or II recall or FDA safety communication for that product within 90 days and (b) a negative close T-1 to close T+2 abnormal return versus XLV at the next earnings release, controlling for revenue growth, consensus revisions, size, momentum and any recall-day return. Use primarily as a conditioning flag for sizing put/debit-spread exposure, not as a standalone entry.
- Who already knows: Medtech sell-side cites MAUDE spikes anecdotally for specific issues; quality and regulatory consultants monitor it for clients (REASONED). No systematic earnings-signal use is known (UNKNOWN). Priced in: low until a recall or FDA letter makes headlines.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None known to sell MAUDE-derived equity signals (UNKNOWN); none of the brief's listed vendors cover it.. Why ours differs: Direct regulator-mandated event counts at product-code granularity, free and weekly, versus consumer review scraping (seed 24) that does not exist for prescription devices.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Neudata, 'Medtech for investors: an alternative data perspective' (adjacent vendor coverage, not MAUDE-derived) - https://www.neudata.co/intelligence/medtech-for-investors-an-alternative-data-perspective; YipitData medtech offering (hospital purchasing data for investors) - https://www.yipitdata.com/investor/medtech-investors; Apify openFDA device-event scraper (tooling, not a signal vendor) - https://apify.com/parseforge/openfda-device-event-scraper.md; Innolitics MAUDE alerts tool - https://innolitics.com/portfolio/maude-alerts/index.md; No vendor selling a MAUDE-derived equity signal appeared in results
- Literature found by reviewer: Barbosa Slivinskis, Agi Maluli, Broder (2025), 'A Machine Learning Algorithm to Predict Medical Device Recall by the FDA', West J Emerg Med 26(1) 161-170 (recall prediction with lead times up to 12 months) - https://www.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/pmc/articles/PMC11908527/; GWU thesis: classifiers on MAUDE report text forecast recall severity for cardiac implantable electronic devices - https://scholarspace.library.gwu.edu/concern/gw_etds/kk91fm55c; UCLA Anderson Review: how device makers reacted to publication of the once-hidden alternative summary reporting database - https://anderson-review.ucla.edu/how-medical-device-makers-reacted-to-publication-of-a-once-secret-database-of-millions-of-adverse-events; openFDA device event endpoint documentation (weekly updates; MAUDE caveats) - https://open.fda.gov/apis/device/event
- Data sources:
  - openFDA device adverse-event (MAUDE) endpoint, count by date_received and event_type, filtered by manufacturer and product code [DATA (200 here with curl -g; optional free openFDA key raises rate limits)] https://api.fda.gov/device/event.json?search=device.manufacturer_d_name:INSULET+AND+date_received:[20260101+TO+20261231]&count=date_received (update: Weekly; history: 1991 onward per openFDA (REASONED))
  - openFDA device recalls (outcome label) [DATA (working-sources file)] https://api.fda.gov/device/recall.json?limit=1 (update: Weekly; history: ~59,705 records)
  - openFDA 510(k) endpoint for product-code mapping [DATA (200 here)] https://api.fda.gov/device/510k.json?limit=1 (update: Weekly; history: 1976 onward (REASONED))
- Reviewer source checks: https://api.fda.gov/device/event.json?search=device.manufacturer_d_name:INSULET… -> 200 Needs curl -g (brackets); first attempt failed on curl globbing, not on the ser…; https://api.fda.gov/device/510k.json?limit=1 -> 200 ; https://api.fda.gov/device/recall.json?limit=1 -> DATA (working-sources file) Not re-probed
- Cost: $0 (optional free openFDA API key for higher rate limits)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 15%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): MDR counts scale with installed base and with reporting-policy changes (summary reporting, literature reports, a single root cause generating thousands of duplicate reports), so Insulet-style increases may reflect growth or a known, already-disclosed issue rather than new quality costs; and recall-day moves may absorb the entire effect.
- Red team (reviewer): MDR velocity is dominated by reporting mechanics (installed-base growth, summary-reporting batches, duplicate reports from a single root cause, litigation-driven filings), and the cost channel into the quarter's gross margin is negligible until a recall, which is then priced on its own announcement day, so the earnings-day residual is likely zero and any apparent effect is a proxy for high-growth names with rising installed bases.

## N27 Mid-quarter non-earnings 8-K items (2.04, 2.05, 2.06, 3.01, 4.01, 4.02, 5.02 finance-officer departures) as a universe-wide negative-surprise flag

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Universe-wide; most informative for small caps with thin analyst coverage. Target: EPS surprise, guidance, distress (nowcast).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Split the composite: 2.05 and 2.06 lose their negative sign prior (impairments are non-cash and usually excluded from adjusted EPS; restructurings are often read as positive), and 4.01 is dropped unless a disagreement is disclosed, because routine auditor rotations dominate. (2) 5.02 needs a departure-versus-appointment and CFO/CAO-versus-other text classifier, as the working-sources file already notes; retirements with a named successor are excluded. (3) Added the Audit Analytics finding of positive long-run returns after restatement resolution as an explicit sign risk. (4) Confidence 20 to 25 because the data is free, structured, universe-wide and point-in-time, and the signal doubles as a control for the other kept signals; the earnings-day residual remains the open question. (5) Optional: fold FDA warning-letter postings (reachable page) into the same flag set. API keys needed: none (SEC requires only a declared User-Agent).
- Mechanism: Within four business days of the triggering event, companies must file an 8-K under Item 2.04 (triggering events accelerating obligations), 2.05 (exit or disposal costs), 2.06 (material impairments), 3.01 (delisting notice), 4.01 (auditor change), 4.02 (non-reliance on prior financials) or 5.02 (officer departures, which can be classified as CFO/CAO versus other). These items are structured in the EDGAR submissions API's 'items' field and arrive weeks before the Item 2.02 earnings 8-K. A mid-quarter filing of any of these means the quarter will carry charges, restatement or control risk, or a leadership gap; small-cap consensus revisions lag such filings, so a composite flag predicts negative EPS surprise and a negative earnings-day reaction beyond the filing-day move. Applies to every filer in the universe.
- Industry context: Since 2004 the SEC has required companies to disclose a list of specific corporate events within four business days, long before the quarterly report. Impairments, restructuring charges, restatements and auditor or CFO changes are the events most associated with bad quarters.
- Measurement window: Window from the prior earnings date to 5 trading days before the next scheduled release; the final reading is the last qualifying 8-K acceptance timestamp. Filings are public within 4 business days of the event (SEC rule), typically 10-70 days before earnings.. Lead time: 20 days.
- Universe coverage: 100% of ~1,300 names covered; the negative-flag set (4.02, 2.04, 3.01, CFO/CAO departure) is expected to fire for roughly 2-4% of firm-quarters, i.e., about 25-50 events a quarter (REASONED; 3.01 and 2.04 are rare above the $500M cap).
- Hypothesis: Tested item by item, not as one composite: an 8-K with Item 4.02 (non-reliance), Item 2.04 (acceleration of obligations), Item 3.01 (delisting notice), or a text-classified CFO/CAO departure under Item 5.02 (resignation or termination, excluding retirement with a named successor), accepted between the prior earnings date and 5 trading days before the next scheduled release, predicts a negative EPS surprise versus final consensus and a negative close T-1 to close T+1 abnormal return versus the sector ETF, controlling for the filing-day return, consensus revisions, size, momentum and sector. Items 2.05 and 2.06 are tested separately with no sign prior (restructurings and non-cash impairments are frequently excluded from adjusted EPS and can be read as clean-up). Item 4.01 is excluded unless the filing discloses a disagreement.
- Who already knows: Quant funds and news-analytics vendors parse 8-K items in-house (REASONED), so the filing-day reaction is efficient. LITERATURE: Lerman and Livnat (2010, Review of Accounting Studies, 'The New Form 8-K Disclosures') document significant filing-day reactions to the expanded 8-K items; whether a residual earnings-day effect remains is UNKNOWN and is the testable question. Priced in: filing-day move yes; subsequent consensus adjustment incomplete for thinly covered names (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None of the brief's listed alt-data vendors sell 8-K item signals; news-analytics providers (not named, UNKNOWN) may encode them.. Why ours differs: Free, structured, timestamped and universe-wide; used not as a filing-day trade but as a conditioning flag and control for the other nine signals, with the earnings-day residual as the target.
- Vendors found by reviewer: sec-api.io Form 8-K item search API and Item 4.02 return analysis (data vendor) - https://sec-api.io/resources/stock-price-reactions-to-item-4-02-disclosures-in-sec-form-8-k-filings; Audit Analytics restatement and CEO/CFO departure datasets (widely used data vendor) - https://blog.auditanalytics.com/long-term-capital-market-consequences-of-financial-restatements/ and https://blog.auditanalytics.com/ceo-and-cfo-departures-2016-2018/
- Literature found by reviewer: Lerman and Livnat (2010), 'The New Form 8-K Disclosures', Review of Accounting Studies 15(4) 752-778 (verified; periodic reports keep their information content; good-news 8-Ks get larger event-day reactions than bad news) - https://ideas.repec.org/a/spr/reaccs/v15y2010i4d10.1007_s11142-009-9114-7.html; Audit Analytics: 5,111 non-reliance restatements 2005-2016, negative at announcement but positive long-run abnormal returns up to 3.28% after resolution - https://blog.auditanalytics.com/long-term-capital-market-consequences-of-financial-restatements/; sec-api.io: CARs over 1-20 business days for 8,000+ Item 4.02 filings 2004-2023 - https://sec-api.io/resources/stock-price-reactions-to-item-4-02-disclosures-in-sec-form-8-k-filings; McVay and Rabier, 'How resilient are firms' financial reporting processes to the sudden loss of a CFO', The Accounting Review (adverse reporting events roughly double in the following year) - https://www.citedrive.com/en/discovery/how-resilient-are-firms-financial-reporting-processes-to-the-sudden-loss-of-a-cfo-evidence-from-sudden-deaths; Practitioner report: ~-1% the day after a CFO exit and a further ~-2% over 30 days (methodology unverified) - https://the-cfo.io/2023/12/19/cfo-exit-can-result-in-a-share-drop-of-over-3-research/; Hu et al., Review of Finance 2025: after forced CEO turnover, incoming CEOs release bad near-term earnings news first - https://econpapers.repec.org/RePEc:oup:revfin:v:29:y:2025:i:3:p:887-921.
- Data sources:
  - EDGAR submissions API (items field, acceptanceDateTime) [DATA (working-sources file; declared User-Agent, <=10 req/s)] https://data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK0000320193.json (update: Continuous; history: 8-K item codes since Aug 2004)
  - EDGAR latest 8-K atom feed (intraday polling) [DATA (working-sources file)] https://www.sec.gov/cgi-bin/browse-edgar?action=getcurrent&type=8-K&count=100&output=atom (update: Rolling; history: Latest only)
  - EDGAR daily index (point-in-time backfill) [DATA (working-sources file)] https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/daily-index/2025/QTR4/ (update: Daily; history: 1994 onward)
  - SEC Form 3/4/5 bulk data (insider selling after a flag as a conditioning variable) [DATA (working-sources file)] https://www.sec.gov/files/structureddata/data/insider-transactions-data-sets/2025q2_form345.zip (update: Quarterly; history: 2006q1-2026q3)
  - FDA warning letters list (optional extra negative flag salvaged from N23) [DATA (200 here)] https://www.fda.gov/inspections-compliance-enforcement-and-criminal-investigations/compliance-actions-and-activities/warning-letters (update: Weekly; history: ~2017 onward on current site (REASONED))
- Reviewer source checks: https://data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK0000320193.json -> DATA (working-sources file) items field with 8-K item codes and acceptanceDateTime; https://www.sec.gov/cgi-bin/browse-edgar?action=getcurrent&type=8-K&count=100&o… -> DATA (working-sources file) ; https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/daily-index/2025/QTR4/ -> DATA (working-sources file) ; https://www.sec.gov/files/structureddata/data/insider-transactions-data-sets/20… -> DATA (working-sources file) 
- Cost: $0 (SEC requires only a declared User-Agent and under 10 requests per second)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 25%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Markets price the 8-K at filing and analysts of larger names revise promptly, so the earnings-day residual may be zero or even positive (kitchen-sink quarter followed by relief); the flag is correlated with low momentum and small size, which the controls must absorb; and these filings are rare enough that a 2-10 day event strategy gets few trades per quarter.
- Red team (reviewer): Lerman and Livnat show the filing-day reaction is efficient and Audit Analytics shows post-restatement drift turns positive, so the earnings-day residual after a negative 8-K item may be zero or positive (kitchen-sink quarter followed by relief); any negative result is likely a proxy for small size, low momentum and distress, exactly the factors the controls must absorb.

## N28 Quarter-end unit counts and closure / coming-soon flags from store-locator sitemaps and location pages

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Multi-unit chains: fast-casual and casual restaurants, fitness clubs, car wash, wax/personal-care services, specialty retail (company-operated and franchised). Target: revenue, guidance (both).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Crowdedness lowered 3 to 2: Thinknum, SafeGraph (opened_on/closed_on), ChainXY and Tastewise sell store-count and opening/closure data at scale and sell-side does manual locator counts; the idea survives the brief's vendor test only because the daily, free, quarter-end-exact diff with 'coming soon' flags is cheaper and faster than the vendors' 30-90 day cycles. (2) signal_noise lowered 4 to 3 and the trigger raised to 15%: a one-unit slip is ~0.2-0.5% of quarterly revenue for a 300-unit chain, far below comp-sales noise, so the signal is re-aimed at the unit-count and opening-guide targets rather than the revenue surprise. (3) Legal risk raised 2 to 3 (AMBER): robots.txt files permit crawling and sitemaps exist for crawlers, but site terms of use for restaurant and fitness chains commonly prohibit automated access (REASONED; terms not read here); each target's terms must be checked before the daily job runs, and bot-blocked sites must not be fetched through residential proxies unless terms allow it. (4) Universe narrowed to high-unit-growth names. (5) No backtest is possible without forward collection; Common Crawl gives only coarse monthly backfill, so Phase 1 should start the daily snapshot now and treat the first two quarters as calibration. API keys needed: none; optional paid residential proxy ($10-20/month) only for sites whose terms permit.
- Mechanism: For multi-unit chains reported revenue is units x average unit volume, and the quarter-end system unit count plus net openings are disclosed only at the release. Location pages (Yext/Rio-SEO style locations.<brand>.com or /locations/ paths) are created at or just before opening, often flagged 'coming soon', and are removed or flagged 'permanently closed' at closure. A daily diff of the location sitemap gives net unit adds through the quarter-end day, one day after quarter end, which can be compared with the pro-rated annual opening guide. Opening slippage versus guide implies a revenue shortfall and a unit-growth guidance cut; a spike in removals precedes closure/impairment charges. (REASONED mechanism; TESTED HERE that sitemaps expose per-location pages for Sweetgreen, European Wax Center, Life Time and Wingstop.)
- Industry context: Unit-growth chains are valued on new-unit cadence, and revenue models are built unit by unit, so opening delays are punished at earnings. Location pages are SEO assets created at opening and deleted at closure, so the public web carries the unit roster in near real time.
- Measurement window: Full fiscal quarter, daily snapshots; final reading is the quarter-end-day count (available T+1 after quarter end, typically 25-45 days before the release). 'Coming soon' page count is a forward read on next quarter's openings.. Lead time: 25 days.
- Universe coverage: ~3-4%: about 40-50 names where unit growth is a first-order revenue driver and the locator is reachable (Sweetgreen, Wingstop, European Wax Center, Life Time confirmed; CAVA, Dutch Bros, Shake Shack, Planet Fitness, Mister Car Wash, Five Below, Texas Roadhouse, Boot Barn need a different fetch path). Mature chains with low unit growth (Cheesecake, Brinker, Dine, Wendy's, Papa John's, Bloomin') are dropped.
- Hypothesis: For chains whose unit growth contributes at least a third of consensus revenue growth (new-unit revenue share), a shortfall of at least 15% in net new location pages versus the pro-rated annual opening guide over the fiscal quarter, measured 1 day after quarter end and at least 20 days before the release, predicts (a) a reported quarter-end unit count below the sell-side's modeled count, (b) a cut or no raise to the annual opening guide, and (c) a negative T+1/T+2 abnormal return versus the sector ETF, controlling for consensus revisions, same-store-sales expectations where published, size and momentum. The 'coming soon' page count is tested as a predictor of next quarter's openings.
- Who already knows: Sell-side models unit growth from company guidance and press releases and does occasional manual locator checks; Thinknum has marketed store-location/store-count datasets (REASONED). Placer.ai sells foot traffic, not unit rosters. Consensus assumes the guided opening cadence, so the exploitable gap is intra-quarter slippage in opening timing and unannounced closures, which are rarely priced before the release for sub-$5B names (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Thinknum (store locations/counts; REASONED), Placer.ai (traffic only). Why ours differs: Exact quarter-end cut from daily sitemap diffs, free, with per-page creation/removal dates and 'coming soon'/'temporarily closed' flags; vendors deliver monthly aggregates and do not cover sub-$2B chains well.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Thinknum Store Locations dataset (1,643 public companies, 10y4m coverage) - https://www.thinknum.com/datasets/store; SafeGraph Places with opened_on and closed_on fields (restaurant scorecard) - https://www.safegraph.com/?p=4823; ChainXY openings/closures data via Kalibrate - https://kalibrate.com/insights/blog/location-analytics-101-for-restaurant-brands/; Tastewise Chain Growth Hub (monthly location counts per chain) - https://tastewise.io/solutions/restaurant-chain-growth-data; Apify Retail Store-Locator Expansion Tracker (scraper marketed as an expansion signal) - https://apify.com/xtracto/store-locator-expansion; ScrapeHero store-location datasets - https://www.scrapehero.com/store/?p=57529; Placer.ai (foot traffic, not rosters) - https://www.placer.ai/anchor/articles/restaurant-outlook-2024-year-of-new-location-expansion-plans
- Literature found by reviewer: No academic paper found; open-source precedent: stiles/locations store-locator scrapers on GitHub - https://github.com/stiles/locations
- Data sources:
  - Sweetgreen sitemap (location pages) [DATA (200 here; robots.txt permits)] https://www.sweetgreen.com/sitemap.xml (update: Continuous; snapshot daily; history: None; build forward, Common Crawl for coarse backfill)
  - European Wax Center locations sitemap [DATA (200 here; robots.txt permits)] https://locations.waxcenter.com/sitemap.xml (update: Continuous; snapshot daily; history: None; build forward)
  - Life Time sitemap [DATA (200 here; robots.txt permits)] https://www.lifetime.life/sitemap.xml (update: Continuous; snapshot daily; history: None; build forward)
  - Wingstop locations sitemap index [DATA (200 here; robots.txt permits; filter menu pages)] https://locations.wingstop.com/sitemap.xml (update: Continuous; snapshot daily; history: None; build forward)
  - Common Crawl index (backfill of historical location pages) [DATA (working-sources file)] https://index.commoncrawl.org/collinfo.json (update: Monthly-ish crawls; history: 2008 to Oct 2026, but sitemap coverage per crawl is incomplete (REASONED))
  - Bot-blocked locators (Planet Fitness, Shake Shack, CAVA, Mister Car Wash, Five Below, Texas Roadhouse, Boot Barn) [BLOCKED (403 here); do not proxy around a block unless the site's terms permit automated access] https://www.planetfitness.com/sitemap.xml (update: n/a; history: n/a)
- Reviewer source checks: https://www.sweetgreen.com/sitemap.xml -> 200 robots.txt: no Disallow; sitemap advertised; https://locations.waxcenter.com/sitemap.xml -> 200 robots.txt: no Disallow; sitemap_index advertised; https://www.lifetime.life/sitemap.xml -> 200 robots.txt: Allow / (only /content/lifetime/en_us/ disallowed); sitemap adverti…; https://locations.wingstop.com/sitemap.xml -> 200 robots.txt: no Disallow; sitemap advertised; https://www.planetfitness.com/sitemap.xml -> 403 Bot-blocked from the container (as the candidate stated for 8 sites); https://index.commoncrawl.org/collinfo.json -> DATA (working-sources file) Backfill only
- Cost: $0; daily cron, ~10 MB/day; optional $10-20/month residential proxy only where robots/ToS permit
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 3 / 3. Confidence: 25%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Openings are often pre-announced and 'coming soon' pages tip sell-side; page creation can lead or lag the actual opening by weeks; franchised closures may be removed late; AUV dilution from new units can matter more than count; several target sites bot-block the container.
- Red team (reviewer): Unit-count slippage is a small revenue lever within a quarter compared with comps and AUV, openings are usually pre-announced and visible to sell-side through the same 'coming soon' pages, and web-page creation and removal dates are a noisy proxy for actual opening and closing dates (weeks of lead or lag, franchise lag), so the measured 'shortfall' is mostly timing noise around a number consensus already has within a few units.

## N29 Nationwide in-stock unit counts and model-year mix from OEM-hosted dealer inventory search tools

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Consumer discretionary leisure-vehicle and outdoor-equipment OEMs selling through independent dealers (powersports, RV, marine, standby generators, outdoor power). Target: revenue, margin, guidance (both).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: Dropped the OEM-hosted inventory search tools (endpoints unknown; Polaris and HOG IR bot-blocked; Winnebago path is a dealer-only portal; Rollick shows OEM-site inventory is opt-in). Replaced with dealer-group inventory feeds that are structured and reachable: MarineMax Shopify products.json (TESTED HERE 200 JSON with brand and model year) and Camping World RV listings (200, needs headless rendering). Measured variable becomes the dealer group's own listed new-unit count and prior-model-year share by brand. Targets: HZO and CWH own inventory, gross margin and guidance (in-universe retailers), and BC/MBUU/MCFT (via Sea Ray, Boston Whaler, Malibu, MasterCraft brand counts at MarineMax) and THO/WGO (via Camping World brand counts) wholesale revenue. Lead time set to 20 days (quarter-end reading at T+1, releases 3-5 weeks later). Legal risk 3: Shopify products.json is the same mild undocumented-but-standard flag as the Allbirds row in the working file; Camping World terms unreviewed. Signal_noise lowered to 3 because listing counts mix allocated and in-transit units and seasonality dominates; crowdedness lowered to 3 because the OEMs disclose channel inventory each quarter.
- Mechanism: These OEMs recognise revenue on wholesale shipment to dealers, so quarterly revenue depends on dealers' willingness to take units, which is governed by dealer floor-plan inventory. Several OEMs host nationwide 'find in-stock inventory' searches listing units by dealer, model and model year. A daily count of listed new units, and the share that are prior-model-year, measures channel inventory: rising stock with a rising stale share precedes shipment cuts, promotional support (margin) and guidance cuts; falling stock precedes restocking shipments. The quarter-end reading is available T+1. (REASONED; OEM commentary on dealer inventory is a standard earnings topic, LITERATURE-free.)
- Industry context: Leisure-vehicle OEMs sell to dealers that finance inventory on floor plans; when lots fill, OEMs cut shipments and fund promotions, hitting revenue and margin a quarter before retail demand weakness is visible. Model-year changeovers make stale inventory visible in listings.
- Measurement window: Daily through the quarter; final reading is quarter-end listed units and prior-model-year share, plus the 4-week trend into quarter end.. Lead time: 20 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.6%: about 7-8 names: HZO, CWH (own inventory), BC, MBUU, MCFT, THO, WGO (brand channel stock at the dealer group); ONEW only if its listing site is structured (UNPROBED)
- Hypothesis: A rise of at least 15% quarter-over-quarter in MarineMax-listed (or Camping World-listed) new units of a brand, with the prior-model-year share up at least 5 points, measured at quarter end (at least 20 days before the release), predicts negative revenue and gross-margin surprises and negative T+1/T+2 abnormal returns for the brand's OEM (BC, MBUU, MCFT, THO, WGO) and for the dealer group itself (HZO, CWH), controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: OEMs discuss dealer inventory qualitatively each quarter (Harley gives dealer inventory units; Polaris gives dealer inventory percentage changes) and sell-side runs quarterly dealer surveys/calls (REASONED). Consensus embeds last quarter's commentary, not the intra-quarter path or model-year mix; the gap is the end-of-quarter level versus what management implied (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None confirmed for OEM-site inventory counts (UNKNOWN). RV Trader / Boat Trader are listing marketplaces, not data vendors (REASONED).. Why ours differs: Daily nationwide count with model-year mix, free, versus quarterly dealer surveys with small samples; cross-checked against dealer-group listings.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Constellation Dealer OEM market data (dealer-level inventory positions by category and supplier; monthly market updates for OPE, marine, RV) - https://constellationdealer.com/ope-oem-market-data; Rollick dealer inventory feeds for OEM websites (OEM-site inventory is opt-in: dealers enroll and feeds must be mapped before units appear) - https://rollick.helpjuice.com/73711-leads/dealer-inventory-feeds; Statistical Surveys Inc (retail registration market-share data for marine, RV, powersports; owned by Trader Interactive) - https://rvnews.com/company/statistical-surveys-inc; NMMA Quarterly Powerboat Inventory Report and monthly new powerboat registrations built on SSI data - https://nmma.org/statistics/publications/quarterly-powerboat-inventory-report; Advan Research Polaris dealer traffic note - https://advanresearch.com/insights/polaris---ceo-the-good-news-is-we-havent-seen-it-get-significantly-weaker-relative-to-where-we-were-expecting-a750c; Harley-Davidson IR: company-published worldwide end-of-quarter dealer inventory series - https://investor.harley-davidson.com/resources/Dealer-Inventory/default.aspx
- Literature found by reviewer: No academic paper found in two searches. Practitioner note: Placer.ai 'Big-ticket recreational goods: no fun found here' (foot-traffic vendor blog) - https://anchor.placer.ai/the-anchor/big-ticket-recreational-goods-no-fun-found-here
- Data sources:
  - MarineMax Shopify products.json (TESTED HERE 200 JSON; vendor = brand, model year in title) [DATA] https://www.marinemax.com/products.json?limit=250&page=1 (update: continuous; archive daily; history: snapshot only; build forward)
  - Camping World RV listings (TESTED HERE 200, client-rendered; headless rendering required; robots.txt not served) [UNPROBED] https://rv.campingworld.com/rvs?condition=new (update: continuous; archive daily; history: snapshot only; build forward)
  - Harley-Davidson IR worldwide end-of-quarter dealer inventory (403 from container; use as the disclosed benchmark the signal must beat) [UNPROBED] https://investor.harley-davidson.com/resources/Dealer-Inventory/default.aspx (update: quarterly with the release; history: Q1 2021 onward per search result)

- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 3 / 3. Confidence: 25%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Listings may be opt-in and cover only part of the dealer base; units in transit or allocated may be counted; management already quantifies dealer inventory on calls; seasonality is strong; OEM site terms may prohibit automated access.
- Red team (reviewer): OEM-site 'find inventory' counts are confounded by opt-in dealer feed enrollment (Rollick documents that dealers must enroll and map feeds), so a rise in listed units can be more dealers joining the feed rather than more stock; HOG publishes end-of-quarter dealer inventory and PII quantifies dealer inventory change in every release, and sell-side runs dealer surveys, so the quarter-end level is not news, only its magnitude; dealer-group listing counts (the clean alternative) measure one retailer's merchandising and new/used mix with heavy seasonality and are a proxy for the consumer-discretionary cycle already captured by sector and momentum controls.

## N30 Product-page ship-date and lead-time wording as a backlog gauge for made-to-order durables

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Home furnishings (made-to-order upholstery and case goods), standby generators, EV makers with configurators, premium fitness equipment, build-to-order boats and RVs. Target: revenue, guidance, margin (both).
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: Mechanism has an ambiguous sign (capacity versus demand), fewer than half the listed names expose a numeric lead time at all (Generac none; RH, Lovesac, Arhaus client-rendered or blocked), the two sites whose rendered data comes from API/query paths disallow those paths in robots.txt (Rivian /api/, Lovesac /*? and /rest/), no vendor or paper validates quoted lead times as an earnings predictor, and the series has zero history so no test is possible before 2028.
- Mechanism: Made-to-order and configured goods publish delivery windows ('ships in 8-12 weeks', 'estimated delivery: month') on product pages or configurators because customers must be told when goods arrive. Lengthening quoted lead times mean the order book is growing faster than capacity (revenue recognised later, pricing power up); shortening lead times alongside discounts mean order intake is slowing (next-quarter revenue and guidance risk, under-absorption pressure on margin). A catalogue-weighted lead-time index is read daily and finalised at quarter end, T+1. (REASONED.)
- Industry context: Made-to-order manufacturers reveal backlog through quoted delivery windows, which is the only public forward indicator for these businesses between quarterly reports. Backlog length also drives pricing power and plant absorption, so it maps to both revenue timing and margin.
- Measurement window: Daily through the quarter by product family; quarter-end index level and the change over the final 6 weeks of the quarter.. Lead time: 25 days.
- Universe coverage: ~2%: about 20-25 names: RH, ARHS, LOVE, LZB, ETD, SNBR, Somnigroup/TPX, PTON, GNRC, RIVN, LCID, MBUU, MCFT, WGO, THO (Airstream build-to-order). TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: rh.com product URL 200 but a 2 KB client-rendered shell; lovesac.com/sactionals 200 with no ship-in wording in static HTML; rivian.com/configurations/list?model=R1S 200 containing a 'DeliveryTimingModal' copy key (delivery timing rendered client-side); generac.com home-backup page 200 without static lead-time text; arhaus.com 430 (blocked). So headless rendering is required and exact fields are UNKNOWN until rendered.
- Hypothesis: A shortening of at least 2 weeks in the catalogue-weighted quoted lead time over the final 6 weeks of the quarter, measured 1 day after quarter end (at least 20 days before the release), predicts a negative revenue-guidance change and negative T+1/T+2 abnormal returns for made-to-order durables makers, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Sell-side tracks lead times through channel checks and management commentary (Generac discussed lead times extensively in 2021-22; RH discloses backlog qualitatively) and EV delivery-estimate changes are followed on enthusiast forums (REASONED). No systematic daily tracking for furniture names is known to me (UNKNOWN). Management's last backlog comment is priced; the intra-quarter drift is not.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): None confirmed for lead-time text (UNKNOWN); Edited and DataWeave track price and assortment, not quoted lead times (REASONED). Why ours differs: Daily SKU-level text-derived backlog proxy, free; no vendor packages quoted lead times for small-cap durables.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Potent Pages (web-crawler development shop marketing page 'inventory availability signals: stock-outs, ship times, backorders' for hedge funds) - https://potentpages.com/web-crawler-development/web-crawlers-and-hedge-funds/inventory-availability-signals-stock-outs-ship-times-backorders; ZenRows finance / alternative-data scraping service - https://www.zenrows.com/solutions/finance
- Literature found by reviewer: No academic paper found. Practitioner: Deepwater Asset Management tracked posted online EV lead times (average US Tesla delivery time ~175 days; undated) - https://deepwatermgmt.com/?p=10669; The Truth About Cars (2021): Rivian to add delivery time estimator to configurator - https://thetruthaboutcars.com/2021/11/rivian-to-add-delivery-time-estimator-to-configurator/
- Data sources:
  - Rivian R1S configurator list (TESTED HERE 200, delivery-timing copy client-rendered) [UNPROBED] https://rivian.com/configurations/list?model=R1S (update: continuous; snapshot daily (REASONED); history: none; build forward)
  - RH product pages (TESTED HERE 200, client-rendered shell) [UNPROBED] https://rh.com/us/en/catalog/product/product.jsp?productId=prod17230005 (update: continuous (REASONED); history: none)
  - Lovesac Sactionals page (TESTED HERE 200, client-rendered) [UNPROBED] https://www.lovesac.com/sactionals (update: continuous (REASONED); history: none)
  - Generac home backup power pages (TESTED HERE 200) [UNPROBED] https://www.generac.com/for-homeowners/home-backup-power/ (update: continuous (REASONED); history: none)

- Cost: $0; Playwright headless rendering, roughly 1 GB/month; per-site ToS review
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 4 / 2 / 3. Confidence: 10%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Lead times move with capacity additions rather than demand; many sites show generic windows that rarely change; client-rendered pages mean no backfill (Wayback unreachable, Common Crawl lacks rendered text), so the history must be built forward over several quarters before any test is possible.
- Red team (reviewer): Quoted lead times move with capacity and supply-chain state as often as with demand: Generac's 2021-22 lengthening came with margin pressure, not pricing power, and shortening can mean capacity added rather than orders slowing, so the sign of the mapping to revenue and margin is unstable; and with no backfill possible (Wayback blocked, Common Crawl lacks rendered text) nothing can be tested for two or more years on fewer than ten names that actually display numeric windows.

## N31 Location-level menu and membership price index from public online-ordering and club pages (price component of same-store sales)

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Restaurants, fitness clubs, car-wash subscriptions, wax and personal-care service chains. Target: revenue, margin (nowcast).
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: Institutional vendors sell menu-price tracking at scale and the fund's version is not more informative for the earnings target (price is pre-guided; traffic is the gap); all four primary sources are either robots-disallowed (order.wingstop.com Disallow: /) or bot-blocked (Olo/Shake Shack 403, Planet Fitness 403, Mister Car Wash 403) and the proposed workaround (residential proxies) is a terms conflict, so legal risk is 4; no clean keyless alternative exists at chain level (Toast's free monitor is aggregate).
- Mechanism: Same-store sales decompose into price/mix and traffic, and management guides the pricing it plans to take. Chains publish location-level prices through online-ordering menus and membership pages (gym tiers by club, unlimited-wash plans by site). A daily fixed panel of 100-300 locations yields the realised menu-price change through the quarter; compared with guided pricing it isolates whether an SSS beat is price- or traffic-led, and flags unannounced mid-quarter increases (positive revenue and margin surprise) or value-menu cuts (traffic worry, margin pressure). Final reading at quarter end, T+1. (REASONED.)
- Industry context: Restaurant and membership chains report same-store sales split into price and traffic, and investors treat traffic-led growth as durable and price-led growth as fragile. Online ordering and club pages have made each location's full price book public.
- Measurement window: Daily, fixed location panel; quarter-average year-over-year price change and dated intra-quarter price steps; final at quarter end.. Lead time: 25 days.
- Universe coverage: ~5%: about 60 names: WING, SHAK, SG, CAVA, BROS, TXRH, EAT, CAKE, BLMN, BJRI, JACK, PZZA, DPZ, WEN, DIN, LOCO, PTLO, FWRG, KRUS, NDLS, PBPB, CBRL, DENN; PLNT, LTH, XPOF, EWCZ, MCW, DRVN. TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: order.wingstop.com 200 (41 KB client-rendered shell; menu JSON endpoint UNKNOWN); order.shakeshack.com (Olo-hosted) 403 from container; mistercarwash.com/locations 403; planetfitness.com 403, so a browser or residential fetch is required for several names.
- Hypothesis: An unguided panel-weighted menu or membership price increase of at least 1.5% implemented within the quarter, measured at quarter end (at least 20 days before the release), predicts a positive same-store-sales/revenue surprise and positive T+1/T+2 abnormal returns, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum; value-menu rollouts or price cuts predict a negative margin surprise.
- Who already knows: Sell-side runs periodic menu-price checks; Datassential, Technomic and Revenue Management Solutions sell menu and price tracking to operators (REASONED, fairly confident these vendors exist and sell menu-price data). Management discloses pricing taken after the quarter; consensus assumes guided pricing, so unguided mid-quarter moves are the gap (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Datassential, Technomic, Revenue Management Solutions (menu price tracking sold to operators; REASONED); Placer.ai (traffic). Why ours differs: Daily, location-level, aligned to the fund's fixed panel and fiscal quarter boundaries, free; operator-facing vendors are monthly and expensive and do not publish by ticker.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Technomic Ignite Menu Price Pulse (paid, quarterly competitive menu pricing) - https://www.technomic.com/ignite-menu-price-pulse/; Revenue Management Solutions Competitor Price Intelligence / Restaurant Price Index (up to 170,000 locations) - https://benzinga.com/z/32945017; Datassential AI-powered competitive menu pricing solution - https://www.spectrumequity.com/news/datassential-unveils-unrivaled-ai-powered-competitive-menu-pricing-solution-for-food-and-beverage-companies/; Toast Menu Price Monitor (free monthly item-level median prices from ~140,000 Toast locations; aggregate, not by chain) - https://pos.toasttab.com/th-us/news/toast-launches-menu-price-monitor; MealMe menu and pricing data via Datarade - https://www.datarade.ai/data-providers/mealme/profile; Actowiz Solutions restaurant menu and pricing intelligence (scraping service, single outlet to 50,000 locations) - https://www.actowizsolutions.com/restaurant-menu-pricing-intelligence-multi-location-scale.php
- Literature found by reviewer: MIT thesis: 'Forecasting financials and discovering menu prices with alternative data' - https://dspace.mit.edu/entities/publication/b4edea52-adc0-4bcf-a1df-e52e030eb310

- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Pricing is usually pre-announced on the prior call and checked by many analysts; online menus can carry delivery-channel premiums that differ from in-store; mix shifts dominate the price component; bot-blocking limits the panel and may breach site terms.
- Red team (reviewer): Menu price is the component of same-store sales that management pre-guides and analysts already check, and three vendors (Technomic, RMS, Datassential) sell it at scale; the unobserved component that drives SSS surprises is traffic and mix, which an online menu does not measure (and online menus carry delivery-channel premiums). The panel also cannot be built without circumventing robots.txt disallows and bot-blocks with residential proxies, a terms conflict.

## N32 Customer-side adoption panel: third-party ATS job postings that name a vendor's product as a required skill

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: B2B software and connected-hardware vendors with distinctive product names (construction, fleet telematics, restaurant POS, CRM/marketing, observability, identity, security, vertical SaaS). Target: revenue, guidance (both).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: Restricted the universe to developer-facing infrastructure and security vendors whose customers hire on Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby (DT, GTLB, ESTC, MDB, PD, TWLO, FROG, OKTA, TENB, RPD, QLYS, VRNS, NTNX, PSTG, BRZE, KVYO, ZETA, SPT, SEMR, DOCU); dropped PCOR, IOT, TOST, PAR, ALRM, APPF, GWRE, QTWO, MANH, PTC, BSY, TRMB because contractors, fleets, restaurants and property managers do not post on those ATSs (Workday-hosted boards are a possible later extension, UNPROBED). Added normalization: mention counts divided by total active postings in the fixed panel, to strip the tech-hiring cycle. Retargeted the hypothesis at guidance and net-new-ARR/NRR surprise rather than reported revenue (ratable). Added a disambiguation rule: count only mentions co-occurring with a product-specific token (e.g. 'Datadog APM', 'GitLab CI', 'Elastic/Elasticsearch', 'MongoDB Atlas') to cut generic-name false positives. Crowdedness stays 2 because Lightcast and Thinknum sell the aggregate; the fund's version is cheaper (free), daily, and employer-level, which is the named improvement.
- Mechanism: When an employer posts a role requiring 'experience with Procore / Samsara / Toast / Braze / Dynatrace', the vendor's product is deployed or being deployed there. Public ATS job-board APIs (Greenhouse, Lever, Ashby, SmartRecruiters) return full job descriptions (TESTED HERE: Greenhouse content=true returns the full HTML `content` with first_published and updated_at), so a fixed panel of several thousand employer boards can be scanned daily for product mentions. Distinct new-mentioning employers track net new logos and seat expansion; disappearing mentions track churn; both lead reported customer counts and net revenue retention. Final reading daily, quarter-end T+1. (REASONED.)
- Industry context: B2B software grows through new logos and seat expansion, and employers advertise the tools a hire must already know. Public ATS boards therefore leak which vendors are being deployed before vendors disclose customer counts.
- Measurement window: Daily over the quarter; distinct-employer mention count, new-mention rate and removal rate versus the same quarter a year earlier, within a fixed employer panel.. Lead time: 25 days.
- Universe coverage: ~1.5%: about 20 developer-facing infrastructure and security vendors with distinctive product tokens (DT, GTLB, ESTC, MDB, PD, TWLO, FROG, OKTA, TENB, RPD, QLYS, VRNS, NTNX, PSTG, BRZE, KVYO, ZETA, SPT, SEMR, DOCU)
- Hypothesis: An increase of at least 20% year-over-year in the panel-normalized share of distinct employers newly posting roles that name vendor X's product during the quarter, measured 1 day after quarter end (at least 20 days before the release), predicts a positive guidance change or net-new-ARR/NRR surprise and positive T+1/T+2 abnormal returns for developer-facing infrastructure and security vendors, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size, momentum and total panel posting growth; a rising removal rate predicts the opposite.
- Who already knows: Lightcast, Revelio Labs and LinkUp sell skills-in-postings data and Lightcast's skill taxonomy includes software product names (REASONED, fairly confident); sell-side occasionally cites 'postings mentioning Datadog' from such vendors (REASONED). The aggregate concept is known; employer-level daily churn within a fixed panel is not packaged, and sub-$5B vertical vendors are rarely covered (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Lightcast, Revelio Labs, LinkUp, Thinknum (REASONED). Why ours differs: Daily, free, employer identity preserved so churn can be counted, and a fixed clean panel that is not polluted by vendor coverage changes; incumbents are monthly, expensive and aggregate-only.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Lightcast Investment Support documentation (states demand for specific software tools in postings is a direct indicator of technology adoption) - https://docs.lightcast.io/lightcast-api/docs/investment-support; Lightcast Job Posting Analytics methodology (skills extracted per posting) - https://docs.lightcast.io/data/docs/job-posting-analytics-jpa-methodology; Revelio Labs job posting data and skills-demand analysis - https://www.reveliolabs.com/blog/job-posting-data-trends; Thinknum job listings dataset on Snowflake Marketplace (~10,000 companies, keyword filterable) - https://www.snowflake.com/?p=84428; TheirStack technographics from job postings (company tech stack pages, e.g. Datadog) - https://theirstack.com/en/company/datadog~dTh8RWNC4JT3wS8uwrjrNw==; jobspipe.dev 'companies using Datadog' from postings - https://jobspipe.dev/companies-using/datadog; O*NET Hot Technologies: free annual count of US postings mentioning each software (Lightcast-sourced; 46.9M postings in 2025) - https://onetonline.org/search/hot_tech/; TheDataCity 'company job posting by software skill' (Lightcast-sourced) - https://docs.thedatacity.com/data-dictionary/company-job-posting-by-software-skill
- Literature found by reviewer: No academic paper found linking product mentions in postings to earnings surprises. Practitioner guide: 'Detect Tech Stack Changes from Job Postings: A Sales Intelligence Guide' - https://runfieldwork-com.pages.dev/blog/tech-stack-changes-from-job-postings/
- Data sources:
  - Greenhouse Job Board API with full content (TESTED HERE 200, 61 jobs with ~8.9k-char content) [DATA] https://boards-api.greenhouse.io/v1/boards/duolingo/jobs?content=true (update: continuous; poll daily; history: current postings only (first_published present); build forward)

- Cost: $0; roughly 5,000 polite requests per day
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 25%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Mentions lag deployment by quarters; Greenhouse/Lever panels skew to tech start-ups rather than the contractors and restaurants that buy Procore or Toast; generic names ('Box', 'Toast') create false positives; the aggregate is already sold to institutions.
- Red team (reviewer): Distinct-employer mention counts are a proxy for the tech-hiring cycle (Greenhouse/Lever/Ashby panels are start-ups and tech firms, so mentions rise and fall with sector hiring and sector momentum) and lag deployment by quarters; SaaS revenue is ratable and mostly known from RPO, so any residual information is about net-new ARR and NRR, which Lightcast already sells to investors as a product-adoption indicator.

## N33 Apple App Store userRatingCount velocity and release cadence for transaction-prompted apps of chains, airlines and service companies

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Restaurants, fitness, car wash, airlines and travel, consumer-health/telehealth, specialty retail and marketplaces (not venture-style consumer tech). Target: revenue, margin (nowcast).
- Seed item: [digital] App downloads, store rankings, and web-traffic trends for consumer tech
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: Reframed the measured quantity as new-rater velocity (a proxy for digital-customer growth), not transactions; target changed from same-store sales to revenue/digital-mix surprise. Collection protocol: fetch each app at least three times per day and keep the daily maximum, difference weekly not daily, and flag any decline as a removal/cache event. Restricted the panel to apps with at least 100,000 cumulative US ratings so weekly increments are in the hundreds or thousands (drops Mister Car Wash at ~5,400 and similar small apps). Added version-release and prompt-policy dummies (release dates from currentVersionReleaseDate) and a rating-average dip flag for outages. Noted AppGoblin as a possible free backfill of weekly ratings history (UNPROBED). Lead time kept at 25 days. Crowdedness stays 2; signal_noise 2; confidence 20 because the mechanism maps to users, not spend.
- Mechanism: Apple's lookup and search API returns cumulative `userRatingCount`, `averageUserRating`, `version` and `currentVersionReleaseDate` for any app. Apps that prompt for a rating after a transaction (order, check-in, wash, flight) accrue ratings roughly in proportion to transacting users, so the seasonally adjusted daily first difference nowcasts transaction and active-member growth through the quarter. A collapse in release cadence signals engineering cuts, and a dip in the rating average signals app outages that depress digital sales. Final reading T+1 after quarter end. (REASONED; TESTED HERE that counters and version dates are exposed.)
- Industry context: Chains push ordering and loyalty through their apps and prompt for ratings after transactions, so rating accrual tracks digital transaction volume. Digital mix is now a large share of sales at many fast-casual chains, so app health maps closely to revenue.
- Measurement window: Daily cumulative counts archived; quarter sum of daily increments versus the same quarter a year earlier; days between version releases; final at quarter end.. Lead time: 25 days.
- Universe coverage: ~4%: about 50 names whose transaction apps have at least 100,000 US ratings (WING, BROS, PLNT, SG, JBLU-type airlines in universe such as ALK, ALGT, ULCC, SNCY; SHAK, CAVA, TXRH, LTH, HIMS, FIVE, OLLI, ASO, BOOT, SFM, CHWY, ETSY, W, CART, BMBL, GRND; verify each count before inclusion)
- Hypothesis: A decline of at least 15% year-over-year in the quarter's net new US App Store ratings (weekly increments of the daily-maximum counter) for a chain's ordering or loyalty app with at least 100,000 cumulative ratings, measured 1 day after quarter end (at least 20 days before the release), predicts a negative revenue or digital-sales surprise and a negative T+1/T+2 abnormal return, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size, momentum, version releases and prompt-policy changes.
- Who already knows: Sensor Tower, data.ai and Apptopia sell download and MAU estimates at scale for all these apps and sell-side cites them; the rating counter itself is a trivial public number that some quant shops likely archive (REASONED). For large consumer-tech apps this is priced; for small chains and regional airlines whose apps are modelled poorly by panels the gap is plausible (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Sensor Tower, data.ai, Apptopia (downloads and MAU estimates at scale). Why ours differs: An exact counter rather than a panel estimate, daily, free, plus version cadence and rating-average dips for outage detection, focused on sub-$10B chains and airlines rather than consumer-tech names.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Apptopia quantitative investors page (downloads, DAU/MAU estimates; rating counts not mentioned) - https://apptopia.com/en/quantitative-investors; Sensor Tower Store Intelligence (downloads, revenue, active users, rankings; estimates, not counters) - https://sensortower.com/product/store-intelligence; AppGoblin (free third-party tracker showing total rating count, average rating and weekly ratings over time) - https://appgoblin.info/apps/com.sensortower.mobile/trends
- Literature found by reviewer: ICIS 2019 short paper (HKU repository): abnormal monthly app downloads and abnormal daily users predict next-month abnormal returns for 326 public firms - https://repository.hku.hk/bitstream/10722/275443/1/Content.pdf; AEA 2025 conference paper: quarterly app downloads (Sensor Tower) positively predict next quarter's earnings - https://www.aeaweb.org/conference/2025/program/paper/tsKtGb3z; 'Predicting Performance Using Consumer Big Data' - https://www.academia.edu/79532237/Predicting_Performance_Using_Consumer_Big_Data; CFA Digest 2018 summary on customer satisfaction/ratings and returns - https://rpc.cfainstitute.org/research/cfa-digest/2018/07/dig-v48-n7-3; arXiv 2306.12119: customer online satisfaction (e-commerce review ratings) and stock returns in China - https://arxiv.org/pdf/2306.12119v1.pdf
- Data sources:
