- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 20%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Rating prompts are controlled by the company and prompt-frequency changes break the series; major versions can reset displayed counts; vendors' download and MAU estimates already capture engagement; the counter measures users, not spend per user.
- Red team (reviewer): Apple's in-app review prompt is capped per user per year, so the counter accrues per newly prompted user, not per transaction: velocity measures new-user acquisition and prompt policy (loyalty pushes, prompt-frequency changes, optional rating resets at version release), not same-store sales or spend per user; app-download and MAU panels (Sensor Tower, Apptopia) are already in sell-side models, so what remains is a noisy residual; and the counter itself is non-monotonic (TESTED HERE: fell by 12 within a day), so daily first differences carry sign noise.

## N34 State-filed Franchise Disclosure Documents: Item 20 outlet turnover and projected openings, Item 19 unit economics for listed franchisors

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Franchisors in restaurants, fitness, personal-care services, automotive services, hotels and tax preparation. Target: guidance, revenue, distress (forward).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: Dropped Wisconsin DFI (unreachable from the container). Reframed from an event signal to an annual conditioning variable: a franchisee-distress flag (terminated plus ceased-operation outlets up at least 50% YoY, or Item 19 median AUV down YoY) applied to the first release after state posting, which for December fiscal-year franchisors is usually the Q1 or Q2 report (lead 10-90 days). Replaced the projected-openings-versus-guidance comparison (apples to oranges: FDD projections are franchised domestic openings by state, guidance includes company-owned and international) with the YoY change in projected openings versus the prior FDD. Backtest plan: California DocQNet and Minnesota CARDS hold multi-year PDFs (REASONED), so roughly 10 years x 35 names gives ~350 annual observations. Signal_noise lowered to 2 (annual, four-month lag, scanned PDFs); crowdedness lowered to 2 (FRANdata sells it; specialists read it).
- Mechanism: Franchisors must update their FDD within 120 days of fiscal year end and file it in registration states; California (DFPI DocQNet), Minnesota (CARDS), Wisconsin (DFI) and Indiana publish the PDFs free. Item 20 tables list outlets opened, closed, terminated, transferred and reacquired by state for three years plus projected openings for the coming year by state; Item 19 gives AUV and margin distributions by cohort. Comparing projected openings with public unit-growth guidance, tracking terminated/ceased-operation counts (franchisee distress) and cohort AUV trends gives a forward read on royalty revenue, unit guidance and franchisee health ahead of the Q1 and Q2 releases. This is regulatory exhaust that lands on digital state portals. (REASONED mechanism; portals TESTED HERE reachable.)
- Industry context: Franchisors earn royalties on franchisee sales and grow through franchisee-funded openings; the FDD is the legally mandated document that tells prospective franchisees the system's true opening, closure and unit-economics history. Registration states publish it, so franchisee health is a matter of public record.
- Measurement window: Annual: prior fiscal-year outlet tables and next-year projected openings, filed late March to April and posted by states within days to weeks; the first affected release is the Q1 report (lead often only 10-30 days), subsequent releases 60-150 days.. Lead time: 10 days.
- Universe coverage: ~2.5%: about 30 franchisors (WING, PLNT, XPOF, EWCZ, JACK, PZZA, DPZ, WEN, DIN, LOCO, DENN, DRVN, WINA, VVV, CHH, WH, HRB, SNA and similar) with FDDs filed in California or Minnesota
- Hypothesis: When a franchisor's newly posted FDD shows terminated plus ceased-operation outlets up at least 50% year-over-year, or projected franchised openings down at least 15% versus the prior year's FDD, measured at state posting (at least 10 days before the next release), the next two releases show a negative unit-guidance change and negative T+1/T+2 abnormal returns, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum; used as a conditioning flag, not a standalone trade.
- Who already knows: FRANdata sells FDD-derived data and benchmarks (REASONED, fairly confident); franchise attorneys and brokers read FDDs; restaurant-specialist analysts and some funds cite Item 19 AUVs (REASONED). For small franchisors the projected-openings-versus-guidance gap and state-level terminations are not systematically priced (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): FRANdata (REASONED). Why ours differs: Free state portals, focused on projected openings versus guidance and on terminated/ceased counts as a franchisee-distress signal at state level, aligned to the fund's event calendar.
- Vendors found by reviewer: FRANdata FDD library and FDD-derived research - https://frandata.com/?p=939; FRANdata FDD shop (sells individual FDDs) - https://shop.frandata.com/Fdd; Research and Markets reselling FRANdata FDD reports - https://www.researchandmarkets.com/s/frandata
- Literature found by reviewer: No academic paper found. Practitioner/legal references: Franchise Law Solutions, FDD Item 20 outlets and franchisee information (five standardized tables, by state, projected openings) - https://www.franchiselawsolutions.com/learn/franchise-your-business/fdd/fdd-item-20-outlets-and-franchisee-information; FTC Franchise Rule page - https://www.ftc.gov/node/60289; Franchising.com on FRANdata report: franchisors providing Item 19 financial disclosure at highest rate ever - https://japan.franchising.com/articles/franchisors_providing_financial_disclosure_at_highest_rate_ever.html
- Data sources:
  - California DFPI DocQNet franchise filings (TESTED HERE 200; SPA search form) [UNPROBED] https://docqnet.dfpi.ca.gov/ (update: annual per franchisor; amendments ad hoc; history: multi-year PDFs (REASONED))
  - Minnesota Commerce CARDS (TESTED HERE 200 after redirect) [UNPROBED] https://cards.web.commerce.state.mn.us/ (update: annual per franchisor; history: multi-year (REASONED))
  - Indiana Securities Division franchise portal (TESTED HERE 200 after redirect; PDF availability UNKNOWN) [UNPROBED] https://securities.sos.in.gov/ (update: annual per franchisor; history: UNKNOWN)
- Reviewer source checks: https://docqnet.dfpi.ca.gov/ -> 200 TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: single-page app with empty <title>; search requires for…; https://www.cards.commerce.state.mn.us/ -> 301 -> 200 at cards.web.commerce.state.mn.us Reachable; https://www.wdfi.org/apps/FranchiseSearch/MainSearch.aspx -> 000 Connection failed through the proxy twice; drop Wisconsin; https://www.in.gov/sos/securities/franchise/ -> 302 -> 200 at securities.sos.in.gov Reachable; whether PDFs are posted is UNKNOWN
- Cost: $0; PDF table extraction (pdfplumber), a few hours per filing season
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 20%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Annual cadence with a three-to-four-month lag; projected openings are conservative legal numbers that routinely sit below guidance; restaurant specialists already read these; many state PDFs are scanned or very large, making extraction error-prone.
- Red team (reviewer): Item 20's historical outlet tables duplicate the 10-K's year-end unit, opening and closure counts, which the market has had for weeks before the FDD posts, and projected openings are legally conservative numbers that routinely sit below guidance; the only incremental content (state-level terminations, Item 19 cohort AUVs) is annual, four months stale by posting, and read by FRANdata and franchise-specialist analysts, so it is a slow conditioning screen rather than an event signal.

## N35 Earnings-date scheduling behaviour: confirmed date versus historical pattern and late confirmation, from daily Nasdaq calendar snapshots and newswire RSS

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Whole universe (conditioning variable for every other signal). Target: EPS surprise (forward).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: Kept only as a conditioning variable (sizing and filtering for other signals), not a standalone trade. Split the feature in two: (a) realized delay versus the firm's own prior same-quarter 8-K Item 2.02 filing dates, built entirely from EDGAR daily indexes (free, point-in-time, backtestable to 2004 with no look-ahead); (b) confirmation lateness, built forward only from daily archives of Business Wire and PR Newswire RSS plus Yahoo calendarEvents, since no free point-in-time confirmation history exists. Nasdaq calendar demoted to a cross-check because its robots.txt disallows all paths and its dates are estimates. Documented the API key need: the Alpha Vantage EARNINGS_CALENDAR cross-check needs a free key from https://www.alphavantage.co/support/#api-key (free tier 25 requests/day); everything else in this signal is keyless. Signal_noise raised to 3 for the EDGAR-based delay feature; legal risk 2 for the Nasdaq robots flag.
- Mechanism: Firms schedule earnings later than their historical pattern when news is bad and earlier when good, and late or unconfirmed dates associate with worse surprises (LITERATURE: Bagnoli, Kross and Watts 2002, Journal of Accounting Research, 'The information in management's expected earnings report date: a day late, a penny short'; Johnson and So 2018, Journal of Financial and Quantitative Analysis, 'Time will tell: information in the timing of scheduled earnings news'). Daily snapshots of the Nasdaq calendar (fields TESTED HERE: lastYearRptDt, time, fiscalQuarterEnding, epsForecast, noOfEsts) plus Business Wire and PR Newswire RSS for 'will report ... results on' announcements give days delayed versus last year's date, whether the date is company-confirmed, and days between confirmation and the event. The feature is final at confirmation, typically 2-4 weeks ahead.
- Industry context: Companies choose their own report date, and boards and auditors finish faster when the numbers are clean, so scheduling leaks the sign of the news. The effect is documented in accounting journals but needs point-in-time date history to exploit.
- Measurement window: From roughly 45 days before the expected date to the confirmation day; feature finalised at confirmation (late confirmers are by definition closer to the event).. Lead time: 7 days.
- Universe coverage: ~100%: all ~1,300 names for the EDGAR delay feature; confirmation feature coverage grows as the newswire archive accumulates
- Hypothesis: A firm whose earnings 8-K has not been filed by its prior-two-year same-quarter median filing date plus 5 calendar days (EDGAR point-in-time), or whose company-confirmed date is announced fewer than 10 days before the event (newswire archive), shows a negative EPS surprise and negative T+1/T+2 abnormal return relative to otherwise similar firms, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum; used to condition the size and direction of other event signals.
- Who already knows: Wall Street Horizon sells earnings-date revision data (DateBreaks) to institutions (REASONED, fairly confident); the academic effect has been public since 2002 and quant funds use it, so it is probably largely priced for liquid large caps; decay in sub-$5B names is uncertain (UNKNOWN).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Wall Street Horizon (REASONED). Why ours differs: Free reconstruction from daily Nasdaq snapshots, newswire RSS and EDGAR point-in-time dates, used mainly as a conditioning variable for the other signals rather than as a standalone trade.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Wall Street Horizon earnings date revision data (Deltix/TABB study 'Generating Alpha with Earnings Date Revisions' uses it) - https://www.wallstreethorizon.com/news/tabb_deltix; Wall Street Horizon blog: 'More proof that you can make money trading on earnings date revisions' - https://wallstreethorizon.com/blog/more-proof-that-you-can-make-money-trading-on-earnings-date-revisions-from-wall-street-horizon; FactSet Insight: earnings date revisions amid COVID-19 (FactSet tracks date revisions) - https://insight.factset.com/earnings-date-revisions-amid-covid-19
- Literature found by reviewer: Bagnoli, Kross and Watts (2002), 'The Information in Management's Expected Earnings Report Date: A Day Late, a Penny Short', Journal of Accounting Research 40(5): 1275-1296 - https://ideas.repec.org/a/bla/joares/v40y2002i5p1275-1296.html; Johnson and So, 'Time Will Tell: Information in the Timing of Scheduled Earnings News' (firms advancing dates outperform delayers by about 260 bps in the following month; WSH-hosted copy, updated July 2017) - https://wallstreethorizon.com/news/So; Deltix/TABB, 'Generating Alpha with Earnings Date Revisions' (S&P 500; strongest returns when a date is advanced in the second half of the quarter) - https://www.wallstreethorizon.com/news/tabb_deltix
- Data sources:
  - EDGAR daily index (historical 8-K filing dates for the expected-date model; point-in-time) [DATA] https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/daily-index/2025/QTR4/ (update: daily; history: multi-decade)
  - EDGAR latest 8-K atom feed (actual release filings) [DATA] https://www.sec.gov/cgi-bin/browse-edgar?action=getcurrent&type=8-K&count=100&output=atom (update: continuous; history: rolling window)
  - Business Wire home RSS (TESTED HERE 200; archive daily for 'will report results on' confirmations) [DATA] https://feed.businesswire.com/rss/home/?rss=G1QFDERJXkJeEFpRVQ== (update: continuous; history: recent items only; build forward)
  - PR Newswire news-releases RSS (TESTED HERE 200) [DATA] https://www.prnewswire.com/rss/news-releases-list.rss (update: continuous; history: recent items only; build forward)
  - Yahoo quoteSummary calendarEvents (confirmed vs estimated earnings date; anonymous crumb handshake) [DATA] https://query2.finance.yahoo.com/v10/finance/quoteSummary/AAPL?modules=calendarEvents (update: daily snapshot; history: forward-looking only; build forward)
  - Alpha Vantage EARNINGS_CALENDAR (cross-check; free API key required) [KEY] https://www.alphavantage.co/query?function=EARNINGS_CALENDAR&horizon=3month&apikey=demo (update: daily; history: forward-looking only)
