- Cost: $0 (SEC fair-access limits; parse servicer-report exhibits, PDF/HTML)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 3 / 1. Confidence: 30%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Securitized pools are a selected, seasoning-biased subset (recent vintages, specific credit tiers) so pool trends can diverge from the on-balance-sheet book; management pre-signals credit trends; and for Harley the equity reacts to motorcycle retail sales far more than to HDFS credit, so the finance line may be a second-order driver of the announcement return.
- Red team (reviewer): CarMax's announcement return is driven by used-unit comps, gross profit per unit and SG&A far more than by the CAF provision, and the securitized pools are seasoned, near-prime subsets that diverge from the on-book Tier 2/3 book; management pre-guides provision direction, ABS desks and rating-agency surveillance already read these reports, and the KBRA-style aggregate auto-ABS indices are public, so the un-guided residual is small and 8-12 events a year give weak statistics.

## N44 State liquor-license and food-service-license exhaust (Texas TABC, Florida DBPR) for restaurant and retail unit openings and closures

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Unit-growth restaurant chains, casual dining with closure risk, grocery/c-store chains that hold beer-wine licenses, in Texas and Florida. Target: revenue (unit growth), guidance, distress (both).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Texas TABC covers only alcohol licensees, so drop Dutch Bros, Cava and Sweetgreen (no or negligible alcohol) from the Texas leg; Florida DBPR, which licenses all food service, becomes the primary source and TABC the secondary for alcohol-serving chains. (2) The TABC pending-application dataset is a snapshot with submissions only from March 2026 and no history: archive it daily from now and do not backtest on it; backtest openings from original_issue_date in the license register (point-in-time caveat: issue dates are known only after issuance) and closures from expiration/cancelled status_change_date, knowing only the latest status survives so closure history is partial. (3) Restrict to company-operated chains with at least 15% of units in TX+FL (TXRH, BJRI, CAKE, BLMN, FWRG, KRUS, PTLO, PLAY, EAT, DRI, plus alcohol-licensed retailers SFM, MUSA, CASY where present) and match the 'owner' field to operating-subsidiary legal names; franchised systems are out of scope. (4) Target development guidance (annual unit-growth guidance change and the quarter's unit count versus the guided pace) rather than revenue surprise, since a few units move revenue by under 1%. (5) Add company store-locator / 'coming soon' page diffs as the control for what the sell-side already sees. (6) Lead time revised to ~5 trading days for the quarter's realized net count; pending applications give a forward 30-90 day look only out of sample from mid-2026.
- Mechanism: A new restaurant or alcohol-selling store must obtain a TABC license (Texas) or a DBPR food-service license (Florida) before opening; Texas publishes pending original license applications daily with owner entity, address and submission date, and Florida publishes new food-service licenses by fiscal year plus weekly closure orders. Pending applications therefore reveal the opening pipeline 1-3 months ahead, and license status changes (cancelled/expired/change of ownership) reveal closures and refranchising that companies only disclose at quarter-end. Net licensed units in TX+FL, pro-rated by the chain's share of units in those states, nowcasts unit growth against annual guidance and flags closure clusters.
- Industry context: For growth restaurant chains the stock is a multiple on unit growth, and a slip of a few openings from one quarter to the next changes revenue and the credibility of the annual target. Closures and refranchising are the quiet side of the ledger that management reports only at quarter-end.
- Measurement window: Daily status changes; quarter's net openings/closures countable within days of quarter-end (TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: TABC License Information 128,684 rows with status_change_date and original_issue_date; Pending New Primary and Subordinate License Applications dataset returns application_id, owner, address, submission_date, status; Florida newfood_2526.xlsx and EOS weekly closure extract downloaded, extract columns include License Number, Business Name, Address, Date, Conditions for Closure). Pending applications give a forward look of ~30-90 days on openings (REASONED).. Lead time: 5 days.
- Universe coverage: ~1.5%: roughly 15-20 company-operated restaurant chains with at least 15% of units in TX+FL, plus a few alcohol-licensed retailers; coffee and fast-casual chains without alcohol are covered only by the Florida leg
- Hypothesis: For company-operated restaurant chains with at least 15% of units in Texas and Florida, a shortfall of at least 10% in the quarter's net new licensed TX+FL units (Florida DBPR food-service licenses plus TABC original licenses matched to the operating entity) versus the pace implied by annual unit-growth guidance pro-rated by TX+FL unit share, measured 3 trading days before the release, predicts a cut or miss on unit-development guidance and a negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus XLY, controlling for consensus revisions, comps guidance, sector, size and momentum; the pending-application count, archived daily from 2026, is tested only out of sample as a forward one-to-two-quarter indicator.
- Who already knows: Sell-side tracks openings from company press releases, web store locators and management's monthly updates (Dutch Bros, Cava and Sweetgreen publish unit counts quarterly and sometimes intra-quarter); closures are usually only visible after the fact. REASONED: partially priced for openings, lightly priced for closures and refranchising.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Placer.ai (foot traffic, includes new-location detection) and SafeGraph (POI open/close datasets) sell at scale; UNKNOWN whether any vendor packages state license pipelines for funds.. Why ours differs: License pipelines are leading (filed before opening) rather than lagging POI detection, are free and daily, and carry the legal owner entity, which exposes refranchising and franchisee churn that POI datasets miss.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Apify 'US Liquor License Monitor: New, Pending & Changed Filings' (low-cost scraper over state registers) - https://apify.com/registryfeeds/liquor-license-monitor; Apify 'New Liquor License Leads - TX & CA (Restaurant/Bar Openings)' - https://apify.com/rook-data-tools/new-liquor-license-leads; Nomad Data 'US Liquor Licenses Data' (data broker use-case page naming investors as an audience) - https://www.nomad-data.com/data-use-cases/us-liquor-licenses-data-blog-nomad-data; dataplor location intelligence for financial markets (openings/closures) - https://www.dataplor.com/resources/blog/how-location-intelligence-is-evolving-for-financial-markets/; Placer.ai retail solutions (visitation around openings) - https://www.placer.ai/solutions/retail; Coresight Store Intelligence Platform (openings/closures inferred from location databases) - https://coresight.com/store-intelligence-platform-overview/
- Literature found by reviewer: No paper linking state license pipelines to restaurant earnings surprises was found; Lowenstein Sandler alternative data report 2024 (legal context on data provenance) - https://www.lowenstein.com/media/rbnbulvn/alt-data-report-2024_final.pdf; Cornell eCommons document on restaurant/license data (seen in results, content not verified) - https://ecommons.cornell.edu/server/api/core/bitstreams/70543b00-ddc3-4afc-8f75-d8f09e0b07b0/content
- Data sources:
  - Florida DBPR Hotels & Restaurants public records (new food-service licenses by fiscal year, change-of-ownership, weekly emergency-closure extracts) [PROBED 200] https://www2.myfloridalicense.com/hotels-restaurants/public-records/ (update: Weekly extracts; fiscal-year files updated continuously; history: FY2016-17 through FY2025-26 files listed)
  - TABC License Information (Socrata 7hf9-qc9f) [PROBED 200] https://data.texas.gov/resource/7hf9-qc9f.json?$limit=5 (update: Daily; history: Full register with original_issue_date back to the 1980s; only latest status per license)
  - TABC Pending Original License Applications (Socrata mxm5-tdpj) [PROBED 200] https://data.texas.gov/resource/mxm5-tdpj.json?$limit=5 (update: Daily; history: Snapshot only; submissions from March 2026; archive daily)
  - Nasdaq earnings calendar (release dates) [DATA] https://api.nasdaq.com/api/calendar/earnings?date=2026-11-12 (update: Daily; history: One day per request)
- Reviewer source checks: https://data.texas.gov/resource/mxm5-tdpj.json?$limit=5 -> PROBED 200 JSON rows with application_id, license_type, applicationstatus ('Pending - In R…; https://data.texas.gov/resource/7hf9-qc9f.json?$limit=5 -> PROBED 200 Fields confirmed: trade_name, owner, license_status, status_change_date, origin…; https://www2.myfloridalicense.com/hotels-restaurants/public-records/ -> PROBED 200 264 KB page listing new food-service license files by fiscal year, change-of-ow…
- Cost: $0
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 20%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Two states are a partial sample; license issue dates can precede openings by months or follow soft openings; franchise entities obscure the parent; and the most-watched growth chains already drip-feed opening counts, so the residual surprise is small relative to trading costs.
- Red team (reviewer): Unit openings are guided, drip-fed on calls and visible on 'coming soon' pages, so the residual is a handful of units worth under 1% of revenue; license issue dates are loosely coupled to opening dates (soft openings, delayed build-outs), two states are a non-random sample, closure clusters can be read as positive pruning so the sign is unstable, and the announcement return is dominated by comps and margins rather than unit counts.

## N45 Sentinel-2 finished-goods yard fill at RV plants and dealer lots (white-roof brightness) as a wholesale-shipment and destocking nowcast

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: RV manufacturers and their component suppliers; RV dealers. Target: revenue, guidance (both).
- Seed item: [physical] Satellite parking-lot car counts (use only as a crowded benchmark; assume mostly priced in)
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: Sign of the signal is ambiguous (plant-yard fill rises with transport/driver bottlenecks or chassis staging as readily as with weak dealer pull; RV OEMs largely build against dealer orders, REASONED), two of the four fiscal quarter-ends for THO (Jan 31) and WGO (late Nov, late Feb) fall in the northern-Indiana snow season where white-roof brightness is unreadable, 10 m mixed pixels over gravel/asphalt are an untested instrument, and the quantity consensus actually uses (RVIA monthly wholesale shipments) is public ~3-4 weeks after month-end, which for Thor (reports ~7-8 weeks after quarter end, REASONED) is before the print. No vendor or paper on RV-yard imagery was found; coverage is 5 names. Not worth the hand-digitizing and validation effort at 10% confidence.
- Mechanism: Finished RVs wait outdoors on plant lots in Elkhart County, Indiana and Forest City, Iowa and on dealer lots; their white or light roofs are strongly reflective against asphalt and grass, so the bright-pixel fraction inside a hand-digitized yard polygon in 10 m Sentinel-2 imagery tracks yard occupancy every ~5 days when clear. Rising plant-yard fill means production is outrunning dealer pickup (wholesale shipments slowing, production cuts ahead) which hits Thor and Winnebago revenue and, one week later, LCI and Patrick content sales; falling dealer-lot fill with stable retail means restock orders. Applies to THO (Jayco Middlebury, Keystone Goshen, Heartland, Airstream Jackson Center OH), WGO (Forest City IA, Grand Design Middlebury IN), suppliers LCII and PATK, and dealer Camping World (CWH).
- Industry context: RV makers build to dealer orders and recognize revenue on wholesale shipment, so dealer destocking shows up first as units piling up on plant lots. Elkhart County assembles most US RVs, concentrating the physical signal in a few square miles.
- Measurement window: Whole fiscal quarter at ~5-day revisit; final reading is the last clear scene before the announcement (typically 3-10 days prior; Midwest winter cloud cover will leave gaps, REASONED). Validation step required: confirm that 10 m pixels resolve yard-fill changes at lots holding hundreds of units (REASONED, not yet tested).. Lead time: 5 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.4%: 5 names, Thor (THO), Winnebago (WGO), LCI Industries (LCII), Patrick Industries (PATK), Camping World (CWH); Forest River (Berkshire, private) yards add an industry read.
- Hypothesis: A rise of at least 15% in the yard-fill bright-pixel fraction at Thor and Winnebago plant lots over the fiscal quarter versus the same quarter a year earlier, measured from the last clear Sentinel-2 scene at least 3 days before the announcement, predicts a negative revenue or guidance surprise for THO/WGO and for LCII/PATK the following week, controlling for already-published RVIA wholesale shipments, consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Sell-side uses RVIA monthly wholesale shipments (published ~3-4 weeks after month-end, so the quarter's last month usually lands after Thor/Winnebago report), dealer surveys and Statistical Surveys retail registrations; management gives dealer-inventory counts quarterly. REASONED: industry-level direction is priced; plant-specific yard inventory is not observed by consensus.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Orbital Insight and RS Metrics sell satellite parking-lot and yard analytics at scale (RS Metrics also covers metals yards); UNKNOWN whether either sells RV plant-yard fill specifically.. Why ours differs: Plant-side finished-goods inventory rather than consumer parking traffic, measured on free 10 m imagery at every revisit, for the exact facilities of two tickers plus the suppliers that lag them by a week.
- Vendors found by reviewer: RS Metrics (satellite asset-level data; sources Maxar/Airbus high-resolution imagery; EVTracker factory production) - https://www.nanalyze.com/2019/02/satellites-forecast-commodity-prices/; RS Metrics imagery sourcing (Cambridge JFQA supplementary appendix) - https://static.cambridge.org/content/id/urn%3Acambridge.org%3Aid%3Aarticle%3AS0022109023001448/resource/name/S0022109023001448sup001.pdf; Nomad Data - RV market data use case (inventory dynamics; not satellite) - https://nomad-data.com/data-use-cases/uncovering-recreational-vehicle-market-trends-with-diverse-data-sources
- Literature found by reviewer: The Data Score: Satellite data, 3 good and 3 bad use cases (parking-lot counts a poor fit for fast-changing retail estimation) - https://thedatascore.substack.com/p/satellite-data-3-good-and-3-bad-use; Institutional Investor: Satellite Spyware Over Tesla, WalMart and the World - https://bsporigin.institutionalinvestor.com/article/2bsuk1r6j7pss4oxpnda8/premium/satellite-spyware-over-tesla-walmart-and-the-world; RV PRO: Northcoast Research calls Winnebago, Thor dealer inventories 'unsustainable' - https://rv-pro.com/news/trends-northcoast-research-call-winnebago-thor-industries-dealer-inventories-unsustainable/; Spader RV Brief (dealer-lot inventory commentary) - https://go.ncmassociates.com/rs/986-XKR-909/images/Spader-RV-Brief.pdf
- Data sources:
  - Microsoft Planetary Computer STAC item search (sentinel-2-l2a) for scene discovery and COG reads with SAS token [DATA] https://planetarycomputer.microsoft.com/api/stac/v1/search (update: ~5-day revisit per site; history: 2015-06 to present)
  - Sentinel Hub Statistical API (CDSE edition) for polygon time-series without downloading scenes [KEY] https://sh.dataspace.copernicus.eu/api/v1/statistics (update: Per scene; history: Sentinel-2 from 2015)
  - AWS Open Data Sentinel-2 COGs (alternative keyless source) [DATA] https://sentinel-cogs.s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/?list-type=2&prefix=sentinel-s2-l2a-cogs/ (update: Per scene; history: 2017+)
- Reviewer source checks: https://planetarycomputer.microsoft.com/api/stac/v1/search -> 200 (POST); GET returns 400 TESTED HERE: Elkhart bbox, Sept 2025: 14 sentinel-2-l2a scenes, 7 with cloud co…; https://sh.dataspace.copernicus.eu/api/v1/statistics -> KEY (per Phase 0 file; not re-probed) Free CDSE account + OAuth client credentials.; https://sentinel-cogs.s3.us-west-2.amazonaws.com/?list-type=2&prefix=sentinel-s… -> DATA (per Phase 0 file; not re-probed) Keyless COG bucket, 2017+.; https://www.rvia.org/news-insights/rv-industry-shipments -> 200 Public monthly RV wholesale shipment releases (the candidate's /news-insights/r…
- Cost: $0 (free CDSE account for Statistical API; compute only)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 10%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): 10 m pixels may not resolve occupancy changes reliably, snow and wet pavement change brightness, yards also hold chassis and trailers that are not finished goods, and RVIA data plus dealer checks already give the sell-side the wholesale trend, leaving only timing as the edge.
- Red team (reviewer): Plant-yard occupancy is not a demand variable: OEMs build mostly against dealer orders, so yards fill when transport is backed up or chassis are staged (REASONED), making the sign ambiguous; the industry read consensus relies on (RVIA wholesale shipments, Statistical Surveys retail registrations, management dealer-inventory counts) is already public before Thor prints; and the instrument (bright-pixel fraction at 10 m over gravel, wet pavement and snow) has never been validated against a known yard count.

## N46 Theme-park posted ride wait times (queue-times.com public API) as an in-park attendance and operations index for Six Flags and United Parks

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Regional theme parks and marine parks. Target: revenue (attendance), margin (nowcast).
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: Posted wait times are a supply-confounded proxy (rides open, staffing, virtual queues, paid skip-the-line) for attendance, a quantity Placer.ai and INRIX already sell at scale and the companies themselves pre-announce; Thrill Data already publishes daily/weekly wait statistics by park, so the wait-time read is not scarce. The API is live-only (archiver needed, no backtest), the multi-year crowd calendars are JS-rendered pages outside the API's stated terms (.json variants return 406), and coverage is 2 names with one quarter (Q3) that matters, leaving almost no tradeable events. The ride-downtime component is novel but its link to a quarterly per-cap/EBITDA surprise is tenuous.
- Mechanism: Parks post ride wait times continuously; queue-times.com republishes them through a free JSON API with ride open/closed flags. For a given ride capacity, posted waits scale with guests in the park, so the operating-hours average wait across a company's rides, aggregated over the fiscal quarter, is an attendance index, while the share of operating hours with rides flagged closed measures operational problems (staffing, weather, breakdowns) that depress per-capita spend. Applies to Six Flags Entertainment (post-merger company incl. Cedar Fair parks) and United Parks & Resorts (SeaWorld/Busch Gardens), whose quarterly attendance is the headline number.
- Industry context: Regional park operators report attendance and per-capita spend each quarter, and the Q3 summer season decides the year. Attendance is the figure the stocks trade on, and ride availability drives both guest satisfaction and in-park spend.
- Measurement window: Whole fiscal quarter (season-weighted: Q2 Apr-Jun, Q3 Jul-Sep is the critical quarter, Q4 Halloween events); the API is live-only so a 5-10 minute archiver must run continuously (TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: parks.json and /parks/50/queue_times.json return 200 with per-ride wait_time, is_open, last_updated; coverage lists Six Flags Entertainment Corporation 26 parks, Cedar Fair 11, SeaWorld Parks 10). Monthly crowd-calendar HTML pages exist per park (TESTED HERE 200) but their historical depth is UNKNOWN.. Lead time: 25 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.2%: 2 names, Six Flags Entertainment (FUN) and United Parks & Resorts (PRKS); Herschend and Universal parks in the feed are private or part of too-large parents.
- Hypothesis: A decline of at least 5% YoY in operating-hours average posted wait across a company's US parks over the fiscal quarter (ride-capacity-normalized), measured 3 days after quarter-end, predicts attendance below consensus and a negative T-1 to T+1 abnormal return versus XLY, controlling for rain days, consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Sell-side leisure analysts use Placer.ai foot traffic and company mid-quarter commentary; Six Flags and United Parks sometimes pre-announce attendance trends. REASONED: direction of attendance is largely in consensus; ride-downtime and weekend/event-night intensity are not.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Placer.ai sells theme-park foot traffic at scale; Thrill Data and queue-times publish wait statistics publicly; no vendor known to sell wait-time-derived attendance to funds (UNKNOWN).. Why ours differs: Free, minute-level, measures load inside the park (and ride downtime, a per-cap and cost driver) rather than device visits at the gate, and isolates Halloween-event nights and weather days that drive quarterly surprises.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Placer.ai theme-park visitation (via HL Advisors breakdown of attractions-industry visitor trends) - https://hladvisors.com/wp-content/uploads/2025/12/Understanding-the-Data-A-Breakdown-of-Attractions-Industry-Visitor-Trends-IAAPA-Changes.pdf; INRIX: trip data used to predict and understand attendance rebound at theme parks (Six Flags, SeaWorld; trips track Six Flags revenue) - https://inrix.com/blog/inrix-data-used-to-predict-and-understand-attendance-rebound-at-theme-parks/; Thrill Data (public daily/weekly wait-time reviews by park) - https://www.thrill-data.com/day-in-review/2026-08-01; Apify queue-times scraper (third-party, unaffiliated; notes attribution requirement) - https://apify.com/nickslam/queue-times-scraper
- Literature found by reviewer: Blooloop: analysts' sources reporting declining visitation; single-park observations not representative of a chain - https://blooloop.com/?p=335749; queue-times.com Real Time API page (free, attribution 'Powered by Queue-Times.com' required; no commercial-use clause seen) - https://queue-times.com/en-US/pages/api
- Data sources:
  - queue-times.com public API: park list and per-park live queue times (attribution 'Powered by Queue-Times.com' required per API page) [UNPROBED] https://queue-times.com/parks.json (update: Minutes (live); history: Live only via API; crowd-calendar pages per park and month at https://queue-times.com/parks/50/calendar/2025/07 (depth UNKNOWN))
  - NOAA NCEI Access Data Service for rain/temperature at each park (weather control) [DATA] https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/access/services/data/v1?dataset=daily-summaries&stations=USW00014825&startDate=2025-07-01&endDate=2025-09-30&format=json (update: Daily; history: Full GHCN-Daily period of record)
- Reviewer source checks: https://queue-times.com/parks.json -> 200 TESTED HERE: 17 groups, 140 parks; Six Flags Entertainment Corporation 26, Ceda…; https://queue-times.com/parks/50/queue_times.json -> 200 Per-ride wait_time, is_open, last_updated.; https://queue-times.com/en-US/pages/api -> 200 TESTED HERE: 'Access to the Real Time API is free, though you are required to d…; https://queue-times.com/parks/50/calendar/2019/07 -> 200 TESTED HERE: month pages linked back to 2016/05 for Cedar Point with summary te…; https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/access/services/data/v1?dataset=daily-summaries&stati… -> DATA (per Phase 0 file; not re-probed) Keyless GHCN-Daily weather control.
- Cost: $0 (small always-on archiver; confirm commercial-use terms on the queue-times API page: UNKNOWN)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 2. Confidence: 8%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Posted waits are supply-driven as much as demand-driven (staffing, paid skip-the-line products, virtual queues, new rides), parks with fewer rides open show longer waits at lower attendance, and Placer.ai already gives consensus an attendance read, so only the operations component may be new.
- Red team (reviewer): Waits scale with (guests / open ride capacity), and the denominator moves more than the numerator: parks with fewer rides open, new virtual-queue systems or paid line-skips show longer posted waits at lower attendance, so the index can read 'strong' in a weak quarter; attendance direction is already in consensus via Placer.ai, INRIX and company pre-announcements, leaving no residual on 2 names.

## N47 eBay Browse API new-with-tags listing supply and ask-to-MSRP ratios by brand as a channel-excess and markdown nowcast

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Wholesale-heavy apparel, footwear, accessories and outdoor/drinkware brands. Target: margin, guidance, revenue (both).
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: Only source is the eBay Browse API under an API License Agreement that (per EcommerceBytes' account of the 2025 update) defines 'Restricted APIs' as those offering market-trend, pricing or sales-volume data and requires intermediate copies to be deleted when no longer needed; a stored daily listing-count panel built for investment research is AMBER at best and the full agreement could not be read from the container (developer.ebay.com returns 403). The data is snapshot-only, so the first YoY reading is 12+ months away and no backtest is possible; the mechanism is weak because most wholesale overflow clears through TJX/ROST/BURL and brand outlets rather than eBay, and NWT listing supply rises with brand heat and reseller arbitrage as much as with channel excess. A first-party alternative (markdown share on brand DTC sites via Shopify products.json, DATA in the Phase 0 file) measures a different mechanism and belongs to a separate candidate (N48-type), not a rescue of this one.
- Mechanism: When a brand over-ships wholesale or retailers cancel orders, excess current-season goods leak to liquidators and small resellers who list them on eBay as new with tags; the official eBay Browse API returns active-listing counts and asking prices by keyword and condition. A rise in a brand's NWT listing count (normalized by total apparel listings) and a fall in the median ask relative to MSRP for current-season items signals channel inventory excess, which precedes markdowns, wholesale order cuts, gross-margin pressure and guidance cuts. Applies to brands that sell mostly through wholesale partners and off-price rather than their own stores.
- Industry context: Apparel and footwear brands recognize wholesale revenue on shipment, so excess stock in the channel surfaces later as markdowns, returns and order cuts. Liquidators and resellers are the pressure valve, and eBay is the largest open marketplace where that valve is observable.
- Measurement window: Daily snapshots over the whole fiscal quarter with extra weight on the last 8 weeks; final reading the day before earnings. REASONED: one quarter of history must be built before the first live use because the API is snapshot-only.. Lead time: 1 days.
- Universe coverage: ~3%: about 35-40 names, e.g. Under Armour (UAA), VF Corp (VFC), Columbia (COLM), Crocs (CROX), Deckers (DECK), Steve Madden (SHOO), Wolverine (WWW), Carter's (CRI), Hanesbrands (HBI), PVH, Capri (CPRI), Tapestry (TPR), G-III (GIII), Oxford (OXM), Levi's (LEVI), YETI, Guess (GES).
- Hypothesis: A rise of at least 30% YoY in normalized active NWT listing count for a brand's current-season items over the last 8 weeks of its fiscal quarter, measured 2 days before earnings, predicts gross margin below consensus and a negative T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus XLY, controlling for consensus revisions, reported inventory-to-sales ratio, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Brands monitor their own gray-market leakage; sell-side relies on quarterly inventory disclosures and off-price channel checks. REASONED: not systematically used by equity investors; inventory levels are disclosed quarterly so the level is priced, the intra-quarter change is not.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): EDITED and DataWeave sell retail pricing and markdown analytics at scale to retailers; StockX publishes sneaker resale data; UNKNOWN whether any vendor sells eBay NWT supply by brand as a fund signal.. Why ours differs: Measures the overflow channel (liquidation supply) rather than first-party shelf prices, uses an official free API with brand-level SKU matching, and is aligned to each brand's fiscal quarter.

- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 1 / 3. Confidence: 5%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Listing supply also rises with brand popularity and arbitrage demand, counterfeits distort counts, most excess goes to TJX/Ross rather than eBay, and brands already disclose inventory and markdown expectations, so the residual may be noise after costs.
- Red team (reviewer): Listing supply is a popularity and arbitrage variable: a hot brand draws more resellers and more NWT listings while its margins expand, so the index loads on brand momentum with the wrong sign; the residual after removing that, counterfeits and the off-price channel that absorbs most excess is noise, and it cannot be tested for at least a year because no history exists.

## N48 Retailer promotional-email cadence and depth index (self-subscribed mailbox) as a markdown-pressure nowcast for specialty retail and DTC brands

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Specialty apparel, footwear, home furnishings, beauty, sporting goods and DTC brands with large email programs. Target: margin, EPS surprise, revenue (nowcast).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Drop the Milled backfill: milled.com and /terms return 403 from the container, the full-archive is a paid Pro product, and robots.txt disallows /search, /sites/ and category message lists with Content-Signal ai-train=no; mailbox-only, so legal_risk stays 2. (2) Because there is no lawful history, replace the YoY index for the first four quarters with a cross-sectional within-quarter escalation score: promo intensity in the last 14 days of each retailer's fiscal quarter divided by its weeks 1-10 average, ranked across retailers each quarter; switch to YoY once a year of own history exists. (3) Restrict live use to mid-caps with thin sell-side promo coverage and treat the large names (M, KSS, GPS, DKS, ULTA, BBWI) as a control group, since weekly promo checks already exist there. (4) Run two mailboxes per retailer with different personas and no purchase history to detect personalization; drop retailers where the two diverge. (5) Claim a margin-direction effect only (no revenue claim). (6) crowdedness lowered to 2 because MailCharts sells 'promotion rate vs last year' per brand and practitioners (Hedgeye) already count promo emails; signal_noise lowered to 2 for personalization and planned calendars.
- Mechanism: Retailers reach for deeper and more frequent promotions (site-wide percentage-off, extra-off clearance, lowered free-shipping thresholds, 'last chance' language) when sell-through lags plan, and these messages are sent to every subscriber. Subscribing a dedicated mailbox to ~150 retailers' lists and parsing daily emails yields a promo-intensity index per ticker (frequency x depth x breadth) that, compared with the same weeks a year earlier, reads merchandise-margin pressure for the quarter and end-of-quarter clearance pushes. Applies to retailers with mostly first-party, non-personalized broadcast email programs.
- Industry context: Specialty retail profits hinge on merchandise margin, which is set by how much a retailer must mark down to clear inventory. Email is the cheapest lever a merchant pulls when traffic or conversion lags, so promotional cadence reveals the merchant's own read on the quarter.
- Measurement window: Daily over the whole fiscal quarter, final reading the day before earnings; backfill history from a public email archive (Milled, UNPROBED, ToS to check) to avoid a one-year warm-up.. Lead time: 1 days.
- Universe coverage: ~5%: roughly 60 mid-cap names after excluding large caps with weekly sell-side promo checks (used as controls); ~100 names collected.
- Hypothesis: Among mid-cap specialty retailers and DTC brands with thin sell-side promo coverage, the top quintile of within-quarter promo escalation (last-14-day promo intensity relative to the quarter's weeks 1-10, measured 1 day before earnings from a self-subscribed mailbox) shows gross margin below consensus and a negative T-1 to T+1 abnormal return versus XRT relative to the bottom quintile, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum; YoY index version to be tested once four quarters of history exist.
- Who already knows: Sell-side does weekly in-store and online promo checks for the biggest names; marketers buy email-intelligence from MailCharts; retailers' guidance embeds the planned promotional calendar. REASONED: broad promo direction is priced; intra-quarter escalation versus plan, especially in the last two weeks, is less so.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): MailCharts sells e-commerce email marketing intelligence; EDITED and DataWeave sell markdown and pricing analytics at scale to retailers; sell-side promo checks are informal. These are not typically consumed as quarter-aligned signals by small funds.. Why ours differs: Zero cost, daily, built from the retailer's own broadcast messaging (what it chose to say to all customers), aligned to fiscal quarters and scored for escalation versus the prior year rather than absolute discount level.
- Vendors found by reviewer: MailCharts (per-brand promotion rate vs last year vs MailCharts index; e.g., Target page) - https://mailcharts.com/companies/270; Milled (archive of ~52.7M marketing emails from ~103k brands; Pro tier full archive back to 2012) - https://milled.com/for-brands; Jacquard Holiday Marketing Report 2025 - https://www.jacquard.com/resources/news/jacquard-holiday-marketing-report-2025/
- Literature found by reviewer: Hedgeye: Promotional email from retailers (low promo-email count treated as a red flag for Tory Burch) - https://app.hedgeye.com/insights/38793-promotional-email-from-retailers; L.E.K.: Promotional activity in apparel showed no sign of slowing this holiday season (email volume and discount depth, 2017/2019) - https://www.lek.com/insights/promotional-activity-apparel-showed-no-sign-slowing-holiday-season; Digital Commerce 360: Retailers send more email but ease up on promotions (MailCharts data, Top 1000) - https://www.digitalcommerce360.com/2022/07/30/email-marketing-analysis-by-type-and-category/amp/; MailCharts blog: email marketers stick to frequency/promo-rate patterns in 2022 holiday campaigns - https://www.mailcharts.com/blog/email-marketers-stick-to-frequency-promo-rate-patterns-in-2022-holiday-campaigns; Milled Terms and Conditions (dated 2018-05-25 per snippet; full text not retrievable from container) - https://milled.com/about/terms
- Data sources:
  - Own dedicated mailbox(es) subscribed to retailer broadcast lists (IMAP; rules-based parser for %-off, breadth, thresholds, 'last chance' language) [UNPROBED (user-side mailbox; no container test applicable)] https://developers.google.com/gmail/api/guides (update: Daily as emails arrive; history: From subscription date forward; no backfill)
- Reviewer source checks: https://developers.google.com/gmail/api/guides -> not probed Mailbox is a user-side action; any IMAP provider works.; https://milled.com/ -> 403 Blocked from the container.; https://milled.com/terms -> 403 Terms not retrievable from the container.; https://milled.com/robots.txt -> 200 TESTED HERE: blocks AI crawlers, Content-Signal search=yes ai-train=no; Disallo…
- Cost: $0 (one mailbox; optional Gmail API key is free)
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 2. Confidence: 18%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Promotions follow planned marketing calendars and are increasingly personalized (one mailbox sees one treatment), heavy promotion can accompany strong demand, and for the largest names the sell-side already tracks promo depth weekly, so the residual edge may be confined to mid-cap names with thin coverage.
- Red team (reviewer): A single mailbox sees one personalized treatment of an increasingly segmented program, promotions follow planned marketing calendars that guidance already embeds, heavy promotion often accompanies strong demand (traffic-driving), and MailCharts already sells the 'promotion rate vs last year' metric while sell-side counts promo emails for the large names; the untested residual, if any, is confined to thinly covered mid-caps and cannot be validated for at least a year.

## N49 State DOT bid-letting results: apparent-low-bid totals and bid-to-engineer's-estimate ratio per contractor

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Engineering & construction: heavy-civil/highway contractors (Construction Partners, Granite, Sterling Infrastructure, Primoris, Tutor Perini) and USACE dredging (Great Lakes Dredge & Dock).. Target: guidance (awards/backlog) and margin (both).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Narrow primary names to ROAD and GVA, whose revenue is dominated by state/local public lettings; demote STRL (transportation share shrinking as E-Infrastructure grows, REASONED), PRIM (mostly utility/energy) and TPC (lumpy mega-projects) to secondary; run GLDD as a separate test on FPDS/USAspending USACE dredging awards, which post with a lag. (2) Build a subsidiary-to-ticker name map from each 10-K Exhibit 21 because ROAD and GVA bid under local company names (REASONED). (3) Data: NCDOT per-letting XLS bid tabs (TESTED HERE 200, 970 KB, application/vnd.ms-excel), CODOT bid-tab archives and the Mississippi MDOT bid system are machine-usable now; FDOT LettingMain is a district-selector form and ALDOT/Caltrans are SPAs whose result routes still need to be established; TDOT apparent-bid PDFs are 403 from the container; TxDOT Tableau is dropped unless a JSON route is found; constructionbids.ai is a vendor aggregator and is not used. (4) Use posted award status rather than apparent low bid where available (NCDOT, WVDOH), otherwise apply a rejection haircut. (5) Remove the bid-to-engineer's-estimate margin claim from the event hypothesis; keep it as a slow multi-quarter factor for later study. (6) Lead time revised to 20 days; signal_noise held at 3 because awards are lumpy and management already guides awards growth.
- Mechanism: State DOTs let highway contracts on fixed monthly dates and publish bid tabulations within days: apparent low bidder, bid amount, engineer's estimate and all competing bids. Summing a contractor's apparent-low bids over the quarter nowcasts its new awards and backlog, the number these stocks trade on, and the ratio of winning bid to engineer's estimate plus the gap to the second bidder ('money left on the table') is a leading indicator of future gross margin because contracts are fixed-price and underbidding to fill capacity shows up 2-6 quarters later as margin compression (REASONED). Construction Partners discloses its states (AL, FL, GA, NC, SC, TN, TX), Granite is California/West, Sterling is Texas/Southeast, so each ticker maps to a handful of DOT letting pages; GLDD's revenue is dominated by USACE dredging awards.
- Industry context: Highway contractors win most work through sealed-bid public lettings where the low responsive bidder wins and every bid plus the agency's estimate is published; backlog and bid margin are the two numbers these stocks trade on. Because contracts are fixed-price, bidding below the engineer's estimate to keep crews busy shows up later as margin compression.
- Measurement window: All lettings with bid-opening dates inside the fiscal quarter; last letting results are posted within ~1-10 days of opening, so the final reading is in hand ~Q-end+10 while earnings come 4-6 weeks after Q-end. Awards generally follow bid opening by 30-60 days, so for backlog use award dates (REASONED).. Lead time: 20 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.2% primary: ROAD and GVA; secondary STRL, PRIM, TPC; GLDD via USACE awards as a separate test.
- Hypothesis: A 20% or larger YoY increase in a contractor's quarterly sum of state DOT awards (apparent-low bids haircut for rejections) attributed via subsidiary mapping across NC, CO, MS, FL and AL lettings with bid openings inside the fiscal quarter, measured 5 trading days before the release, predicts a positive backlog/awards surprise versus management's awards guidance and a positive T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus XLI for ROAD and GVA, controlling for consensus revisions, announced acquisitions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Contractors press-release large awards, and sell-side E&C analysts track state DOT budgets, IIJA flows and company-reported backlog; some track state letting volumes in aggregate (REASONED). Dodge Construction Network and ConstructConnect sell project and bid-result data to contractors (REASONED real vendors; whether they aggregate by public-company low bidder is UNKNOWN). Large awards are priced via press releases; the many sub-$50M jobs that make up most of ROAD/STRL backlog, and the bid-to-estimate margin signal, are not.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Dodge Construction Network, ConstructConnect (construction project/bid data), Infotech Bid Express (bid results platform) - all REASONED.. Why ours differs: Per-ticker aggregation of apparent-low-bid totals and bid-vs-estimate ratio from free DOT postings within days of each letting; vendors sell project leads to contractors, not a per-company backlog and margin signal timed to the print.
- Vendors found by reviewer: constructionbids.ai (aggregates state DOT bid results; bidder names not shown for some states) - https://constructionbids.ai/bid-results/fl/fdot; Infotech Bid Express (bidx.com redirects here; results access without account UNKNOWN) - https://www.infotechinc.com/bidx/
- Literature found by reviewer: TDOT Apparent Bid Results per letting (2020-2026 archive seen in results) - https://www.tn.gov/content/dam/tn/tdot/construction/2026_bid_lettings/june-26,-2026-letting/20260626_ApparentBidResults.pdf; CDOT Bid Tabs 2025 archive - https://www.codot.gov/business/bidding/bid-tab-archives/2025; WVDOH award status report per letting - https://transportation.wv.gov/highways/contractadmin/Lettings/AwardStatus2025/Award%20Status%20for%2010.28.2025%20letting.pdf; Construction Partners $3.14B backlog covering 80-85% of next-12-month revenue (press) - https://www.chartmill.com/news/external/13930/is-construction-partners-3-14b-backlog-signaling-more-upside-ahead; Construction Partners expects 10-15% contract awards growth, $3.09B backlog (press) - https://rallies.ai/news/construction-partners-expects-10-15-contract-awards-growth-records-309b-backlog; Sterling Infrastructure Q3 2025 earnings call transcript (backlog $2.58B) - https://www.insidermonkey.com/blog/sterling-infrastructure-inc-nasdaqstrl-q3-2025-earnings-call-transcript-1640424/

- Cost: $0 (PDF parsing and headless browser in the container).
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 3 / 1. Confidence: 25%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Awards are lumpy and a few large jobs dominate; apparent low bid is not an award (rejections, protests, DBE issues); companies pre-release large wins so the aggregate is partly public; current-quarter revenue is driven by backlog burn and weather, not new awards; private work and roll-up acquisitions (ROAD) dilute the public-letting share; TxDOT/Caltrans data require browser automation that may be brittle.
- Red team (reviewer): Awards are lumpy and a few large jobs dominate the quarterly sum; apparent low bid is not an award (rejections, protests, DBE issues); ROAD and GVA bid under subsidiary names that must be mapped and ROAD's backlog also moves with private work and roll-up acquisitions; management already guides awards growth so part of the aggregate is in consensus; and the cleanest machine-readable state (NC) is a minority of either company's footprint while FL, AL, TX and CA postings still need brittle automation.

## N50 Rooftop-weighted hail-swath index from NOAA SPC reports and NEXRAD hail signatures as a storm-demand nowcast for roofing and exterior building products

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Building products: asphalt shingles and roofing (Owens Corning, Carlisle), roofing distribution (QXO/Beacon), siding, gutters and exterior (Louisiana-Pacific, Gibraltar), insulation installers (TopBuild, IBP).. Target: revenue and margin (roofing volumes and price/mix) (both).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Make NCEI SWDI nx3hail (radar MESH) tile counts the primary instrument and use SPC public reports only as a cross-check, because public reports are population-biased and would double-count density once housing-unit weighting is applied. (2) Weight 0.1-degree tiles by ACS housing units (Census key). (3) Lag structure: index over the trailing two to three quarters excluding the current month, since claims-to-reroof lags run one to nine months; the reporting quarter's own hail mostly informs guidance, not the quarter. (4) Names: OC and QXO (post-Beacon) primary, CSL secondary; drop BLD, IBP, JELD and NX (insulation/windows have a weak hail link); ROCK optional. (5) Backtest from 2008 (SWDI counts for June 2008 returned 70,931, so history exists) against OC roofing-segment volumes and T-1 to T+2 returns, with controls for the prior-year hail base (storm comps), asphalt cost and housing starts. (6) Crowdedness lowered to 2: storm demand is the most-discussed swing factor and sell-side surveys already cite hail/wind reports.
- Mechanism: Hail of 1.5 inches or larger damages roofs; insurers pay for replacement and re-roofing follows one to nine months later, pulling shingle, underlayment, siding, gutter and insulation volumes through distributors. A hail index = sum over storm days of (NEXRAD MESH hail-signature cells or SPC public reports at or above a size threshold) weighted by housing units in the affected 0.1-degree tile (Census) and lagged one to two quarters nowcasts the storm-driven share of roofing volumes in the reporting quarter (REASONED). TESTED HERE: SPC daily hail CSVs return lat/lon/size per report and NCEI SWDI nx3hail returns counts per day, per bounding box and per 0.1-degree tile (1,764 hail-signature cells nationally on 2025-06-15).
- Industry context: Roughly four-fifths of asphalt-shingle demand is replacement, and the replacement cycle is pulled forward by hail and wind events that trigger insurance claims (REASONED). Because insurers settle and roofers work through backlogs over months, a spring hail season shows up in second- and third-quarter shipments and pricing.
- Measurement window: Hail days in the reporting quarter and the two prior quarters (weighted toward the prior one to two quarters); data is near-real-time so the final reading is available at quarter end, ~4 weeks before Owens Corning reports.. Lead time: 25 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.3%: OC, QXO primary; CSL secondary; ROCK optional.
- Hypothesis: A one-standard-deviation positive anomaly in the housing-unit-weighted NEXRAD MESH hail index over the trailing two to three quarters (excluding the current month), measured 5 trading days before the release, predicts a positive roofing-volume/revenue surprise and positive T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus XLI for OC and QXO (CSL secondary), controlling for the prior-year hail base, consensus revisions, asphalt cost, housing starts, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Everyone knows storms matter: OC and Beacon discuss 'storm demand' qualitatively each quarter and sell-side reads ARMA shingle shipment statistics, which are quarterly and typically published after OC reports (REASONED). Verisk and CoreLogic sell hail-exposure and roof-claim analytics mainly to insurers (REASONED). A quantified hail-exposure times rooftop-density index tied to the building-products print is uncommon in sell-side work (REASONED), so the sign is priced but the magnitude is not.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Verisk, CoreLogic (hail and claims analytics for insurers; REASONED). None of the brief's listed alt-data vendors.. Why ours differs: Free, daily, radar-derived swaths weighted by rooftop density and lagged to the re-roofing cycle, aimed at a building-products revenue surprise rather than insurer losses.
- Vendors found by reviewer: TickerTrends: Roofing sector alternative data overview (blog exists; reachability 200; content not read) - https://blog.tickertrends.io/p/roofing-sector-alternative-data-overview
- Literature found by reviewer: Roofing Contractor: Texas remains No. 1 for most major hail events (storms roughly 30% of annual roofing demand) - https://www.roofingcontractor.com/articles/100873-texas-remains-no-1-for-most-major-hail-events; Benzinga: Baird's roofing survey positive for Beacon Roofing, Carlisle (hail and wind reports tied to shipments, 2016) - https://www.benzinga.com/analyst-ratings/analyst-color/16/07/8189798/bairds-roofing-survey-positive-for-beacon-roofing-carlis; Owens Corning Q2 2024 earnings call transcript (storm demand commentary) - https://www.marketbeat.com/earnings/reports/2024-8-6-owens-corning-stock/; Roofing Contractor: Owens Corning reports declines in Q3 from slowed demand - https://www.roofingcontractor.com/articles/101531-owens-corning-reports-declines-in-q3-from-slowed-demand; futuresearch.ai aggregator: why US roofing shingle sales are declining ('uniquely quiet' 2025 storm season; verify against filings) - https://futuresearch.ai/app/p/a/why-are-us-roofing-shingle-sales-declining-and; JLC: Turning weather data into action, how contractors leverage storm trends - https://www.jlconline.com/projects/disaster-resistant-building/turning-weather-data-into-action-how-contractors-can-leverage-storm-trends/
- Data sources:
  - NCEI Severe Weather Data Inventory, NEXRAD hail signatures (nx3hail) with tilesum and count statistics [DATA (TESTED HERE 200; JSON with CENTERLAT, CENTERLON, FCOUNT, DAY, SHAPE)] https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/swdiws/json/nx3hail/20250601:20250701?stat=tilesum:-97,33 (update: Daily; history: At least 2008 (TESTED HERE: 70,931 cells for 2008-06-01 to 06-08))
  - NOAA SPC daily hail reports CSV (cross-check only) [DATA (TESTED HERE 200 for 2025-06-15 and 2010-06-15 files)] https://www.spc.noaa.gov/climo/reports/250615_rpts_hail.csv (update: Daily, same-day preliminary; history: At least 2010 (TESTED HERE); earlier REASONED)
  - NCEI Storm Events Database bulk CSV (verified events, lagged) [DATA (TESTED HERE 200; files through d2026_c20261026)] https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/pub/data/swdi/stormevents/csvfiles/ (update: Monthly with ~2-3 month lag (REASONED); history: 1950 onward (REASONED))
  - Census API (ACS housing units per tract for rooftop weighting) [KEY (per Phase 0 file)] https://api.census.gov/data/key_signup.html (update: Annual ACS; history: ACS 5-year vintages)
- Reviewer source checks: https://www.spc.noaa.gov/climo/reports/250615_rpts_hail.csv -> 200 TESTED HERE: 78 lines; columns Time,Size,Location,County,State,Lat,Lon,Comments.; https://www.spc.noaa.gov/climo/reports/100615_rpts_hail.csv -> 200 History to at least 2010 confirmed.; https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/swdiws/json/nx3hail/20250601:20250701?stat=tilesum:-9… -> 200 JSON tile sums by day.; https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/swdiws/json/nx3hail/20080601:20080608?stat=count -> 200 COUNT 70,931; history to at least 2008.; https://www.ncei.noaa.gov/pub/data/swdi/stormevents/csvfiles/ -> 200 Listing includes StormEvents_details d2024-d2026 files.; https://api.census.gov/data/key_signup.html -> KEY (per Phase 0 file; not re-probed) Free key.
- Cost: $0 (free Census key).
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 3 / 1. Confidence: 25%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Storm demand is the most-discussed swing factor in roofing and is partly captured by distributor commentary mid-quarter; asphalt cost pass-through and pricing often matter more than volume; public hail reports are biased toward populated areas, double-counting density; claim settlement and roofer labour constraints spread demand unpredictably; roofing is only a part of OC's and none of several names' EBIT.
- Red team (reviewer): Storm demand is the most-discussed swing factor in roofing: sell-side surveys already cite hail and wind reports, OC and distributors describe storm activity every quarter and mid-quarter, so the sign is in consensus and the index may only reproduce what analysts already model; price and asphalt pass-through, distributor destocking and roofer labour constraints can swamp volume in any given print, and roofing is only part of OC's EBIT.

## N51 Sunbelt pool-permit counts from city and county open-data portals as a new-pool-construction nowcast

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Pool and irrigation: pool shells (Latham), pool equipment (Hayward), pool distribution (Pool Corp), with irrigation permits as a side read for Toro.. Target: revenue and guidance (both).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Dedupe to one building permit per project: Austin's POOL matches since 2025 split into 1,643 building, 1,499 electrical and 1,166 plumbing permits for the same projects (TESTED HERE), so raw counts triple-count. (2) Tighten matching: work_class New and permit_type Building with 'swimming pool/pool' in the description, excluding fence, heater, pool house, demolition, repair and remodel. (3) Add datasets located in this review: Dallas Socrata e7gq-4sah (updated 2026-11-10), San Antonio CKAN building-permits, Phoenix CKAN phoenix-az-building-permit-data; Mesa has no permit dataset in the Socrata catalog; Florida datasets (Miami-Dade, Orange County hubs reachable) still need identifying since the Sand States are ~66% of starts per Latham's 10-K (secondary source). (4) Benchmark the metro series monthly against the public NAHB Pool Construction Permit Index (Construction Monitor data); only use the signal if it leads or refines that index. (5) Primary names SWIM and HAYW; POOL secondary; verify SWIM average daily dollar volume >= $10M before trading. (6) Crowdedness lowered to 2 because the NAHB index and Construction Monitor already publish or sell permit counts; lead time revised to 20 days.
- Mechanism: Every in-ground pool needs a building permit, and large Sunbelt cities publish issued permits daily on Socrata or ArcGIS portals with description, sub-type and issue date. Weekly counts of pool permits across FL/TX/AZ/CA metros nowcast new-pool starts, which drive essentially all of Latham's volume (fiberglass and vinyl shells ship at dig stage, REASONED) and the new-construction share of Hayward's and Pool Corp's sales (roughly 20-25%, REASONED). Permits lead installation by two to eight weeks, so the quarter's permits map to the same and following quarter's revenue. TESTED HERE: Austin's permit dataset returns ~160-310 permits per month with 'POOL' in the description for 2024-2026, and Los Angeles' building-permit dataset returns ~250-390 per month.
- Industry context: New in-ground pool construction fell by roughly half from the 2021 peak and the public pool names trade on whether it is stabilising; permits are the earliest hard count because a pool cannot be dug without one. Texas, Florida, Arizona and California account for over half of US pool starts (REASONED), so a handful of metro portals cover much of the market.
- Measurement window: Permits issued during the fiscal quarter plus the preceding four weeks; portals update daily, so the final reading is available at quarter end, three to six weeks before SWIM/HAYW/POOL report.. Lead time: 20 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.2-0.3%: SWIM and HAYW primary, POOL secondary; TTC side read dropped.
- Hypothesis: A 10% or larger YoY change in deduplicated new-pool building permits across Austin, Los Angeles, Dallas, San Antonio, Phoenix and identified Florida metros over the fiscal quarter plus the preceding four weeks, measured 5 trading days before the release and conditional on the metro series not already being reflected in the latest public NAHB Pool Construction Permit Index, predicts a same-sign revenue surprise and T-1 to T+2 abnormal return versus the sector ETF for SWIM and HAYW (POOL secondary), controlling for consensus revisions, rain days, cooling-degree days, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Pool Corp and Hayward cite third-party pool-construction counts (P.K. Data, REASONED) that are quarterly, paid and lagged, and sell-side builds on those plus weather; Pool Corp's mid-quarter commentary leaks some of the signal. BuildFax (Verisk) and BuildZoom sell permit data (REASONED real vendors). Weekly metro-level permit flow timed to the SWIM/HAYW print is not widely used (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): BuildFax (Verisk), BuildZoom (permit data); P.K. Data (pool-industry research) - all REASONED.. Why ours differs: Free, daily, metro-level pool-permit counts available three to six weeks before the print; vendor data is quarterly or priced for enterprise and not pool-specific.
- Vendors found by reviewer: Construction Monitor pool permit data (daily leads, API/FTP; feeds NAHB Pool Construction Permit Index) - https://shop.constructionmonitor.com/pool-permit-data; PK Data (pool-industry permit research, cited by Aqua Magazine) - https://www.aquamagazine.com/news/article/15121536/pkdata-shrinking-to-greatness
- Literature found by reviewer: NAHB Eye on Housing: Pool permitting falls lower in 2025 (monthly Pool Construction Permit Index) - https://eyeonhousing.org/2026/01/pool-permitting-falls-lower-in-2025/; NAHB blog: Pool trends (Feb 2026) - https://www.nahb.org/blog/2026/02/pool-trends; Crosstown LA: Los Angeles's pool party may be coming to a close (LA pool permits, Aug 2025) - https://xtown.la/2025/08/18/pool-permits-los-angeles-decline/; Pool Magazine: Florida pool permits, a look at the numbers - https://www.poolmagazine.com/pool-news/florida-pool-permits-a-look-at-the-numbers/; Pool Magazine: Texas pool permits down third straight year - https://www.poolmagazine.com/pool-news/texas-pool-permits-down-third-straight-year-in-a-row/; Pool & Spa Marketing: Pool permits rise in 2025; market stabilizes after the pandemic boom - https://www.poolspamarketing.com/?p=37901; Pool Magazine: new pool construction has halved (Pool Corp starts ~120k in 2021 to under 60k in 2025; Latham 10-K Sand States ~66% of starts) - https://www.poolmagazine.com/pool-news/the-62-billion-paradox-new-pool-construction-has-halved-the-industry-has-never-been-steadier/

- Red team (reviewer): The NAHB Pool Construction Permit Index built on Construction Monitor data already gives a public monthly national read and management quotes PK Data, so a handful of Sunbelt portals adds metro granularity but may carry no information beyond the public index by the print; description matching over-counts (fences, heaters, demolitions, duplicate trade permits), new construction is only roughly 15-25% of POOL and HAYW sales (REASONED), rain days drive the quarter more than permits, and SWIM, the one pure play, is thin for the account size.

## N52 Municipal fleet-procurement approvals from the Legistar Web API (fire apparatus, refuse trucks, sweepers, ambulances, buses) as an OEM order nowcast

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Specialty vehicles and municipal machinery: REV Group, Oshkosh (Pierce, McNeilus), Federal Signal (Elgin, Vactor), Blue Bird, Alamo Group, Douglas Dynamics, Miller Industries.. Target: guidance (orders/backlog), with revenue where backlogs are short (sweepers, snowplows, tow trucks) (forward).
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: Coverage is too thin and non-random to nowcast OEM orders: across 11 keyless Legistar clients (Columbus, Denver, Long Beach, Mesa, Milwaukee, San Antonio, Fresno, Sacramento, Oakland, Pittsburgh, San Jose) the fire-apparatus/pumper/fire-engine title filter yields only about 20-28 matters per year combined, a third naming an OEM in the title, while Phoenix shows 5 'fire apparatus' matters in eight years (two of them maintenance and loan agreements) and 8 of 20 large-city slugs (Chicago, LA, Miami, Jacksonville, San Diego, Fort Worth, Tucson, Cincinnati) return 500 or need tokens; the industry ships thousands of apparatus a year (REASONED). Fire backlogs run two to three years, so a quarter's municipal approvals are marginal to the print, and OEMs disclose orders and backlog themselves; cooperative-purchasing contracts (Sourcewell, HGACBuy, state contracts) mask brand in titles. Refuse trucks, sweepers and ambulances show similarly sparse counts (11, 11 and 17 ever in Phoenix). Blue Bird's EPA Clean School Bus awards are public press events already in consensus.
- Mechanism: City councils must approve vehicle purchases above thresholds by resolution or ordinance, and Granicus Legistar exposes each matter's title, text, dates, status and often cost through a public JSON API per client (TESTED HERE: Phoenix matters filtered on 'fire apparatus' returned adopted ordinances with dates and requester). Counting approvals and dollar totals naming OEM brands (Pierce, E-ONE, KME, Ferrara, Spartan, McNeilus, Elgin, Vactor, Blue Bird, Horton, Wheeled Coach) during the quarter nowcasts municipal order intake by OEM, which feeds the reported orders and backlog that REV Group, Oshkosh Vocational and Federal Signal shares trade on (REASONED). For Blue Bird, EPA Clean School Bus award lists and school-board agendas are the analogue.
- Industry context: Fire trucks, refuse trucks, sweepers and school buses are bought almost entirely by local governments, whose purchases are approved in public meetings and recorded in agenda-management systems. The makers' shares react to quarterly order and backlog numbers because production is sold out years ahead and pricing is set at order.
- Measurement window: Matters passed within the fiscal quarter (plus matters introduced but not yet passed as a pipeline); the API is live, so the final reading is available at quarter end, five to six weeks before REV reports and about four weeks before FSS/OSK.. Lead time: 35 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.6%: 7-9 names: REVG (~$2B), OSK (~$8B), FSS (~$6B), BLBD (~$1.5B), ALG (~$2B), PLOW (~$0.7B), MLR (~$0.6B), SHYF, TEX (utilities); caps REASONED.
- Hypothesis: A 25% or larger YoY increase in the count and dollar value of council-approved purchases naming a company's brands across Legistar clients during the fiscal quarter, measured 5 trading days before the release, predicts a positive orders/backlog surprise and positive T-1 to T+2 abnormal return vs XLI for REVG, OSK, FSS and BLBD, controlling for consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: OEMs report orders and backlog quarterly; trade press covers large apparatus orders; FAMA shares fire-apparatus industry statistics with members (REASONED). Sell-side tracks municipal budgets qualitatively. No vendor is known to aggregate council procurement approvals by OEM (UNKNOWN); GovSpend and DemandStar sell government purchasing data to suppliers (REASONED). Fire-apparatus backlogs run two to three years (REASONED), so a quarter's order flow is not in consensus revenue but drives the backlog and guidance reaction.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): GovSpend, DemandStar/Onvia (government purchasing data for suppliers; REASONED). None of the brief's listed alt-data vendors.. Why ours differs: Free API access with brand-level resolution timed to the print; vendor products serve bid-chasing suppliers, not a per-OEM quarterly order count.
- Vendors found by reviewer: cpo.soar.com civic-meeting summaries (publishes council fire-apparatus approvals with vendor and price) - https://cpo.soar.com/articles/6088460/Clearwater/Pinellas-County/Florida/Council-approves-438-million-purchase-of-three-fire-apparatus-funding-adjusted-to-avoid-future-general-fund-commitments
- Literature found by reviewer: Fresno Legistar item: two Smeal apparatus via dealer, noting Smeal and Spartan are REV Group subsidiaries (brand in body text, not title) - https://agendas.fresno.gov/en/legislation/id-25-1608-20587; FireRescue1: Ariz. city approves $16M bulk purchase of fire apparatus - https://www.firerescue1.com/fire-apparatus/ariz-city-approves-16m-bulk-purchase-of-fire-apparatus; citymeetings.nyc: NYC council hearing on fire apparatus purchase and replacement challenges - https://citymeetings.nyc/city-council/2025-03-14-1000-am-committee-on-fire-and-emergency-management/chapter/fire-apparatus-purchase-and-replacement-challenges; cpo.soar.com: Westerville authorizes Pierce pumper via State of Ohio cooperative contract, delivery ~3 years - https://cpo.soar.com/articles/6371411/Ohio/Westerville-council-authorizes-purchase-of-Pierce-pumper-to-replace-Engine-112-delivery-estimated-3-years
- Data sources:
  - Legistar Web API matters endpoint with OData filter (per client; Seattle and Phoenix verified keyless) [DATA] https://webapi.legistar.com/v1/phoenix/matters?$filter=substringof('fire apparatus',MatterTitle)&$top=50 (update: Live as agendas are published (TESTED HERE: filter query works for Phoenix; the 'nyc' client returned 403, so some clients require a token).; history: Back to 2015-2016 for probed clients (per Phase 0 file).)
  - CivicPlus AgendaCenter (HTML/RSS plus PDF agendas) for non-Legistar municipalities [DATA] https://www.loudoun.gov/AgendaCenter (update: Per meeting; history: Agendas back to 2016 (per file).)
  - EPA Clean School Bus Program awardee lists (Blue Bird) [UNPROBED] https://www.epa.gov/cleanschoolbus/awarded-clean-school-bus-program-funding (update: Per funding round (REASONED).; history: 2022 onward (REASONED).)
- Reviewer source checks: https://webapi.legistar.com/v1/phoenix/matters?$filter=substringof('fire appara… -> 200 TESTED HERE: 5 matters ever (2017-2024), including a maintenance contract and a…; https://webapi.legistar.com/v1/{client}/matters?$filter=substringof('fire appar… -> 200 for 13 of 20 slugs; 500 for chicago, tucson, cincinnati, sandiego, fortworth, lacity, miami, jacksonville TESTED HERE: seattle 0, sanjose 8, oakland 10, longbeach 101, mesa 60, denver 6…; https://www.loudoun.gov/AgendaCenter -> DATA (per Phase 0 file; not re-probed) HTML/RSS plus PDF agendas.; https://www.epa.gov/cleanschoolbus/awarded-clean-school-bus-program-funding -> 404 Candidate URL is dead; https://www.epa.gov/cleanschoolbus/clean-school-bus-prog…
- Cost: $0.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 1 / 1. Confidence: 8%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Legistar covers a few hundred mostly large cities, a small and non-random slice of roughly 19,000 municipalities and 13,000 school districts; cooperative-purchasing contracts (Sourcewell, HGACBuy) and many small towns never surface; brand attribution from titles is noisy (dealer names instead of OEMs); and because orders already sit in multi-year backlogs, a quarter's approvals move the stock only at the margin.
- Red team (reviewer): A sample of a few dozen council approvals per year across a non-random set of large Legistar cities cannot track an OEM's order intake of thousands of units, one bulk order (e.g., a $16M Arizona purchase) swamps the quarterly count, brand attribution lives in attachments rather than titles for most clients, and because orders sit in two-to-three-year backlogs that the OEMs disclose, a quarter's approvals carry no information about the print beyond what is already reported.

## N53 FracFocus job-level disclosures: pumper, proppant and water volumes by supplier as an oilfield-services nowcast

- Category: Digital. Sector focus: Pressure pumping, frac sand, produced/sourced water logistics, completion chemicals.. Target: revenue, EPS surprise (nowcast).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: (1) Data: replace the blocked fracfocus.org page with the reachable bulk zip on www.fracfocusdata.org (TESTED HERE, 444 MB, refreshed 2026-11-11); start archiving a weekly snapshot immediately, because the dump has no reliable submission-date field for every row and the as-of backfill curve cannot be reconstructed from a single final snapshot (any backtest on final data is look-ahead on month 3). (2) Universe: drop the water names (no water-supplier attribution exists in the registry), Innospec, Solaris, Core Lab and Cactus; keep pumpers and AESI. (3) Measurement: months 1-2 only, with month 3 used only after the lag curve has been measured on at least two archived quarters. (4) Control: add total basin job count as a regressor so the test isolates supplier share, which is the only part not already in vendor and spread-count products. (5) signal_noise lowered 3->2 for supplier-field inconsistency and the pricing (not volume) driver of pumper revenue; confidence 25->20. Vendor and literature web check could not be run this turn (search budget exhausted); Enverus, Rystad and Primary Vision claims in the spec remain unverified.
- Mechanism: Every hydraulically fractured well in the major producing states must be disclosed on the FracFocus registry with job start and end dates, operator, total base water volume and an ingredient list in which each ingredient carries a supplier name (often the pressure pumper or the proppant/chemical supplier) and mass. Counting jobs and summing proppant mass and water volume by supplier per week nowcasts completions revenue for pumpers and tons or barrels for sand and water names. Texas ties the disclosure to the completion report, due within about 30 days of completion (16 TAC 3.29; REASONED), other states allow up to 60 days (REASONED), so an as-of-date backfill curve must be modeled.
- Industry context: Completions, not drilling, drive revenue for pumpers, sand and water companies, and activity swings within quarters as operators exhaust or extend budgets. Each frac job consumes thousands of tons of sand and hundreds of thousands of barrels of water, so job-level disclosures sum to supplier volumes.
- Measurement window: At 5 days before earnings (about 30-40 days after quarter end), months 1-2 of the quarter are near-complete and month 3 is roughly half reported; the point-in-time estimate must be corrected with the historical reporting-lag curve.. Lead time: 5 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.5-1%: 6-8 names. Pressure pumpers LBRT, PUMP, ACDC, PTEN (NexTier) via supplier-attributed job counts; proppant AESI only where the sand supplier field names it; drop WTTR, ARIS (FracFocus carries base-water volume with no water supplier field), IOSP, SEI, CLB, WHD (no attributable rows).
- Hypothesis: For pressure pumpers (LBRT, PUMP, ACDC, PTEN) and, where the supplier field names it, Atlas (AESI), a change of at least 10% in FracFocus jobs attributed to the company as ingredient supplier (pumpers) or in proppant mass on jobs naming it as sand supplier (AESI), over months 1-2 of the quarter versus the same months of the prior quarter, measured 5 trading days before earnings on the as-of weekly snapshot and corrected with a disclosure-lag curve estimated from archived snapshots, predicts the sign of the revenue surprise and the close T-1 to close T+2 abnormal return versus OIH, after controlling for total FracFocus job count in the same basins (the crowded activity factor), consensus revisions, the quarter's WTI change, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Primary Vision's weekly frac spread count is widely circulated, and Enverus and Rystad sell completions datasets built partly from FracFocus, so crew-count activity is largely priced (REASONED). The narrower slice, supplier-attributed proppant tons and water barrels and per-job intensities, is less commonly built, and small-cap sand/water names get thin coverage.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Enverus, Rystad Energy, Primary Vision (frac spread count).. Why ours differs: Supplier-attributed volumes (proppant tons for Atlas, water barrels for Select/Aris, job counts per pumper) instead of crew counts; free; explicit as-of backfill modeling for a point-in-time estimate.
- Data sources:
  - FracFocus bulk data download (registry is public domain, REASONED). TESTED HERE: returns HTTP 403 from the research container with both curl and browser user-agents, so the monthly bulk CSV/SQL dump must be fetched from another network or by a human browser session. [UNPROBED] https://fracfocus.org/data-download (update: continuous disclosures; bulk dump refreshed roughly weekly (REASONED); history: 2011+ (REASONED))
- Reviewer source checks: https://fracfocus.org/data-download -> 403 Bot-blocked from the research container with a browser-like UA; fracfocus.org r…; https://www.fracfocusdata.org/digitaldownload/FracFocusCSV.zip -> 206 TESTED HERE: range GET returns 206 Partial Content, content-type application/x-…; https://www.rrc.texas.gov/resource-center/research/data-sets-available-for-down… -> 200 Texas RRC data-sets page loads (completion reports are a Texas-only cross-check…
- Cost: $0.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 20%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): The supplier field is inconsistently filled (often the operator or a placeholder), the disclosure lag varies by state and the completeness curve is unstable, vendor datasets already include FracFocus so top analysts' estimates may embed it, and the registry is blocked from the research container.
- Red team (reviewer): Pumper revenue surprises in 2025-26 are driven by frac pricing and fleet idling decisions that management guides, not by job counts, and the activity component of job counts is already embedded in consensus through spread-count services and vendor datasets built on this same registry; the residual (supplier share) is both small and noisily attributed because many disclosures list the operator or a placeholder as supplier. Without archived vintages, any backtest uses final data and overstates month-3 completeness (look-ahead). (Vendor/literature web check not run: search budget exhausted this turn.)

## N54 Sentinel-2 stockpile footprint and ship presence at Core's Baltimore terminal and Warrior's Mobile pad as an export-shipment nowcast

- Category: Physical. Sector focus: Coal producers with owned or dedicated export terminals (met and thermal export).. Target: revenue, margin (inventory), EPS surprise (nowcast).
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: Two-name universe with a sampling design that cannot measure the quantity it claims: a Panamax/Capesize loads in 1-3 days, and the probe shows roughly one clear Sentinel-2 look per week (6 in 41 days over Curtis Bay), so quarterly berth-occupancy days are estimated from about 12 samples with a standard error larger than the 15% threshold. Both terminals also handle third-party coal (CONSOL Marine Terminal takes other producers' tons; McDuffie is a multi-user Alabama State Port Authority terminal; REASONED), so throughput is not issuer-specific. Pad area without height is a crude tonnage proxy, and met-coal revenue surprises are dominated by realized price and contract timing, which are guided. No live AIS is available at $0 (MarineCadastre lags a year). Vendor/literature web check not run this turn (search budget exhausted).
- Mechanism: Core Natural Resources owns the CONSOL Marine Terminal in Baltimore (Curtis Bay), the export outlet for its Pennsylvania Mining Complex and Leer met coal, and Warrior Met Coal ships essentially all output through the McDuffie terminal in Mobile. Sentinel-2 (10 m, 5-day revisit) resolves the stockpile pad area and bulk carriers at berth; pad-area change plus berth-occupancy days over a quarter nowcasts terminal throughput (shipments) versus mine output (inventory build or draw). Exporters recognize revenue on shipment, not production, so quarterly shipments can diverge materially from steady output. TESTED HERE: Planetary Computer STAC returned 6 Sentinel-2 L2A scenes with under 30% cloud over Curtis Bay between 1 October and 10 November 2026.
- Industry context: Coal exporters book revenue when cargoes load, and loading cadence depends on contracts, weather and rail, so quarterly shipments can diverge from steady mine output. Ownership of a single terminal makes the physical measure clean.
- Measurement window: Full quarter; last clear scene typically within 5 days of quarter end; processing is immediate; earnings follow 30-45 days after quarter end.. Lead time: 25 days.
- Universe coverage: <0.2%: 2 names (CNR, HCC); AMR only via a shared public pier.
- Hypothesis: An increase of at least 15% in quarter-average berth-occupancy days (Sentinel-2 ship detections) together with a decline in stockpile pad area at Core's Baltimore terminal versus the prior quarter, measured 25 days before earnings, predicts a positive export-shipment and revenue surprise and a positive close T-1 to close T+2 abnormal return versus XLE, controlling for the quarter's met-coal benchmark price change, consensus revisions, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Satellite stockpile products exist for large commodity hubs (RS Metrics MetalSignals, Kayrros for crude tanks) and Chinese port coal stocks are widely tracked; UNKNOWN whether any vendor covers CONSOL Marine Terminal or the McDuffie met pad. Export tons by customs district appear in Census trade data about six weeks after month end (crowded but late). Company guidance on export tons covers part of the signal.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): RS Metrics, Kayrros, Orbital Insight (now within Privateer; REASONED) for stockpiles generally; Kpler and Vortexa for dry-bulk AIS.. Why ours differs: Facility-specific, free imagery, combines pad area with berth occupancy, targets small caps rather than global commodity balances.
- Data sources:
  - Microsoft Planetary Computer STAC search (sentinel-2-l2a; TESTED HERE for bbox [-76.60,39.20,-76.54,39.26], Oct-Nov 2026) [DATA] https://planetarycomputer.microsoft.com/api/stac/v1/search (update: every 5 days per tile (2-3 days with both satellites); history: 2015-06 to present)
  - Element84 Earth Search STAC (mirror of Sentinel-2 COGs on AWS) [DATA] https://earth-search.aws.element84.com/v1/collections (update: same as Sentinel-2; history: 2015+)
  - NOAA MarineCadastre AIS daily zips (historical ship calls at the terminal for backtest calibration only; files lag about a year) [DATA] https://coast.noaa.gov/htdata/CMSP/AISDataHandler/2024/AIS_2024_01_01.zip (update: annual batch; history: 2010-2025 referenced)
