- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Many large plants sit on intrastate pipelines or have two or three supply meters (some behind LDCs) that are not posted, so the mapping may be incomplete and the ratio to output unstable; postings can be aggregated or masked for confidentiality at some points; and for CF the market already prices outages because the company and trade press (Argus, CRU) report them within days, leaving little residual by the print.
- Red team (reviewer): Scheduled quantity at a point is a nomination, not metered consumption, and for multi-meter or intrastate-fed plants it measures only a fraction of input; the resulting 'utilisation' series would load on announced turnarounds and on regional gas price spreads, both already public, and any apparent earnings edge would be a proxy for the benchmark product price move over the quarter.

## G05 AD/CVD determination calendar and duty-rate magnitude from the Federal Register API, paired with Census import unit values, as an in-quarter pricing and import-competition signal for domestic metals, building-products and chemicals producers

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Materials and industrials: steel products, aluminum extrusions, wood cabinets, tires, lumber, titanium dioxide and other chemicals. Target: EPS surprise, margin, guidance (both).
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: The determination notice is itself the price-moving event: the cash-deposit rate is public and traded on publication day, analysts revise within days, and by the earnings date the magnitude is in consensus, so what remains is post-news drift, a proxy for analyst-revision momentum rather than a nowcast of the quarter. The within-quarter effect is diluted by contracts and inventories, Census unit-value confirmation arrives with a 6-week lag (one month visible before most prints), and the cross-section is a handful of events a year with small covered-product shares for most of the 75-90 names listed (CLF, STLD, CMC, MHK are diversified). In 2025-26 Section 232/301 tariff actions dominate pricing of the same products (REASONED), swamping AD/CVD case effects. Data is lawful and keyless, but the mechanism does not produce an earnings-window signal.
- Mechanism: Commerce's preliminary and final AD/CVD determinations set cash-deposit rates on named exporters from the publication date, and critical-circumstances findings make them retroactive 90 days; importers stop ordering or re-price within weeks, and the domestic producer's spot price and order book move inside the quarter in which the determination lands. The direction is known once the case calendar is published, but the rate magnitude (which can be 5% or 300%) and the import-volume and unit-value response in the quarter are not, and both are observable: the rate from the Federal Register notice and the response from Census monthly HS imports. Applies to domestic producers with a large share of sales in a covered product and to listed importers or distributors of the same product.
- Industry context: Antidumping and countervailing duties are imposed product-by-product and country-by-country after a petition by domestic producers; Commerce sets the rate in scheduled preliminary and final determinations and CBP collects cash deposits from the publication date, so imports of the covered product often fall sharply and domestic prices rise within the same quarter. Rates vary enormously (single digits to several hundred percent) and the petitioners' public guidance rarely quantifies the effect on their own quarter.
- Measurement window: Determination notices are dated events within the quarter (the Federal Register notice is final the day it publishes); the import-volume response comes from Census HS data with a ~6-week lag, so before the print it covers months 1-2 of the quarter. Lead time is the gap between the last relevant notice and the print, typically 2-8 weeks.. Lead time: 20 days.
- Universe coverage: ~6%: about 75-90 names. Steel and aluminum: Insteel (IIIN), Northwest Pipe (NWPI), Century Aluminum (CENX), Kaiser (KALU), Constellium (CSTM), Olympic Steel (ZEUS), Ryerson (RYI), TimkenSteel/Metallus (MTUS), Commercial Metals (CMC), Steel Dynamics (STLD), Cleveland-Cliffs (CLF), Worthington Steel (WS), Atkore (ATKR), Mueller (MLI); building products: American Woodmark (AMWD), MasterBrand (MBC), JELD-WEN (JELD), Louisiana-Pacific (LPX), Boise Cascade (BCC), UFP (UFPI), Quanex (NX), Mohawk (MHK); tires: Goodyear (GT); chemicals: Tronox (TROX), Kronos (KRO), Chemours (CC); paper: Clearwater (CLW); solar: Array (ARRY), Shoals (SHLS), First Solar near the cap; mattresses: Sleep Number (SNBR), Tempur Sealy (TPX).
- Hypothesis: A preliminary or final AD/CVD cash-deposit rate that is at least 20 percentage points above the preceding stage's rate (or the petition's alleged margin for a preliminary determination), or an affirmative critical-circumstances finding, published in the 60 days before the print on a product that is at least 30% of a domestic producer's revenue, predicts a positive EPS surprise versus final consensus and a positive T-1 to T+1 abnormal return versus XLB/XLI for the producer, and the opposite for listed importers of the product, controlling for consensus revisions over the same 60 days, the relevant commodity price change, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Trade lawyers, the petitioning producers and their sell-side analysts follow each case, and the headline outcome (affirmative/negative) is in consensus by the time the schedule is published; Panjiva/ImportGenius users see import volumes with a few weeks' lag. What is not modelled is the cross-section: dozens of simultaneous cases with rates that surprise by tens of points, and the Census unit-value response in the quarter, which analysts cite qualitatively rather than quantify (REASONED). Priced-in share is high for the direction, low for magnitude and timing.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Panjiva (S&P Global) and ImportGenius sell bill-of-lading import data; no vendor is known to sell an AD/CVD determination-magnitude event panel for equities (UNKNOWN).. Why ours differs: Event-level duty-rate magnitudes parsed from the Federal Register full text, joined to the company's covered-product share and to Census unit values, built as a scheduled-event panel rather than qualitative commentary; keyless and same-day.
- Data sources:
  - Federal Register API filtered to International Trade Administration notices (antidumping, countervailing, preliminary/final determination, cash deposit); records carry docket_ids (e.g. A-570-106), publication_date, abstract, full_text_xml_url [DATA] https://www.federalregister.gov/api/v1/documents.json?per_page=100&order=newest&conditions[agencies][]=international-trade-administration&conditions[type][]=NOTICE&conditions[term]=antidumping (update: Daily at 8:45 ET (REASONED); TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: 200; 354 ITA 'preliminary determination' documents since 2025-10-01; 6,830 ITA 'cash deposit' documents since 2015; single-document JSON exposes docket_ids and full-text XML for rate-table parsing; history: 1994+ via the API (REASONED); 2015+ verified by count)
  - Commerce ITA AD/CVD proceedings page (case list, scope, rates) [UNPROBED] https://www.trade.gov/data-visualization/adcvd-proceedings (update: TESTED HERE: 200 (243 KB HTML; embedded data not parsed); history: All active orders (REASONED))
  - ACCESS (Commerce AD/CVD case docket; Angular SPA, calendar path not found) [UNPROBED] https://access.trade.gov/ (update: TESTED HERE: 200 (SPA shell); frn_schedule.aspx 404; history: UNKNOWN)
  - CBP CSMS messages (implementation of cash-deposit instructions) - only the GovDelivery subscription page is reachable; the RSS returned 406 and cbp.gov is Akamai-blocked from the container [UNPROBED] https://public.govdelivery.com/accounts/USDHSCBP/subscriber/new (update: TESTED HERE: subscription page 200; bulletins.rss 406; cbp.gov 403. Use a dedicated mailbox subscription instead of scraping.; history: n/a)
  - Census international trade API, HS imports by country (volume and unit-value response) [KEY] https://api.census.gov/data/timeseries/intltrade/imports/hs?get=CTY_CODE,GEN_VAL_MO,GEN_QY1_MO&time=2026-08 (update: Monthly, ~6-week lag; history: 2013+)
  - USITC DataWeb (tariff-line imports with AD/CVD case flags) [KEY] https://dataweb.usitc.gov/ (update: Monthly; history: 1989+ (REASONED))
  - USITC active investigations page and EDIS (injury determinations) - blocked from the container (403) [UNPROBED] https://www.usitc.gov/trade_remedy/731_ad_701_cvd/investigations/active.htm (update: TESTED HERE: 403 Akamai for usitc.gov and edis.usitc.gov; history: n/a)
- Reviewer source checks: https://www.federalregister.gov/api/v1/documents.json?per_page=100&order=newest… -> DATA (phase0; candidate TESTED counts) Keyless; full-text XML for rate tables.; https://www.trade.gov/data-visualization/adcvd-proceedings -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Embedded case data not parsed.; https://access.trade.gov/ -> 200 (TESTED HERE) SPA shell only.; https://public.govdelivery.com/accounts/USDHSCBP/subscriber/new -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Email subscription page; CSMS RSS 406 and cbp.gov 403 per candidate.; https://www.usitc.gov/trade_remedy/731_ad_701_cvd/investigations/active.htm -> 404 (TESTED HERE); usitc.gov root 200 Host answered today (not the 403 seen earlier) but the path is gone; EDIS still…; https://api.census.gov/data/timeseries/intltrade/imports/hs?get=CTY_CODE,GEN_VA… -> KEY (phase0; free Census key) ~6-week lag: only month 1 (sometimes 2) of the quarter is visible before a 3-5 …; https://dataweb.usitc.gov/ -> KEY (phase0; free account) Not exercised.
- Cost: $0 (Federal Register API keyless; Census key free; USITC DataWeb account free).
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 1 / 1. Confidence: 6%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): The schedule and the petitioners' arguments are public for months, so the equity reaction happens on the determination day, not at earnings; the within-quarter revenue effect is diluted by contracts and inventories; and the cross-section of affected mid-caps is small for any one case, so the signal is a handful of events a year.
- Red team (reviewer): Any apparent earnings-window effect would be the continuation of the determination-day repricing and the associated analyst revisions, i.e. a news-momentum and revisions factor; the sample of events is too small and clustered by product and year to separate that from a genuine surprise.

## G06 Japan customs provisional trade statistics (first-20-days and monthly, by HS line to the United States) as a sell-in nowcast for US marine, powersports, golf and equipment names dependent on, or competing with, Japanese exports

- Category: Second-order. Sector focus: Consumer discretionary hardgoods (boats, outboards, motorcycles, golf), industrial equipment dealers and rental. Target: revenue, margin (promotion), guidance (nowcast).
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: No in-universe name has a sign-unambiguous, revenue-material dependence on a Japan-dominant HS line: the cleanest line (8407.21 outboards) maps to Marine Products (below the $500M cap) and to Brunswick as a competitor with ambiguous sign; Harley/Polaris sell different segments from Japanese motorcycle exports; Acushnet/Callaway source clubs mainly from China/Vietnam, not Japan (REASONED); Komatsu/Kubota also build in the US. Exports in a month are shipments into the Japanese OEMs' US subsidiaries' inventory, so alignment with the US buyer's quarter is loose, and the Japanese OEMs disclose North American unit sales in their own results. The HS-by-country detail is only in e-Stat (free appId) or a form-driven search, publishing ~20 days after month end, which leaves 5-10 days before early printers (BC, PII, HOG) rather than the 14 claimed. Lawful and cheap, but the mapping is too weak to stake effort on.
- Mechanism: Japan publishes provisional export values and quantities by 9-digit HS line and destination country about 20 days after month end, with a first-20-days flash around day 10, far ahead of US Census import data; for product lines where Japan is the dominant origin and the US buyer or competitor is a listed mid-cap, the export series is a direct sell-in measure for the quarter. Examples: HS 8407.21 outboard motors (Yamaha, Honda, Suzuki, Tohatsu ship to US boat builders and dealers), HS 8711 motorcycles (Honda, Kawasaki, Yamaha, Suzuki versus Harley-Davidson and Polaris), HS 9506.31 golf clubs (Srixon, Bridgestone, Mizuno versus Acushnet and Topgolf Callaway), HS 8429.52 excavators and HS 8701.9x tractors (Komatsu, Hitachi, Kubota into rental fleets and dealers). Rising outboard exports mean boat builders are taking engines for current production; rising Japanese motorcycle or club exports mean more competing supply and promotional pressure for the US brand.
- Industry context: Japan is the dominant source of outboard engines, mid-size motorcycles, premium golf equipment and compact construction and agricultural machinery sold in the United States, and its customs service publishes export detail faster than almost any other country. US boat builders and dealers buy those engines for current-quarter production, while US brands in motorcycles and golf price against the arriving Japanese supply.
- Measurement window: Three calendar months of the US company's fiscal quarter; month 3 provisional by HS line is available ~20 days after quarter end (REASONED from Japan customs release calendar; TESTED HERE: 'October 2026 first 20 days provisional' XML published 2026-11-10), and most target companies print 4-6 weeks after quarter end. JPY/USD must be used to convert values; quantities are preferred.. Lead time: 10 days.
- Universe coverage: ~2.5%: about 30 names. Marine: Brunswick (BC, Mercury competes with Japanese outboards), Malibu Boats (MBUU), MasterCraft (MCFT), Marine Products (MPX, uses Yamaha outboards), MarineMax (HZO), OneWater (ONEW). Powersports: Harley-Davidson (HOG), Polaris (PII), BRP (DOOO). Golf: Acushnet (GOLF), Topgolf Callaway (MODG). Equipment dealers/rental and OEM competitors: Herc (HRI), Custom Truck (CTOS), Titan Machinery (TITN), Terex (TEX), Oshkosh (OSK), Manitowoc (MTW), Astec (ASTE), AGCO, Alamo (ALG). Components: Fox Factory (FOXF, Shimano-adjacent bicycle demand). Mapping of each HS line to each name is REASONED and must be validated with the company's 10-K supplier/competitor disclosures.
- Hypothesis: A year-on-year change of at least 15% in Japan's provisional export quantity to the US for the mapped HS line(s) over the three months of the company's fiscal quarter, measured 20 days after quarter end (at least 10 days before the print), predicts a same-sign revenue surprise versus final consensus for the dependent buyers (boat builders, dealers, rental fleets) and an opposite-sign margin surprise for the domestic competitors (Harley, Polaris, Acushnet, Callaway), with a same-sign T-1 to T+1 abnormal return versus XLY/XLI, controlling for consensus revisions, the JPY/USD change over the quarter, sector, size and momentum.
- Who already knows: Japanese brokers and the Japanese OEMs' analysts read these lines for Yamaha, Honda and Komatsu; US consumer-durables analysts rely on company dealer-inventory commentary, SSI registration data (Statistical Surveys Inc., paid) for boats and powersports, Golf Datatech for golf, and Komatsu's monthly KOMTRAX (N36) for equipment. Japan's HS-line exports to the US are not, to my knowledge, in US mid-cap previews (REASONED). Priced-in share is low for the sell-in leg, moderate for the competitive-supply leg because the Japanese OEMs' own quarterly results (reported before many US peers) reveal regional sales.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Panjiva/ImportGenius (US bills of lading, lagged); Statistical Surveys Inc. and Golf Datatech sell retail registrations/sell-through (REASONED, paid). No vendor is known to package Japanese exporter-side statistics for US equities (UNKNOWN).. Why ours differs: Exporter-side official data published 3-4 weeks earlier than US import data, free, at HS-9 granularity with quantities, and mapped to the US names that buy or compete with those exact lines.
- Data sources:
  - Japan Ministry of Finance customs: latest provisional trade statistics press release (XML/HTML) - e.g. value of exports and imports, October 2026 first 20 days [UNPROBED] https://www.customs.go.jp/toukei/shinbun/trade-st_e/2026/2026102e.xml (update: First-20-days flash ~10 days after period end; full-month provisional ~20 days after month end (TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: XML 200, title 'Value of Exports and Imports October 2026 (First 20 days Provisional)', kohyoymd 'November 10, 2026'); history: Press-release archive by year on customs.go.jp (REASONED))
  - Japan customs Trade Statistics search (HS line by country, CSV output) [UNPROBED] https://www.customs.go.jp/toukei/srch/indexe.htm (update: TESTED HERE: index page 200; query interface not exercised; history: 1988+ (REASONED))
  - e-Stat API 3.0 for Trade Statistics of Japan (statsCode 00350300); free appId required [UNPROBED] https://api.e-stat.go.jp/rest/3.0/app/json/getStatsList?appId=YOUR_APPID&statsCode=00350300 (update: TESTED HERE: host reachable, returns STATUS 100 authentication error without appId (so KEY in practice); registration is free; history: Monthly HS-level tables for many years (REASONED))
  - e-Stat English file listings for trade statistics by commodity and country (download links need a browser session; not visible to curl) [UNPROBED] https://www.e-stat.go.jp/en/stat-search/files?page=1&layout=datalist&toukei=00350300&tstat=000001013141&cycle=1 (update: TESTED HERE: 200 but no file-download links in the raw HTML; history: UNKNOWN)
  - Census HS imports by country (US-side confirmation, ~6-week lag) and Eurostat Comext (EU-origin competitors, API 200 TESTED HERE, updated 2026-10-16) [KEY] https://api.census.gov/data/timeseries/intltrade/imports/hs?get=CTY_CODE,GEN_VAL_MO,GEN_QY1_MO&time=2026-08&I_COMMODITY=840721 (update: Monthly; history: 2013+)
- Reviewer source checks: https://www.customs.go.jp/toukei/shinbun/trade-st_e/2026/2026102e.xml -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Press-release XML carries aggregate values by region and broad commodity group …; https://www.customs.go.jp/toukei/srch/indexe.htm -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Form-driven search; CSV output not exercised; scriptability UNKNOWN.; https://api.e-stat.go.jp/rest/3.0/app/json/getStatsList?appId=YOUR_APPID&statsC… -> 200 but STATUS 100 authentication error without appId (TESTED HERE): KEY Free e-Stat appId required for HS-line-by-country monthly tables.; https://www.e-stat.go.jp/en/stat-search/files?page=1&layout=datalist&toukei=003… -> 200 (TESTED HERE) No download links in raw HTML.; https://api.census.gov/data/timeseries/intltrade/imports/hs?get=CTY_CODE,GEN_VA… -> KEY (phase0; free Census key) US-side confirmation, ~6-week lag.
- Cost: $0 (e-Stat appId free; Census key free).
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 7%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Exports in a month are shipments that land and are invoiced weeks later and are partly inventory moves by the Japanese OEMs' US subsidiaries, so quarter alignment is loose; many lines mix end-markets and destinations (US and Canada via the same distributor); the Japanese OEMs report their own regional results before US peers print, which may put the information in consensus; and FX swings dominate value series.
- Red team (reviewer): The series would load on JPY/USD and on the Japanese OEMs' own inventory and model-changeover cycles, not on the US company's quarter; a same-sign test on 'dependent buyers' versus an opposite-sign test on 'competitors' with ambiguous mappings gives enough degrees of freedom to find a spurious fit in a 30-name universe.

## G07 BTS port-level northbound truck and loaded-container crossings (and INEGI plant-level light-vehicle production where accessible) as a cross-border freight and Mexico-plant nowcast for truckload carriers and Mexico-concentrated auto suppliers

- Category: Second-order. Sector focus: Industrials (truckload, LTL and logistics with Mexico cross-border lanes) and consumer discretionary (auto suppliers with Mexican plants). Target: revenue, guidance (nowcast).
- Verdict: REJECT. Reject reason: The differentiating leg does not exist in the data: BTS resource keg4-3bc2 carries no loaded/empty container measures at all (TESTED HERE), leaving the headline northbound truck count that transport analysts and the press already cite each month, published with a ~6-week lag so only months 1-2 of the quarter are visible. The supplier leg is dead on arrival: INEGI is bot-blocked from the container and the plant-to-supplier mapping is undisclosed. What remains is a widely published macro freight series applied to carriers with 10-20% lane exposure, i.e. a Cass/DAT-style factor already in consensus; crowdedness set to 1 under the 'widely published' rule.
- Mechanism: US-Mexico freight for Laredo-centric carriers is a direct function of northbound loaded trucks and containers crossing at Laredo, Otay Mesa, El Paso, Nogales and Pharr, which CBP counts and BTS publishes monthly by port and measure; a carrier with 10-20% of revenue on those lanes sees its cross-border loads move with the port counts. Upstream, INEGI's registry of light-vehicle production by plant and model (published ~5 business days after month end) tells which Mexican assembly plants ran harder, and a supplier whose 10-K places its main plants next to those assembly plants (e.g. seat, axle, thermal and electronics makers in Saltillo, Silao, San Luis Potosi, Aguascalientes, Hermosillo) sells into that production in the same month. The two series together nowcast cross-border revenue for carriers and plant-level volume for suppliers.
- Industry context: Laredo is the largest US land port and handles the majority of US-Mexico truck freight, much of it automotive; several truckload carriers built Mexico businesses around it and disclose Mexico revenue separately. Mexican assembly plants are clustered in a few states and their suppliers sit within a few kilometres, so plant-level production is supplier-level volume.
- Measurement window: BTS: monthly, ~6-week lag (TESTED HERE: September 2026 rows present on 2026-11-11), so months 1-2 of the quarter are available 2-3 weeks before carriers that print 3-4 weeks after quarter end. INEGI (if API access is obtained): all three months of the quarter available ~5 business days after quarter end (REASONED), 3-5 weeks before supplier prints.. Lead time: 10 days.
- Universe coverage: ~3%: about 35-40 names. Carriers and logistics: Werner (WERN), Schneider (SNDR), Knight-Swift (KNX), Landstar (LSTR), ArcBest (ARCB), Forward Air (FWRD), Hub Group (HUBG), RXO, Universal Logistics (ULH), Covenant (CVLG), Heartland (HTLD), Marten (MRTN), P.A.M. Transport (PTSI, below the cap). Auto suppliers with Mexico-heavy footprints (REASONED from 10-K plant lists): Gentherm (THRM), Dana (DAN), American Axle (AXL), Visteon (VC), Lear (LEA), Adient (ADNT), BorgWarner (BWA), Modine (MOD), Gentex (GNTX), Standard Motor Products (SMP), Dorman (DORM), Stoneridge (SRI), Holley (HLLY).
- Hypothesis: A year-on-year change of at least 5% in northbound loaded truck containers summed over Laredo, Otay Mesa, El Paso, Nogales and Pharr for months 1-2 of the fiscal quarter, measured about 40 days after month 2 (at least 10 days before the print), predicts a same-sign revenue surprise versus final consensus for carriers with at least 10% Mexico cross-border revenue and a same-sign T-1 to T+1 abnormal return versus IYT, controlling for the Cass Freight Index change, diesel price change, consensus revisions, sector, size and momentum; the supplier leg is tested only once INEGI plant-level data are obtained.
- Who already knows: Transport analysts cite Laredo crossings qualitatively and the AMIA/INEGI monthly auto production headline is reported in the press the day it is released, so the Mexico auto production level is priced; some sell-side desks build Laredo truck indices (REASONED). The unpriced piece is the port-by-measure detail (loaded versus empty containers, rail versus truck) mapped to each carrier's lane mix, and plant-level production mapped to supplier plant lists, which requires a manual 10-K mapping that previews do not do (REASONED).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Panjiva/ImportGenius (ocean bills of lading; irrelevant to truck lanes). No vendor is known to sell port-level truck crossing panels for carrier equities (UNKNOWN). DAT and Cass sell freight-rate and volume indices (national, not cross-border).. Why ours differs: Free official counts at the port and measure level, weighted by each carrier's disclosed Mexico exposure; the INEGI leg maps assembly-plant output to supplier plants rather than using the national headline.
- Data sources:
  - BTS Border Crossing/Entry Data (Socrata keg4-3bc2): port_name, port_code, border, date, measure (Trucks, Loaded Truck Containers, Empty Truck Containers, Rail Containers, Personal Vehicles...), value [UNPROBED] https://data.bts.gov/resource/keg4-3bc2.json?$where=border='US-Mexico Border' AND measure in('Trucks','Loaded Truck Containers')&$order=date DESC&$limit=5000 (update: Monthly, ~6-week lag (TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: 200; latest date 2026-09-01 for Laredo and Cross Border Xpress); history: 1996+ (REASONED; dataset is the long-running BTS series))
  - INEGI Registro Administrativo de la Industria Automotriz de Vehiculos Ligeros (RAIAVL): monthly production, sales and exports by brand, model and plant [UNPROBED] https://www.inegi.org.mx/datosprimarios/iavl/ (update: Monthly, ~5 business days after month end (REASONED). TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: inegi.org.mx pages and the API demo path are blocked by Radware bot manager (validate.perfdrive.com) from the container; the open-data zip path guessed returned an HTML 404. Access needs the INEGI indicators API with a free token (https://www.inegi.org.mx/servicios/api_indicadores.html) from a non-container host, or a monthly manual download.; history: 2005+ (REASONED))
  - Eurostat Comext monthly trade API (EU-origin auto parts and vehicles to the US; lower value, ~6-7 week lag) [UNPROBED] https://ec.europa.eu/eurostat/api/dissemination/statistics/1.0/data/ext_st_eu27_2020sitc?format=JSON&partner=US&stk_flow=EXP&indic_et=TRD_VAL&sitc06=TOTAL&lastTimePeriod=3 (update: Monthly (TESTED HERE: 200, dataset updated 2026-10-16); history: 2002+ (REASONED))

  - Carrier 10-K segment disclosures of Mexico revenue share (weighting) [DATA] https://data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK0000793074.json (update: Annual; history: 2001+)

- Cost: $0 (BTS keyless; Socrata app token free; INEGI token free but must be obtained outside the container).
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 1 / 1 / 1. Confidence: 5%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Port counts are dominated by shippers and lanes the listed carriers do not serve, so carrier revenue may track their own contract wins more than the aggregate; the BTS lag means only two of three months are visible and the third can swing with tariff timing; and Mexico auto production is priced on the INEGI release day, with suppliers' sales mix across plants undisclosed, making the supplier leg a REASONED mapping with no history until INEGI access is obtained.
- Red team (reviewer): Aggregate bridge crossings count Mexican drayage movements for all shippers, so the series tracks total US-Mexico trade and tariff timing, not any listed carrier's loads or rates; a same-sign result would be a proxy for the industrial-production and freight cycle already captured by the Cass index, diesel and sector momentum.

## G08 Control-state item-level alcohol sales (Utah ABS monthly Sales Analysis workbook, Montgomery County MD warehouse and retail sales) as a brand-mix and new-launch velocity nowcast for listed spirits, beer and wine owners

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Consumer staples: spirits, beer, wine and ready-to-drink. Target: revenue, margin (mix), guidance (nowcast).
- Verdict: KEEP_WITH_CHANGES. Changes: 1) Vendor/literature check not performed (WebSearch budget exhausted); NABCA, NielsenIQ and Circana remain REASONED as the at-scale sellers of the national version, so crowdedness is lowered to 2. 2) Sources pruned to what returns data: keep Montgomery County (backtestable, 2017+) and Utah (forward-only, archive from FY25 P11); drop Iowa (Socrata resource 404 after migration, new location unknown), NC ABC (prices only), TTB (no files located), PA LCB (no files) and Virginia (challenge page). 3) Coverage correction: Utah state stores do not carry beer at or below 5% ABV, so Boston Beer's largest brands (Twisted Tea Original, Truly) are absent from the Utah ledger (REASONED); SAM and TAP rely on Montgomery County alone. 4) Universe cut to 4 names and the idea is reclassified as a component of a pooled brand-level panel with a 30-event minimum, not a stand-alone signal. 5) Signal-noise lowered to 2 (two small jurisdictions, one atypical), lead time to 12 days, confidence to 10. 6) Texas mixed-beverage receipts retained only as an on-premise control (latest 2026-10-31, TESTED HERE). 7) Keys to create: optional free Socrata app tokens for data.montgomerycountymd.gov and data.texas.gov; no key for the Utah xlsx.
- Mechanism: In control jurisdictions the state (or county) is the sole wholesaler, so its monthly sales ledger by item and supplier is a census of what retailers and bars bought from each brand owner; the quarter's revenue for a listed brand owner is depletions times price, and item-level control-state data give the depletion trend, the price mix (bottle size, YoY by item) and the ramp of new SKUs weeks before the company reports. The signal is the gap between the brand owner's item-aggregated YoY in these ledgers and the depletion growth consensus assumes, with the edge concentrated in brand mix and launches rather than category totals. Applies to Boston Beer (Samuel Adams, Twisted Tea, Truly, Sun Cruiser), MGP/Luxco, Molson Coors, Brown-Forman and Tilray's beer brands.
- Industry context: Seventeen US states and Montgomery County, Maryland control the wholesale (and often retail) sale of spirits, so their ledgers record every bottle a brand owner ships into those markets, by SKU and month, as public records. Brand owners report revenue on shipments to distributors, which track depletions with a lag, so a control-state depletion trend observed mid-quarter leads the reported line.
- Measurement window: Months 1-2 of the fiscal quarter for most names (Utah's September workbook and Montgomery County's September rows were both posted by 2026-11-05/10, TESTED HERE, i.e. ~5-6 weeks after month end; Boston Beer prints ~3.5 weeks after quarter end so month 3 arrives after the print); full quarter for off-cycle reporters such as Brown-Forman (fiscal quarter ends 31 Oct/31 Jan, prints ~5 weeks later).. Lead time: 12 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.3%: 4 names. BF.B (spirits; both ledgers), MGPI/Luxco (spirits; both ledgers), TAP (beer; Montgomery County only, Utah state stores carry only beer above 5% ABV), SAM (Montgomery County only for Twisted Tea/Truly at 5% ABV, Utah only for higher-ABV SKUs such as Twisted Tea Extra and Utopias - REASONED). TLRY dropped (beer is a small share of a cannabis company). Not a stand-alone strategy: a component of a pooled brand-level consumer nowcast panel.
- Hypothesis: For BF.B, MGPI, TAP and SAM, a gap of at least 5 percentage points between the brand owner's item-aggregated year-on-year wholesale case growth in the Montgomery County ledger (plus Utah 9L cases for spirits names) over months 1-2 of the fiscal quarter and the depletion growth implied by final consensus revenue, measured about 40 days after month 2 and at least 10 days before the print, predicts a same-sign revenue surprise and a same-sign T-1 to T+1 abnormal return versus XLP, controlling for consensus revisions, the company's own reported prior-quarter depletion trend, Texas mixed-beverage receipts growth (on-premise control), sector, size and momentum. Trading requires at least 30 pooled events across the brand-level panel; Montgomery County 2017+ is the only backtestable ledger, Utah is forward-only.
- Who already knows: NABCA sells control-state brand-level data to every major supplier and to the sell side, and NielsenIQ/Circana scanner data are the standard consensus inputs, so the category trend and the big brands' trends are priced (REASONED). What is less used is the SKU-level ramp of launches and the small-state item detail for mid-cap owners whose brands are too small to be called out in Nielsen commentary (MGP/Luxco, Tilray beer, Boston Beer's newer brands).
- Vendors selling similar (generator): NABCA (control-state brand data, paid), NielsenIQ and Circana (scanner), Beverage Information Group (REASONED). Consumer Edge and Earnest sell card-panel spend, not brand depletions.. Why ours differs: Free, item-level and monthly from two official ledgers rather than a NABCA licence, with the analysis focused on new-SKU velocity and mix for the mid-cap owners rather than category totals.
- Data sources:
  - Montgomery County, MD Warehouse and Retail Sales (Socrata v76h-r7br): supplier, item_description, item_type, rtl_sales, whs_sales by calendar month [DATA (TESTED HERE 200, rows through 2026-09)] https://data.montgomerycountymd.gov/resource/v76h-r7br.json?$where=calendar_year=2026 AND supplier='BOSTON BEER CORPORATION'&$limit=5000 (update: Monthly, ~5-6 weeks after month end; history: 2017+ with gaps)
  - Utah DABS monthly Sales Analysis Report xlsx (item, class, size, 9L cases, YoY) - spirits and >5% ABV beer only [DATA (TESTED HERE 206)] https://abs.utah.gov/wp-content/uploads/Sales-Analysis_FY27_P03_Sep.xlsx (update: Monthly, ~5-6 weeks after month end; history: FY25 P11 (~May 2025) onward on the page; earlier UNKNOWN (ask DABS))
  - Texas mixed-beverage gross receipts (on-premise control) [DATA (TESTED HERE 200)] https://data.texas.gov/resource/naix-2893.json?$limit=1 (update: Monthly; history: 2007+ (REASONED); latest obligation_end_date 2026-10-31)
- Reviewer source checks: https://abs.utah.gov/wp-content/uploads/Sales-Analysis_FY27_P03_Sep.xlsx -> 206 (TESTED HERE, range request served) Monthly item-level workbook downloadable; archive on the page only from FY25 P1…; https://data.montgomerycountymd.gov/resource/v76h-r7br.json?$select=calendar_ye… -> 200 DATA (TESTED HERE) Rows through 2026-09 (11,382 items); BOSTON BEER CORPORATION rows present (e.g.…; https://data.iowa.gov/api/views/m3tr-qhgy.json -> 404 (TESTED HERE); data.iowa.gov root 200 Socrata resource gone after migration; new location UNKNOWN. Dropped.; https://abc2.nc.gov/Pricing/PriceList -> 200 (TESTED HERE) Price list only, no brand sales; dropped.; https://www.ttb.gov/statistics -> 200 (TESTED HERE) JS shell; release files not located; dropped as a source, national trend taken …; https://data.texas.gov/resource/naix-2893.json?$select=obligation_end_date_yyyy… -> 200 DATA (TESTED HERE) Latest obligation_end_date 2026-10-31; establishment-level on-premise receipts,…; https://www.pa.gov/agencies/lcb -> 200 (TESTED HERE) No data files located; dropped. Virginia ABC returned a Cloudflare challenge in…
- Cost: $0.
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 2 / 2 / 1. Confidence: 10%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): Two small jurisdictions (about 1.3% of US population, with Utah's atypical consumption mix) may not represent national depletions, the listed universe is five to seven names, NABCA already gives the sell side the same information at national scale, and shipment timing versus depletions can diverge by a quarter when distributors destock.
- Red team (reviewer): One county of 1.0 million people plus one atypical state cannot represent national depletions, and the sell side already receives the full 17-state version from NABCA and scanner data from Nielsen/Circana, so any gap versus consensus is more likely measurement noise or a regional mix effect than information; distributor shipment timing diverges from depletions by a quarter when wholesalers destock, and with four names the test cannot separate a brand-level effect from the beer-versus-spirits category trend that XLP and peer prints already price.

## G09 NHSN weekly state-level inpatient occupancy and admissions, bed-weighted to each operator's state footprint from CMS Hospital Enrollments, as a quarterly volume nowcast for hospital, rehab, LTCH and behavioral operators and healthcare staffing firms

- Category: Regulatory. Sector focus: Healthcare services: acute-care, behavioral, rehabilitation and LTCH operators, travel-nurse staffing, hospital supply. Target: revenue (admissions), margin (contract labour), guidance (nowcast).
- Verdict: REJECT. Changes: Vendor/literature web searches could not be run: this turn's shared WebSearch budget was exhausted before my calls, so vendors_found and literature_found are empty rather than guessed; the candidate's own Kaufman Hall / Strata and Definitive Healthcare references were not independently seen. Data checks: verified the NHSN field list (no all-cause admissions), hospitals-reporting counts across the 2024 regime change and 2026 erosion, absence of any facility-level NHSN HRD dataset in both Socrata catalogs, the CMS Enrollments header (no beds), the CMS POS file as the correct bed-weight source, and the PBJ ~4-month lag. Lowered signal_noise 2 to 1, lead time 21 to 12 calendar days, universe 20-25 names to 9, confidence 12 to 8. Legal risk stays 1 (US government public-domain data, Socrata token optional). Reject reason: The hypothesis as written cannot be measured: NHSN HRD publishes all-cause occupancy (a census) but admissions only for confirmed COVID, flu and RSV, so the 'at least 3% change in weighted total admissions' test has no data behind it, and occupancy = admissions x length of stay diverges from the adjusted-admissions metric operators guide on. The proxy quality is poor: state totals are dominated by non-profit systems and the listed operators are a small minority of beds in nearly every state (REASONED; HCA, the one large for-profit, is outside the universe), so the bed-weighted index mostly measures other people's hospitals, i.e. regional respiratory seasonality that analysts already read directly from the same public NHSN pathogen series. The year-on-year base is broken for the only recent history: May-Oct 2024 had ~36% of hospitals reporting (TESTED HERE), making Q2-Q3 2025 comparisons unusable, and 2026 reporting compliance has slid from 89% to 80% for the newest week, so level counts drift with the panel. Universe is 9 names (not 20-25) and roughly 4 clean quarters, far too few for a stand-alone test; the pre-2024 facility file is COVID-distorted. Lead time is 10-17 calendar days (THC prints ~3 weeks after quarter end), not 21, and the quarter's last week is ~80% complete when first published. Data is free and legal, but I would not stake effort on it ahead of the other candidates.
- Mechanism: Since the November 2024 CMS condition of participation, every hospital reports weekly bed capacity, occupancy and admissions to CDC's NHSN, and CDC publishes jurisdiction-level weekly totals within about a week of the week's end. Hospital operators' quarterly revenue is same-facility admissions times revenue per admission, so weighting each state's occupancy and admissions change by the share of an operator's beds in that state (from the CMS Hospital Enrollments file, which lists each facility's legal entity and state, mapped to the parent via Exhibit 21) gives an operator-specific volume index that is final about ten days after quarter end, three to four weeks before the print. Occupancy also drives contract-labour demand for staffing firms. The facility-level NHSN HRD file that the gap assumed could not be found in the open-data catalogue (UNKNOWN), so this version is state-weighted rather than facility-exact.
- Industry context: Hospital operators' quarters are driven by admissions volume and the mix of payers, and most of them are concentrated in a handful of states; since late 2024 every hospital must report bed occupancy and admissions weekly to CDC, which publishes state totals within a week. A state's occupancy change is therefore a close proxy for the operator's own facilities when the operator holds a large share of that state's beds.
- Measurement window: Full fiscal quarter of weekly readings; the quarter's last week is published about 6 days after it ends (TESTED HERE 2026-11-11: latest weekendingdate 2026-10-31 for all jurisdictions; dataset updated 2026-11-06). Operators print 4-5 weeks after quarter end.. Lead time: 12 days.
- Universe coverage: ~0.7%: 7 operators (CYH, UHS, ARDT, THC, SEM, EHC, ACHC-unverified) plus AMN and CCRN, i.e. 9 of ~1,300 names. SGRY (ASCs) and the supply/device names are not covered by NHSN and should not be counted.
- Hypothesis: Not restated as a live signal. If revived only as a validation study: bed-weighted (POS CRTFD_BED_CNT) year-on-year change in state pctinptbedsocc over the fiscal quarter, computed from weeks with at least 85% of hospitals reporting in both base and current year (so Q4-2025 onward only), predicts same-sign same-facility adjusted-admissions surprise for THC, UHS, CYH, ARDT, controlling for the NHSN flu/COVID/RSV admission share and consensus revisions. The admissions leg of the original hypothesis is dropped because no all-cause admissions field exists.
- Who already knows: Hospital analysts use the respiratory share of admissions (the S09 content), the operators' own mid-quarter commentary, Strata/Kaufman Hall monthly flash reports (paid, aggregate) and Medicare claims (lagged); state-weighted NHSN occupancy by operator is not in current previews (REASONED, per the brief). Occupancy itself is a public weekly number, so the national level is known; the operator-specific weighting and the quarter-end total are the unpriced part.
- Vendors selling similar (generator): Kaufman Hall / Strata (National Hospital Flash Report, aggregate), Definitive Healthcare (facility reference data, paid). No vendor is known to sell NHSN-based operator volume indices (UNKNOWN).. Why ours differs: Free weekly official reporting rolled up to the listed operator by bed-weighted state footprint, available ten days after quarter end; cheaper and faster than claims-based or survey-based volume estimates, though coarser than the facility file would be.
- Data sources:
  - CDC NHSN Weekly HRD Metrics by Jurisdiction (Socrata ua7e-t2fy): numinptbeds, numinptbedsocc, pctinptbedsocc, numinptbedshosprep; respiratory admissions only [DATA (TESTED HERE 2026-11-11, 200)] https://data.cdc.gov/resource/ua7e-t2fy.json?$where=jurisdiction='TX'&$order=weekendingdate DESC&$limit=200 (update: Weekly, ~6 days after week end, but the newest week is ~80% complete at first publication and backfills; history: 2020-08-08 onward; May-Oct 2024 covers only ~36% of hospitals (voluntary period); 2026 coverage eroding to 80-87%)
  - CMS Provider of Services file (quarterly): CRTFD_BED_CNT by CCN and STATE_CD for bed weights [DATA (TESTED HERE 2026-11-11, 200; header verified)] https://data.cms.gov/sites/default/files/2026-10/e5a8afbb-a1f4-4d3b-bdb9-3968e194da0e/Hospital_and_other.DATA.Q3_2026.csv (update: Quarterly; history: Multi-year via data.cms.gov catalog)
  - CMS Hospital Enrollments monthly CSV: CCN to legal-entity name and state (no beds) [DATA (TESTED HERE 2026-11-11, 200)] https://data.cms.gov/sites/default/files/2026-02/8a659c8c-a372-4796-9752-a22de42a5720/Hospital_Enrollments_2026.02.02.csv (update: Monthly; history: 2022-11 onward)
  - Operator 10-K Item 2 Properties tables (licensed beds by state) via EDGAR submissions API, for the entity-to-parent mapping instead of Exhibit 21 fuzzy matching [DATA (EDGAR submissions API is DATA in Phase 0; this specific CIK not probed)] https://data.sec.gov/submissions/CIK0001108109.json (update: Annual; history: 2009+ (XBRL era))
  - HHS Protect facility-level capacity file (discontinued) for validating operator-exact occupancy vs reported same-facility admissions [DATA (TESTED HERE 2026-11-11, 200)] https://healthdata.gov/resource/anag-cw7u.json?$where=hospital_pk='050191'&$order=collection_week DESC (update: Discontinued 2024-04-21; history: 2020-07 to 2024-04)
- Reviewer source checks: https://data.cdc.gov/resource/ua7e-t2fy.json?$where=jurisdiction='USA'&$order=w… -> 200 (TESTED HERE 2026-11-11) 326 USA rows, 2020-08-08 to 2026-10-31. FIELD LIST VERIFIED: there is NO total-…; https://data.cdc.gov/resource/vdzy-6i9v.json?$limit=2 -> 200 Admission levels/rates by jurisdiction; respiratory pathogens only.; https://data.cdc.gov/resource/mpgq-jmmr.json?$limit=1 -> 200 Preliminary HRD by jurisdiction, updated 2026-11-04 per catalog.; https://data.cdc.gov/resource/rhwp-grxi.json?$limit=1 -> 200 Historical HRD by jurisdiction, updated 2026-11-06 per catalog.; https://data.cdc.gov/api/catalog/v1?q=NHSN%20hospital%20respiratory&limit=30&on… -> 200 Catalog search on data.cdc.gov and healthdata.gov (q=NHSN hospital facility) re…; https://www.cdc.gov/nhsn/psc/hospital-respiratory-reporting.html -> 403 CDC page blocked from the container; irrelevant since no facility-level file is…; https://data.cms.gov/sites/default/files/2026-02/8a659c8c-a372-4796-9752-a22de4… -> 200 Header verified: ENROLLMENT ID, CCN, ORGANIZATION NAME, DOING BUSINESS AS NAME,…; https://data.cms.gov/sites/default/files/2026-10/e5a8afbb-a1f4-4d3b-bdb9-3968e1… -> 200 CMS Provider of Services file (found via data.cms.gov/data.json, quarterly). He…; https://healthdata.gov/resource/anag-cw7u.json?$where=hospital_pk='050191'&$ord… -> 200 Discontinued HHS Protect facility file; latest collection_week 2024-04-21; faci…; https://data.cms.gov/data.json -> 200 3,528 datasets. PBJ Daily Nurse Staffing CY2026Q2 file is listed with release d…
- Cost: $0 (Socrata app token free).
- Scores (crowdedness / signal-noise / legal): 3 / 1 / 1. Confidence: 8%.
- Strongest reason it is wrong (generator): State totals are dominated by non-profit systems whose volumes may not move with the listed operator's, so the bed-weighted index could be a market-wide seasonality proxy already captured by the respiratory share; operators guide on volumes and give mid-quarter colour at conferences; and without the facility-level file the universe is seven operators plus two staffing names, too few for a stand-alone test.
- Red team (reviewer): The bed-weighted state occupancy index is a proxy for regional respiratory-season intensity, which is already public in the same NHSN release (flu/COVID/RSV admissions) and already used in sell-side hospital previews, while the operator-specific residual is unobservable because the listed operators hold a small share of each state's beds and NHSN has no all-cause admissions at all; any apparent predictive power in a backtest would therefore be the respiratory-share factor plus a broken 2024 base (36% reporting panel) masquerading as an operator volume nowcast, and with ~9 names and ~4 clean quarters nothing would survive a factor control.
